# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

PoseStudio is an early-stage, open-source Qt6/C++ desktop application for 3D character creation, posing, and scene building. The codebase today is a main window shell, an asset manager side panel backed by SQLite, a Preferences dialog, a menu bar (with File → New / Open / Save of `.pss` project files — the whole scene, see Project files below), and a Vulkan-backed 3D viewport (`src/viewport/`) that draws an infinite floor grid through a real orbit camera and renders lit, textured geometry — both Wavefront `.OBJ` models and **fully rigged, morphable character figures** imported from their native scene format (`.duf`/`.dsf`) — and, since 0.4.0, **rigged characters from FBX files** (`import/fbx/`, read with ufbx), conformed to PoseStudio's own RIG STANDARD (`scene/ik/rigroles`, `rigconformer`, `docs/RIG_STANDARD.md`: every bone its role by structure, every joint the standard's frame, order, limits and name) so the whole posing system works on them. The figure pipeline is now the largest part of the project: per-material-zone geometry + physically-based materials, baked character-shape morphs, a skeleton with GPU dual-quaternion skinning, joint-driven pose correctives, Catmull-Clark subdivision, and an interactive posing UI (click-a-joint + rotate gizmo, anatomical joint limits, full-body IK by dragging a joint — a whole-body joint-space solve with planted feet, balance, stepping and self-collision, see `scene/ik/` — pose save/load, undo/redo) — see the 3D Viewport section below. The viewport also offers a selectable shade-mode picker (eighteen modes including a photoreal PBR mode), MSAA anti-aliasing, and image-based lighting (IBL) from an HDR environment — with an Environment properties tab for live control (the analytic key/fill/rim rig is retained for the non-photoreal shade modes), and a Transform properties tab that shows and sets the selected joint's rotation as three 0-100 dials (Bend / Side-Side / Twist, which B / S / T and the mouse turn in the viewport — see The Transform tab below). A **user manual** ships inside the app (Help → User Manual, F1; `docs/manual/`, see the User manual section) and every user-visible change updates it; a **What's New window** (`src/help/whatsnewdialog`, Help → What's New…) opens the running version's CHANGELOG section once, on the first start of a new version. The animation/timeline systems and final-frame (production) rendering described in README.md are still roadmap — they don't exist in code yet.

## Build

Requires Qt 6 (developed against 6.11.1, MSVC 2022 64-bit), CMake 3.21+, and the **LunarG Vulkan SDK** (https://vulkan.lunarg.com — supplies the Vulkan headers, loader, validation layers, and the `glslc` shader compiler). `find_package(Vulkan REQUIRED)` aborts configuration with a clear message if the SDK is absent, so the viewport can't be built without it.

```
cmake -B build -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH="C:/Qt/6.11.1/msvc2022_64"
cmake --build build
```

If `CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH` isn't passed, `CMakeLists.txt` falls back to the hardcoded path `C:/Qt/6.11.1/msvc2022_64` — never commit a change to that fallback to point at your personal Qt install.

GLM (math), AMD's Vulkan Memory Allocator (VMA), tinyobjloader (the OBJ importer's parser), stb_image (`.hdr` environment loading), and tinyexr (`.exr` environment loading) are pulled in header-only via CMake `FetchContent` at pinned tags/commits (see the "3D VIEWPORT DEPENDENCIES" block) — a fresh clone needs only Qt + the Vulkan SDK, nothing vendored. tinyobjloader, like VMA, stb_image, and tinyexr, is a single-header lib whose implementation is compiled in exactly one TU (`import/obj/objimporter.cpp` via `#define TINYOBJLOADER_IMPLEMENTATION`; stb_image in `viewport/stb_impl.cpp` via `#define STB_IMAGE_IMPLEMENTATION`; tinyexr in `viewport/tinyexr_impl.cpp` via `#define TINYEXR_IMPLEMENTATION` — its ZIP inflate rides on the miniz sources already compiled into the target, so no zlib). They're *populated* (downloaded) but their own CMake isn't run; we consume their headers directly via `target_include_directories`. GLM's Vulkan-convention flags (`GLM_FORCE_RADIANS`, `GLM_FORCE_DEPTH_ZERO_TO_ONE`) are set once project-wide as `target_compile_definitions` — never per-file, or translation units disagree on matrix layout (a silent-corruption bug).

The **FBX importer** (`import/fbx/`, 2026-11-03) adds **ufbx** (`ufbx.h` + `ufbx.c`, C99, MIT OR Unlicense — the official FBX SDK is proprietary and GPL-incompatible, never) the same way: `FetchContent` at a pinned tag, `ufbx.c` compiled straight into the target like miniz, `${ufbx_SOURCE_DIR}` on the include path, its own CMake never run.

The **native figure importer** (`import/figure/`, see the 3D Viewport section) adds two more `FetchContent` deps under the same no-configure treatment: **nlohmann/json** (header-only JSON, kept out of Qt so the importer core stays Qt-free — `QJsonDocument` would drag Qt in) and **miniz** (to inflate gzip-compressed scene files). miniz isn't header-only — it ships C sources — so two accommodations exist: (1) `project()` enables the **C** language (`LANGUAGES C CXX`) — without it CMake silently classifies miniz's `.c` files as non-buildable `<None>` and they never link; miniz's split sources (`miniz.c`/`miniz_tinfl.c`/`miniz_tdef.c`/`miniz_zip.c`) are compiled directly into the target via `target_sources`; and (2) a tiny shim header `cmake/miniz-shim/miniz_export.h` (on the include path) satisfies miniz.h's `#include "miniz_export.h"` — that file is normally generated by miniz's CMake, which we skip; for a static in-binary build its export macros are no-ops.

The build links `Qt6::Widgets`, `Qt6::Gui`, `Qt6::Sql`, `Qt6::Concurrent` (the off-thread IBL bake), and `Vulkan::Vulkan` (the loader; `QVulkanInstance`/`QWindow` live in Qt6::Gui). `Qt6::Network` is linked for exactly two things, the anonymous install ping (`src/core/installping.*`, see the Install ping paragraph below) and the startup update check (`src/core/updatecheck.*`, the paragraph after it); no Qt 3D/Quick modules. `CMAKE_AUTOMOC`/`AUTOUIC`/`AUTORCC` are all on, so new `Q_OBJECT` classes, `.ui` files, and resources just need to be added to the `add_executable` source list / `resources.qrc` — no manual moc/rcc invocation.

Two post-build mirror steps copy on-disk content next to the executable on every build (both true mirrors — `rm -rf` then `copy_directory`, not overlays, so renamed/removed source files don't linger): (1) `resources/Maquettes/` (the built-in asset library, real on-disk content — not embedded via `resources.qrc`, since the Asset Manager browses libraries through `QDir`, not Qt's resource filesystem); and (2) the compiled SPIR-V shaders. Shaders are GLSL under `src/viewport/shaders/`, compiled to `*.spv` by `glslc` via a per-shader `add_custom_command` (gated through the `PoseStudioShaders` custom target that `PoseStudio` depends on), then mirrored to `<applicationDirPath>/shaders` where `VulkanRenderer` loads them at runtime. Add a new shader by dropping the `.vert`/`.frag` in that folder and appending its name to the `SHADER_SOURCES` list in `CMakeLists.txt`.

**Cross-platform posture**: PoseStudio is meant to be fully cross-platform (Windows, macOS, Linux). The stack is portable by construction — Qt6, C++17, CMake, SQLite, Vulkan — and the codebase carries no `#ifdef` platform branches; platform differences are absorbed by Qt (window/surface creation via `QVulkanInstance`/`QWindow`) and CMake rather than hand-written per-OS code. Accommodations already in place: writable data lives in `QStandardPaths::AppDataLocation` (an installed app's own directory is read-only on macOS/Linux — see the Database section); the Windows icon resource (`resources/PoseStudio.rc`) and the VERSIONINFO resource (`resources/version.rc.in`, a `configure_file()` template stamped from `project(VERSION)` into `<build>/version.rc` so there is still one copy of the number; Explorer's Properties → Details, Task Manager, AV/SmartScreen heuristics) are added to the target only `if(WIN32)`; and because macOS runs Vulkan through **MoltenVK** (a Vulkan-on-Metal translation layer), `VulkanContext` enables the spec-mandated `VK_KHR_portability_subset` device extension whenever a device advertises it (a no-op on Windows/Linux). **Caveat:** only the Windows/MSVC build has been exercised so far — the macOS/Linux paths are correct by construction but unbuilt/unrun, and the one known runtime unknown on macOS is whether Qt's `QVulkanInstance` auto-adds the *instance*-level portability-enumeration extension (verify on real Apple hardware before claiming macOS support).

The one automated test is the **IK harness** — `tools/ikharness/ikharness.cpp`, CMake target `PoseStudioIkHarness` (built by default, Qt-free and Vulkan-free: it links only `scene/ik/*` + `scene/armature*`), registered with CTest as `ik-harness`. It runs unit phases for the IK math/constraints plus a drag-loop simulator that drives the REAL engine loop (the `Armature`) on a dumped figure skeleton — see the FBIK section for what it gates and how to get a dump (`POSESTUDIO_DUMP_SKELETON`, passed to the harness as its first argument or `POSESTUDIO_IK_SKELETON`; without one the real-figure phases are skipped). Run it before claiming any IK change works. **The real-figure gates are calibrated against the BASE figure's skeleton** (dump the reference generation's basic female preset, the unmorphed rig): a dump of a morphed CHARACTER (different joint centres, hip 3.4cm lower, a figure-scale dial) fails three phases on the UNCHANGED code — the strained release steps once and drifts a foot 13cm, the 45cm stepping chest drag takes 0 steps (chest 26cm from the cursor), the pinned-hand crouch reaches 9.8cm of its 10cm gate — so a character dump is not a regression signal; it is a real robustness gap (the stepping trigger and crouch depth are tuned to one rig's proportions) recorded here for a future round (found 2026-09-09 by A/B-ing stashed sources). UPDATE 2026-09-10: the knee-and-feet round closed two of the three (the strained pull no longer steps, the chest drag steps again on both rigs), and the pinned crouch's depth gate was relaxed (9cm; base 10.7-11.4, character 9.2-9.8); the rig-coverage round that followed extended the suite to EIGHT dumps (`D:/PoseStudio/ikdumps/`, every figure generation; all eight pass 43/43 since the second self-collision round of 2026-09-18 (39/39 since the kneel round of 2026-09-17; the mesh-fitted volumes round of the same day keeps 43/43 and reads each dump's `.mesh` sidecar — the figure's body mesh sample, written by the same dump — to fit the self-collision volumes as the app does; all eight have one since the same day's skin-weight fix) — the live-contact round of 2026-09-11 added the all-fours, rise and kneel phases, the wheel-rotate round the two mid-drag rotation phases (removed 2026-09-30), the heel-lift round of 2026-09-16 the deep crouch, the landing round the lift-then-lower, the self-collision round the arm-across and foot-across, the sitting round of 2026-09-17 the sit, the second self-collision round the FK limb sweeps, the hand-onto-forearm, the foot-onto-shin and the legs-crossed; the finger raise's head gate, long open on one generation, is conditional on the shoulder NOT ending limit-bound: on that rig the pre-posed shoulder reaches its 40° of forward raise and the finisher's trunk stage leans the chest 9° to put the fingertip on the cursor, the designed reach strategy, and the head moves 6cm with it — a slow raise never reads as up-intent, so nothing holds the chest) — run them all before claiming an IK change works, and re-dump after any dump-format change. There is no linter. The one CI workflow is the release pipeline below. The harness itself is five TUs in `namespace ikharness` — `ikharness.cpp` (main + `--custom`), `report.{h,cpp}` (gate/info rows), `simulator.{h,cpp}` (the scenario runner over the real `Armature` loop + the idle-tremble metrics), `unitphases.{h,cpp}`, `realphases.{h,cpp}` (every `[real]` phase and its gates — since 2026-10-29 the `RealPhases` class: the state every phase shared as a local of one 5,000-line function (the report, the rig, the T-pose rest pre-pose, a rest-pose probe armature, the floor scenarios' sizing) and the helpers (`run`, `runIdle`, `limitBoundEnd`, `phaseLegs`, `indexOf`, `under`) as members, and sixteen phase FAMILIES — `handPhases` … `tailPhases`, each a member function holding its `if (report.wants("…"))` blocks VERBATIM — run in the file's order by `runAll()`; add a phase by writing its block into the family it belongs with, and a family by declaring it in the class and calling it from `runAll` in order). **Since the solver replacement of 2026-09-18** (the FBIK section) the suite is 152 phases (the four GROUP phases and the three ARM-SWING phases of 2026-10-27 — see THE GROUP and THE STRIDE SWING; the two MID-DRAG ROTATE phases went on 2026-09-30 with the X/Y/Z wheel — see THE JOINT MOUSE MODE; the TRANSFORM-DIALS phase of the same day, an Armature test that is no IK — see The Transform tab; the four FIGURE-MOVE phases of 2026-09-28, which replaced the two scoped trunk phases — see THE FIGURE MOVE; the three LANDING BOUNCE phases of the same day; the three DIGIT phases of the same day before them, which replaced the finger-grab raise — see FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG; the six SCOPED-IK phases of 2026-09-26 before them; the two tiptoe phases, the two girdle phases and the four hand-lay phases of 2026-09-25 before them) (120 then; round 90's two determinism phases and the three REACH-DOWN phases of 2026-09-25 since) — the joint-constraint unit phase went with the code it tested, the `[unit] joint-space solver` phase came in, then the stand-back-up phase, the four getting-up phases (out of a kneel by the hip and the chest, out of a sit by the chest and the head) the six foot-placement phases, the four knee-drag phases, the four elbow-drag phases, the two hip-sway phases, the three hip-hinge phases, the three girdle-grab phases, the hips-back walk, the five trunk-follow phases, the six limb-grab phases, the two lifted-foot phases, the two kneel-limb phases, the lunge, the two crouch-lean phases, the four trunk-bow phases, the five seated phases, the three posed-start phases, the eight head-right phases the four floor-seat phases, the user-sized kneel, the pectoral grab, the kneel sat back onto the heels, the kneel taken onto all fours by the chest, the prone descent by the hips, the chest lifted off all fours onto her knees and the half kneel — and all eight rigs pass 120/120 on the one remaining solver (the four rigs that carry an eyelash follower were RE-DUMPED that day, after the follower merge stopped appending twelve duplicate bones — see the follower-addons paragraph; the old dumps sit in `ikdumps/before-follower-name-merge-2026-09-18/`); the history in this paragraph up to that date describes gates of the removed position-space path, several of which were re-set then (each annotated in `realphases.cpp`). **Since 2026-09-21 every figure is put ON THE FLOOR at import** (FIGURES STAND ON THE FLOOR, in the FBIK section), so the eight reference rigs were RE-DUMPED that day (the base rig rested 8.4mm over y = 0 as authored, the male 4.6; the old dumps sit in `ikdumps/before-grounding-2026-09-21/`) and pass 120/120. Every real-figure phase also reports `left/right asymmetry gained (mm)` — the worst distance between a right-side joint and its left twin reflected across the sagittal plane through the hips, end less start — and it is GATED (5mm, `Scenario::asymmetryGateMm`) BY CONSTRUCTION on every SYMMETRIC gesture that takes no step (`RunResult::symmetricGesture`: every grab a centre bone, no waypoint with a sideways part, symmetric pins and pre-pose, no noise or wheel nudge): such a problem has a symmetric answer, and what the pose gains instead is one degree of freedom standing in for a dearer one — legs folded over to one side, a corkscrewed spine — which the side view such a drag is looked at from cannot show. It is applied only on a rig that is ITSELF symmetric at rest (every base figure is, to 0.0002mm; `RunResult::symmetricGesture` also needs the pose the drag FOUND to be symmetric): a body's own asymmetry is amplified, rightly — a leg a millimetre longer stands 4 degrees more bent at the knee under a level pelvis (near straight a leg's length goes as the SQUARE of the knee's angle: 18mm at the knee), and skin that differs left to right kneels 9mm askew on a perfectly symmetric skeleton — so on a character the row is reported, and the sweep lists lopsided results apart. It reads 0.0 on every symmetric gesture on the eight base rigs, and its first two catches are fixed (2026-09-21): getting onto all fours landed the hands 35-60mm lopsided on six rigs (the idle arms' hang, below: its collision stop was ORDER-DEPENDENT), and a sit pushed 12cm past the seat ended with both knees 5-7cm to one side on two generations (the floor's rows on the pelvis's flesh were lopsided: see THE FLOOR STOPS HER FLESH). For a day it had been silently OFF on the main figure family: that family's rest pose read 19mm "lopsided", because `…Carpal2` names one bone of the palm on the left and ANOTHER on the right — the pairing below now checks a name's twin by its mirrored bind position — a reminder that a gate that cannot fire says nothing. **THE CHARACTER SWEEP** (`tools/ikharness/sweep/`: `dump.sh <folder>` dumps every figure preset listed on stdin through the real application — so a dump is the rig the app would pose: morphed joint centres, figure scale, grounding — `run.sh <dumps> <results>` runs the whole harness on each, four at a time, and `summarize.py` sorts the failed gates into NEAR-MISSES (and lists rows of the left/right asymmetry gate apart, uncounted: on a character they are the body's own as often as a symmetry break), a gate calibrated on the reference rigs exceeded by a quarter at most, and REAL ones, which it lists; its exit code is their number) is the instrument behind "it works on any body": the reference rigs are eight BASE figures, and a custom character differs in exactly what the rig's thresholds were tuned on. On 23 custom characters of two generations (a toon, a child, a dwarf, an alien, a heavy build, several realistic women and men) this round's first sweep found four bugs no reference rig shows — figures that do not stand on the floor, the solver's active-set hair trap, a down intent that never ended, a pin-hold reach in absolute centimetres — and the one of 2026-09-21 read 16 of 23 with no real failure (6 with every gate green), 33 near-misses and 12 real misses on seven characters (body-volume penetration of 12-14mm against a 10mm gate on crossed limbs of three, a top-heavy toon's bow taking two steps and not returning exactly, the newest generation's heaviest character's kneel landing at 117mm in one tick, a child-scale walk's knee twist of 23 degrees on a dwarf, one lay-down's hold jumping 37mm, one character whose rig is itself 6mm asymmetric). After the next round the same sweep reads 16 of 23 with no real failure, 31 near-misses, 12 real misses on seven characters and four lopsided rows on two (the newest generation's heaviest character's 117mm kneel landing is GONE: it was the mis-taken pelvis bone pricing its thighs; new is a dwarf's hands rising 32mm off their plant on all fours); after the all-fours round (2026-09-22) 17 of 23 with no real failure, 34 near-misses, 10 real on six — the same crossed-limb penetrations on three, the toon's stepping bow, the dwarf's walk, and one rig whose own mirror asymmetry is 6mm — two lopsided rows on one, and the new kneel-onto-all-fours phase passes on all 23; with the segment-end joint cap (the self-collision section, 2026-09-22) 20 of 23, 32 near-misses and 7 real on three — the dwarf's walk, the toon's bow and the asymmetric rig — the three crossed-limb penetrations gone (0.0mm); with the prone descent (2026-09-22) 37 near-misses and 8 real, the new one a toon's 223mm hand twitch in the prone hold. After the half-kneel round (2026-09-22) 30 near-misses and 8 real on three (the toon's bow, two heavy bodies' kneel-up, the asymmetric rig), 20 of 23 with no real failure; with the kneel-up's seat lift (2026-09-23) 31 and 6 on two — the toon's bow and the asymmetric rig — 21 of 23. With the bow's follow gate and the unfold (2026-09-23) 34 near-misses and ONE real — the asymmetric rig's own mirror row — 22 of 23; the knee-draw round of the same day (a hard kneeling knee, the thigh's lateral reach, the release's floor allowance) leaves it there: 34 and one, 22 of 23, the four characters whose kneeling knee slid 38-68mm holding it to 0.0001. The prone rounds of the same day (the descent's hand-ceiling release, the exact bind, no lift-off under a descent) leave it at 34 and one too, and the weight-shift round (2026-09-25) at 32 and one, 22 of 23 (the two near-misses gone are the kneel-up's on two heavy bodies) — and the sweep earned its keep twice on the way: the full ceiling release read a heavy character's prone as a 270mm collapse with 38mm through the floor, and a fold-only release two more characters' at 205-409, before the rig's descent rule brought all three down onto their hands (the heavy character's hip joint 443 → 203mm). THE IN-APP GALLERIES ARE RUN ON CUSTOM CHARACTERS TOO (three galleries on six bodies, 2026-09-21: the scripts name bones and drag in pixels, so they run on anything): that audit found what no harness number could — a click on a character's chest grabs a PECTORAL bone, whose grab trailed the cursor by 4cm; a whole generation reclined 41 degrees when sat back onto her heels; and the test tool itself silently posing the wrong limb where another covers it on some body. The dumps are vendor rig data and live outside the repository; run the sweep after any change to contacts, the floor, or anything sized from the rig. THE GALLERIES WERE RUN ON THE OLDEST AND NEWEST GENERATIONS TOO (2026-09-25): no IK flaw — every miss was the gestures' base-figure sizing (a knee raise moves the hips 134-142mm where the base's wide A-pose moves them 220 and the gate reads 150; a hand pulled forward-and-up from a T-pose arm ends over the head; the bow's balance/step knife edge, where the newest generation rests 17mm short with its feet still while the base steps twice) or THE T-POSE ARMS: the oldest generations import with their arms horizontal, which the hang rightly reads as POSED (90° from down, outside `kArmHangCone`), so they ride every trunk drag stiffly and weigh in front — the bow rested 37mm short with them and 0.1mm with the arms first turned down (the harness's shoulder pre-pose; `fk lShldr 0 0 -65` / `fk rShldr 0 0 65` in a script — `pose` there loads a FILE). By design: the bind is the figure as imported and Reset Pose must return to it; the manual says to lower the arms first. AND ON FOUR CUSTOM BODIES AFTER ROUNDS 91-96 (2026-09-25: a toon, a stylized narrow figure, a heavy male, the newest generation's heavy build — the weight shift, the reach-down fold, the contrapposto, the tiptoe and the collar box had been looked at in-app only on the base figure): the heavy male green in all four galleries with no pop; the toon's kneel-to-all-fours rose into a plank (the kneel hold's release, above — the one fix); the rest gesture sizing (a knee raised straight up in the front view stands OUT to the side on a narrow stance once the weight shift has moved the hips: the knee is held at the cursor, the direct-manipulation contract) and the known soft spots (the prone-up's folded arm unfolding in a tick popped a head 105mm and a hand 145 on two bodies — both gone since 2026-09-26 — the head's with the palm eases' lead cap, the hand's with the unloaded hand's lift-off and the knee share, see AND ON A HALF-FLAT PRONE; the toon's prone rests 21cm short).

**Packaging & releases** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, `packaging/`, `docs/RELEASING.md`): pushing a `v*` tag builds and publishes a Windows installer + portable zip to a GitHub Release — Qt via `install-qt-action` (version pinned in the workflow's `QT_VERSION`; includes `qtimageformats`), Vulkan SDK via LunarG's silent installer (found through the `C:/VulkanSDK/*` fallback glob in CMakeLists), `windeployqt --no-compiler-runtime` for the Qt runtime, the **Visual C++ runtime app-local** (exactly the `msvcp140*`/`vcruntime140*` DLLs a `dumpbin /dependents` scan of the staged binaries imports, copied from the building VS's `Microsoft.VC14x.CRT` redist folder — `--compiler-runtime` silently deployed nothing on the runner (no `VCINSTALLDIR`) and would only have yielded the admin-only `vc_redist` installer, so a machine without the runtime failed at launch with a missing VCRUNTIME140.dll; the UCRT is OS-provided on Win10/11), the **Khronos Vulkan loader** (`vulkan-1.dll` — the exe imports it, and a machine whose display driver has no Vulkan support has none, so without a bundled copy Windows refused to START the app before `main()`, ahead of the viewport's no-Vulkan fallback and the install ping; the SDK installer's VulkanRT helper puts a version-matched loader in the runner's System32, which the stage step copies next to the exe — the app folder wins the DLL search — with its license under `licenses/` from `packaging/licenses/`; verified by launching the packaged app with every ICD disabled: it starts, degrades to the fallback, and pings), the **license texts** (`LICENSE.txt` — the project's GPL v3 — next to the exe, and `licenses/` from `packaging/licenses/`: LGPL v3 for Qt, the ICU and Mesa notices for the runtimes windeployqt ships beside it, the Vulkan loader's, and `NOTICES.txt`, the index, rendered with `QT_VERSION` so it names the exact Qt shipped and its source archive — LGPL v3 §4 asks for the prominent notice, the LGPL+GPL texts, and a relink path, which the separate Qt DLLs are; §4(c)'s runtime notice is Help → About Qt, `QApplication::aboutQt()` in `menumanager.cpp`; add a file to `packaging/licenses/` whenever a new redistributable joins the package — the exe's own compiled-in libraries (GLM, VMA, tinyobjloader, stb, tinyexr, nlohmann/json, miniz, and since 2026-11-03 ufbx) are NOT yet listed there), and Inno Setup (`packaging/PoseStudio.iss`) for the installer. Per-user install, no admin; the `AppId` GUID in the .iss must never change (it's how upgrades find existing installs). The stock HDRI set (~50 MB, CC0) is deliberately NOT in the repo — it's an asset zip on the `stock-content-v1` pre-release that the workflow downloads and the installer places into `{userdocs}\My PoseStudio Library\hdri` (`onlyifdoesntexist uninsneveruninstall` — never clobber or uninstall user content); this is the packaging step the "HDRIs are user content" note above defers to. The release-notes header comes from `packaging/release-notes-template.md`; a `workflow_dispatch` run builds everything but publishes nothing (artifacts only) — use it to test pipeline changes. Full maintainer guide: `docs/RELEASING.md`.

## Architecture

**Source layout**: `src/` is organized into one folder per subsystem — `core/` (non-visual services: the database bootstrap + shared connection (`database.h`: `appDatabase()`), the `AssetLibraries` repository (`assetlibraries.h` — the ONE reader/writer of the AssetLibraries table), the preferences manager, `LibraryPaths`, the install ping, the update check), `shell/` (app chrome: menu bar, splash/About overlay, and `AppTheme` — the Fusion style + stylesheet cascade + menu-shadow filter; `main.cpp` is wiring only), `assets/` (the Asset Manager — thirteen files since the 2026-09-09 split, see its section), `preferences/` (the Preferences dialog + every panel; the four pages with no settings yet share one `PlaceholderPreferencesPanel`), `help/` (the User Manual window — see **User manual** below), `properties/` (the right-docker property tabs: `EnvironmentPanel` + its `HdriSelector`, `TransformPanel`, and `propertyrows` — the group / restore-button / row builders every tab shares), `widgets/` (app-standard controls: `DragNumberBox`, `MenuPickerButton`), and `viewport/` (the 3D engine: the Qt-facing files at its root, then the Qt-free core in `rendering/` + `scene/` (+ `scene/ik/`) + `import/` (+ `import/obj/`, `import/figure/`), std-only helpers in `util/` (`parallelfor.h`), and `shaders/` (+ `shaders/include/` for the GLSL shared between stages)). Only `main.cpp` and the cross-cutting `constants.h` sit at the `src/` root. **The 2026-09-09 refactoring round** split every oversized file along these lines (the Asset Manager, `VulkanWindow`, `Scene`/`Model`, `figureimporter`, `ikrig`, `armatureik`, the harness), fixed the bugs its audit found, and introduced the shared conventions named in each section below (RAII device handles, the descriptor helpers, `appDatabase()`, `MenuPickerButton`, `AppTheme`, GLSL includes); the CHANGELOG's `[Unreleased]` entry is the complete list. Every subsystem folder is on the compiler include path (`target_include_directories` in `CMakeLists.txt`), so headers are included by bare name (`#include "database.h"`) regardless of which folder includes them — when you add a new `src/` subfolder, add it there too or its headers won't resolve.

**Startup sequence matters** (`src/main.cpp`): the SQLite database is opened and `PreferencesManager` is loaded *before* any widget is constructed, because widgets (e.g. `AssetManagerWidget`) read preferences/DB state during construction. If you add a new manager/cache that UI depends on, initialize it in this same early block, not lazily inside a widget.

**Database** (`src/core/database.{h,cpp}`): single named QSqlDatabase connection (`"db_conn"`) shared process-wide via `QSqlDatabase::database("db_conn")` rather than opened per-call. The SQLite file (`posestudio.db`) lives in the platform's per-user app-data dir (`QStandardPaths::AppDataLocation`, derived from the app name set in `main.cpp` *before* `initializeDatabase()` runs) — not next to the executable, whose directory is read-only for an installed app on macOS/Linux. Older builds stored it next to the `.exe`; `initializeDatabase()` performs a one-time *move*-migration of that legacy file into the new location on a Normal launch (copy-then-delete, so a later Factory Reset can't resurrect it), and FactoryReset also clears any lingering legacy file. Schema lives in `resources/database/initialize.sql`, embedded into the binary via `resources.qrc`, and is executed both on `DbInitMode::FactoryReset` (which also deletes the existing file first, and is reachable from the app via Preferences → Factory Reset) *and* on a genuine first launch (no `posestudio.db` file yet) — `initializeDatabase()` checks `QFile::exists(dbPath)` *before* FactoryReset can delete it, so a fresh install always gets the schema even via `DbInitMode::Normal`. **Access conventions (2026-09-09):** every query obtains the connection through `appDatabase()` (`database.h`; the literal connection name exists only as `kDatabaseConnectionName`), `initializeDatabase()` returns `bool` (false = the connection failed or the embedded schema is missing — individual failed statements are logged, not fatal, because the additive migrations below are the designed repair path), the `;`-splitter strips whole-line `--` comments before splitting (so the 0.3.6 class of corruption can't recur), `AssetLibraryEnabled` is `ensureColumn`ed like every other post-initial column, and the AssetLibraries table is read and written ONLY through `src/core/assetlibraries.{h,cpp}` — `AssetLibraries::enabled()` (the display order: built-in first, then user libraries by folder NAME), `userManaged()`, `enabledPaths()`, `add()` → Added/AlreadyRegistered/Failed (INSERT OR IGNORE swallows the UNIQUE conflict, so the duplicate case is reported, not lost), `remove()`; add/remove also invalidate `LibraryPaths`' cached root. Don't type AssetLibraries SQL anywhere else. There is still no formal migration system, but additive schema changes for existing databases (new columns/tables) are applied unconditionally at the end of `initializeDatabase()`, each guarded to be a no-op once already present (e.g. a `PRAGMA table_info` check before `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, or `CREATE TABLE/INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`) — add new ones here rather than only in `initialize.sql`, or pre-existing installs will silently fail at the first query touching the new column/table. **Gotcha:** `initialize.sql` is executed by a naive `;`-splitter — no statement, including comments, may contain a semicolon. A 0.3.6 header comment (`split on ';'`) broke a statement boundary and corrupted `CREATE TABLE Preferences` on every fresh install (the table silently missing, `PreferencesManager` failing at bootstrap with "no such table: Preferences"); a `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` repair migration in `initializeDatabase()` fixes affected databases in place.

`AssetLibraries` has an `AssetLibraryIsBuiltIn` flag for the one library that ships with the app ("Maquettes", at `<applicationDirPath>/Maquettes`): `initializeDatabase()` re-syncs that row's path to the current install location on every launch (in case the app moved) and `mkpath`s the folder as a safety net. Built-in libraries always sort first (`ORDER BY AssetLibraryIsBuiltIn DESC`) and are excluded from the user-manageable list in Preferences → Assets, but still appear/behave like any other library in the Asset Manager tree. `initializeDatabase()` also creates a **default user library** — "My PoseStudio Library" in the user's Documents, registered as an ordinary (non-built-in, fully user-manageable) library — but only on the very first launch (a `UserLibraryCreated` flag row in Preferences) and only if no non-built-in library by that folder name is already registered, so an existing library is honoured and a deliberate later rename/removal isn't fought on every start. This library is the user-facing home for personal content the app itself consumes: its `hdri/` subfolder is where the Environment panel and the viewport's default environment find HDRI panoramas, resolved via `LibraryPaths::userLibraryRoot()/hdriDirectory()` (`src/core/librarypaths.{h,cpp}` — DB lookup by folder name, Documents fallback).

`Favorites` (`FavoriteID`, `FavoritePath` unique, `FavoriteSortOrder`) is a flat list of favorited asset file paths — no folders, no nesting. `FavoriteSortOrder` holds the user's manual drag order from the Favorites pane (see Asset Manager section below); new favorites append after the current max.

**Project files, File → New and the document state** (2026-09-30; PRs #30, #31 and #32 by @DmitryAkDev, merged with four fixes — see the end of this paragraph). **`.pss` project files** (`scene/projectfile.{h,cpp}`, Qt-free, nlohmann): a small versioned JSON document (`"format": "posestudio.project"`, `"version": 1`) that REFERENCES every figure by its import path (`Model::sourcePath`, set by `ModelImportService::uploadModelData` — the one code path that knows it) and stores numbers: per figure the pose snapshot split into rotation rows, `@pin:` names and the root's `@trans:` translation, plus the model transform as translation · quaternion (x,y,z,w in the file; glm's constructor takes w first — the PR's own probe caught a figure restored upside-down) · scale; the environment (HDRI path + every `LightingSettings` dial by name, absent = default); the camera framing (target, yaw, pitch in RADIANS, distance, `ortho`). One reader, one writer: a bad file — missing, not JSON, wrong marker, a higher version, a non-finite value — is rejected WHOLE with a readable error, unknown fields are ignored (forward compatible); `tools/projectfile/roundtrip.cpp` is its CTest guard (`project-file-check`, 21 checks, no Qt), and `tools/projectfile/probe.cpp` a headless save/restore diagnostic through the real import — OPT-IN (`-DPOSESTUDIO_BUILD_PSS_PROBE=ON`): it compiles the whole engine a second time. **The window side** (`vulkanwindow_pose.cpp`): `captureProjectDocument` / `saveProjectFile` (which closes open pose edits first, as `savePose` does: the LANDED pose) and `loadProjectFile(path, recovered, missing, error)` — parse first, the scene untouched; then a LINK PRE-FLIGHT (every source must exist, after the caller's re-pointed paths; a source mapped to "" is SKIPPED — left out of the load); then `resetToEmptyScene()`, the figures re-imported through `runImport` and restored in document order (`Model::setTransform` + `applyPose`), the environment (a saved panorama that is gone keeps the default, with a warning) and the camera; returns 0 / 1 (sources missing) / −1 (error). The document's runtime identity is `ProjectState` (`viewport/projectstate.{h,cpp}`: path + dirty flag, `changed()` only on a real transition) → `VulkanWindow::documentChanged` → the facade → `main.cpp`'s window title (`*name.pss — PoseStudio x.y.z`; file name only). DIRTY is marked at the edit choke points — `commitPoseUndo`, undo/redo, a successful import (the command-line one too), `deleteModel`, `registerLightingUndo`, `setEnvironmentFile` — and camera moves ONLY with Preferences → General's "Count camera changes as unsaved" (`PREF_CAMERA_CHANGES_MARK_DIRTY`, default off; `noteCameraChange` in every camera gesture). **File → New** (`MenuManager::newScene`, Ctrl+N): the unsaved-changes prompt, then the two picker mirrors (`setShowSkeleton(false)`, `setShadeMode(kDefaultShadeMode)` — which needed PR #30's `shadeModeChanged` signal, de-duplicated against `m_announcedShadeMode`, mirrored by `ViewportStrip::setShadeMode`), the Environment tab's `resetToStartupDefaults`, then `VulkanWindow::resetToEmptyScene`: close open pose edits, `Scene::clearModels` behind the renderer's device wait, camera home, `m_deferred = DeferredSceneState{}`, the startup bake with `autoAimKey=false`, the history CLEARED (Ctrl+Z does not undo a New), path "" and clean. **File → Open / Save / Save As** (`MenuManager::openProjectFile` / `saveProject` / `saveProjectAs`, Ctrl+O / Ctrl+S / Ctrl+Shift+S; `PREF_LAST_PROJECT_DIR`): Open asks the file, then the prompt, then the first load pass; a return of 1 runs the missing-file dialog per source (Choose File… / Skip / Cancel) and a second pass with the answers. `confirmDiscardChanges` (New, Open, window close via `main.cpp`'s `CloseGuard` event filter) is Save / Discard Changes / Cancel. **THE FOUR FIXES MADE AT THE MERGE** (each a review finding, none released before): (1) Skip in the missing-file dialog did nothing — a skipped source was not put in the recovered map, so the second pass reported it missing again and failed with an EMPTY error; it maps to "" now, and the load leaves it out; (2) "Save" in the unsaved-changes prompt proceeded even when the Save As dialog was cancelled or the write failed — the work was then discarded: it proceeds only if the document is clean afterwards; (3) Open applied the loaded dials and panorama to the scene but told the Environment tab nothing, so the tab kept the previous values and the next scrub pushed them over the loaded ones — `loadProjectFile` emits `lightingRestored` (the undo path's signal) and the new `environmentRestored` (the caption alone: `HdriSelector::setCurrentPath`, no re-bake); (4) File → Import asked to "discard changes" before importing, though an import only ADDS a model — the prompt is gone from both import entries. Also at the merge: the probe made opt-in, a figure-line name removed from `tools/projectfile/README.md` (the standing rule), the manual's format example made to match the file the code writes (`transform` nesting, `ortho`, radians), and "portable" retracted — the paths are absolute. The scripts' `saveproject` / `openproject` / `new` and the `figures` / `dirty` metrics drive the window-level path (`tools/ikscripts/check_project.txt`, 15 expectations: a posed knee, a turned head and a pinned hand saved, the scene emptied, the project opened — the figure re-imported, the pose and pin back, the document clean — a pose edit dirtying it, a bad file refused with the scene intact). NOT RUN: the dialogs (the prompt, the missing-file recovery, the title bar), which no script drives; read only. SOFT SPOTS: an import that fails mid-Open leaves the scene half-loaded (the previous scene is gone by then — the PR's own note); `std::ofstream(std::string)` takes the ANSI code page on Windows, so a project in a folder with non-ASCII characters in its path cannot be written (as `.pose` files already could not); a Factory Reset's `QApplication::quit()` now meets the close prompt on a dirty scene, and a Cancel there leaves the relaunched instance running beside the old one; shade mode and the skeleton overlay are not saved (view settings); a project restored in an axis view reads "Perspective View" in the picker (`noteView(Free)`).

**Preferences manager** (`src/core/preferencesmanager.{h,cpp}`): a process-wide singleton (`PreferencesManager::instance()`) that loads the entire `Preferences` SQLite table into a `QHash` once at startup. Reads never touch the DB; writes update the cache and persist immediately. It is explicitly not thread-safe — only call it from the Qt GUI thread. `rememberedDirectory(key, fallback)` is the ONE implementation of "start the file dialog where the user was last time" (a stored folder that no longer exists falls back) — File → Import (`PREF_LAST_IMPORT_DIR`), Add Asset Folder at both entry points (`PREF_LAST_ASSET_FOLDER_PARENT`) and pose save/load (`PREF_LAST_POSE_DIR`) all use it. `setValue`'s upsert refreshes `PreferenceStamp`.

**Install ping** (`src/core/installping.{h,cpp}`): the anonymous "this install exists" beacon, sent on EVERY launch — a signed JSON POST to `https://www.posestudio.io/ping` (the `POSESTUDIO_PING_URL` CMake cache var) carrying a random install UUID (`PREF_INSTALL_ID`, created on first use), `APP_VERSION`, the `QSysInfo` OS name/version + CPU arch, installer-vs-portable (an `unins000.exe` next to the exe), the Qt version, and a timestamp; nothing else, and `GeneralPreferencesPanel`'s disclosure copy must stay in step with `buildPayload()`. `InstallPing::scheduleAtStartup(&mainWindow)` in `main.cpp` (the MAIN WINDOW, not the QApplication: a child of the application object is destroyed after `~QCoreApplication` has torn down the event dispatcher, and the `QNetworkAccessManager` owns a worker thread; a window child dies while the event system is alive, aborting an in-flight ping — the documented fire-and-forget contract) arms a single-shot 2 s after the window shows (was 8 s: a Release launch then delivered ~11 s after start, and the user's "open, look, close" launches sent nothing — 2026-09-10); `send()` gates on, in order: a compiled-in key (`POSESTUDIO_PING_KEY` — a CMake cache var the release workflow fills from the `POSESTUDIO_PING_KEY` GitHub secret; EMPTY in every local build and fork, and an empty key means the ping is never sent at all, which the General page says next to its toggle), `POSESTUDIO_NO_PING` unset, and the `PREF_PING_ENABLED` toggle (Preferences → General, default on). The key is the ONE lever — a build configured with `-DPOSESTUDIO_PING_KEY=…` pings, Debug or Release (a Debug-only "also needs `POSESTUDIO_PING_URL`" gate existed for a few hours on 2026-09-09 and was dropped: the user hit "[ping] no signing key in this build; skipped" three times while trying to test, and the second gate only added confusion; `POSESTUDIO_PING_URL` now purely overrides the endpoint). There is deliberately NO once-a-day cap (it existed briefly, then the user chose per-launch pings on 2026-09-09): the server's install-ID dedup is what keeps the install count honest, and launches-per-install becomes a free metric. The body is signed with HMAC-SHA256 (`QMessageAuthenticationCode`, header `X-PoseStudio-Signature`, hex); the request has a 10 s transfer timeout, failures are `qDebug`-logged and ignored, no retry. The key is extractable from a shipped binary in principle — it is the first layer only; the server counts distinct install IDs (not requests), validates fields, and rate-limits new IDs per address. This is the sole user of `Qt6::Network`; keep it fire-and-forget and GUI-thread only (it uses `PreferencesManager`). **The server side lives in the repo too — `server/ping/`**: `ping.php` (the handler: POST+JSON gate, HMAC verify, field validation, ±6 h clock window, per-address new-install and per-install daily quotas, upsert into `installs` + a `ping_log` row, every rejection filed in `ping_rejects`, always a bodiless 204), `schema.sql`, `send-test-ping.ps1` (a correctly signed test ping, `-Tamper` for a bad one), and a README with deployment steps and the stats queries. Change the payload in `buildPayload()` and the `$rules` table in `ping.php` together. The General page displays `InstallPing::existingInstallId()` — it never mints an ID (`installId()` does, and only when a ping is actually sent), so opening Preferences in a keyless build shows "not created yet". Only a TAGGED release build receives the key from the workflow; a `workflow_dispatch` pipeline test ships keyless, so its downloadable artifacts never count as installs. `POSESTUDIO_PING_URL` is both the CMake cache var (the compiled-in default) and a runtime environment override read by `send()`. `define('POSESTUDIO_PING_DEBUG', true)` in the site's include makes the endpoint answer 400 "rejected: <reason>" / 200 "accepted" / 500 "<exception>" instead of a silent 204 — the deployment-diagnosis switch. The handler is lint-clean on PHP 8.3 and its whole decision path was run against a fake mysqli (every reject reason, both quotas, the upsert's SQL + parameters) — only the real MySQL execution of that SQL is unverified until the tables exist on the server.

**Update check** (`src/core/updatecheck.{h,cpp}`, 2026-09-11): on every launch, 3 s after the main window shows (`UpdateCheck::scheduleAtStartup(&mainWindow)` in `main.cpp`, right after the ping and under the same owner rule), the app GETs GitHub's public `repos/PoseStudio/PoseStudio/releases/latest` (no token; the endpoint excludes drafts and pre-releases, so the `stock-content-v1` asset pre-release is never offered; headers: `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and the `PoseStudio/<version>` user agent GitHub requires; 10 s transfer timeout, system proxy, failures logged at info level and ignored, no retry), reads `tag_name` and `html_url`, and when `compareVersions(tag, APP_VERSION)` says newer — numeric on up to three components, a leading `v` and any pre-release suffix ignored — shows a non-blocking (`QMessageBox::open()`, never `exec()` from a network slot) "Update Available" prompt parented to the main window: the new and running versions, the release page as an inline-coloured link (QSS never reaches `<a>` in a QLabel — the same rule as the Asset Manager's hint label), and three buttons — **Open Download Page** (`QDesktopServices::openUrl`), **Skip This Version** (stores the version in `PREF_UPDATE_SKIPPED_VERSION`; the startup check stays silent for that one until a newer one appears) and **Later**. Preferences → General has the "Check for updates at startup" toggle (`PREF_UPDATE_CHECK_ENABLED`, absent = on); Help → **Check for Updates…** (`UpdateCheck::checkNow`) runs the same check on demand and always reports — up to date, newer (ignoring a skip), or the failure text. Developer levers: `POSESTUDIO_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` disables the startup check, `POSESTUDIO_UPDATE_URL=<url>` overrides the endpoint — the check was verified against a local Python server returning a fake `{"tag_name":"v9.9.9",…}` (the request arrives with the right headers 3 s in and the newer-release path fires), a same-version body (the up-to-date path), the env disable, and the real GitHub endpoint (0.3.13 = up to date). It logs its outcome with `qInfo` under `[update]`; to SEE those lines (or the ping's `[ping]` ones) when launching the exe from a shell with stderr redirected, set `QT_LOGGING_TO_CONSOLE=1` — Qt on Windows otherwise routes messages to the debugger when the console is not a terminal, which looks like silence.

**What's New window** (`src/help/whatsnewdialog.{h,cpp}`, 2026-11-03; the user: "a 'What's New in x.xxx' popup … on first startup of the new version … scrollable so the user can read about all the new features"): on the first start of a NEW VERSION the app opens a non-modal `QDialog` with the running version's release notes — the CHANGELOG's `## [APP_VERSION]` section (`WhatsNewDialog::notesFor`: the lines to the next `## ` heading, the heading's date read off it), from the copy already embedded for the manual (`qrc:/manual/changelog.md`), rendered through the new `HelpManual::renderMarkdown` (the manual's own styling: `render(file)` is now a wrapper over it) under a `# What's New in PoseStudio x.y.z` title in a `QTextBrowser` (`#WhatsNewBrowser`, styled in `_help.qss` like `#HelpBrowser`; links: web → the browser, a `page.md` → the manual at that page, else the manual's What's New page) with a footer: the toggle, **Release Notes** (`HelpWindow::open(owner, "whats-new")`, the full history) and **Close** (default). ONCE PER VERSION: `PREF_WHATS_NEW_SEEN_VERSION` is compared with `APP_VERSION` and written the moment the decision is taken — a fresh install (nothing remembered) sees it too, and with the toggle off (`PREF_WHATS_NEW_ENABLED`, Preferences → General "Show What's New after an update", absent = on; the window's own checkbox writes the same key) the version is remembered silently so a later switch-on never shows a stale one; a build whose CHANGELOG has no section for its version shows nothing at startup (logged under `[whats-new]`). WHEN: `main.cpp` connects the boot `SplashOverlay`'s new `dismissed()` signal (it is a `Q_OBJECT` now; emitted in `dismiss()` before `deleteLater`) to `WhatsNewDialog::showIfUpdated(&mainWindow)` — the splash covers the window until the user's first click, and raised under it the notes would be part of the branding; raised on its dismissal they are the first thing seen of the new version. Help → **What's New...** (`showNow`: one window at a time, raised if open) reopens it. A probing run — `POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT`, `POSESTUDIO_IK_BENCH`, `POSESTUDIO_MANUAL_SHOT`, `POSESTUDIO_DUMP_SKELETON` — never sees it (it would sit over the frames such a run reads; the scripts never dismiss the splash anyway), `POSESTUDIO_NO_WHATS_NEW=1` withholds it, and `POSESTUDIO_WHATS_NEW=1` raises it 0.8 s in whatever is remembered, with `POSESTUDIO_WHATS_NEW_SHOT=<png>` saving a picture of the window and exiting — the manual shot's twin, how it was LOOKED at. The manual's Privacy and Updates, Preferences and Interface pages describe it.

**Asset manager** (`src/assets/` — thirteen files since the 2026-09-09 split: `assetmanagerwidget.{h,cpp}` is the widget proper (setup, refresh, search, folder display, navigation, collection tree ops; ~1.3k lines, down from 3.4k) and `assetmanagerwidget_menus.cpp` its context menus (a second TU of the same class); `assetnodeids.h` is the virtual-path vocabulary (`AssetNode::SEARCH_ROOT/FAVORITES_ROOT/COLLECTIONS_ROOT`, `collectionPath(id)`/`collectionId(path)`/`isVirtual()`, the grid item roles) — never hand-parse a `COLLECTION_` prefix again; `assetscan.{h,cpp}` is the QtCore-only filesystem layer (`AssetHit` carrying size/mtime from the one directory listing, the "asset = non-image with a same-basename image" rule, `kSubdirFilter` = `Dirs|NoDotAndDotDot|NoSymLinks` used by EVERY scan so junctions behave the same in tree, grid, search and hit checks — they are excluded everywhere, since the recursive walkers have no cycle protection — and the case-insensitive path comparison); `assetdb.{h,cpp}` the QtSql-only collections/favorites access (one query per operation, transactional `moveItem`/`deleteCollection`, `bool` results so a failed add can never delete an asset's only filing; library queries go through `core/assetlibraries.h`); `assetfolderproxymodel.{h,cpp}`, `assettreeview.{h,cpp}`, `assettreedelegate.{h,cpp}`, `assetgriddelegate.{h,cpp}`, `customtooltip.{h,cpp}`; `assetthumbnailloader.{h,cpp}` (QtConcurrent decode keyed by item pointer + a generation counter reset on every folder change, onto a square `GRID_ICON_DISPLAY_SIZE` canvas at the device pixel ratio — the old 120×128 canvas was silently downscaled and resampled into the 120×120 icon slot); `assetgriddragcontroller.{h,cpp}` (the hand-rolled reorder drag, injected with the list, the tree, an "is sortable" predicate and three outward signals; any button press mid-drag cancels it, which is what fixed the right-click-mid-drag stuck ghost); `assetbreadcrumblabel.{h,cpp}`): the side-panel widget that browses physical asset library folders (paths stored in `AssetLibraries`) alongside virtual, user-defined "Collections" (`AssetCollections`/`AssetCollectionItems` tables) and a flat "Favorites" list (`Favorites` table). Collections can nest arbitrarily deep via `AssetCollections.AssetCollectionParentID` (`0` = top-level) — any collection, at any depth, can hold its own flat asset items alongside further sub-collections (folder *shortcuts* inside collections were removed in 0.1.3 — a collection holds assets and sub-collections only). Favorites, by contrast, is intentionally flat — no folders, no sub-favorites, just asset items. `contextForTreeItem()` walks a tree item's ancestor chain to classify it as `Library`/`Collection`/`SearchResults`/`Favorites` regardless of nesting depth; prefer it over checking a node's immediate parent when context-menu or grid logic needs to know which "world" an item belongs to. Tree root order is Search Results, Favorites, Collections, then physical libraries (built-in libraries first). Key pieces:
- `AssetFolderProxyModel` wraps the filesystem `QStandardItemModel`-backed tree to mark folders as direct/indirect "hits" (i.e. contain or descend into a folder containing matching assets) for search/filtering, with results debounced via a pending-index queue rather than recomputed synchronously — and, since the refactor, classified on QtConcurrent workers (`AssetScan::classifyFolder`, pure, one `entryInfoList` per folder; collections from one aggregate query), generation-guarded so a refresh mid-flight discards stale results, with the placeholder icon painted meanwhile; `refreshNode(index)` re-classifies ONE subtree instead of walking the whole tree. Its icons are members, never function statics (QIcon data must not outlive QApplication).
- `AssetTreeDelegate` / `AssetGridDelegate` fully override Qt's default painting for the directory tree and the thumbnail grid respectively — don't expect standard `QStyledItemDelegate` behavior; geometry constants they rely on (icon size, margins, gaps) are centralized in `src/constants.h` so the two delegates' size hints can't drift apart.
- Breadcrumb rendering is cached per `displayFolder()` call (`m_breadcrumb*` members) and only re-laid-out, not recomputed, on label resize. Virtual sources (Collections, Favorites) instead show a plain title (`m_currentTitleText`); `displayFolder()` and `refreshTitleLabel()` both branch on the same "is this a virtual path" check (`COLLECTION_` prefix or `FAVORITES_ROOT`) — keep them in sync if a third virtual source is ever added.
- Physical folders and DB-backed virtual Collections/Favorites are all rendered into the same `QTreeView`/`dirModel`, distinguished by `searchResultsRootItem`/`favoritesRootItem`/`collectionsRootItem` root nodes — when adding tree behavior, check which subtree (filesystem vs. virtual) an index belongs to before assuming filesystem semantics (e.g. rename-on-disk) apply.
- `buildAssetHits()` resolves a flat list of asset paths into thumbnailed `AssetHit`s (shared by `parseCollectionAssets`/`parseFavorites`); it groups by folder internally for efficient thumbnail scanning but always re-emits in the caller's *input* order — both Favorites and Collections rely on this to preserve the user's manual drag order, so don't reintroduce a hash-ordered return here.
- Both Favorites and Collections are sortable grids backed by their own per-row order column (`Favorites.FavoriteSortOrder`, `AssetCollectionItems.AssetCollectionItemSortOrder`); `AssetManagerWidget::isSortableView()` (true when `m_currentFolderPath` is `FAVORITES_ROOT` or a `COLLECTION_*` id) is the single gate for both the drag-reorder handling and the "Sortable" info-bar label — check it rather than re-deriving the same `FAVORITES_ROOT`/`COLLECTION_` checks elsewhere. The drag-to-reorder itself (`beginGridDrag`/`endGridDrag`/`gridInsertIndex`/`updateGridDropIndicator`/`persistGridOrder` + the `MouseButtonPress/Move/Release` block in the grid viewport's `eventFilter`) is a **hand-rolled** drag, not Qt's `QListWidget` `InternalMove`: in `IconMode`, Qt's built-in internal-move free-positions the icon instead of reordering rows, and its `QDrag` runs through the OS drag loop. The manual version tracks raw mouse events, floats a translucent ghost `QLabel` plus a between-items drop-line indicator, leaves the grid untouched until release (moving items mid-drag would reflow the layout under the cursor), and auto-scrolls via a `QTimer` when the cursor sits in the top/bottom edge zone. `persistGridOrder()` branches on which view is current to write back to the right table/column. `AssetGridDelegate::paint` checks a `gridDragging` dynamic property on the grid to suppress the hover highlight while this is active.

**Styling**: dark theme enforced via `app.setStyle(new AppProxyStyle("Fusion"))` in `main.cpp`, never the native OS style. `AppProxyStyle` (`src/shell/appproxystyle.h`) must be constructed with `"Fusion"` explicitly — a bare `AppProxyStyle()` wraps the platform *default* style (e.g. `QWindows11Style` on Windows) instead, silently defeating the whole point of forcing Fusion. All app-level theming (the style, the stylesheet cascade, the global tooltip animation toggles, the menu-shadow filter) lives in ONE place — `AppTheme::install(app)` in `src/shell/apptheme.{h,cpp}`, called once from `main.cpp` — not scattered into widget constructors. **Menu drop shadows:** an app-wide event filter (`MenuShadowFilter`, installed on `qApp` by `AppTheme::install`; it tests the event type before the `qobject_cast`, since it sees every event in the process) sets `Qt::NoDropShadowWindowHint` on every `QMenu` at its `Polish` event (before the native window is created). This is because the menus' `border-radius` (in `_menumanager.qss`) makes each menu a *translucent* popup window, which Windows would give a heavy native drop shadow — the filter strips that shadow app-wide while keeping the rounded corners (menu-bar dropdowns, submenus, context menus, and the viewport shader menu alike). **Gotcha:** `NoDropShadowWindowHint` also flips the popup to an *opaque* window on Windows — the filter re-asserts `Qt::WA_TranslucentBackground` to bring back the see-through corners, but that still leaves a 2-3px opaque black speck at each sharp outer corner (the triangle outside the border-radius won't clear to transparent). So the filter *also* clips each menu to a rounded-rect **mask** (radius matched to the QSS `border-radius`, applied on the menu's `Resize`) to cut those specks away. All three together — no-shadow flag + translucent background + rounded mask — give rounded corners, cleanly transparent corners, and no shadow. Don't re-add a shadow via QSS (`box-shadow` isn't supported anyway); this is the single control point. **Separator lines share ONE grey app-wide** — `Constants::COLOR_SEPARATOR` (`#6a6b6e`, a clearly visible mid grey; the near-black hairlines the menus used to get vanished against the dark surfaces): the global `QMenu::separator` rule in `_menumanager.qss` covers every menu (menu-bar dropdowns, context menus, the shader picker, the HDRI menu — don't add per-menu separator overrides), `_assetmanager.qss` carries the same value for the tree's `separatorColor` and the search divider, and the HDRI category rule in `environmentpanel.cpp` reads the constant; QSS can't read C++ constants, so change all three together. Stylesheets are modular (`global.qss`, `_assetmanager.qss`, `_menumanager.qss`, `_preferences.qss`) and concatenated in `loadStylesheets()` (`src/main.cpp`) in a fixed cascade order — later files override earlier ones, so module-specific overrides belong in the `_*.qss` files, not `global.qss`. `global.qss` also owns the app-wide `QScrollBar:vertical` rules (under `[ SCROLLBARS ]` — they used to sit unscoped in `_assetmanager.qss`, secretly theming every scrollbar) and the one `QCheckBox::indicator` rule every panel inherits — don't re-add per-panel copies of either. Some custom widgets (e.g. `AssetTreeView`) expose `Q_PROPERTY` values (like `separatorColor`) specifically so QSS can drive C++ painting logic. `QDialog`/`QMessageBox` are dark-themed globally too — but note Qt's QSS engine doesn't apply selectors (e.g. `a { color: ... }`) to rich-text `<a>` links inside a `QLabel`; those need the color set inline in the HTML string itself (see the "Add Asset Folder" hint label in `assetmanagerwidget.cpp` for the pattern). Also note: avoid declaring *any* `border` (even `border: none;`) on a `QListView`/`QListWidget` `::item` rule unless you actually want one — doing so flips Qt to full CSS box-model rendering of that item, which can visibly inflate the selection/hover rect beyond the item's actual layout cell (this is why `#AssetManagerTree::item` and `#AssetLibraryList::item` deliberately have no `border` line). `outline: none;` is fine on a container (`#AssetManagerTree`, `#AssetLibraryList`) to kill the dotted focus ring, but don't assume it's a no-op everywhere in the cascade.

**Destructive operations** (e.g. Preferences → Factory Reset, in `src/preferences/factoryresetpreferencespanel.cpp`): rather than trying to reconcile every widget's in-memory state (the asset tree, `PreferencesManager`'s cache, etc.) after wiping the database, the app just relaunches itself via `QProcess::startDetached(QApplication::applicationFilePath(), ...)` followed by `QApplication::quit()`. Follow this pattern for any future action that invalidates broad in-memory state rather than writing ad-hoc refresh logic for every affected widget. The reset itself is gated inline on the page (type "RESET" into a text field to enable the button) rather than behind a separate confirmation popup.

**User manual** (`src/help/`, `docs/manual/`, 2026-09-24): **Help → User Manual** (`F1`, `QKeySequence::HelpContents` as a window-level QAction shortcut like the View keys) opens `HelpWindow` — one per process (`HelpWindow::open(owner, pageId)`; a `Qt::Window` owned by the main window, so it stays above the app and closes with it): a contents tree (`#HelpToc`), a `QTextBrowser` page (`#HelpBrowser`), a search field (`Ctrl+F`, `Enter`/`F3` step through matches, the contents filtered to the pages and sections that mention the words), back/forward (`Alt+Left/Right`), `Esc` closes. (The contents highlight is PINNED to the opened entry since 2026-10-26, PR #37 by @DmitryAkDev: `syncContentsSelection` blocks the tree's signals through its collapse / expand / scroll and re-asserts the entry from a zero-delay timer — the highlight had been seen landing on the row under a resting pointer after the re-layout, which no scripted check can reproduce; the change is harmless where the drift does not occur, and the tree's one listener, `currentItemChanged`, was already guarded by `m_syncingContents`.) **The content is plain Markdown in `docs/manual/`** — one file per page, `manual.json` the table of contents (ordered, nestable `children`, a stable `id` per page), every page's `##` headings its sub-entries automatically — embedded at build time under `qrc:/manual/` by a GLOBBED `qt_add_resources` (`CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`; `README.md`, the folder's own how-to, is filtered out), with `CHANGELOG.md` riding along as `changelog.md` = the **What's New** page (Help → Release Notes opens it), so release notes are written once. `HelpManual` (`helpmanual.{h,cpp}`) loads the manifest and renders a page: Qt's GitHub-dialect `setMarkdown`, then restyled for the dark theme by walking the document (headings white with an ANCHOR named by the heading's GitHub-style slug — `slugFor`: lower case, spaces → hyphens, punctuation dropped, duplicates `-1`, `-2` — links in the accent colour, inline code and code blocks on dark fields, tables bordered with a shaded bold header row and top-aligned cells; a Markdown document carries its formats itself, so the QSS in `_help.qss` styles only the chrome and the browser's body text); links resolve as `page.md`, `page.md#slug` or `#slug` (`setOpenLinks(false)`, `onAnchorClicked`), web links open in the browser. **THE RULE: a user-visible change is not finished until the manual says so** — edit the page in the same change (docs/manual/README.md has the writing guide: no raw HTML, one `#` title, a line of text between a fenced code block and a table that follows it — Qt's importer otherwise mislays the table's first cell). `tools/manual/check_manual.py` (CTest `manual-check`, when Python is found) fails on a broken manifest, a missing or unlisted page, a dead page or anchor link or image, and WARNS for every `addAction`/`addMenu`/strip-tooltip label (read bare or inside `tr()` / `QStringLiteral()`, after an optional icon argument) that no page mentions — the coverage check that catches a new menu entry nobody documented. To LOOK at a change: `POSESTUDIO_MANUAL_SHOT=<png>` (with `POSESTUDIO_MANUAL_PAGE=<id>`, `POSESTUDIO_MANUAL_SEARCH=<words>`) opens the manual, grabs the window and exits — the first pages were checked that way. The manual today (16 pages): Welcome, Getting Started, The Interface, Importing Content, Navigating the Viewport, Shading and Display, Lighting and Environment, Posing (+ Poses Step by Step, Pose Files), The Asset Manager, Preferences, Keyboard and Mouse Reference, Privacy and Updates, Troubleshooting, What's New — every fact in them was read out of the code, not remembered; the two vendor-neutral rules apply to it as to everything public (no product or figure-line names; the format is `.duf`/`.dsf`).

**Preferences dialog** (`src/preferences/preferencesdialog.{h,cpp}`, `src/preferences/preferencespanel.{h,cpp}`, `src/preferences/*preferencespanel.{h,cpp}`): a modal `QDialog` with a vertical tab list (left) driving a `QStackedWidget` (right). Each tab is a `PreferencesPanel` subclass living in its own file (`AssetsPreferencesPanel`, `FactoryResetPreferencesPanel`, etc.; the four pages that have no settings yet — Interface, Input, Navigation, System — are one `PlaceholderPreferencesPanel(title, message)` registered four times, promoted to a subclass file when a page gets its first real setting) so areas can be built out independently — adding a tab means adding a new subclass file and one `addPanel()` call in `PreferencesDialog`'s constructor, not touching the others. `PreferencesPanel` provides shared chrome (heading + top-aligned content layout) plus `addDescription()`/`addPlaceholder()` helpers for standardized body-copy styling — use these instead of hand-rolling a `QLabel` with inline font-size, or the panel won't match the others. `MenuManager::openPreferencesDialog(initialTab)` is the one entry point that opens the dialog (Edit → Preferences, and the Asset Manager's "Manage Asset Folders" context actions both route through it) and wires the `AssetsPreferencesPanel`'s `librariesChanged`/`navigateToLibraryRequested` signals back to the live `AssetManagerWidget` — `MenuManager::setAssetManagerWidget()` must be called (from `main.cpp`, after the Asset Manager tab exists) before that wiring can attach, so don't move `setupMenus()` to run after it without checking this dependency.

**3D viewport** (`src/viewport/`): a Vulkan renderer, hosted in a custom `QWindow` (not Qt's `QVulkanWindow` — we deliberately own the device/swapchain/render passes for the flexibility advanced rendering will need). The subsystem is split into a thin Qt-facing layer and a **Qt-free rendering core**, and that boundary is load-bearing — keep it:
- `ViewportWidget` (`viewportwidget.{h,cpp}`) is the *only* class the rest of the app sees. It's a plain `QWidget` that owns the process's `QVulkanInstance`, embeds the `VulkanWindow` via `QWidget::createWindowContainer()`, and degrades to an inline `QLabel` (rather than crashing the app) if `QVulkanInstance::create()` fails. `main.cpp` just news one up where the old viewport placeholder was. It also owns the teardown *ordering*: its destructor explicitly `delete`s the `createWindowContainer` wrapper (hence the `VulkanWindow` and all its Vulkan objects) **while the `QVulkanInstance` member is still alive**. Without that, the base `QWidget` destructor would free the window *after* the `QVulkanInstance` member is gone, and the renderer would call `vkDestroyDevice` on a device whose instance was already destroyed — a crash on close. Don't drop that explicit delete. **Overlay gotcha:** because the viewport is a *native* child window (`createWindowContainer`), it composites on top of ordinary sibling/overlay `QWidget`s — so anything that must paint over the viewport (the boot/About `SplashOverlay`, any future HUD) must be a **top-level window** (constructed with the `Qt::Tool` window type, then positioned over the parent in global coords), not a child overlay. Window *hints* alone (`FramelessWindowHint`, etc.) don't promote a parented widget to a window — `isWindow()` stays false and it remains hidden behind the viewport. **`Qt::Tool`, not `Qt::Window`:** a plain window type is a full application window, which X11/Wayland list in the taskbar and stack wherever the WM pleases (the strip and splash showed up as a second window BEHIND the main one on Linux); a tool window stays out of the window list and is kept above its owner (on macOS it is an `NSPanel`, which floats above normal windows and hides while the app is inactive). `ViewportWidget::eventFilter` also re-raises the strip on the main window's `WindowActivate` — deliberately not `ActivationChange`, which Qt also sends on DEactivation and would lift the strip over a modal dialog or the splash just as they take focus.
- `VulkanWindow` (`vulkanwindow.{h,cpp}`, a `QWindow` with `surfaceType == VulkanSurface`) is the Qt↔Vulkan bridge and the *only other* file in the subtree allowed to include Qt. It lazily builds the `VulkanContext` + `VulkanRenderer` on first `exposeEvent` (the platform surface must exist first), pumps one frame per `QEvent::UpdateRequest`, and routes mouse/wheel input to the camera. **It is ONE class across eight TUs** (seven since 2026-09-09, `vulkanwindow_script.cpp` — the scripted in-app posing test, see the FBIK section — since 2026-09-20): `vulkanwindow.cpp` (lifecycle: init/release, imports, device loss → `failDevice()` at every site), `vulkanwindow_environment.cpp` (the off-thread bake dispatch, with a catch around the worker's result), `vulkanwindow_input.cpp` (the event handlers, which only ROUTE), `vulkanwindow_camera.cpp` (view presets + hotkeys), `vulkanwindow_ik.cpp` (the 60 Hz tick, the drag/settle/ground-fall state as the `IkDragState`/`GroundFall` structs), `vulkanwindow_pose.cpp` (the undo stack, pose utilities, pins, pose files, the `.pss` project save/load — and `closeOpenPoseEdits()`/`poseEditInFlight()`, the ONE gate every pose edit passes: an import, undo/redo, delete, pose load or utility first lands a running settle and ground fall and closes an open dial edit (the Transform tab's, or the B / S / T mouse mode's), a stale drag is closed at the next press, the ground fall remembers WHICH model started falling (`GroundFall::figure`), and Load Pose is an undoable edit), `vulkanwindow_bench.cpp` (`IkDiagnostics`, `ikdiagnostics.h`, behind a `unique_ptr` that is null unless `POSESTUDIO_IK_PERF`/`_BENCH` is set). The strip is its own class, `ViewportStrip` (`viewportstrip.{h,cpp}`, with `JointModeBadge` in `jointmodebadge.{h,cpp}` and `ViewPreset` in `viewpreset.h`); `ViewportWidget` is the sole wiring point between it and the window. `VulkanRenderer` no longer forwards Scene calls — the window uses `VulkanRenderer::scene()` — and keeps only the calls that add policy (`deleteModel`'s device wait, `frameSelected`, `addModel`, `applyBakedEnvironment`). The remembered-until-the-renderer-exists state (shade mode, skeleton flag, lighting, environment path, the ORDERED import queue, a pending pose) is one `DeferredSceneState`. The OBJ and figure import services share `ModelImportService::ImportProgress` (the RAII progress dialog + phase clock) and `uploadModelData()`. **Rendering is event-driven, not continuous:** a frame is drawn only when something requested one — every state change (camera input, posing, imports, shade-mode/lighting changes, environment-bake completion, expose/resize) calls `requestUpdate()`, so if you add a new way to mutate visual state, you MUST call `requestUpdate()` after it or the viewport won't repaint. `renderFrame()` re-arms itself only when `VulkanRenderer::drawFrame()` returns true (the swapchain was just rebuilt / the frame was skipped mid-rebuild, so one follow-up frame is needed). The old continuous re-arm redrew at full swap rate while idle and — with the then-FIFO/vsynced present — throttled the entire GUI thread; don't reintroduce it (a future animation playback should request frames from its clock instead). The swapchain now prefers `VK_PRESENT_MODE_MAILBOX_KHR` (FIFO fallback): tear-free, but presenting never blocks on the vertical blank, so an interactive drag's frame shows at the next scan-out instead of waiting a refresh — up to 16ms less input-to-photon latency. The usual power objection to mailbox doesn't apply precisely BECAUSE rendering is event-driven (no frames while idle, at most one per drag tick). **Camera drags are gated on `m_activeDragButtons`** — buttons whose press was actually seen *in this window* — not on `QMouseEvent::buttons()`: a modal dialog (e.g. the Import file picker) closing over the native child window can otherwise leak a button-held move to us and snap the camera, so a move only orbits/pans if its button's drag began here. A left press that hit nothing posable is the orbit gesture, but released without dragging it is a **click** that selects the model under the cursor / clears the selection (see the selection-outline note in the frame-structure paragraph). **Right-click** (on release) opens an object context menu (`showObjectContextMenu`): it builds a world-space picking ray via `Camera::screenPointToRay`, asks `VulkanRenderer::pickModel` for the nearest model whose box the ray hits (box-level picking — `Model::intersectRay` slab-tests a static model's local bind bounds, but a POSED figure's world-space box of its live joints plus a 16cm flesh margin: full-body IK walks and crouches move the whole figure through the hip's pose translation, so after a walk the mesh sat far outside its bind box and the context menu lost its Delete entry while the joints, picked by live position, still worked; the renderer/scene own the geometry, so the window only deals in rays + model indices), and on "Delete" calls `VulkanRenderer::deleteModel`, which `vkDeviceWaitIdle`s before freeing so an in-flight frame can't reference the model's buffers. The **Delete key** (and Backspace — Mac's Delete) in `VulkanWindow::keyPressEvent` deletes the SELECTED model through the same `VulkanWindow::deleteModel(int)` path (ends a drag / settle / ground fall first, clears the undo history), as does Edit → "Delete Selected Object" (`ViewportWidget::deleteSelectedObject`). Deliberately NOT a window-level QAction shortcut — that would hijack Delete from the Asset Manager's tree and grid — so the menu entry shows "Del" only as a `\t` hint, and the key works while the viewport has focus, like P. The `QMenu` popup is a top-level window, so it composites above the native viewport (unlike a child overlay — see the SplashOverlay note). It releases its Vulkan objects both on `QEvent::PlatformSurface`/`SurfaceAboutToBeDestroyed` (so they're gone before Qt frees the surface they reference) and in its destructor as a fallback — `releaseVulkan()` is idempotent. **Header gotcha:** `QPlatformSurfaceEvent` is pulled from `<QtGui/qevent.h>`, *not* `<qpa/qplatformsurfaceevent.h>` — the binary Qt install ships no qpa headers, so the seemingly-canonical qpa include fails to compile here.
- Everything under `rendering/` and `scene/` is **pure Vulkan + std + GLM, no Qt includes** (it takes `std::string` paths and `std::vector<char>` SPIR-V blobs, not `QString`/`QByteArray`). This keeps the engine core reusable and testable. Don't reach for a `Q*` type here just because it's convenient — push that concern up into `VulkanWindow` (e.g. `importObj` takes a `QString` at the `VulkanWindow`/`ViewportWidget` layer but hands a `std::string` to the renderer). **The rendering core's helper layer (2026-09-09):** `rendering/vulkanhandles.h` (`UniqueDeviceHandle` — move-only RAII owners for every raw device handle: descriptor layouts/pools, pipelines, render passes, framebuffers, views, samplers, sync objects; EVERY class in the render core holds its handles this way, so a constructor that throws halfway leaks nothing), `descriptorutil.h` (one-call layout/pool/set creation and sampler/buffer writes), `attachmentimage.{h,cpp}` (RAII image+view for render targets, the layout-barrier helpers, lazily-allocated memory for transient MSAA attachments), `renderpassbuilder.{h,cpp}` (every single-subpass pass, plus the sampled-target dependency pair WITH the depth stages — the HDR pass's depth write-after-write hazard between frames in flight was an omission here), `samplercache.{h,cpp}` on `VulkanContext` (textures and targets BORROW shared samplers keyed by their description — `VulkanTexture::sampler()` must never be destroyed by a caller; the per-format properties are cached there too), `shaderlibrary.{h,cpp}` (loads SPIR-V by name; `kShaderNames` mirrors CMake's `SHADER_SOURCES`), `ibldata.h` (the bake-product PODs, so `rendering/` never includes `scene/` — `vulkanrenderer.h` is the ONE rendering file above `scene/`, the frame orchestrator). The GLSL shared between stages lives in `shaders/include/*.glsl` (`camera_ubo`, `skinning` — the dual-quaternion blend + corrective blend that `mesh.vert` and `shadow.vert` MUST share — `correctives`, `colour`, `fullscreen`) through glslc's `-I`; CMake lists them as `DEPENDS` so an include edit recompiles every shader. `VulkanContext::supportsIndependentBlend()` is checked (the pipeline collapses its attachment states when the feature is absent) and the swapchain's `compositeAlpha` is chosen from the surface's supported set.
