Ownership/lifetime is layered by how often things change: `VulkanContext` (instance handle, physical+logical device, queues, VMA allocator) is created once and lives until the surface dies. `VulkanSwapchain` (swapchain images, render pass, framebuffers — a SINGLE-SAMPLE colour-only pass with `loadOp DONT_CARE` since 2026-09-09: only the fullscreen composite draws there, and the full-resolution 4× colour + depth targets it used to clear and resolve every frame were pure bandwidth) is the resolution-dependent layer — `recreate()` rebuilds it on resize/out-of-date but deliberately *keeps the render pass* (it depends only on formats + sample count, which don't change). **MSAA:** the viewport renders multisampled — `VulkanContext::sampleCount()` picks the best of {4×, 2×} the device supports for both colour and depth (else single-sample), chosen once at device creation; the offscreen HDR target (not the swapchain any more) builds the transient multisampled colour/spec/depth attachments and a render pass whose subpass **resolves** the MSAA colour into its single-sample resolve image (the resolve attachment + `pResolveAttachments` only exist when `samples>1`, so there's a clean single-sample fallback). Every `VulkanPipeline` reads `context.sampleCount()` for its `rasterizationSamples` (all pipelines share the one MSAA render pass, so they must match). The renderer's two clear values (colour, depth) are unchanged — the resolve attachment is `DONT_CARE`/not cleared. This is what anti-aliases the figure silhouette. `VulkanRenderer` owns the per-frame state (command buffers, sync) and the swapchain plus the renderables — the `Scene` (imported meshes) and the `Grid` — and drives `acquire → record → submit → present`. Resize is coalesced: `notifyResize()` just sets a pending extent + flag (no-oping when the extent is unchanged — expose events report the current size too, and an un-obscure mustn't force a rebuild), and the actual rebuild happens at the next `drawFrame()` (on `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DATE_KHR`/suboptimal/the flag) after a `vkDeviceWaitIdle`; `drawFrame()` returns whether a follow-up frame is needed (see the event-driven-rendering note above). Sync follows the standard pattern with one subtlety — `imageAvailable` semaphores and `inFlight` fences are per *frame-in-flight* (`kMaxFramesInFlight`, in `vulkancommon.h`), but `renderFinished` semaphores are per *swapchain image* (a submit-signalled semaphore mustn't be reused until that image's present completes), so they're resized whenever the swapchain image count changes.

`VK_CHECK` (`vulkancommon.h`) throws `VulkanError` on any non-success `VkResult`; `VulkanWindow::initializeVulkan()` is the catch boundary. VMA is header-only and must be compiled in exactly one TU — `vma_impl.cpp` is that file (`#define VMA_IMPLEMENTATION`), bound to the statically-linked loader (`VMA_STATIC_VULKAN_FUNCTIONS 1`), which is why `VulkanContext` can call `vmaCreateAllocator` without supplying a function-pointer table.

`VulkanPipeline` wraps one `VkPipeline` + layout. The *shared* fixed-function state (dynamic viewport/scissor — so pipelines survive resizes untouched — triangle-list topology) lives in the class; the knobs that differ between pipelines are passed in a small `PipelineConfig` (push-constant size/stages, alpha-blend on/off, depth test/write on/off, cull mode, primitive topology + line width — `LINE_LIST` drives the posing skeleton overlay — plus optional vertex-input bindings/attributes and descriptor-set layouts). Add a field there only when a real use case needs it — don't expose all of Vulkan's pipeline state speculatively. The mesh path has two pipeline *variants* (opaque + transparent) sharing one shader/layout. **Frame structure:** each frame first records the **key-light shadow pass** (`Scene::recordShadowPass` — a depth-only render pass on the `ShadowMap` (`rendering/shadowmap.{h,cpp}`, D32 2048², single-sample, static depth-biased; TWO samplers over the one image — comparison/PCF for lit-tests plus a NEAREST non-comparison twin whose raw depths feed the ground shadow's PCSS blocker search) via `shadow.vert` (same skinning as `mesh.vert`, joint set bound at index 0) into an ortho frustum fitted per frame around the models **and their floor projections along the light** (a posed figure's bounds = its bind AABB unioned with the posed bone positions + a flesh margin — the bind box alone let a leaning head/arm cross the fitted map's edge, transient dark bands while posing) (so ground shadows land inside the map; skipped, map left fully-lit, when there are no casters — the light matrix then parks on a degenerate projection every receiver reads as lit); the fitted `lightViewProj` rides in the camera UBO); then the **offscreen HDR scene pass** (`rendering/hdrtarget.{h,cpp}` — linear RGBA16F with the same MSAA colour/depth/resolve structure as the swapchain pass, so every scene pipeline builds against *its* render pass unchanged; `resize()` rebuilds images/framebuffer but keeps the pass, so pipelines never rebuild) draws the scene in **LINEAR HDR** — `mesh.frag`'s PBR mode and `background.frag` no longer tonemap inline — starting with the **HDRI backdrop** (`background.vert/frag` — PBR mode only: a fullscreen triangle sampling the prefiltered environment cube along the per-pixel eye ray, depth test/write off, following the same exposure/rotation dials as the shading so backdrop and subject always agree; runs even with no models). The backdrop has its own **Environment-panel "Backdrop" group** of `LightingSettings` dials (UBO `params3.zw`/`params4`, live, no re-bake, all with per-row restore buttons + Restore-All coverage): **Mode** (Off = skip the pass, flat viewport grey — the default, so the HDRI lights the figure without being drawn behind it; Environment = infinite sphere; **Dome** = ground projection — the eye ray intersects a finite dome (radius dial) about a nominal tripod-height capture point, ground-plane hits winning below the horizon, so the panorama's floor lands ON y=0 and the figure stands on the environment's floor), **Blur** (defocus in prefiltered-mip steps, default 1), **Brightness** (backdrop-only exposure — the photographic "background a stop down"), and **Dome radius**; the Mode picker is the app's button+`QMenu` pattern, deliberately reusing the `#EnvironmentHdriButton` object name to inherit its styling; after the scene pass, **screen-space subsurface scattering** (`sssblur.frag` run twice at full res: a masked, per-channel-width Gaussian — red diffuses farthest, the diffusion profile that makes skin read as flesh — whose mask is the HDR target's **alpha channel**, written by the opaque mesh pass as the per-pixel translucency (`material3.z` tells `mesh.frag` which alpha semantics apply — opaque writes the mask, the transparent pass keeps blend opacity); the backdrop and the clear write 0, and the grid/skeleton/transparent pipelines set `PipelineConfig::colorWriteAlpha=false` so their blending can't contaminate the mask (blend factors still read src alpha — only the framebuffer write is masked); H into a full-res scratch target, V back INTO the HDR resolve in place, so everything downstream just sees diffused skin — eyes/cloth/props at mask 0 stay untouched). **The blur diffuses DIFFUSE light only**: the HDR pass has a SECOND colour attachment (specMS + spec resolve; single-sample fallback colour/depth/spec) that the PBR opaque path routes its whole specular sum into (`mesh.frag`'s `outSpec`: key GGX + env split-sum + top coat + rim), and the SSS V-pass samples it (set binding 1) and adds it back over the blurred diffuse — blurring specular smeared every glint into a wet-looking film that NO material tuning could dry out (the definitive skin-realism fix; real skin pipelines all separate the two). Only the opaque mesh pipeline writes attachment 1 (`PipelineConfig::colorAttachmentCount=2` + `writeColorAttachment1`; every other scene pipeline masks it off, which needs the `independentBlend` device feature — enabled in `VulkanContext`, universal on desktop); the transparent shells keep spec in-line (their pixels have mask ≈ 0, so the blur skips them anyway); then **bloom** (PBR mode only — `rendering/postprocess.{h,cpp}`: half-res soft-knee bright-extract at post-exposure luminance 1.0 → separable 9-tap Gaussian, H into a second half-res RGBA16F target and V back into the first); and finally the swapchain render pass runs ONLY the fullscreen **composite** (`composite.frag`: HDR + bloom×strength, **ACES applied here** when PBR + the tonemap dial (so bloom thresholds/blurs see real radiance), passthrough for the stylized modes which still author display-ready values — drawn through the *unchanged* MSAA swapchain pass, where a fully-covered fullscreen triangle makes MSAA a no-op, so the swapchain pass carries neither MSAA nor depth any more — see `VulkanSwapchain` above). **Selection outline:** the SELECTED model (`Scene::selectedModelIndex`/`setSelectedModel` — a freshly imported model is selected; `setActiveFigure`, hence every joint click, selects that figure; a plain left CLICK — press + release within the platform drag distance, `VulkanWindow::selectModelAtClick` — box-picks the model under the cursor or clears the selection on empty space, and clearing also drops the figure's joint selection so no gizmo lingers on an unselected model; a real orbit drag never touches it) wears a Blender-style silhouette outline in the theme's accent blue. It is a screen-space effect on a coverage mask, not geometry: right after the shadow pass, `Scene::recordOutlinePass` draws the selected model — every mesh except opacity-masked cards (lashes/brows), whose solid quads would bump the outline; translucent shells keep their silhouette — through `shadow.vert` + `outlinemask.frag` (the shadow pass's skinned position-only vertex shader, whose push matrix is now a generic `viewProj`: the light's there, the camera's here) into the `OutlineMask` (`rendering/outlinemask.{h,cpp}`: an R8 target at the context's MSAA count with a single-sample resolve, owned and resized by the renderer alongside the HDR target, no depth — the mask is the object's whole projected silhouette, so the outline shows through occluders like the joints pick through the mesh; its sampled image is moved to the read layout at creation/resize because the composite binds it every frame while the pass runs only with a selection). `composite.frag` (set-0 binding 2; the post set layout grew to three samplers, unused bindings duplicate-written) dilates the mask by the outline width — the max over two rings of bilinear taps — and paints `outlineWeight = dilated − coverage`: zero inside the object, (1 − coverage) on its MSAA fringe (so the outline blends with the object exactly as the object blends with the background — an MSAA-resolved mask, not a single-sample one, is what makes the inner edge match), full within the width, softened along the outer edge by the bilinear taps. Applied AFTER tonemapping, so the colour is exact under any exposure. The colour is `PostProcess`'s linear-decoded `#5b87cc` (the QSS accent blue — hover borders, DragNumberBox glyphs; `#314D7A`, the selection FILL blue, is too close to the viewport grey to read as a line); the width is 2 LOGICAL pixels × the window's device pixel ratio (`VulkanRenderer::setUiScale`, fed from `devicePixelRatio()` on init/expose/resize). Picking is box-level (`pickModel`), so a click just outside a figure's mesh but inside its bounds still selects it. The scene passes, in order:
- **Mesh** (`scene/scene.{h,cpp}` + `mesh.vert/frag`): the lit, **skinned** pipeline for imported geometry (OBJ meshes *and* figure zones). Interleaved `Vertex` (pos/normal/uv + `uvec4 joints`/`vec4 weights` for skinning + a baked per-vertex `ao` — filled at import by `import/aobaker.{h,cpp}`, a BVH + cosine-hemisphere ray bake over the model's own OPAQUE triangles (transparent shells/cards receive but never cast — a clear cornea must not darken the iris), parallel across cores, run on the final post-morph post-subdivision mesh in both import services (skipped over a triangle cap so huge OBJs don't stall); the PBR mode multiplies the ENVIRONMENT terms by it — SH diffuse + ambient fill × `ao`, env specular × `ao²`, rim × `ao` — while the key light relies on its real shadow map; static bind-pose AO, superseded dynamically when SSAO lands — plus a baked `vec4 tangent` (UV-aligned + bitangent handedness in w; `import/tangentgen.{h,cpp}`, Lengyel accumulation on the final mesh in both import services; the shaders' `tangentFrame()` prefers it and falls back to the screen-space cotangent frame when w = 0)) vertex buffers, a per-frame camera/lighting UBO in descriptor **set 0** (binding 0, vert+frag — carries the view/viewProj matrices, camera position, the **3-point studio lighting rig** (key/fill/rim direction+colour, world-space and fixed relative to the subject so lighting stays consistent as the camera orbits; `Scene::record` sets them), an ambient term, the shade-mode param, and the **9 SH coefficients** of the lighting environment's diffuse irradiance (the diffuse half of IBL)), per-material textures in descriptor **set 1** (binding 0 = diffuse *sRGB*, binding 1 = detail normal/bump *linear*, binding 2 = **roughness map** *linear*, binding 3 = **spec-mask map** *linear* — 2/3 are per-texel multipliers on the material's scalar roughness / specular weight (the source's per-region variation: T-zone oil, matte body, glossy lips), and when the spec mask decodes the material's *undiscounted* reflectivity fold rides in `material.w` so the mask texels do the real scaling (`MaterialRefs::specularWeightWithMap`; shader clamps texel×scalar to the ≤1 dielectric ceiling); binding 4 = **translucency map** *sRGB* (transmitted tint / per-region weight) and binding 5 = the **micro-detail (pore) normal** *linear* — the newer skin shader's tiled grain layer, packed into `material3.w` as floor(tiles)+fract(weight) since the push block is at its 128-byte limit, applied over the base perturbation and NOT gutter-filled (it tiles/wraps); each binding has a 1×1 fallback — white / flat-normal / white / white / white / flat-normal — so the shader always samples and one pipeline serves textured and untextured meshes; the gutter fill covers the four UV-mapped ones), a per-**model** POSE set in descriptor **set 2** — binding 0 the joint storage buffer, bindings 1/2 the pose-corrective weights + delta table (see the correctives paragraph in the figure section) — (binding 0, vertex stage — per joint a unit **dual quaternion** (2 vec4s: rotation (xyz,w) + 0.5·(0,t)·q) converted losslessly from the rigid `poseGlobal·inverseBind`; ONE BUFFER + SET PER FRAME IN FLIGHT, uploaded lazily at record time from the CPU-side matrices — pose changes rewrite the matrices at 60 Hz while up to two recorded frames still execute, and a single shared buffer was overwritten mid-read: vertices skinned by half-updated matrices streaked into momentary exploded-geometry artifacts, and the same torn frame corrupted the shadow pass), the scene-wide **image-based-lighting maps** in descriptor **set 3** (binding 0 = prefiltered specular cubemap, binding 1 = BRDF LUT, binding 2 = the key-light **shadow map** (comparison sampler), binding 3 = the same map's **raw depths** (non-comparison sampler — the grid's PCSS blocker search); 2/3 written once at scene creation — `applyBakedEnvironment` rewrites only 0/1; fragment stage — see the IBL paragraph below; in the PBR mode the key light's diffuse+specular are shadowed by a 3×3-PCF lookup through the UBO's `lightViewProj` with **normal-offset sampling** (the lookup point is pushed off the surface along the geometric normal by ~1–3.5 shadow texels, growing as N·L falls — the raster-time static bias alone under-compensates at grazing key angles, where self-shadow acne drew soft streak lines down every figure's shadow-side limbs), while the environment terms stay unshadowed — that's AO's job), and a 128-byte push constant — exactly the guaranteed minimum, so no further vec4 fits — (`MeshPushConstants` = `mat4 model` + `vec4 baseColor` [`a` = opacity] + `vec4 material2` [dual-lobe specular: `x/y` = lobe 1/2 roughness, `z` = lobe ratio (1 = single-lobe material → the blended `material.x` path, so OBJ/glossy surfaces are untouched; <1 → the PBR mode blends two GGX lobes for key + environment, real skin's broad-sheen-plus-tight-reflection), `w` = translucency weight — × the set-1 translucency map's luminance for per-region subsurface (scales the wrapped-Lambert locally; cloth/props authored at 0 stop wrapping like skin) and the **backlit transmission** term (light through thin regions — glowing ears/nostrils — tinted by the translucency map × a warm absorption profile, deliberately NOT shadow-gated since it IS the light passing through the occluder)] + `vec4 material3` [`x` = Top Coat weight (the thin clear/oily layer skins author at ~0.35-0.45, reflectivity folded in; rendered as a second 4%-F0 GGX layer over key + environment), `y` = its roughness] — and, preceding those two in the actual struct, `vec4 material` [`x` = roughness, `y` = normalMode **packed with the authored detail-map strength** (floor = mode 0/1/2, fract×8 = strength — the source's dialed "Normal Map" / "Bump Strength" values, e.g. 2.5/4 on heavily-detailed skins; rendering those maps at a fixed 1× left the surface so coherent that the broad specular sheen read as WET PLASTIC on smooth body regions — the strength is parsed per channel in `materialparser`, carried through `FigureMaterial`/`MeshData::normalStrength`, and scales the shader's tangent-space XY / bump gradient; strength-1 content, e.g. most prior-generation skins and OBJs, renders exactly as before), `z` = metalness, `w` = specular (F0) weight — 1 = the standard 4% dielectric, skin ~0.5; in the PBR mode `f0 = 0.04·w`, the key-light GGX runs undamped (materials tame themselves now), the environment split-sum's grazing `brdf.y` bias also scales by `min(w,1)` so matte-authored surfaces stay matte at glancing angles, and the environment specular is **horizon-faded** (squared fade as the reflection vector sinks below the vertex normal's hemisphere — a detail-map-bent normal otherwise reflects environment light from under the surface, which reads as an oily film on bumped skin). The environment specular carries three further **anti-wet-plastic measures**, each verified against a real repro (a heavily normal-mapped skin viewed from its environment-lit back side): **specular anti-aliasing** (lobe roughness widened by the shaded normal's screen-space variance PLUS its zoom-independent deviation from the geometric normal — a 2.5×-strength stretch-mark ridge otherwise draws hot white streaks), the **Frostbite dominant-direction correction** (the prefiltered env is sampled along mix(R, n, α) per lobe, not raw R — a wide lobe must average the hemisphere around the surface, not mirror whatever R hits, e.g. a bright ceiling softbox), and a **high-roughness energy fade** (grazing `brdf.y` scaled by (1−rough), and the whole env-spec term fading as roughness saturates — a hemispheric lobe's light is what the diffuse term already integrates; leaving it adds a milky 4% white veil pinned to the roughness map's roughest blotches)]). The vertex shader applies **DUAL-QUATERNION skinning** (weights blend the joints' dual quaternions, sign-aligned against the first joint's hemisphere, normalized, then applied as one rigid transform — `shadow.vert` uses the identical blend so the cast shadow matches). DQS, not linear matrix blending, is load-bearing: the figure format authors its skin weights AND its pose correctives against dual quaternions (`skin_settings.general_map_mode: DualQuat` in the base `.dsf`), and LBS collapses deep bends — at a 155° knee a vertex 8cm from the joint shrinks to 5cm, folding the calf into a shapeless blob that the pose correctives (sculpted as corrections ON TOP of DQS) made worse instead of fixing. The blend precedes the model + view-projection transforms; a **static** model (OBJ, or any figure at bind pose) binds a single identity joint and its vertices default to weight `(1,0,0,0)`, so the same path leaves them untransformed. Each `Model` owns its joint buffer + a runtime skeleton and recomputes the matrices in `computeSkinMatrices()` when `setBoneRotation()` poses a joint. The fragment computes `baseColor * diffuse` albedo, a roughness-driven Blinn-Phong + Fresnel specular, and optional normal perturbation from the detail map via a screen-space cotangent frame (no vertex tangents; mode 1 = tangent-space normal, mode 2 = bump — mode 2 wired but not fed yet). **Selectable shade modes (viewport "shader picker"):** `mesh.frag` is an **über-shader** that branches on `cam.params.x` — a per-frame uniform in the camera UBO (which also now carries `view`, for the view-space normals matcaps need) — so switching modes is a one-int UBO write with **no pipeline swap** and instant effect. The branch is coherent across a draw (uniform), so it's cheap. **The picker is TABLE-DRIVEN** (`scene/shademode.h`: `kShadeModes[]`, one row per picker entry — name, the mesh.frag `fragMode` that shades the surface, `FillKind` (Shaded / None / HiddenLine), `wireframe`, `wireLevel`, `separatorAfter`; `kDefaultShadeMode` = row 0 = PBR Shaded): the index plumbed `ViewportWidget → VulkanWindow → VulkanRenderer → Scene::setShadeMode` is a ROW of that table, never a mesh.frag mode — `Scene::shadeModeSpec()` resolves it and `Scene::isPbr()` (the PBR family, `fragModeIsHdr`: fragMode 1 or the specular view 14) gates the backdrop, the key-light/rotation coupling, and the renderer's HDR post chain. The eighteen rows, in picker order, fall into four separated groups by what the user wants to see — lit shading of the real materials: PBR Shaded (frag 1), Texture Shaded (0), Flat Texture Shaded (8), Cartoon Shaded (5) | untextured form studies: Matcap (2, the studio one), Clay Shaded (6), Lighting Only (7 — the standard name for a white-material lighting check; Clay is lit too, so a "(Lit)" qualifier was rejected as misleading), Silhouette (13) | wireframes: Wireframe, Hidden Line Wireframe (the technique's name), Clay Shaded Wireframe (6 + wire), Texture Shaded Wireframe (0 + wire) | data views: Albedo (10), Ambient Occlusion (12), Roughness Map (15 — the map itself), Specular Only (14 — the specular lighting component, not a map), Normals (9), UV Checker (11). **Wireframe machinery:** `Mesh::record` takes a `MeshDrawKind` (Opaque / Transparent / Wire / DepthOnly), pushed as `material3.z`; mesh.frag returns a flat grey for Wire (`params4.w` = the row's `wireLevel`: light on the dark clear colour in the wire-only modes, near-black over a lit surface) and nothing for DepthOnly. `Scene` builds two more mesh-shader pipelines: `m_wirePipeline` (`PipelineConfig::polygonMode = LINE`, depth test + write, alpha and the spec attachment masked, a NEGATIVE depth bias (-1/-1) so a surface's own edges win over its coplanar fill; needs the `fillModeNonSolid` device feature — `VulkanContext` enables it when present, `supportsWireframe()`, else the wire modes show their surface alone) and `m_hiddenLinePipeline` (`colorWriteRgb = false` + alpha/spec masked: a depth-only surface fill through the colour pass, so Hidden Wireframe shows the clear colour where the surface is and only the camera-facing edges; `Model::recordDepthFill` draws OPAQUE meshes only so an eye's iris wires stay visible through its shells, while `Model::recordWire` draws EVERY mesh, cards and shells included). `Scene::record` runs: the surface per `FillKind` → the wire overlay if `wireframe` → the posing overlay (one shared host-mapped line buffer, one draw). mesh.frag's own numbering is unchanged (modes 3-4, the old skin/metal matcaps that no picker row reached, were deleted in the 2026-09-09 round — the picker table is the contract; the numbers stay vacant); four modes were ADDED for the picker: `12` Ambient Occlusion (the baked `vAo` as grey), `13` Silhouette (one flat light fill — pose readability), `15` Roughness Map (the scalar roughness × its map as grey, WITHOUT the lit modes' specular-AA widening — the authored data), and `14` the Specular Only view, which runs the PBR branch (`mode == 1 || mode == 14`) and outputs only the accumulated MATERIAL specular — key GGX lobes + environment split-sum + top coat, captured BEFORE the rim, which is a light rather than a material property — at linear HDR with alpha 0 (not skin), so it sits in the PBR family for the composite's ACES + bloom: highlight breadth shows the roughness map, highlight strength the specular weight and mask, exactly as PBR renders them. Modes by mesh.frag number: `0` Rendered (the skin/subsurface Blinn-Phong above, lit by the analytic 3-point rig), `1` **PBR** (**the default** — the photoreal, **image-based-lit** mode: Cook-Torrance GGX with split-sum IBL — diffuse from the environment's SH irradiance (set 0), specular from the prefiltered environment cubemap + BRDF LUT (set 3) — plus **one** analytic key light whose diffuse is a **per-channel wrapped Lambert** (red wraps farthest past the terminator, then green, then blue; energy-conserving via the (1+w)² denominator) — the cheap skin-translucency model driven by the panel's Subsurface dial; do NOT replace it with an additive "warm band": the old one saturated across the whole shadow hemisphere and tinted the figure's entire back red — and a dialable photographic **rim** (reusing the UBO's rimDir/rimColor), ACES-tonemapped; the analytic fill is dropped here because the environment supplies wrap), `2`–`4` **Matcaps** (Studio/Skin/Metal — procedural material-sphere shading from the view-space normal, no texture), `5` Toon/Cel, `6` Clay (matte neutral form study), `7` Lighting-Only (shading on white), `8` Flat Shaded (geometric per-triangle normal via position derivatives), `9` Normals (RGB), `10` Albedo (unlit), `11` UV Checker. The mode is plumbed `ViewportWidget → VulkanWindow (remembers it across the lazy renderer build) → VulkanRenderer → Scene::setShadeMode` → the UBO. The **UI** floats in the viewport's top-right corner: because the viewport is a native child window, it lives in a *top-level* frameless window owned by `ViewportWidget` (same compositing rule as `SplashOverlay`), kept glued to the corner via `syncOverlayPosition()` on the widget's resize/move + an event filter on the top-level window; `ViewportWidget::shaderModeNames()` reads the table's names in order, and the menu adds a separator after each row flagged `separatorAfter`. The overlay strip also holds a **View picker** right of the shader picker (same button+`QMenu` pattern, `#ViewPresetButton`, the field style factored into a `pickerStyle` lambda both share; the menu gets its own `QMenu::item` padding rule — the global one is tight on the right for short labels — and a minimum width equal to the field's): Home View | Top/Bottom View | Front/Back View | Left/Right View, each driving `resetView`/`setAxisView`; its label mirrors `VulkanWindow::currentView()` — a `ViewPreset` (vulkanwindow.h) the window records in `noteView()` from the axis-view keys/menu, Home, flip (Front↔Back, Left↔Right; Top/Bottom stay), and the orbit drag (→ `Free`, shown as "Perspective"), emitted as `viewPresetChanged` — so keys, the View menu, and the picker agree. Then two icon buttons: **Home** (camera back to default framing) and **Ground** (`:/resources/icons/ground.png` — drops the posable figure so its CURRENT POSE's lowest point rests on y=0: `GroundSampler` (`scene/groundsampler.{h,cpp}`, behind `Model::groundGap`; the one-shot `dropToGround` was dead and is gone) CPU-skins a compact per-vertex copy kept for skinned models (`GroundSample`: pos+joints+weights, ~10 MB/figure — device-local buffers are unreadable) with the live `m_poseGlobal` matrices to find the posed min-Y, then shifts `m_transform` and recomputes skin matrices so the transform-dependent bone world positions (picking/gizmo) follow; static models use the bind AABB through the transform, which is exact for them; plumbed `ViewportWidget::groundFigure → VulkanWindow::groundFigure`, which reads `Scene::figureGroundGap` and animates `Scene::translateModelY(index, dy)` on the model that STARTED falling (`GroundFall::figure` — undo can switch the active figure mid-fall). **Several figures:** `figureModel()` is the ACTIVE figure (`Scene::activeFigureIndex`/`setActiveFigure`; and NULL while a different, non-figure model is the selection — the outline says what the posing utilities act on, so with an OBJ selected beside a figure they no-op, while the skeleton overlay keeps following the active figure) — the figure whose joint was last clicked (`selectBoneAt` searches every figure's joints and switches the active figure on a hit, clearing the previous one's selection), else the first figure in the scene; every posing call (selection, gizmo, IK, pins, pose snapshot/save/load, ground) addresses it, user-pin markers draw on every figure, and each pose undo entry carries its figure index (`UndoEntry::figure`) so undo/redo restores the right one; deleting a model shifts the indices and clears the undo history. Before this a second figure in the scene could not be posed at all — every call went to the first skeleton. **The drop is ANIMATED as free fall:** `VulkanWindow::groundFigure` reads the gap (`Model::groundGap`, the same scan) and, for a hovering figure, drives `Model::translateY` from `m_fallTimer` along ½·9.81·t² on real elapsed time (world units are metres: a 1m drop lands in ~0.45s, whatever the frame cadence) until the gap is closed; a figure sunk below the floor is lifted in one step (nothing falls upward); a press or a pose save completes the fall instantly (`finishGroundFall`), a click mid-fall or mid-drag is ignored, and `releaseVulkan` stops it; a figure that lands ON HER FEET then absorbs the landing with her legs, deeper the faster she came down, and ends in the pose she was dropped in (THE LANDING BOUNCE in the FBIK section). SINCE 2026-09-28 THE DROP IS A POSE EDIT — the root's pose translation, not the transform described here, and one undo entry: see THE FIGURE MOVE in the FBIK section. It was not undoable until then — the undo stack holds poses + lighting, not transforms). Output stays linear (the swapchain is `_SRGB` and does the gamma encode); the HDR modes (PBR/matcap) tonemap first. **The selector is a `MenuPickerButton`** (`src/widgets/` — a `QPushButton` that opens a real `QMenu`: one checkable action per table row in an exclusive `QActionGroup`, separators between the row groups; the View picker is a second instance, with `setDetachedCaption("Perspective")` once the camera leaves every named view), *not* a `QComboBox`. This is deliberate: it makes the dropdown look and behave **exactly** like the File/Edit/Help menus — colours, hover, spacing, and top-down drop — for free, because it *is* a `QMenu` and inherits the global `QMenu` QSS in `_menumanager.qss` (`#252627` surface, `#314D7A` rounded hover). A styled `QComboBox` popup was tried first and abandoned: setting any stylesheet on a `QComboBox` makes its popup render item states (`:hover`/`:selected`) inconsistently — the highlight paints as a faint native tint instead of the themed colour, no matter how `QAbstractItemView`/`::item` are styled — and its popup also repositions to align the current row with the field. The button itself is themed to match the menu-bar surface + hover (`#ShaderModeButton`), and triggering an action calls `setShadeMode(i)` and updates the button text. **No back-face culling** — imported winding is inconsistent, so the fragment flips the normal per `gl_FrontFacing` for two-sided lighting. Drawn in two passes over the same models: an **opaque** variant (depth test+write) first, then an **alpha-blended transparent** variant (depth test, write off) for see-through zones — `Scene::record` binds each variant in turn: `Model::recordOpaque` draws each model's opaque partition (`Mesh::isTransparent()` = opacity < 1 decides the partition once, at construction), then the Scene gathers EVERY model's transparent meshes and sorts them **back-to-front** by POSED centroid distance (`Model::posedCentroid`) in one list — a single sort across models, binding each model's pose set as the draw order crosses models — so layered shells (lashes over cornea over sclera) blend correctly from any angle, from any model, in any pose. Cross-file material bases (`/data/...#PBRSkin`) are pre-resolved by `applySceneMaterials` — the referenced doc's base material is appended to an augmented library and the url rewritten to same-file form, so undialed channel defaults merge correctly (formerly the "roughness falls back to default" limitation). (The long-standing "newest-generation eyelash follower renders as opaque grey strips" gap was closed on 2026-10-26: a hierarchical materials preset listed under ANY follower slot is applied to every follower it names — see the follower-addons paragraph.)
- **Floor grid** (`scene/grid.{h,cpp}` + `grid.vert/frag`): an *infinite* ground grid. It is NOT line geometry — it draws one full-screen quad and the fragment shader reconstructs the world point on the `y=0` plane per pixel (ray/plane intersect of the unprojected near/far points), then draws anti-aliased lines via `fwidth` derivatives and fades them out with distance. It writes correct `gl_FragDepth` (so scene geometry occludes it) but is alpha-blended with depth-*write off* (a transparent overlay mustn't occlude things behind the mostly-invisible plane), which is why it's drawn last (after the opaque mesh layer). Its push constants are `viewProj` (vertex inverts it to unproject; fragment uses it for depth) plus the key light's matrix as its first three ROWS — an ortho·rigid transform's bottom row is always (0,0,0,1), and dropping it frees exactly one vec4 for the **shadow dials** (intensity/softness/reach, from the Environment panel's Shadow group) within the 128-byte push minimum. The pipeline binds the scene's set 3 (at index 0), declaring bindings 2 + 3 (the shadow map's comparison and raw-depth samplers): where the key light is occluded the fragment composites a **PCSS soft shadow** (percentage-closer soft shadows with contact hardening) under the grid lines — a Poisson blocker search over the raw depths measures the caster's height above the floor (the search radius shrinks with the Softness dial, and the CENTER is always sampled — sparse ring taps can all miss a small caster like a foot, which showed as salt-noise speckles inside crisp shadows), the PCF kernel radius grows with that height × softness, and the shadow's intensity fades toward the Reach distance — so the shadow is dark and crisp at the figure's contact points and widens/dissolves as it trails away, like a real area-light shadow. A per-pixel kernel rotation (interleaved-gradient hash) turns the sparse taps' banding into unstructured noise, and the world-space scales come straight from the light rows' lengths (no extra uniforms). This is what visually grounds the figure on the otherwise-invisible plane; the fixed calibration the dials scale (contact opacity, spread-per-softness, search radius) is named constants at the top of `grid.frag`. All look tuning (line colour, cell size, major-line interval, fade distance, shadow tint/opacity) lives as named constants at the top of `grid.frag`; there are no colored axis lines yet.

**Mesh rendering & model import**: `Scene` (`scene/scene.{h,cpp}`) is the mesh analogue of `Grid` — it owns the mesh pipeline, the per-frame-in-flight camera UBOs + descriptor sets (set 0), the shared set-1 layout + a 1×1 white fallback texture, and the list of imported `Model`s, exposing `addModel()` and a `record(cmd, camera, frameIndex)` the renderer calls before the grid. A `Model` (`scene/model.{h,cpp}`; `mesh.{h,cpp}` holds only `Mesh` and the push-constant structs since the 2026-09-09 split) is one imported file: a set of `Mesh`es (one per material group) under a shared transform, and it owns the `VkDescriptorPool` its meshes' set-1 descriptors come from (sized to its mesh count) plus an **`Armature`** (`scene/armature.{h,cpp}` + `scene/armatureik.cpp` + `scene/armatureiksolve.cpp` — pure std + GLM, **no Vulkan, no Qt**): the runtime skeleton (`ArmatureBone` list → bones with translation-only binds and oriented rotation frames), the per-joint Euler + pose-translation pose, forward kinematics into the skinning **dual quaternions** the `Model` uploads per frame in flight (`Armature::skinDualQuats/skinVersion`), the selection + highlight twin, the pose snapshot (`capturePose/applyPose`, `@trans:`/`@pin:` rows), the user joint pins, the **model transform** (IK, picking, and grounding all need it, so it lives with the pose), and the whole full-body-IK integration. Every posing call on `Model` is a thin forwarder into it — `setBoneRotation`/`applyPose` additionally settle the model's correctives, which the armature can't know about — and **new posing behaviour belongs in the armature, never in `Model`**: the split (2026-09-07) is what lets the IK harness run the real drag loop instead of a mirror copy. `eulerMatrix` (the pose composition) lives in `ik/ikmath` next to its inverse `eulerFromMatrix` so the two can't drift; `Armature::dump/loadDump` write/read the skeleton-dump format the harness consumes (one bone per line: name, parent, rotation order, local bind translation, orientation Euler degrees, per-axis limits + flags), and `Model`'s constructor writes one when `POSESTUDIO_DUMP_SKELETON=<path>` is set. A static model (an OBJ) still carries an armature: no bones, one identity joint, the transform. Each `Mesh` owns device-local vertex+index buffers, a base color, an optional `VulkanTexture`, and a set-1 descriptor pointing at that texture (or the scene's white fallback when untextured — so one pipeline/shader serves both: `albedo = baseColor * white = baseColor`). GPU buffers go through `VulkanBuffer` (`rendering/vulkanbuffer.{h,cpp}`); textures through `VulkanTexture` (`rendering/vulkanimage.{h,cpp}`, an `_SRGB` sampled image with a full mip chain). Load-time transfers live in `rendering/vulkancommands.{h,cpp}`, which offers two paths: `submitImmediate` (one self-contained transfer = one submit + fence wait) and **`ImmediateBatch`** (record many transfers into a single command buffer, then one submit + one fence wait). `Model`'s constructor builds **one `ImmediateBatch` for the whole model** — every mesh's vertex/index buffers and texture record into it (via the batch-taking overloads of `createDeviceLocalBuffer` / `VulkanTexture`), and it's submitted exactly once. That's the key import-perf decision: the old per-resource `submitImmediate` cost ~3 blocking GPU round-trips *per mesh*, which dominated import time on models with many material groups; batching collapses it to one round-trip per model. Trade-off: all staging buffers are retained (via `ImmediateBatch::retain`) until that single submit completes, so peak staging memory is the sum across the model's meshes rather than one-at-a-time. The 1×1 white fallback texture still uses the plain immediate path (a lone one-off upload). **The scene's file map (2026-09-09):** `scene.{h,cpp}` keeps construction, model management/selection and `record()` as the ordered pass sequence; `sceneposing.cpp` the posing forwarders to the active figure; `scenepipelines.{h,cpp}` the layouts, pools, camera/IBL sets, fallbacks and the five mesh pipeline variants (each built from a fresh `meshBaseConfig()`, so no variant inherits another's blend state); `cameraubo.{h,cpp}` the UBO struct + `fillCameraUbo()` + `keyLightDirection()`; `shadowpass`, `outlinepass`, `lineoverlay` (skeleton, pin markers, the ortho floor line — recorded EVEN WITH NO MODELS, so an empty scene's ortho side views keep their floor line); `bonepicker` (`pickBone`); `posefile` (`readPoseFile`/`writePoseFile` — a truncated or non-finite file is rejected whole, never half-applied); `textureuploadcache`; `correctiveset` (the delta table + per-frame weights + the spline evaluation on a fixed-size stack); `groundsampler`. Transparent shells are sorted ONCE PER FRAME across ALL models by their POSED centroid (`Model::posedCentroid` — each mesh's weight-dominant joint's skin transform applied to its bind centroid), so a translucent prop between the camera and a figure's eye, or a figure lying down, still blends back-to-front; `Model` partitions opaque/transparent meshes at construction and bakes each mesh's constant push data (`m_pushTemplate`). A wire-only shade mode on a device without `fillModeNonSolid` falls back to the Clay surface instead of an empty viewport, and `Scene::setShadeMode` clamps its index.

**Image-based lighting** (`scene/environment.{h,cpp}`, `rendering/iblmaps.{h,cpp}`, `viewport/environmentsource.{h,cpp}`): the PBR/default shading is lit by an HDR *environment*, not a hand-tuned rig — this is what replaced 3-point lighting as the default. `environment.*` (Qt-free core) generates a neutral **procedural studio** environment and bakes three IBL products on the CPU (no offscreen GPU passes — the codebase has no compute/render-to-texture infra; the heavy loops — the prefilter AND, since 2026-09-09, the SH projection and luminance normalization (per-row partials reduced in row order, so the result is deterministic across thread counts) — are multi-threaded via `parallelFor` from `viewport/util/parallelfor.h`, a tiny std-only helper shared by every CPU bake — millions of samples that would otherwise stall startup/HDRI switches for seconds): **9 SH coefficients** for diffuse irradiance (Ramamoorthi form; fed via the set-0 UBO every frame and reconstructed per-normal in `mesh.frag`), a GGX-**prefiltered specular cubemap** (`prefilterSpecular`, mip = roughness), and the environment-**BRDF LUT** (`integrateBrdfLut`) — the standard split-sum decomposition, with SH standing in for an irradiance cubemap. The BRDF LUT is environment-independent (roughness/NdotV only), so it's integrated once and reused; only the SH + prefiltered cubemap re-bake when the environment changes. `IblMaps` (its input PODs `PrefilteredSpecular`/`BrdfLut` live in `rendering/ibldata.h`, which `environment.h` includes — so `rendering/` never includes `scene/`) uploads the cubemap (RGBA16F, cube view, CPU-generated mip chain) + LUT (RG16F) and owns the images/samplers behind descriptor **set 3**; `Scene` owns the set-3 layout/pool/set. The CPU bake and the GPU upload are split so the former can run off the render thread: `bakeEnvironment()` (core, Qt-free) does the heavy CPU work (normalize + SH + prefilter) into a `BakedEnvironment`, and `Scene::applyBakedEnvironment()` does the fast GPU upload + set-3 swap (after a `vkDeviceWaitIdle`, since it swaps maps the pipeline samples). The procedural default is baked synchronously in the `Scene` ctor so set 3 is valid before the first frame. Real panoramas replace the procedural default: `environmentsource.{h,cpp}` is the **codec boundary** — kept in the Qt-facing layer so the render core stays image-codec-free, exactly like model-texture decoding — whose `loadEnvironmentImage()` decodes an equirectangular panorama into an `EnvironmentImage`, dispatching on extension: Radiance `.hdr` via `stb_image`'s `stbi_loadf`, OpenEXR `.exr` via tinyexr's `LoadEXR` (declarations from `<tinyexr.h>`; implementation TU `tinyexr_impl.cpp`). `VulkanWindow` loads `<appDir>/environment.hdr` or `environment.exr` (explicit override, `.hdr` checked first) or a panorama from the **user library's `hdri/` tree** (`LibraryPaths::defaultHdri()` — see `src/core/librarypaths.h`; since 2026-09-09 the `<appDir>/environment.hdr|exr` developer override is folded INTO `defaultHdri()` so the panel's button and the viewport can't disagree, `userLibraryRoot()` is cached (invalidated by `invalidateCache()` whenever a library is added/removed), `hdriDirectory()` is a pure path with `ensureHdriDirectory()` doing the `mkpath` on demand, and `hdriEntries()` returns (path, category, name) precomputed once): the stock `Studio Small 09` by basename (extension-agnostic — `.hdr` or `.exr`) wherever the user filed it, else the first of `LibraryPaths::hdriFiles()` — the **recursive**, category-ordered scan (subfolders = categories, root files first) shared by the Environment panel's menu and its initial button text, so the three can't drift; no panoramas at all just leaves the procedural default. HDRIs are deliberately **user content, not build output** — they live in "My PoseStudio Library/hdri" (user-writable, browsable in the Asset Manager) where users drop their own `.hdr`/`.exr` files; nothing is mirrored next to the executable and packaging/installers are responsible for placing the stock set there. **The bake runs off the UI thread:** `VulkanWindow::beginEnvironmentBake` decodes + `bakeEnvironment`s on a `QtConcurrent` worker (watched by a `QFutureWatcher`) and applies the result on the GUI thread, guarded by a per-request id so a slower earlier bake can't clobber a newer pick — so switching HDRIs (the ~hundreds-of-ms CPU prefilter) never freezes the app; the viewport keeps rendering the current environment until the new one lands. `stb_image` and `tinyexr` are fetched via `FetchContent` (pinned) and each compiled in one TU (`stb_impl.cpp` / `tinyexr_impl.cpp`), same as VMA/tinyobjloader; tinyexr inflates through the target's existing miniz sources (`TINYEXR_USE_MINIZ`), adding no zlib dependency. **Live controls:** the exposure/diffuse/specular/ambient-fill/key (intensity + azimuth/elevation)/subsurface/rim/rotation/tonemap dials — plus the **Shadow group** (enable, intensity, softness, reach: enable off skips the shadow pass entirely in `Scene::recordShadowPass` and parks the light matrix on the always-lit degenerate projection; intensity rides to `mesh.frag` via `params4.z` (blends the figure's self-shadow toward lit) and to the grid via its push-constant dials vec4; softness/reach shape the grid's PCSS penumbra + dissolve) — are `LightingSettings` (`scene/lightingsettings.h`, a plain-POD contract), fed through the UBO (`params`/`params2`/`params3`/`params4`; the key *direction* is computed CPU-side in `Scene::record` from the azimuth/elevation dials into the UBO's `lightDir`, so every analytic shade mode follows it) and driven by the **Environment properties tab** (`src/properties/environmentpanel.*`, a right-docker panel) with no re-bake — only switching the HDRI re-bakes. **The key light auto-aims at the environment's sun on USER HDRI switches:** `bakeEnvironment` derives the environment's dominant light direction from the luminance-weighted linear SH band (`BakedEnvironment::dominantDir` + `dominantStrength` = |L1|/L0 directionality), and when a *user-initiated* bake lands (`beginEnvironmentBake`'s `autoAimKey` — true from `setEnvironmentFile`, FALSE for the startup bake, so a fresh launch shows the authored `LightingSettings` defaults), `VulkanWindow` sets the key azimuth/elevation dials to it (elevation clamped to [15°, 60°] — below-horizon suns can't ground-shadow, near-zenith ones drop the shadow straight down where it reads as a smudge; skipped when directionality < 0.1, i.e. overcast) and emits `lightingRestored` so the panel rows follow — so picking an HDRI re-aims the PBR shadow to where that environment's light visibly comes from, while the dials stay live for manual re-aiming. **In the PBR mode the key also follows the Rotation dial**: `Scene::keyLightDir` applies the inverse of the shader's environment-sampling rotation (mesh.frag's `rotateY` replicated CPU-side), so rotating the environment swings the key highlight and the ground shadow with the visible backdrop; the analytic modes keep the dials as plain world-space directions. **The HDRI selector is `HdriSelector`** (`src/properties/hdriselector.{h,cpp}`, a `QPushButton` + `QMenu`; its thumbnail cache is a member keyed on path+mtime — never a function static, whose QIcon data would outlive QApplication) (the shader-picker pattern — NOT a `QComboBox`, see the styling note there), but the menu's content is a **`QWidgetAction` hosting a `QListWidget`** (`#HdriList`) rather than plain actions — a bare `QMenu` can't cap itself at N rows or show a real scroll bar. The list, rebuilt on every `aboutToShow` so files added/refiled while the app runs appear immediately, shows each panorama (`.hdr`/`.exr`) in the user library's `hdri/` tree **grouped by subfolder** — users categorize panoramas by moving them into subfolders, and each subfolder becomes a bold **category heading** — spaced off the previous section, with a hairline rule under the text (a disabled, unclickable `QListWidgetItem` painted by the file-local `HdriHeadingDelegate`, since QSS can't address individual items; uncategorized root files list first with no heading; a nested folder's heading reads `A/B`) — each panorama with a large (178×89) image **thumbnail** — a same-basename image file in the panorama's own (sub)folder, any common format, first hit wins (the stock previews are `.jpg`; **beware `.webp`: Qt only decodes it with the optional "Qt Image Formats" add-on installed**, so don't ship webp-only previews) — six rows tall with a scroll bar for the rest, the current environment highlighted + scrolled into view, and a left-aligned "Open HDRI Folder…" footer button under it. All its styling (tight rows, menu-style hover, dark scroll bar, the spin boxes' +/− arrow glyphs) lives in `_environment.qss`. **Gotcha:** Qt's style engine ignores QSS `padding-left` on a left-aligned `QPushButton` label (the text lays out from the border box), so the button's left inset for the environment name comes from a transparent spacer icon set in code, not the stylesheet. The analytic lights still live in the UBO for the non-IBL modes: only **Rendered** (mode 0) uses the full key/fill/rim rig; the other analytic modes (Toon/Clay/Lighting-Only/Flat) use just the key + ambient, and PBR uses only the key.

**Import subsystem** (`viewport/import/`): model import is a self-contained, Qt-free subsystem (same "engine core = std + Vulkan + GLM, no Qt" rule as `rendering/`+`scene/`), structured so new formats slot in without touching the renderer, the dispatch, or existing importers. Its center is a **format-neutral IR** — `ModelData`/`MeshData` (`import/modeldata.h`) plus the shared `Vertex` layout (`import/vertex.h`, the geometry contract importers produce and `Scene` uploads) — that carries no format identity of its own. Each importer implements the `MeshImporter` interface (`import/meshimporter.h`: `displayName()` / `extensions()` / `load(path) → ModelData`, throwing on failure), lives in its own per-format folder (today just `import/obj/objimporter.{h,cpp}`, the single tinyobjloader TU — it triangulates, de-indexes unique `Vertex`es, generates smooth normals when the file lacks them, groups faces by material, and records `Kd` as base color), and is dispatched by file extension through `ImporterRegistry` (`import/importerregistry.{h,cpp}`). The registry is built from **one explicit list in its constructor** (`m_importers.push_back(std::make_unique<ObjImporter>())`, mirroring the `SHADER_SOURCES` list in CMake). So **adding a format is: implement `MeshImporter` under a new `import/<fmt>/`, add one `push_back` to the registry, and add the files + the `src/viewport/import/<fmt>` include dir to `CMakeLists.txt`** — nothing else changes. A mesh's texture is expressed as a `TextureSource` — *either* a resolved external file path (OBJ's `map_Kd`) *or* embedded encoded image bytes (e.g. a texture packed into a `.glb`) — so the IR already spans external and embedded sources; importers never decode.

Image decoding is deliberately **not** in the Qt-free core. The Qt-facing `ModelImportService` (`viewport/modelimportservice.{h,cpp}`) owns the orchestration: it looks up the importer (`ImporterRegistry::forPath`), runs `load()`, `QImage`-decodes the meshes' `TextureSource`s (from path *or* embedded bytes) into tightly-packed RGBA8, and calls `VulkanRenderer::addModel(ModelData)` — keeping the renderer free of any image-codec dependency while supporting every format Qt's image plugins do. **`decodeModelTextures` (shared by the OBJ and figure paths) decodes per unique SOURCE, not per mesh**: figure zones commonly reference the same atlas files (face/lips/ears share the face maps; torso/arms/legs the body maps), so each unique file is decoded ONCE — across cores via `parallelFor` (QImage is reentrant) — into a shared, immutable `DecodedImage` (`import/modeldata.h`) that every referencing mesh points at (`MeshData::diffuseImage` etc., `shared_ptr`); the GPU layer keys its uploads on the image's identity (`TextureUploadCache` in `scene/mesh.cpp`), so a shared image is also **uploaded once per model** (one staging copy + mip chain instead of per zone — this decode/upload dedup + parallelism cut a character import to a fraction of its serial time). Decodes are **resolution-capped** (`kMaxTextureSize`, 4096): characters commonly ship 8192² bump/roughness maps that cost 4× a 4K map at every stage (decode, fill, staging, mips, ~341 MB VRAM each) for detail the viewport can't resolve — `readCapped` folds the downscale into the decode via `QImageReader::setScaledSize` (a fast DCT-scaled decode for JPEG). Promote the cap to a Preference if a full-res close-up use case appears. It also **bakes any opacity (cutout) mask into the diffuse map's alpha channel**: the mask (a lash/brow card's shape, an older figure's eye-shell fade) is grayscale-decoded, rescaled to the diffuse dimensions, and written into `.a` — the bake COPIES the diffuse first, so each unique (diffuse, mask) pair gets its own shared image while unmasked users keep the pristine decode; `mesh.frag` computes `alpha = pushOpacity × diffuseTexel.a` (a no-op for unmasked textures, whose alpha is 1). A successful bake sets `MeshData::hasOpacityMask`, which `Mesh::isTransparent()` honours so masked meshes reach the alpha-blended pass even at scalar opacity 1 (a lash card's scalar is 1 — its shape exists only in the mask). And it **gutter-fills** each unique decoded image once (`import/texturegutters.{h,cpp}`, Qt-free): it rasterizes the **union of every user-mesh's UV triangles** into a coverage mask (a shared atlas must keep all its users' islands intact) and floods the unused atlas background with colours dilated from the islands (a pull-push fill across all four RGBA channels — baked alpha included — diffuse and normal/bump alike; the bake runs first so the mask gets the same seam treatment). The coverage raster has three defenses against pathological content, each learned from a real character: triangles identical in (quantized, wrap-folded) UV space are **deduplicated** before rasterizing (fibermesh strands — a 150k-triangle brow follower — map thousands of strands onto one UV strip; re-marking it per strand cost ~3 s per map), a mesh whose UVs span **multiple tiles** skips the fill entirely (a wrapping material samples its whole texture — no background to fill — and its tile-spanning triangles clamp to full-image raster walks), and the per-triangle walk is a **scanline** (per row, each edge's interval bound is linear in y) so cost tracks covered area + bbox height, never bbox area (long thin diagonal lash-card triangles otherwise walk millions of background texels). Without this, the GPU-built mip chain averages the (typically white) atlas background through every UV seam, which shows as bright seam lines at viewing distance; covered texels are untouched, so close-up rendering is unchanged. Keep this behind the decode choke point — any new import path that bypasses `decodeModelTextures` reintroduces the seams (and the per-zone duplication). The full chain is `MenuManager` (File → Import → `.OBJ`, a `QFileDialog`) → `ViewportWidget::importObj` → `VulkanWindow::importObj` (queues the path if the renderer isn't built yet — it's created lazily on first expose) → `ModelImportService::importInto` → `MeshImporter::load` → `VulkanRenderer::addModel` → `Scene::addModel`. `main.cpp` also feeds any `.obj` on the command line through `ViewportWidget::importObj` (drag-onto-exe / "open with"), which is why the queue-until-ready step matters. Imports run on the GUI thread between frames; the batched upload blocks briefly on its single submit but touches no in-flight state, so the next recorded frame just picks the new model up. Because that work is synchronous and can still be slow (large meshes, many textures), `ModelImportService` drives a modal staged `QProgressDialog` (read → per-mesh texture decode → GPU upload) — a `showProgress` flag gates it: interactive imports pass `true`, but the startup/command-line drain (called from inside `initializeVulkan()`/`exposeEvent()`) passes `false`, because a modal dialog's `processEvents()` could re-enter the expose/init path. The progress UI lives only in this Qt layer; the importers and renderer core stay Qt-free. **The File → Import menu is deliberately *not* registry-driven** — it lists all 10 formats as hardcoded entries in `menumanager.cpp` (alphabetical), with `.OBJ` (→ `importObjFile`), **`.DUF (DUF File)`** (→ `importFigureFile`, the character-figure entry) and, since 2026-11-03, **`.FBX (FBX File)`** (→ `importFbxFile`, the FBX character entry — see the FBX paragraph after the follower-addons one) wired and the rest disabled placeholders, so enabling a newly-added importer means flipping its menu entry from disabled to a real `QFileDialog`+import connect there. **Not yet:** non-diffuse PBR maps (normal/metal/rough — OBJ doesn't natively carry them), and the other 8 mesh Import formats are still disabled placeholders.

**Native figure importer** (`import/figure/`): imports rigged, morphable character figures from their **native scene format** — a gzip-compressed-JSON preset (`.duf`) that references geometry/rig/skin/morph/UV data files (`.dsf`) across a content folder. This is a much bigger importer than a mesh format (a figure is geometry + material zones + skeleton + skin weights + morphs), so it does **not** use the static `MeshImporter`/`ImporterRegistry` path (which returns `ModelData` and needs no external context) — it has its own rich IR (`FigureData`/`FigureMesh`/`FigureMaterial` in `figuredata.h`) and its own loader that needs the content-root folders to resolve cross-file URIs. **Legal/naming:** per the standing no-brand rule, no product or figure-line names appear anywhere in this code — and they don't need to: the format is self-describing, so **one figure-agnostic reader handles every figure family** (geometry, bones, weights, morphs, materials all come out of the files) — verified importing and rendering all seven target figure families (male & female base figures across several generations, plus a unified newer figure), including textured, morphed character presets. Qt-free layers, bottom-up:
- `figuredocument.{h,cpp}` — loads one file: read → gzip-sniff (`1f 8b`) → inflate (miniz) → parse (nlohmann). The single miniz-consuming TU.
- `uriresolver.{h,cpp}` — resolves the URL-encoded, root-relative cross-file URIs (`/data/…#id`, `/Runtime/…`) against the content roots (exact-case under ALL roots first, then the per-component case-insensitive fallback per root — so a second root is never paid for with a directory walk under the first; per-directory listings are memoized — so Linux/macOS resolve the mixed-case URIs that NTFS absorbs for free), and **caches** parsed docs so a shared base file isn't re-inflated per reference.
- `geometryparser` / `uvparser` / `materialparser` — parse the base `.dsf` geometry (quads→tris, grouped by material zone), the external UV sets (with per-corner **seam overrides**, so the mesh splits at UV seams; whole-surface smooth normals keep zone boundaries seamless — and SINCE 2026-10-26 ONE SET PER MATERIAL ZONE: the geometry names a default, and each scene material may name the set its zone's maps are painted on (`uv_set`, read by `sceneMaterialUvSets` in `scenematerials` BEFORE assembly, since the UVs are baked into the render mesh; each distinct set is parsed once and shared, refused when its `vertex_count` is another cage's; `subdivideFigure` takes the per-zone set list; `POSESTUDIO_UV_DEFAULT_ONLY` is the A/B, and the import service logs how many zones are on a set of their own). A newer generation's character commonly keeps the previous generation's skin maps and names that generation's layout for its skin zones while its eyes and nails use the new one — the two layouts differ by a few dozen UVs and seam entries, and read through the default alone every skin feature sat slightly off: a crack along the scalp's seam, a patch between the shoulder blades, the throat's notch displaced (the user's report, with three close-ups). The hierarchical-material form's `?uv_set` tracks are NOT read: those presets are applied after assembly, and no content seen names another set through them), and the preset's physically-based materials (base color + texture maps — referenced either as a direct `image_file` path or, in newer generations, an `image` index into `image_library`). **Specular is interpreted from the material's LOBES, never one channel**: skin materials disable the glossy lobe (`Glossy Layered Weight` 0, its roughness left at 0) and author the real response in **Dual Lobe Specular** — broad lobe roughnesses (~0.6–1.0) blended by the lobe ratio, and a reflectivity ~0.3 that IS real skin's F0 (source convention F0 = 0.08 × reflectivity; 0.5 = the standard 4% dielectric). `materialparser`'s ladder (dual lobe when enabled + weighted → glossy lobe [`max(Glossy Roughness, 1 − Glossiness)`, the pair being two dials for one quantity] → struct defaults) yields an effective **roughness** plus a **specularWeight** (lobe weight × reflectivity/0.5, an F0 multiplier — folded in `foldSpecWeight`, which discounts map-carried weight/reflectivity channels by an assumed dark-mask average (the maps aren't sampled at import, and the dialed scalar is often >1 as the map's multiplier) and caps the result at 1 = the standard 4% dielectric; skin lands ~0.3–0.8 → 1–3% F0) and reads `Metallic Weight`; naively reading `Glossy Roughness` took the *disabled* lobe's 0 and rendered skin mirror-smooth — wet plastic under image-based specular, while an eye cornea (glossy lobe at its defaults) correctly stays mirror-smooth so the catchlight survives. Transparency for the eye's clear shells is keyed on a strong **Refraction Weight** (not a "no-map + low-roughness" heuristic, which wrongly turned some figures' untextured skin invisible) — and, for **older generations that predate that shader**, on the format's CORE top-level `transparency` channel (UI name "Opacity Strength"; the value IS the opacity, 0 = invisible): without reading it, an older base figure's cornea/moisture rendered as opaque white balls over the eyes. Either channel may also carry an **opacity (cutout) image mask** — lash/brow cards, the older moisture shells — captured as `opacityImageUri` and baked into the diffuse map's **alpha channel** by the decode layer (see the import-subsystem notes), so masked surfaces render with their real shapes. **Channel-value convention:** `current_value` is the *dialed* value and always wins over `value` (only the channel default) — for scalars *and* colours alike. Reading `value` for colours once rendered every zone with a 75%-grey default tint (textures too dark) and drew black-dialed pupils as bright grey/white dots in the eyes.
- `nodeparser` / `skinparser` — the skeleton (bone hierarchy + rest transforms from `node_library`) and the per-vertex top-4 skin weights (from the `SkinBinding` modifier), for GPU skinning. **Two weight forms** (2026-09-18): the general map (`node_weights`, some assets `vertex_weights`), and the OLDEST generations' per-axis form — no general map at all, but three per-joint ROTATION maps `local_weights.x/y/z` (identical on a finger, different on a hip or shoulder) plus `scale_weights`/`bulge_weights` we don't model; `parseSkinWeights` takes the MEAN of the three axis maps as the general map (a vertex absent from a map counts 0), which is also how a general-weighted rig approximates such a binding. Before this the two oldest figure families imported with NO skin weights: every vertex bound with weight 0, so the shader's zero blend left them at bind — they rendered, and never deformed when posed (found only when the IK harness's mesh sample for them came out empty).
- `morphresolver` / `morphparser` — the character's shape: `morphresolver` evaluates the preset's dialed channels through their **channel-driver formulas** (control → driven morphs); `morphparser` reads each reached morph's sparse deltas, baked into the base vertices at import.
- `correctiveparser.{h,cpp}` — detects + parses a **pose corrective** (joint-driven corrective morph) from a modifier: confirms it's rotation-driven, translates its `push`/`mult`/`spline_tcb` formula into the engine's stack-program IR (`PoseCorrective` in `modeldata.h`), and folds its character/enable gates to constants (dropping any a gate switched off). See the correctives paragraph below.
- `subdivision.{h,cpp}` — **Catmull-Clark** subdivision of the base cage into a smoother render mesh (positions smooth; UVs per-corner + skin weights refined linearly), then assembled per zone like the un-subdivided path. Also carries each corrective's sparse deltas onto the subdivided cage (`carryCorrectivesToSubdivided`, Catmull-Clark being linear) — SPARSELY since 2026-09-09: only the faces/edges/rings touched by a corrective's deltas are evaluated, with the dense pass's exact stencil arithmetic, verified bit-identical by checksum on three figures (the dense per-corrective full-mesh pass was the dominant slice of import time). `GeometryData::Face::sourceIndex` keeps the polylist index stable past a skipped polygon so seam overrides never drift. See the subdivision paragraph below.
- `figureutils.{h,cpp}` — the JSON/URI helpers every parser used to duplicate: `parseChannelRef` (the one parser of `Node:/file#frag?prop`, returning raw AND decoded keys so each caller keeps comparing what it always compared), `urlDecode`, `toLowerAscii`, `valuesArray`, `childOf`, `findGeometryEntry`, `findModifierById`, the `current_value`-over-`value` channel readers next to their deliberate inverse (`restVec3Channels`, the rest-value reader `nodeparser` needs).
- `correctivescan.{h,cpp}` — the parallel pose-corrective discovery (candidate collection incl. the sibling-base fallback, the `?rotation/` prefilter, the serial `parseCorrective` loop — now per-modifier fault-tolerant, so one malformed third-party file can't sink the import; cached documents are reused instead of re-inflated; same-file `#id` gate URLs resolve through the owning document instead of silently gating the corrective off).
- `bonefixups.{h,cpp}` — post-parse skeleton adjustments: joint-centre offsets and the legacy name-based twist-bone lock fallback.
- `scenematerials.{h,cpp}` — applying a preset's materials onto the assembled zones (both preset forms; the cross-file base merge works through `MaterialBaseOverrides` maps instead of copying the whole `material_library`).
- `followeraddons.{h,cpp}` — the post-load follower support (`findPostLoadAddons`, `buildAddonProjection`, `followParentShape`, `mergeAddonFigure` — the base-vertex offset is `FigureData::baseVertexCount`, the WHOLE cage, not the max referenced index, so a corrective on an unreferenced cage vertex can never alias into a follower).
- `figureimporter.{h,cpp}` — orchestrates (≈250 lines: locate base → geometry → morph bake → UVs → skeleton + fixups → skin → correctives → subdivision + delta carry → materials → addons): preset `.duf` → find the figure node's `geometries[0].url` → resolve+load the base `.dsf` → parse geometry → **bake dialed morphs** → parse skeleton + skin weights → **discover pose correctives** → resolve+parse the UV sets (the default + each zone's own) → **subdivide the cage** (and each corrective's deltas) → assemble per-zone meshes (with joint weights) → attach each zone's resolved material → `FigureData` (meshes + skeleton + weights + correctives).

The Qt-facing `FigureImportService` (`viewport/figureimportservice.{h,cpp}`, the figure analogue of `ModelImportService`) resolves the **content-root folder(s)** the preset's `/data/…#id` URIs resolve against — **auto-detected** by walking up from the imported file to the ancestor containing `data/`, **unioned with any user-configured roots** (`Constants::PREF_FIGURE_CONTENT_ROOTS`, a newline-separated path list in Preferences), auto-detected first (most authoritative for a self-contained library) then configured. This matters because content is commonly split: a figure browsed from a **presets-only folder** (`.duf`s with no co-located `data/`) has *no* auto-detectable root, so without the configured fallback its geometry/morphs/skin/UVs (all under `data/`) can't be found and **every** figure fails with "cannot resolve base geometry" — the configured roots are what make that case work. It then runs `FigureImporter`, converts `FigureData`→`ModelData` applying the **cm→world unit scale (÷100)** so a ~180 cm figure lands at grid scale, **puts the figure ON THE FLOOR** (`toModelData`'s last step: the lowest vertex of the converted mesh goes to y = 0, every vertex and every bone's bind shifted by the same amount, logged as `[figure] rests N mm above|below the floor as authored` — a character's morphs move its soles, 61mm BELOW to 54mm above the floor across 23 custom characters, and even the base figures rest 5-8mm up; the IK rig reads the floor as y = 0 in the figure's own bind, see FIGURES STAND ON THE FLOOR in the FBIK section), decodes textures via the shared `ModelImportService::decodeModelTextures` (decode-once shared images — see the import-subsystem notes), and uploads through the **existing lit mesh pipeline** (`addModel`). Routing mirrors OBJ: `ViewportWidget::importFigure` → `VulkanWindow::importFigure` (queues until the renderer exists) → the service; File → Import → **`.DUF (DUF File)`** (the character-figure entry in the alphabetical format list, `*.duf`/`*.dsf` filter), command-line/"open-with" `.duf`/`.dsf`, and **double-clicking a figure asset in the Asset Manager grid** (`AssetManagerWidget::importFigureRequested` → `ViewportWidget::importFigure`, the figure analogue of the `.obj` → `importModelRequested` route) all route here. **Failure feedback (interactive path only, `showProgress`):** `importInto` classifies the failure and responds in kind. A `.duf` that isn't a figure (pose/material/light presets share the extension and all appear in the grid) throws "no geometry reference found" → a `QMessageBox` ("this may be a pose/material preset rather than a figure…"). A real figure whose `data/` library isn't on any known root (empty roots, or a resolve error) is the **recoverable** case: a **"Locate Content Folder…"** prompt → a folder picker → the chosen root is persisted to `PREF_FIGURE_CONTENT_ROOTS` and the import is **retried once** with it folded in (a single-shot `for (attempt < 2)` loop), so the fix is one-time and remembered. The startup/command-line drain (`showProgress=false`) stays dialog-free (returns false silently) to avoid re-entering the expose path. The actual per-attempt work lives in a file-local `runFigureImport()` that `importInto` calls inside that loop. Materials also **merge the `material_library` base** each scene material extends by `url` (`#id`): a scene material only *overrides* channels, so roughness and the transparency-defining channels (which live on the base) are read from the merge — essential for the eye's clear shells. **Current state:** a figure renders textured in bind pose with per-zone roughness-driven specular, cheap **subsurface skin shading** (wrap-lighting + a warm subsurface band, in `mesh.frag`), alpha-blended transparency for the eye's clear shells (cornea/moisture — detected via the material's `Refraction Weight` channel read from the merged base, so the iris shows through), tangent-space **normal-map** support (screen-space cotangent frame — no vertex tangents), and grayscale **bump** maps applied as a mip-stable **texture-space central-difference** perturbation (height sampled at `textureSize`-derived texel offsets — this replaced an earlier screen-space version that was too noisy on the UDIM maps), which is what gives the base skin its surface grain. **Phase 3 (skeleton + skinning) is done and the figure is posable:** `nodeparser` builds the bone hierarchy from `node_library`, `skinparser` inverts the `SkinBinding` modifier into per-base-vertex top-4 weights, `assembleFigureMeshes` attaches those to each render vertex, and `FigureImportService` hands the renderer a `ModelData` with a skeleton (`ModelBone` list). At the GPU layer every `Model` carries a joint-matrix storage buffer (see the mesh-pipeline notes) and a runtime skeleton; `Model::setBoneRotation(name, eulerDegrees)` poses a joint and recomputes the skin matrices. **Phase 2 (character-identity morphs) is done:** `morphresolver` walks the preset's dialed channels through their **channel-driver formulas** — a character dials a *control* (e.g. `CTRL<character> = 1`) whose linear `push`/`mult` formulas drive the real head/body shape morphs (`FHM…`/`FBM…`) — and `morphparser` reads each reached morph's sparse deltas, which the importer **bakes into the base vertices** (`position += weight·delta`) before assembly, so the generic base becomes the specific character (verified on real character presets: face + body shape). **Joint-center adjustment is part of this:** the same full-body/head morph modifiers also carry driver formulas whose *output* is a bone's `center_point/{x,y,z}` (a `FBM<character>` typically has hundreds — e.g. `hip.center_point.y += FBM<character>.value · coeff`), so the rig follows the character's new proportions. `morphresolver` returns these as a per-bone `JointCenterOffsets` map (routed out of the same formula walk that resolves morph weights — a bone `center_point` is *not* a morph channel to propagate), and `figureimporter` adds each offset to the matching `FigureBone::origin` right after `parseSkeleton` — before skin-weight parse, corrective discovery, subdivision, and assembly — in the same native-cm space as `origin` (the cm→world × `figureScale` in `toModelData` then scales mesh and skeleton together). Without this, a *base-figure* rig sits inside a *morphed* mesh: posed limbs pivot at the wrong places and the joint overlay floats off the body. `end_point`/`orientation` outputs are skipped — the bind is translation-only and orientation is read straight from the node, so they don't affect skinning. Verified: an unmorphed base figure shifts 0 bones; a strongly-stylized character shifts ~169/171 (max ~6.4 cm). **Posing is anatomically correct:** a bone's rest transform stays translation-only (the orientation cancels at rest — `T·OR·OR⁻¹`), and `setBoneRotation` applies the rotation in the joint's oriented frame (`localBind · OR · R(euler, rotation_order) · OR⁻¹`), so an elbow/knee/shoulder bends about its true axes (verified). **Joint rotation limits are enforced:** each bone's `rotation/{x,y,z}` channel carries a `min`/`max` range (degrees) and a `clamped` flag (the format's per-channel "use limits") — the joint's anatomical range of motion (a knee ≈ x∈[-11°,155°], the neck ≈ x∈[-30°,25°]). `nodeparser` reads these into `FigureBone` (matching axis by channel *id*, not array position, since the array is ordered per `rotation_order`), they flow through `ModelBone`→`GpuBone`, and `Armature::clampBoneEuler()` — called at the top of `applyBoneEuler()` and in the `applyPose()` loop, the two points every posing path (free-drag, gizmo, pose load, undo) funnels through — clamps each *limited* axis to `[min,max]` in place (so accumulated drags stop cleanly at the limit and saved/queried poses reflect the clamped angle). The format carries **two distinct constraints** and `nodeparser` honours both: `clamped` (move within [min,max]) and **`locked`** (cannot be posed at all — enforced as a hard [0,0] clamp). `locked` is how figures forbid anatomically impossible motion: a mid-limb twist bone (which exists only to spread axial twist across the skin) locks its two bend axes so the limb can never fold where there is no joint — beware that these bones are *not* reliably named "twist" (in several generations the mid-forearm bone is named like a wrist joint, with the real wrist named like a hand), which is why the geometric name-based twist-lock fallback in `figureimporter` missed it; that heuristic now defers to any format-locked axis and remains only for old generations that predate the `locked` flag. Axes with neither flag, and models without a skeleton (OBJ), stay unconstrained. Verified: a base figure reports 399 limited axes across 171 bones, a 300° request on the knee clamps to 155°, on the neck to 25°, and a pose file demanding a 60°/60° bend on the mid-forearm joint's locked axes leaves the forearm straight while its twist axis stays free in its authored range. **Not yet:** limits aren't surfaced in the UI (no visual range indicator on the gizmo), and there's no global "use limits off" override; morph-driven limit *adjustments* (a formula driving `rotation/x?min`) aren't applied — the base node limits are used as-is. **Interactive posing UI:** the **skeleton overlay is hidden by default** — joints are grabbed directly on the figure, so the character isn't cluttered with a bone cage. **The rotate GIZMO (the three axis rings on the selected joint, with their range arcs) and the balance SUPPORT-POLYGON floor overlay were REMOVED on 2026-09-07** at the user's request ("get rid of the gizmo for now"; the floor polygon read as unintuitive) — the code is in git history (`Scene::gizmoAxisAt`/`rotateGizmo`, `Model::selectedBoneFrame`/`boneRotationRange`/`activeSupportHull`, `m_boneGizmoFrame`), and the descriptions of them below are kept as the design record. Today's overlays are the selection outline, the optional skeleton, and the pin markers — plus the **selected-part highlight**: the flesh the selected joint drives tints toward the selection blue. It is skin-weight driven, not an overlay: `Mesh::record` packs the active figure's `m_selectedBone` and its **highlight twin** (the armature's highlight twin (`Model::selectedHighlightTwin()`) — a bend bone's TWIST child, `Armature::isTwistBone`: two locked axes with the free one ALONG the bone, and vice versa, so a grabbed upper-arm joint lights the whole upper arm rather than the half the bend bone's own weights cover.
