Until 2026-09-30 the test was "a child with two locked axes", which a HINGE is too — a finger's or a toe's middle joint: the second joint of every finger was its first joint's twin, the two lit together whichever was clicked, and the user could not tell which finger joint was selected ("Joint 1, 2 and 3 must have their own distinct highlighting"); every digit joint lights its own phalanx now — gated on all eight rigs in the harness's transform-dials phase (no finger or toe joint has a twin; a limb's twist bone still lights with it) and in `gallery_digits.txt` by the scripts' `lit.<bone>`) into `material3.z` alongside the draw kind (kind + 8·(joint+1) + 8192·(twin+1), 3+10+10 bits, exact in a float — the push block is full); `mesh.vert` sums the vertex's `inWeights` on those joints — and on the joints lit WITH the selection, the face rig under a selected head, read off the joint buffer's second half (FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG in the FBIK section) — into the `vSelect` varying; `mesh.frag`'s `main()` now wraps the former body (`shade()`) and mixes a luminance-matched accent blue by `vSelect` afterwards, so it works in every shade mode (wires tint too) and keeps the surface's shading; alpha and the spec attachment are untouched. `mod(material3.z, 8)` is the draw kind everywhere the fragment used to read it raw. `Scene::record` still owns a coloured line list drawn through a dedicated line pipeline (over the mesh, depth test off), but only emits the joint→parent segments when `m_showSkeleton` is on (toggled by **View → Show Skeleton** and the checkable bone-icon **Skeleton** button in the viewport strip — the two stay in sync through `ViewportWidget::skeletonVisibilityChanged`, and the state lives in `VulkanWindow::m_showSkeleton`, remembered across the lazy renderer build like the shade mode); the gizmo rings are emitted **independently of that flag**, whenever a joint is selected. Crucially, **picking is independent of what's drawn**, and since 2026-09-26 **A CLICK PICKS WHAT THE CURSOR IS ON**: `Scene::selectBoneAt` casts the click against every figure's posed SKIN (`Model::pickSurface` → `GroundSampler::pickSurface`: the CPU skin copy kept for grounding, now carrying the OPAQUE meshes' triangles — transparent shells and masked cards are not pickable, so a click on a cornea is a click on the eye behind it — CPU-skinned per click and ray-tested two-sided, Moller-Trumbore over ~130k triangles after a posed-box test, a few ms; `POSESTUDIO_PICK_TRACE` prints the counts and the hit) and the FIRST surface along the ray wins: its bone is the joint that weighs most on the skin at the hit (the three corners' weights blended), its point the grab point; a joint of the hit bone's OWN body projecting within 14px of the click is snapped to — the joints of its rigid segment (`Armature::segmentJoints`), which cannot be hidden, being the segment the cursor is on, its PARENT's joint (where it hangs from: inside the body the cursor is on however deep — the chest joint sits on the spine, 10cm behind the breast's skin, and depth-checked for a day it was refused from a pectoral hit), and its CHILDREN's joints where they lie no deeper along the ray than the hit bone's own joint plus `kSnapDepthSlack` 3cm (a trunk bone's children are lateral PAIRS, and from the side the far collar or thigh socket projects onto the near one's pixel), each offered once (a pectoral is a twist-class bone of the chest, so its segment's near joint and its parent are both the chest), the nearest to the click winning and a tie going to the nearer to the camera; `POSESTUDIO_PICK_TRACE` prints the hit bone and every candidate with its pixel distance and depth — so a click on the knee still grabs the knee, a click at the chest joint's pixel from the front, which lands on the UPPER ABDOMEN's skin, grabs the chest (its child, at 0px), and a click right on the HIP's pixel grabs the hip: the root has no skin of its own (the pelvic flesh is the pelvis bone's), and with the hit bone's own joint the only candidate the snap took the pelvis joint 10px away over the hip at 0px, whose drag then asked the hips 2cm back in its first tick — the pelvis joint to where the hip's pixel was — and popped a foot 165mm on all fours after a prone-up (the in-app floor gallery's catch, the same day: 15mm again with the parent among the candidates; the same pelvis grab made at the PELVIS joint's own pixel never popped, so it was the snap's error, not the girdle grab's). The 2D pick below is the FALLBACK, for a click that misses the skin (just outside the silhouette), and only when nothing stands between the camera and the point it picked (a second cast toward that point; `kFallbackFleshDepth` 10cm of the joint's own flesh is not "something"). Until then the pick was the 2D one alone and saw THROUGH the body: a click on the chest found the shoulder or the arm behind it as often as the chest, since their joints project onto it (the user, 2026-09-26: "selecting body parts is cumbersome ... no body part should ever get selected by clicking through another body part"). A click on the belly at the hip's pixel picks the lower abdomen, whose skin it is; on the face or the side of the head the HEAD (the hit is a face-rig bone's skin, and a face-rig bone selects the head since 2026-09-28: FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG in the FBIK section). `tools/ikscripts/check_picking.txt` asserts it (`selected.<bone>`: the selected joint is that bone or of its rigid segment) — from +X, where her LEFT side is the near one, clicks at the right shoulder's, hand's, thigh's and knee's pixels select none of them; from the front a click at the chest joint's pixel selects neither collar nor shoulder. The galleries' `press <bone>` picks land on the skin now too (their `grabbed by name` fallback keeps the named joint where the surface's bone is another segment's) — as do `pin <bone>` and `click <bone>` (`pin` falls back to the name the same way and says `pinned by name instead`: from the side the hanging hand covers the hip, and a pin meant for the hips landed on a ring finger); `pick2d on|off` in a script, or `POSESTUDIO_PICK_2D=1` for a whole run, is the A/B — the 2D pick alone, its occlusion check off, as before 2026-09-26. The fallback `pickBone` (`bonepicker.cpp`) projects every bone to screen and hit-tests regardless of overlay visibility, and it picks by BONE BODY, not just joint origin (2026-09-07): each parent→child joint segment is the parent bone's body, and a click within 32 px of one selects that bone — a mid-thigh click used to miss (the thigh's joints sit at the hip and knee, farther than any sane radius) and fell through to a plain model click; a click within 14 px of a joint origin still snaps to that joint (the knee picks the shin whose origin it is), leaf bones (toes, fingertips) keep origin picking, and a root's own segment is skipped (a figure node at the origin with the hip a metre up would otherwise be picked from between the legs) — so joints stay clickable even though invisible. In `VulkanWindow`, a **left press that lands on a joint** (nearest projected bone within a pixel radius — `Scene::selectBoneAt`) selects it and begins a full-body-IK drag (see FBIK); with **Ctrl held** it begins a SCOPED IK drag instead — the grabbed chain alone moves (SCOPED IK in the FBIK section; until 2026-09-26 Ctrl+drag was the single-joint FK rotate, `m_posingBone`, which went with the change — a joint is turned by the Transform tab's dials and the B / S / T mouse mode, `Armature::setJointDial` → `nudgeBone` in the oriented frame); a left press on empty space orbits (even while a joint stays selected — `selectBoneAt` returns -1 on a miss but keeps the selection). `setBoneRotation(name, eulerDegrees)` remains the underlying primitive. A **rotate gizmo** (REMOVED 2026-09-07 — design record) drew three axis rings around the selected joint (X/Y/Z = red/green/blue) in the joint's own rotation frame (`Model::selectedBoneFrame` = `parentGlobal·localBind·orient`, so each ring maps to one Euler channel — including twist, which free-drag couldn't reach): a left-press that grabs a ring (`Scene::gizmoAxisAt`) enters an **axis-constrained** rotate where the drag's swept screen-angle about the joint drives that one channel (`Scene::rotateGizmo`), while a press on the joint itself still free-rotates. **Undo/redo is unified** (`VulkanWindow::undo/redo`): ONE chronological stack of typed `UndoEntry`s holds both **pose edits** (each committed drag pushes the pre-edit pose via `Scene::capturePose`/`applyPose`, the pose save/load primitives; applying an undo re-runs correctives like any pose change) and **Environment-panel lighting edits** (the panel brackets every gesture — dial scrub/type-in via `DragNumberBox`'s `editingStarted/editingFinished` signals, restore buttons, tonemap toggle, restore-all, depth-counted so composite edits register once — and calls `registerLightingUndo` with the pre-edit `LightingSettings`; undoing one re-applies the dials and emits `lightingRestored` → `ViewportWidget` → the panel, which syncs its widgets signals-blocked). It's driven by the **Edit → Undo/Redo actions** (`QKeySequence::Undo/Redo` — these window-level shortcuts are what make `Ctrl+Z` work while the panel has focus; focused text fields still keep their own Ctrl+Z via ShortcutOverride) with `VulkanWindow::keyPressEvent` as the in-viewport fallback. **Driven figure scale is applied:** `morphresolver` accumulates the preset's `scale/general` driver-formula contributions into `FigureData::figureScale`, which `FigureImportService` folds into the cm→world scale (so a taller/shorter character lands at the right height, mesh and skeleton together). **Only formulas targeting the FIGURE root node count** (the geometry-bearing scene node, matched by asset-url fragment): bone-targeted `scale/general` outputs are *propagating-scale rigs* — e.g. a head-scale control driving ~80 per-bone channels — and summing them into the figure scale once imported a teen character at 3× size (78 × the head dial stacked on top of the real figure dial). Per-bone scale isn't modeled (the bind is translation-only), so those outputs are dropped, not misapplied. Verified: the teen figure resolves 0.91 (its −0.09 figure dial), an oldest-generation child preset 0.54 — both landing at correct heights. **Pose save/load is wired:** `Model::capturePose()`/`applyPose()` (bind pose + named Euler rotations) round-trip through `Scene::savePose/loadPose` (a plain-text `.pose` file, one `name rx ry rz` line per posed joint — safe because figure bone names are space-free tokens), forwarded out through `VulkanRenderer`→`VulkanWindow`→`ViewportWidget`; File → **Save Pose… / Load Pose…** drive it, and a `.pose` path on the command line / "open-with" applies onto the imported figure (queued behind it like the figure itself). **Pose correctives are done:** joint-driven corrective morphs that fire as a function of bend angle to fix the mesh crumpling ("candy-wrapper") at deep elbow/knee/shoulder/hip bends. They aren't listed by the preset, so `figureimporter` discovers them **structurally** — a morph whose driver formula is driven by a joint `rotation/{x,y,z}` channel — scanning the corrective/flexion folders (plus each dialed character's own morph folder) under `<figure>/Morphs`; this is figure-generation-agnostic (the corrective files are named differently from one generation to the next, but the structure is identical — detection is never keyed on file/brand names). **Point-release figure generations inherit the previous generation's correctives via a SIBLING-BASE fallback**: a variant figure whose own Morphs tree has no corrective packs (its tree holds only head/FACS content — the source app links it to the earlier generation through its content database) gets the packs of a sibling figure directory whose base file's digit-stripped name matches AND whose base cage has the identical vertex count — morph-compatible by construction, so the delta indices land verbatim; only attempted when the own tree is empty, so same-generation figures never pay the sibling parse. **Weight fidelity is exact to the source app** (verified against the authored files): only formulas whose OUTPUT is the modifier's own `?value` channel contribute to its weight (vendor character corrective files bundle dozens of morph-driven joint-center/orientation adjustment formulas in the same modifier — summing those produced garbage), gate urls are URL-DECODED before matching dialed morph ids (vendor ids contain spaces), and the driven weight is CLAMPED to the morph channel's authored `min/max` — load-bearing for linear-ramp drivers: a knee-EXTENSION flexion is authored as `rotation/x × -1/11` and relies on the clamp to stay 0 through the 155° of flexion (unclamped it hit **-14** and cratered every deeply-bent thigh). `POSESTUDIO_DUMP_CORRECTIVES=1` prints each active corrective + weight whenever a pose settles. The scan is **parallel and pre-filtered** for import speed: candidate files load (read + gzip-inflate) across cores in bounded chunks, and a file whose inflated text lacks the literal `?rotation/` is skipped before the (dominant) JSON DOM parse — provably safe, since `correctiveparser`'s driver detection requires that exact literal form, and the dialed-morph folders hold hundreds of plain shape morphs that can never pass it; only the cheap `parseCorrective` walk stays serial (its gate resolution shares the non-thread-safe resolver cache). The dialed identity-morph documents are likewise **prefetched in parallel** (`UriResolver::prefetchDocuments`) before the bake loop. `correctiveparser` translates each driver formula (the `push`/`mult`/`spline_tcb` vocabulary the base correctives actually use) into a small stack-program IR (`CorrectiveOp`/`CorrectiveFormula`/`PoseCorrective` in `modeldata.h`), **folding the character/enable gates (the mult-stage drivers) to constants at import** — resolved to the dialed weight if the preset dials that channel, else the channel's own default `value` — so a non-loaded character's correctives (gate → 0) are dropped at import while a figure's default-on "base joint correctives" master toggle (default 1) keeps the base set live. At the engine the correctives are **blended on the GPU, live** (2026-09-09): `CorrectiveSet::build` (`scene/correctiveset.{h,cpp}`, owned by the `Model`; was `Model::buildRuntimeCorrectives`) maps every corrective's base-vertex deltas through each mesh's `baseVertex` array onto the render vertices (a seam vertex feeds several) into ONE static device-local **delta table** per model (set 2 binding 2: 16-byte `{corrective, dx, dy, dz}` entries grouped per vertex) and stamps each touched vertex's packed run — `Vertex::correctiveRange` = (first entry << 8) | count, vertex attribute 7, written into the upload copy so the importer's data stays pristine — while the per-frame **weights** ride a host-mapped storage buffer PER FRAME IN FLIGHT (set 2 binding 1, the joint-buffer scheme). `mesh.vert` and `shadow.vert` both sum weight·delta over the vertex's run BEFORE skinning (the two blends MUST match — shadow and outline trace the corrected surface), so the GPU skins the *corrected* bind mesh exactly as the old CPU re-morph produced it. The weights are re-evaluated at record time whenever the pose moved (`Armature::skinVersion` — every posing path bumps it: drag ticks, gizmo/wheel nudges, IK settles, pose loads; a Catmull-Rom spline through the driver knots, clamped per-segment to avoid overshoot × the folded gate) and copied into the current frame slot when it is behind `m_correctiveVersion` — a weight change costs a few hundred bytes per frame, never a geometry re-upload, so **the correctives track a drag continuously**. This replaced a CPU re-morph (base + Σ weight·delta into a rebuilt vertex buffer behind a `vkDeviceWaitIdle`) that could only afford to run once a drag ENDED — the figure visibly deformed (uncorrected) through every drag and snapped right at mouse-up, the first in-app complaint about posing feel. `refreshCorrectives()` remains as the explicit "pose settled" hook (`setBoneRotation`/`applyPose`/`Scene::finalizePose`): it evaluates now and hosts the `POSESTUDIO_DUMP_CORRECTIVES` dump (which also prints the delta-table size at import). Normals are NOT corrected (they weren't on the CPU path either — the deltas are small and the detail maps dominate). A model without correctives binds 16-byte zero placeholders so its set 2 stays complete. Verified end-to-end on two figure families (113 and 97 correctives discovered): deeply-bent elbows/knees fire the right correctives (22 / 8 active respectively) and deform cleanly. **Catmull-Clark subdivision is done** (`import/figure/subdivision.{h,cpp}`, wired in `figureimporter` at `kSubdivisionLevels = 1`): the low-res base cage is smoothed into the render mesh so limbs/torso/face read round rather than faceted. Positions follow the smooth Catmull-Clark rules on the watertight cage (face/edge/vertex points; boundary edges use the cubic B-spline rule); UVs are kept **per polygon corner** and refined *linearly* (each sub-face's corners get their parent face's corner UVs averaged), so texture seams split exactly as before; skin weights are refined by blending (edge/face points average their contributors' top-4, vertex points keep theirs). The subdivided cage is then run through the *same* de-index/seam-split/smooth-normal assembly as the base path, so `Scene`/`Model`/skinning/correctives are all unchanged — they just see ~3–4× the vertices (a ~16.5k base cage → ~56k affected render verts at level 1). **The corrective machinery needed no change** because Catmull-Clark is a *linear* operator: each corrective's sparse base-cage delta field is carried onto the subdivided cage by the same stencils (`subdivideDeltaField`) at import, so the GPU delta table (`base + Σ weight·delta`, see above) is built directly for the dense mesh. Verified: the subdivided figure renders smoother, textures + skinning intact, and the base correctives still fire (22/113 active on a bent-elbow/knee pose, re-morphing the dense zones). Cost: extra import time (subdivision + subdividing the delta fields — the latter parallelized across cores via `parallelFor`, one independent full-mesh stencil pass per corrective) and a larger delta table (a full-figure table runs to a few hundred thousand 16-byte entries). **Post-load follower addons are merged:** a character preset can declare *follower* figures to be loaded after it — a `scene_post_load_script` extra block whose `PostLoadAddons` settings list wearable `.duf`s plus a materials preset each. This is how an entire figure generation attaches its **eyelashes**, and how stylized characters ship **replacement anatomy** (e.g. an anime character whose preset dials every standard eye surface to cutout 0 and supplies separate cartoon eyeball/eye-socket/brow/lash figures — without addon support she imported with empty eye sockets). `figureimporter` parses the block (`findPostLoadAddons` — the preset key under `Presets` is NOT fixed: usually `Mat` but e.g. `Brow Mat` for one vendor's brows, so every entry carrying a `PresetFile` is collected and applied; the appliers no-op on non-material documents), loads each addon through the same `loadFigureFile` pipeline (they're self-describing wearable figures; recursion is one level — addons can't pull addons), applies its presets, **shape-follows** it, and merges it (`mergeAddonFigure`): skeletons merge by bone NAME (shared bones resolve to the parent's so the follower tracks pose; follower-only bones — e.g. anime-eye lens joints — are appended) — and "name" is the node's `name` FIELD (`FigureBone::nodeName`), NOT its `id` (which stays the engine's bone name: parent links, skin joints, driver urls, pose files and picking all use it): a file's ids only have to be unique within it, and one figure generation's base file renames THIRTY of them away from the bones' real names (the upper chest is id `chest_2` / name `chestUpper`, the mid-forearm twist bone id `lWrist` / name `lForearmTwist` — the "forearm bone named like a wrist" of the limits note — and id `lCarpal2` is the name of a DIFFERENT carpal), while its followers use the names. Matched by id (until 2026-09-18), that generation's eyelash follower failed to find twelve of its bones and they were APPENDED as follower-only: an invisible, limit-free SECOND SPINE (upper abdomen to upper neck, five bones) hanging off the figure's lower abdomen plus six face bones — pickable, riding the lower abdomen rigidly while the real spine bent away from them, and taking half the trunk's mass share in the IK balance model (found when an ad-hoc harness run grabbed the duplicate `chestUpper` and its "spine" turned 160°); an id match remains the fallback for a bone with no name match, and mesh `baseVertex` indices are offset out of the parent correctives' base-vertex space. **The fit model has three load-bearing rules, each learned from real content:** (1) ordinary followers are authored against the BASE figure and must be **shape-follow projected** (`followParentShape`): each render vertex gets the morph delta of the nearest parent base-cage vertex (nearest-vertex is fine — the cage is dense; off-surface strand tips inherit their root's delta) — without this, fibermesh eyebrows float in front of a morphed forehead. (2) An addon whose geometry declares a **populated graft** (non-empty `hidden_polys`/`vertex_pairs` — e.g. the anime character's custom eyeballs) is vendor-fitted in place and must NOT be projected (double-shifts off the face); note a *generic* follower still carries an *empty* `graft` object, which doesn't count — and never re-fit by snapping to parent joints either: follower rigs deliberately place bones away from the parent's (a flat anime eye pivots from *deep inside the head* so it can slide across the face like a curved screen). (3) Projection deltas are sampled **only from visible-zone cage vertices**: a stylized preset hides the stock surfaces its addons replace AND its morph stashes that hidden geometry (shrinks the stock eyeballs into the head), so followers sampling near hidden zones would inherit garbage deltas. Addon failures skip just that addon, never the figure. Materials presets come in **two forms** and both are applied: ordinary `scene.materials` docs (`applySceneMaterials`, shared with the main path) and **`preset_hierarchical_material`** docs (`applyAnimationMaterials`) whose values ride in single-key `scene.animations` tracks (`<node>#materials/<zone>:?<property>` → `keys[0][1]` — diffuse color/map, Cutout Opacity, Refraction Weight, Glossy Roughness, Normal/Bump maps; the anime-eye "lens over iris card" only turns transparent through this form). **A PRESET REACHES EVERY FOLLOWER IT NAMES** (2026-10-26; the user, with a close-up: "the eyelashes are not shading correctly"): a hierarchical preset addresses each figure BY NODE NAME (`<node>#materials/<zone>:?…`), and the format's own loader applies it to the whole hierarchy whichever slot lists it. The newest generation's eyelash follower carries only a grey placeholder material in its own file; its real materials — the Cutout Opacity mask, the normal map, a near-black diffuse — are in that generation's "add-on maps" preset together with the TEAR follower's, and the base figure and several characters list that preset under the Tear slot alone (others under the Eyelashes slot, which is why some characters of the generation always rendered right). Applied per slot, the lashes rendered as opaque grey strips — the "known gap" from 2026-08-17 until this day. Now `loadFigureFile` loads every follower and applies its OWN slot's presets first (both forms, zone-matched, as before), then applies every OTHER slot's presets to it through `applyAnimationMaterials(…, onlyFigureNode = true)`: only the tracks whose node prefix (URL-decoded; a `name://@selection/<node>:` form reduced to the node) equals the follower's `FigureData::figureNodeId` or `figureNodeName`, recorded by `loadFigureFile` from the scene node that carries the geometry (`findFigureNode`, which `findGeometryUri` now reads through). The scene-materials form stays with its own slot (no node to filter by; no content needs it across slots), and the base figure receives nothing from the addon block (its materials are the character preset's own). A follower's own-slot preset is never re-applied from another slot (a preset listed under three slots, as one character does, applies once per follower). `POSESTUDIO_ADDON_PRESETS_OWN_SLOT` is the A/B. LOOKED AT (close-ups through the scripted `closeup head`): the base figure's lashes dark and masked, a character with the preset under its tear slot likewise; a character with it under the eyelash slot, a previous-generation character (integral lashes) and the stylized character with per-follower presets pixel-identical with the step on and off. Verified: the anime character renders her cartoon irises (23 zones, was 16) plus followed brows, a standard character gains her previously-missing lashes (18 zones, was 16) with eyes/scale unchanged, and a realistic character's fibermesh eyebrows land on her morphed brow ridge instead of floating in front of the base-shape forehead. **Not yet:** the *identity*-morph driver-formula evaluator (`morphresolver`) still approximates spline/other ops as no-ops (the corrective path has its own real spline evaluator); the gizmo's axis-drag isn't gimbal-exact (it drives one Euler channel) and HDRI *selection* isn't on the undo stack (only the lighting dials and poses are — an HDRI switch is a re-bake, not a cheap state re-apply); addon **graft** metadata (`hidden_polys` — parent polygons a graft is meant to hide) isn't processed (so far redundant: these presets also dial the replaced surfaces invisible); generic base-authored followers (clothing fitted to an arbitrary character) would need real auto-fit projection, and a posed follower rotates about the *parent's* joint pivots, not its own. The core figure pipeline — import, character morphs, materials, rig, skinning, correctives, subdivision, follower addons, and the posing UI — is complete.

**FBX CHARACTER IMPORT — a rigged character from any FBX file, conformed to the rig standard** (2026-11-03; `import/fbx/fbxfigureimporter.{h,cpp}` (Qt-free, ufbx), `viewport/fbximportservice.{h,cpp}` (the Qt-facing orchestration, the twin of `FigureImportService`), `scene/ik/rigconformer.{h,cpp}` (the conformer, Qt-free); the user: "We want to create the ability to import FBX rigged characters and somehow allow posing and apply the IK system to these", and then the rig standard — "any figure imported is converted to our rig standard": the roles paragraph below is the first half, this is the second). File → Import → `.FBX (FBX File)` (`MenuManager::importFbxFile`), a double-click on an `.fbx` in the Asset Manager, or the file on the command line all go `ViewportWidget::importFigure` → `VulkanWindow` (which dispatches the figure route by extension) → `FbxImportService::importInto` → `FbxFigureImporter::load` → `conformRigToStandard` → `ModelImportService::uploadModelData` (the shared decode + upload: embedded textures arrive as bytes, files by path, so the decode-once, gutter-fill and opacity-bake machinery applies unchanged). **THE READER** (`FbxFigureImporter::load`): ufbx loads the file with `target_axes` right-handed Y-up, `target_unit_meters` 1, `space_conversion MODIFY_GEOMETRY`, missing normals generated, animation ignored. Every mesh with a skin deformer takes up to four cluster weights per vertex, its vertices placed at their BIND positions (the clusters' bind matrices applied to the geometry, blended by weight — exact at bind, so a file saved mid-pose imports at rest); an unskinned mesh rides the nearest ancestor bone. Materials: the unified PBR channels (base colour, roughness, metalness, normal map, opacity) with the classic ones as fallback (diffuse, bump + factor, transparency); textures through a `TextureFinder` — embedded content first, then the file's own paths (absolute; relative to the FBX; relative to the folder ABOVE it, since exports keep `textures/` beside `formats/`), then a file of that name anywhere under the FBX's folder (depth 8) or the folder above (depth 3), lowercase basename index, first found wins. The skeleton: every node a cluster binds plus its ancestors; SKINLESS LEAVES PRUNED (an exporter's `_end` markers — left in, one at ankle height was read as a foot and planted on the floor, and the leg could not move; 41 on one character) but each kept as its parent's TIP (`ModelBone::tip`, the direction of a bone with no joint below it); the figure's tree FIRST among the scene root's children (a shadow-catcher plane with a bone attribute listed before the figure made itself the root); the facing read off the bones' floor-plane COVARIANCE (the lateral axis is the widest spread, exact to a fraction of a degree where the toes alone, splayed and few, are not; the toes give the sign) and baked into geometry and skeleton; centred on the first bone with two children or more; the lowest vertex onto y = 0. The bones go out RAW — names, hierarchy, bind positions, identity frames, `XYZ`, no limits — with an `FbxImportInfo` the service logs (`[fbx] <path>: up +Z, front -Y, right +X; unit 0.01 m; 102 bones (41 skinless leaves left out), 10 skinned + 0 rigid meshes, 24 materials; skinning linear; 1.65 m tall; turned 0.1 deg to face +z; put -0.0 mm onto the floor; textures: 35 embedded, 0 files, 0 missing`). **THE CONFORMER** (`conformRigToStandard(ModelData&)`, the design record is `docs/RIG_STANDARD.md`'s "Conforming a rig that carries no anatomy"): a PROVISIONAL armature (every frame along its bone, no limits, twist helpers locked across by name — `nameSaysTwist` with nothing real below, a neck named "NeckTwist" being a neck — or by a repeated segment word, "thigh1"/"thigh2"), its IK rig built with the meshes' skin counts as the body mesh, the roles read off it, then per bone BY ROLE: the LIMITS (`limitsFor`: the reference generation's authored ranges per role — a knee −11..155, an elbow −135..20, a thigh −115..35 with −26..85 of abduction, a collar −10..55 of shrug, each finger joint, the toes, the ball, the neck, a spine of any length mapped onto the reference's four links, the head, the jaw, the eyes; the right side with its y and z channels mirrored; a segment's own twist LOCKED only when a twist helper lies ON THE CHAIN to its next joint — a leaf helper beside the chain carries skin alone and the segment keeps its twist (locked on top of leaf helpers a leg had no twist at all: a knee swung out 2.4cm of 8, a hand could not turn its palm); a helper locked on every channel; the upper arm's elevation range shifted by the bind's elevation against the reference's 46°), the ROTATION ORDER (the reference's: `YZX` trunk and legs, `XYZ` arms, `XZY` forearm and thumb, `ZYX` feet and toes), and the FRAME (`frameAlong`: the minimal rotation taking a world axis — X arms/hands/fingers, Z feet/toes/pectorals — onto the bone's direction BY ROLE, `directionFor`: a chain bone toward its next joint through the helpers between, the hand toward the mean of its fingers' bases, the ball toward the mean of its toes', a leaf toward its tip, a single-bone toe along its foot; a leaf twist helper takes its segment's frame; THE TRUNK WORLD-ALIGNED — the hips, pelvis, spine links, neck and head at identity, as the reference authors them whatever the spine's curve: a frame along a leaning neck turned its flexion into flexion-and-roll, and a ball framed at its first toe listed, the pinky on one side and the big toe on the other, stood 66/82/59° off and mirrored the sides 25mm apart). `orientationEuler` is `eulerFromMatrix(frame, "ZYX")`, the composition `Armature::build` reads. THREE ENGINE RULES came with it, each bit-identical on the eight reference rigs (r71 → r74): (1) **side tokens** in `mirroredBoneName` — a lone `L`/`R` (either case) bounded by `_ . - :` or the name's ends, or `Left`/`Right` bounded likewise or by a change of case, the first swapped (`Thing_L_Part`, `part.L`, `part_l`, `LeftPart`) — and `Armature::pairMirrorsByRole` after the rig is built for what the names and the places leave unpaired (the same role, index, joint and ordinal on the other side); read by the prefixes alone, one rig's toes and twists paired with themselves, 474mm "lopsided" at rest to the harness and flipped in place by Mirror Pose; (2) **a Helper-role bone that is no twist bone** — a game rig's corrective "share" bone beside a knee or an elbow, a control, a skinless leaf — is a Carpal-class bone whose limb end is its first non-helper ancestor (`classifyBones`): a click on it selects that joint and it lights with it (left a Body bone, a click on the knee grabbed the share bone, whose token mass the rig promoted to the FOOT); `IkRig::helperNode` reads the Helper role too, so the rig never plants one (the third generation's childless skinless toe stubs were planted before: its lifted-foot phases moved by tenths of a millimetre, r67 → r71); and `Armature::isTwistBone` reads a zero-length leaf (a game export's twist bone AT its parent's joint) along its parent's bone; (3) **the hang reads an arm at its BIND elevation as unposed** (`kArmHangBindSlackDeg` 12 within the bind, `kArmHangBindMaxDeg` 75: a T-pose stays posed, as the manual says) — one rig's A-pose stands 63° off the vertical, three outside `kArmHangCone`, and every idle arm rode as posed. Also: the harness's digit phase gates the carpal rule only on a rig that has carpals, its mirror phase gates the geometric mirror only on a rig symmetric at rest (an info row otherwise, with the rig's own asymmetry), and the script runner's `hips` / `lowest` metrics find the hips through the alias layer (the literal reference name read 0.0 and 1e9 on an FBX rig). **THE DRIVEN HELPERS AND LINEAR SKINNING** (the same day; the user, with a close-up of a bent elbow on the character-creation rig: "I am noticing some odd geometry when bending elbows and such. In Daz, this is corrected with JCMs, what can we do with our universal IK system"): a game-style rig keeps its correctives IN THE SKELETON — twist helpers along a segment, "share" bones at a knee or an elbow — driven by expressions the FBX export loses; imported rigid, all of a segment's twist happened at its joint (the candy wrapper) and all of a fold at one joint (the pinched, hose-like elbow). The conformer's third pass gives each a DRIVER (`ModelBone::drivenBy` + `driveFactor`, carried into `ArmatureBone` and `Armature::Bone`; applied at the top of `Armature::computeSkinMatrices`, the one place every pose change passes through, before the hierarchy walk — a driven bone is never an unknown of a solve nor a dial, and a pose file's value for it is overwritten on load): a Helper-role bone standing AT a real joint (within `kDriverAtJointM` 1cm) takes HALF of that joint's rotation — as the joint's child counter-rotated by −½ (the knee's share bone under the shin: the skin then follows half the bend), as its sibling +½ (the ball's share bone under the foot) — and a LEAF twist helper the SHARE of its segment's twist (the format order's first letter, the length axis) that its place along the segment says, the skin twisting in proportion to its distance from the joint (a chain's local values are the differences of the shares, the first against the segment's whole twist; every helper at the joint falls back to an even ramp); each copies the driver's frame and rotation order so the channels correspond. A twist helper ON the chain (carrying the next joint) is the solve's and gets none. AND A RIG AUTHORED FOR LINEAR BLEND SKINNING IS SKINNED LINEARLY (`MeshData::linearSkinning`, set by the FBX reader from ufbx's `skinning_method`; `Model::m_linearSkinning`): under dual quaternions the outside of its bends bulged. The flag rides the JOINT BUFFER's second half — entry 0's `.y`, beside the lit flags (the shadow pass's push block is two matrices, full) — and `skinning.glsl`'s `linearSkinning()` / `linearSkinPoint` / `linearSkinVector` blend each joint's dual quaternion AS the rigid transform it is, in `mesh.vert` and `shadow.vert` alike; the native figures keep the dual-quaternion blend (the flag reads 0). Probes: `POSESTUDIO_FBX_NO_DRIVERS` (the helpers rigid, as before), `POSESTUDIO_FBX_DQ_SKINNING` (dual quaternions as before). On the character-creation rig 22 helpers are driven (16 twists, 4 share bones at the knees and elbows, 2 at the balls); LOOKED AT in close-up, three builds side by side (as before | drivers + dual quaternions | drivers + linear): a 70% forearm twist spreads along the forearm where it pinched at the wrist, a 60% upper-arm twist along the upper arm where it creased at the shoulder, a 75% knee fold rounds the kneecap where the thigh's front collapsed into the knee, and the dial-bent elbow seen from behind and below has an edge and a point where it was a bent hose — linear a little leaner at the bend than dual quaternions. The eight reference rigs are bit-identical (r76 against r75) and the base figure's in-app suite pixel-identical: no native bone is driven and the dual-quaternion path is the same code. NOT DONE: synthesized correctives (bake, at import, the difference between the skinned mesh and a volume-preserving smoothing of it at sample angles into the existing corrective set — the fully general answer for rigs without helper bones, and for the native generations with fewer correctives). VERIFIED on eight FBX files of three families (a rigging add-on's export with `DEF-`/`ORG-` names, a character-creation tool's, and that tool's exports from three authoring packages — Y-up and Z-up, 1.57-1.88m, embedded and external textures): all eight import, render textured on the floor facing +z, and conform `complete` (102 bones with 41 leaves pruned, 100/5, 109/3; spine 3 links, neck 2 or 1; every twist helper, finger and toe joint placed; the game-engine exports' corrective bones helpers); the harness on the character-creation rig reads 132 of 157 phases passing (35 failed at the first cut, 30 at the second, 27 at the third, 25 at the fourth: the kneel family — see OPEN — and gestures the harness sizes in centimetres for the base figure, an arm raise of +60cm that her shorter arm reaches only on tiptoe, a bench path 40mm out of her reach, a bow resting 22mm short); the eight reference rigs 158/158 bit-identical through every cut (r72, r73, r74 against r71, the mirror phase's new info row excepted); the in-app galleries on that rig: the side gallery 26/27 (a chest bow's gaze −18° against a gate of −30), limbs 34/35 (the knee raise's weight shift carries the hips 83mm, against a gate sized for the base's wide A-pose), floor 56/72 (every miss the kneel family's: she squats where the base kneels — the all-fours, the sit on the heels, the half kneel, the prone descent — while the recline, the lay-down, the sit-up and both stand-ups pass and read right on the sheet), hands 23/28 (the base-sized targets land off her body: a hand to the head ends above it, a hand to the chest at the collar), perspective 12/13; the side and floor sheets LOOKED AT. **THE KNEEL ON A ONE-BONE FOOT** (the same day, after an afternoon of traces had not found it): on the character-creation family the kneel stalled with the knees 10-15cm over the floor, the foot at its −45 dorsiflexion limit with the shin horizontal. A DUMP EXPERIMENT found it in a minute where the traces had not: copies of the rig's dump with the ankle's limit widened to −70 landed the knees; the ball's limit widened to −90 changed nothing. And the per-tick POSITIONS (`POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT_POS`) on the two figures showed why the reference kneels at the same −45: its ankle stays planted and FLAT while the knee comes down — the shin leans 76° forward over the fixed foot — because its foot's pitch is TWO JOINTS' (the ankle −45..65 and a METATARSAL joint between it and the ball, −20..25: 65-70° of dorsiflexion in all), while the imported foot is one bone (its share bone beside the ball a locked helper) and had 45. The conformer gives a foot with no Metatarsal role the metatarsal's range at the ankle (−70..85; `limitsFor`'s `footHasMetatarsal`). With it the imported rig reads 141 of 157 (132 before): the kneel plants both knees and the hips arrive (the knee rests 1.9cm over its clearance against a gate of 1.5), the user-sized kneel, all fours, the sit back onto the heels, the half kneel, the knee drawn up and the kneeling hand raise pass, the kneel-ups miss their mean-lag gate by a millimetre; in the app the floor gallery reads 65 of 72 on her (56 before: she KNEELS where she squatted — the kneel onto all fours, the sit back onto the heels, the half kneel with one knee down and the other foot forward, the kneel-up off all fours — with what is left the gallery's base-sized hand drags), the side gallery 26 of 27 as before; the sheet LOOKED AT. THE LESSON: when two rigs reach the same channel limit and only one gets there, edit the DUMP's limits and re-run before reading another trace — the bisection costs a minute a variant. **THE SPINE'S TOTAL RANGE** (the same day): a spine of N links was mapped onto the reference's four by the NEAREST link — a three-link spine took links 1, 3 and 4 and had a third less flexion than the reference's trunk (−60..85 against −85..125 in all), and the bow rested 22mm short, the crouching lean 19, the Ctrl drag of the lower spine moved 23mm of the 40 asked. `mapChain` now interpolates the reference's links at each link's relative place and RENORMALIZES per channel and side so the N links together carry the reference chain's total (four links reproduce it exactly, one link takes the whole); the neck likewise over its two reference links (a one-link neck, the game-engine exports', had the first link's range alone). On the character-creation rig the count stays 141 of 157 but the misses move: the bench path's resting lag 40 → 9.7mm, the gentle pull's hip inside its gate, the hand-on-head pop 153 → 112 (the elbow reaching its full fold before the hand reaches the top of her head, then the shoulder re-routing the arm: the known basin-flip family, her proportions'), the Ctrl drag of the lower spine 23 → 32mm of 40; the bow still rests 25mm short because this skeleton's TOP SPINE JOINT sits 15cm above the hips where the reference's sits 30 — the gesture, sized by that lever, asks a 12cm reach of a trunk that short and rests 2.5cm off the arc with the pelvis, the links and the feet all reading as the reference's: the rig's proportion, left alone. What else fails on her is base-sized gestures (the arm raise, the tiptoe return, the elbow swing's 35mm of a 40 gate) and near-misses (the kneel's knee 1.9cm over its clearance against 1.5, the kneel-ups' mean lag 25-28 against 25, a side step's knee 13° against 12). **THE OTHER FAMILIES** (2026-11-03, the day after; the user: "lets continue towards perfection!"): the harness on the rigging add-on's export (`DEF-`/`ORG-` names, a T-pose, one toe block), the game-engine export (`thigh_r`, the hips named `pelvis`, the pelvis bone a leaf) and the character-creation tool's Blender export, beside its own. Seven things, each bit-identical on the eight reference rigs (r85 against r76: the dial-mirror row alone moved on every rig, by its measure's change below — millimetres to degrees): (1) THE BALANCE MASSES' SIDE is read off the name's side tokens (`sideOfRaw` in balancecontroller.cpp, the `mirroredBoneName` rule): by the FIRST LETTER alone every thigh of a suffix-sided rig was a CENTRE thigh, the class's share split six ways across both sides and their helpers, a leg's subtree read 3.5 percent of the body, a thigh socket was no girdle grab (`isGirdleJoint` wants 5 percent) and a hips drag by the socket moved the hips a hundredth of the way; `lowerarm_r` read as LEFT and `ring_01_l` as RIGHT the same way. (2) A HELPER IS NO RIDING PART (`m_ridingDescendants`; `IK_HELPER_RIDERS`): a leaf twist helper beside the shin carries the LOWER calf's skin and, no segment, answers for it down to the ankle — read as the knee's riding part it put the knee "within the kneel band" with the knee 25cm up, and the game-engine rig's kneel planted both knees in mid-air and stalled (its whole floor family: 13 phases). (3) THE BALL BY ROLE (`armatureiksolvedrag.cpp`): the standing foot's ball is a joint with children under the ankle, and a rig whose toes are ONE childless block found none — rigid ankles, no heel lift, no kneel; the Ball role stands in where the structure finds nothing (51 to 2 failures on that family's floor phases). (4) A SKINNED ZERO-LENGTH LEAF AT THE HIPS of a rig with no pelvis bone is the PELVIS (rigroles.cpp `atHips`; classed a helper first it cost the game rig its seat — helpers are no contacts). (5) THE ALIAS LAYER'S ONE COLLISION (`bonealiases.cpp`): a rig that calls its hips "pelvis" and has such a leaf resolves the canonical "pelvis" to the Pelvis-role bone; EVERY OTHER literal name is right as read — the rule "the role wins over a name whose bone carries another role" reached the reference rigs (the third generation's `lBigToe` is its Ball by role, and the toe phase grabbed another toe; 36-94 rows moved on every rig) and was narrowed the same hour; and the layer builds the rig ONLY for a lookup the names and the aliases both fail (built on every lookup for a day, the harness's probes on the reference rigs carried rigs built before their body mesh — skeleton-sized floor clearances — and the floor scenarios sized off them put the seat 25mm over the floor instead of 110: every prone and all-fours phase moved; built never, an FBX rig's role-resolved names failed on every probe and the floor scenarios sized to zero). (6) THE HARNESS: its probes pre-pose by RESOLVED names (five sites posed `lShldr` by the raw canonical, which only the reference T-pose generations carry literally — the rigging add-on's probe stayed in its T-pose while the run lowered the arms, and every hand-lay target read off its hand landed at the far knee, 566mm of head travel); the simulator builds the rig right after the body mesh, as the app does at import; the girdle-grab phase's pelvis bone is the hips' child over BOTH feet, else the Pelvis role (over one foot it was the thigh, and its "thigh socket" the knee); the dial-mirror measure is what the dials TURNED — every bone's world rotation away from the bind, the left's against the right twin's reflected across the sagittal plane, the worst angle in DEGREES (gate 0.1; a float frame's noise floor reads 0.03-0.05) — gated where the pair's FRAMES mirror (to 0.05 degrees) and information where the rig's own frames differ (a rig conformed from an asymmetric bind — the character-creation export's thighs 2.7mm apart in depth — turns its sides about axes 1.55 degrees apart: the rig's own). Two measures preceded it the same day, each wrong for a body that is not the reference's: POSITIONS less the rig's worst rest asymmetry (a Blender export's clavicles 1mm apart read as 2.2mm of dial mismatch, and the character sweep's one lopsided rig passed only because its face bones' 6mm of rest asymmetry was subtracted from every pair) and DISPLACEMENTS (a character whose knee stands 6mm further from its socket on one side read 4.8mm for the same 46 degrees — the lever's, not the dial's; two sweep characters flagged real). A dial is a rotation. (7) The verbose dial print names each side's channel, range and sign — which is what found the two DIAL bugs the rotation measure exposed, both in `armaturedials.cpp` and both invisible on the reference rigs: (8) THE SIGN OF A SIDEWAYS-SWINGING EVEN FORWARD-BEND CHANNEL — a rib helper's, a palm bone's: the bend dial of a joint that carries nothing fore and aft — read as "forward" was +1 on both sides, and on a conformed rig, whose two sides carry near-identical frames rather than mirror images, the same local sign is an UNMIRRORED turn (the Blender export's rib helpers 10 degrees apart for the same dial); it takes the side-bend rule's own sign now, outward for a side's bone and toward her left for a centre one. (9) A ZERO-LENGTH LEAF AT ITS PARENT'S JOINT lies the way its parent's segment lies (`limbDirection`): a rigging add-on's palm bones are each a leaf beside the finger they stand for, nothing of their own says which way is distal, and read as "+axis" the right side's — whose mirrored frame's length axis points proximally — swung the other way for the same dial (4 degrees apart). The eight reference rigs read the same dial numbers to the fourth decimal after both (7 of 7 directions, 0.0485 degrees of mirror on seven and 0.0396 on the newest). RESULTS (of 157): the character-creation rig 139 (141 before the masses' side moved it: its bench path passes, its walking-bow settle, a chest sideways and a crouching chest flip on the knife edges a re-massed body moves), its Blender export 136 (136), the rigging add-on's 120 (106), the game-engine export 141 (127: the whole floor family, the girdle grabs and the pelvis-bone pin). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 30 near-misses and NO real failure, 23 of 23 (its two "real" rows on the day were the dial gate's displacement measure on two lopsided characters, 0.04-0.05 degrees by the rotation measure); THE IN-APP SUITE on the base figure 520/520 with NO shot changed in any of the fifteen scripts; the galleries on the character-creation FBX figure: side 26/27, limbs 34/35, perspective 12/13 as before, hands 22/28 (23: a palm 0.747 against 0.75 on the akimbo's left hand), floor 61/72 (65 before: her per-side masses, a third again heavier in the legs than the centre-split had them and still lighter than the reference's, move the base-sized prone-up — both hands end in the air where one landed — and the stand-up by the hips from that pose ends folded double, chest tilt 137 degrees; identical with the old riders, so the masses': the masses-by-role round revisits it, looked at on the sheet). **THE BIPED EXPORT** (2026-11-03, the same day; the user, with a close-up of a hand whose fingers were bent every way: "when her hands change pose on initial posing her fingers become a bit mangled"): a realistic female character rigged on an animation package's standard biped — `Bip001 L Finger0` … `Finger4`, each with a `Nub` end marker, `LUpArmTwist`/`1`/`2` and `ForeTwist`/`1` as leaves with nubs of their own, `Spine` / `Spine1` with the clavicles and the neck hanging off `Spine1`'s far end and `Spine2` a skinned leaf beside them, `Toe0` the one toe bone, no pelvis bone (the thighs off `Bip001 Pelvis`, the root). Eight things, each bit-identical on the eight reference rigs but the one noted: (1) THE THUMB BY NEARNESS (`rigroles.cpp`, `kThumbNearShare` 0.8): a finger's digit with no name token was its PLACE across the hand, the thumb foremost — and the index's knuckle, further forward than the thumb's base at the wrist, was the thumb: the thumb got a finger's frame and limits, the index the thumb's, and the first relaxed hand (IDLE HANDS RELAX) curled the index sideways and folded the thumb like a finger — THE USER'S REPORT. The thumb is the digit whose first joint sits nearest the wrist (its chain begins at the carpus; a finger's at the knuckle, a palm's length out: 0.27 of the index's on the reference, 0.44 here) where one does by a fifth; the other four keep their order by place. (2) THE THUMB'S FRAME IS THE PALM'S (`rigconformer.cpp` `thumbFrame`, `kThumbRollDeg` −28.5): measured on six native generations (scratch `handframes2.py` over the dumps), a FINGER's authored bend axis is within 5 degrees of the least rotation taking X onto the bone (the conformer's rule, kept), but the THUMB's stands 27-54 degrees off it and is, on the four modern generations to a degree, the back-of-hand normal (the index and little fingers' bases about the wrist, its sense by side) rolled 28.5 degrees about the thumb's length — the thumb lies out of the palm's plane and flexes into it. The right thumb's X runs back up the bone (the reference's convention, which the mirrored limits need). (3) A TWIST-NAMED LEAF LIES ALONG ITS SEGMENT (the conformer's `directionOf`): the provisional frame took a leaf's direction from its TIP — the pruned nub — and the biped's nubs sit 12cm square to the arm, so no twist bone had its free channel along the bone (`isTwistBone`) and every twist helper was "other": unlimited, undriven, a joint of its own (`POSESTUDIO_CONFORM_TRACE` prints the provisional pass per bone: the name's twist, the locks, the rig's helper flag, the direction). 14 twist helpers now, 14 driven. (4) THE TOP SPINE LINK IS NEVER THE GIRDLE (`IkRig::isGirdleJoint`): the two-link spine's top — the collars' and the neck's link — stands two bones from the root and 18cm up, inside the girdle rule's reach, and every grab of the chest was a HIPS drag: the trunk never bent, a bow by the chest was a walk of two steps, and the pinned seat moved 97mm under a lean (the pin on the grabbed joint sits out). By role now: the link the collars leave is a chest however near the hips. (5) THE BONE CLASSES FOLLOW THE ROLES (`classifyBones`): a Finger or a Toe is a Digit; a Carpal, a Metatarsal, a Heel, or a Ball the toes FAN FROM is the hand's or the foot's own, whatever the structural rules read — while a ball with no toe below it (the one-bone toe block of a game rig's, a rigging add-on's or this biped's foot) IS the toes and keeps the structure's reading, a digit that curls (classed the foot's own for an hour, the rigging add-on's toe phase skipped: the foot model's ball is a role, not a class). THE ONE MOVE ON THE REFERENCE RIGS: the oldest generation's toes block (its ball by role) and the third generation's nameless mid-foot stub (a metatarsal by role) are the foot's own now, not digits — the oldest generation's big toe dragged no longer bends the block with it (the digit paragraph's soft spot; its toe phase reads the tip rising 11mm where it read 17.7, passing). (6) A FOOT-CLASS JOINT BOUND BELOW THE FLOOR KEEPS ITS BIND HEIGHT as its clearance (`IkRig::build`): the biped's cluster binds `Toe0` 11cm UNDER a sole that rests on the floor (`POSESTUDIO_FBX_BONE_TRACE` prints each bone's place and where it came from: the cluster, the bind pose, the parent — a clusterless bone RIDES ITS PARENT's bind now, as a marker does, instead of standing where the file's current pose put it), and clamped at 0 the ball's clearance stood 11cm over the joint: every floor reading of the rig began 111mm "through the floor" and the foot model held the ball at a height the foot never has. (7) THE ROOT IS THE SEAT where the rig has no pelvis bone (`IkRig::m_rootIsSeat`, read by the contact seed and detect rules): the buttocks' flesh is the root's own there, and excluded from the contacts a figure sat on the floor had no seat — reclined by the chest she leaned on nothing, laid down by it she arched her spine to its limits 15cm off the floor (the rigging add-on's export has no pelvis bone either). (8) A FINGER'S BEND DIAL IS ITS LONG SIDE (`armaturedials.cpp`): the geometric rule reads the way a joint carries its limb forward or down, and a finger hanging in an A-pose does neither — the sideways-swing rule of the morning (outward for a side's bone) was made for a rib helper's and a palm bone's EVEN channel and caught the knuckles too: the main family's index knuckle (its sweep 0.75 lateral against 0.66 down) read EXTENSION on the Bend dial for the day it stood unqualified, and the character-creation rig's thumb extended once its frame was the palm's. The outward rule is for even channels only (`kDialEvenShare`), a Finger-role joint's Bend is its flexion, and the harness's transform-dials phase gates it on every rig (`finger joints whose Bend dial's 100 is the short side`, 0 of 30). ALSO: the neck is the top's centre child with the most below it, of two on the midline (`Spine2`, a skinned leaf, stood on it to 1e-8), and such a leaf is a rider of its link (Pectoral, no side) rather than "other" — a selectable joint with no limits. RESULTS: the biped rig 97 → 127 of the 155 phases that run on it (158, three skipped for parts it has not) in the harness — the whole floor family, the girdle grabs, the pinned seat, the recline and the lay-down (the pelvis 94 degrees back, the seat a live contact); the eight reference rigs 158/158, bit-identical through every cut but (5)'s two foot bones and the new dial rows; the four FBX families as before (139 / 136 / 120 / 141) with the character-creation rig's thumb dial moving to its flexion; the 23-character sweep 30 near-misses and 0 real; in-app 520/520 on the base figure with NO shot changed in any of the fifteen scripts. LOOKED AT on the biped: the relaxed hands, the thumb and the index dialled, a bow by the chest's skin (the pelvis 18, the spine 32 and 17 degrees — at the chest JOINT, 18cm up on this rig, the bow's lever is a waist's and the harness's joint-sized bow phases read short), a kneel with the feet lying flat, a deep crouch. SOFT SPOTS: the harness's trunk gestures are sized by the chest joint's height, a waist's on a rig whose chest is one long bone; the seat's rise and roll read the root's flesh, fitted like any bone's; the thumb's roll is one number for every rig.

**THE STANDARD'S NAMES ARE APPLIED ON IMPORT** (2026-11-03, the same evening; the user: "are we converting the rig to our proprietary rig system like we discussed … should we use standard bone names? I noticed they are retaining the original names"): the conformer's fourth pass (`rigconformer.cpp`, after the drivers) renames every bone the standard names to `RigRoles::standardName(tag)` — `hips`, `spine1`, `upper_arm_twist1_l`, `thumb2_r`, `ball_l` — and leaves the face rig, the helpers and the unplaced as the file had them (a collision with a kept name keeps the bone's own, and a name TWO bones would carry — two pectorals of a side — is applied to NEITHER, since the refactoring round of the same day: applied to the first it unmade the standard-named rig; `RigConformReport::renamed` and `collisions`, logged). The skin and the drivers bind by index; what changes is what a POSE FILE carries — every conformed rig shares the standard's frames, orders and ranges, so a pose saved on one FBX character loads on every other (a scratch check loads one file of standard names on the biped and the character-creation rig and reads the same angles) — and what the Transform tab reads. The native figures are never renamed (their correctives, pose files and content are keyed to their names, the standard's first convention). AND A STANDARD-NAMED RIG IS MASSED BY ROLE (`IkRig::assignMassesByRole`, `m_standardNamed`: every bone the standard names carries that name — the conformer's rigs, or a figure authored to the standard): the name table classes by fragments of the reference's names, and on the standard's it put every `spineN` in the one "spine" class — a third of the reference's trunk, the game-engine export's short bow since round 127; by role the lower links share the abdomen's fraction and the upper the chest's, the twists their segment's, the toes the ball's, as the table splits the reference (`massClassOf` / `massShareOf`, shared with `fillMassesByRole`). The reference rigs carry their own names and keep the table, bit for bit. The five FBX dumps were RE-DUMPED (`D:/PoseStudio/ikdumps/fbx11/`) with the standard's names (85 / 82 / 85 / 75 / 67 bones renamed), and on them the harness reads: the character-creation rig 139 (139), the rigging add-on's 120 (120), the Blender export 134 (136: two knife-edge rows by a hair — the all-fours spine curl 25.5 of 25 degrees, the stepping chest drag 5.4mm of 5 and its foot 8.2 of 8 degrees), the game-engine export 142 (141: its bow rests 34mm short where it rested 43, its bench passes), the biped 128 (127: the sit-up from lying passes, a spine grab rests 2.2mm of 2); the eight reference rigs 158/158 and bit-identical (r89 against r88); a pose file of standard names (`shin_l 60 0 0`, `thumb1_l 0 32 0`, `index1_l 0 0 -50`, `head 0 25 0`, `upper_arm_r 0 0 40`) loads the same angles on the biped and the character-creation rig in the app; the in-app suite 520/520 with only the landing gallery's two wall-clock frames changed (the fall and the dip, with the harness running beside it); the biped galleries side 21/27, limbs 28/35, floor 59/72, hands 22/28 (one new soft spot: the prone-up's second hand walked out from under her turns the head 30 degrees a tick for three ticks on this rig — the trunk's new weight moved a knife edge of the weakest gesture). OPEN: the native rigs' masses are still the name table's (a role reading would move the newest generation's collar, which the table classes as an arm), and a LEAF twist helper's share is dropped from its segment on the name table and by role alike (measured 2026-11-03: giving the segment the whole share costs the character-creation rig 9 phases and its Blender export 10 — the rules are calibrated on the dropped masses; a tuning round with every rig re-gated); the walking-bow settle phase is a knife edge on three families; a hand laid on top of the head flips basins on the game rig (242mm); the skinning switch is per model from the file (no UI); no pose correctives; the harness alias table still by name; a rig setup panel for what the inference cannot place.

**Full-body IK (FBIK)** (`scene/ik/` + `scene/armatureik*.cpp`, Qt-free: std + GLM): **left-drag a joint** — a PLAIN drag, no modifier — IK-drags it: the grabbed joint follows the cursor in a camera-parallel plane through its grab point, **the mouse wheel during the drag moves that plane's depth** (`VulkanWindow::wheelEvent`: `kIkDepthPerWheelNotch` = 2% of the plane's camera distance per notch, the plane held ≥10cm in front of the camera, the raw target re-derived from the CURRENT cursor through the moved plane so the joint stays under the pointer), and the whole body follows anatomically with the **ground contacts as the anchors** (never the hip). **THE GRAB POINT** (`Armature::setIkGrabPoint`, set by `Scene::selectBoneAt` from the pick): a click picks a bone by its BODY as readily as by its joint (`BonePick::child` / `along`; the 3D point is the picked segment's nearest to the cursor's ray), and what the user has hold of is the point under the cursor — the drag plane passes through IT (`Scene::ikGrabPointWorld`) and the targets are where it should go. Until 2026-09-19 the plane went through the bone's JOINT and the joint was asked to the cursor: a mid-thigh click hauled the hip socket — the pelvis, once a thigh-socket grab was a pelvis drag — a thigh's length down to the pointer in the drag's first ticks (very likely the old unreproduced "figure wants to distort" report). What the solve drags is the nearest REAL joint of the RIGID SEGMENT the point lies on (`rigidSegment`: twist bones are part of their segment — a thigh is one piece from socket to knee whether the rig cuts it into a bend and a twist bone, hangs leaf twist helpers beside the shin, or has neither; `isTwistBone` is two locked axes with the free one ALONG the bone, which an elbow hinge with two locked channels is not): nearer the far joint than `kSegmentGrabNear` 0.35 of the way, the FAR one — the knee for a thigh, the elbow for an upper arm, the hand for a forearm, the foot for a shin, each with its own drag's semantics (a planted foot under a swung knee, the hand that tends to stay, the slide) — else the near one (the socket: a thigh grabbed by the hip is the pelvis girdle's). A twist bone's own joint is a body point too (mid-thigh dragged as a joint in its own right rested 5-15cm short, the planted foot holding the knee it had no say over). The far joint's target is the cursor's place DILATED about the near joint by 1 / share (`dragIkTo`): with the far joint there the point is on the cursor exactly, and a cursor INSIDE the point's sphere (it drags in a plane: a thigh pulled up in a side view) becomes a knee target as far inside the knee's in proportion, so the knee's own answer — the height kept, the swing found for it — puts the grabbed point at the cursor's height; OUTSIDE the sphere the excess passes on 1:1 (dilated too, a forearm raised 90cm asked the hand 2.25m up and lifted her off the floor); and as far as the near joint does NOT stay put (r = the share of the far joint's travel that is the limb translating, read off the last tick) the dilation is blended toward the plain MEASURED offset (far joint = cursor + where it stands from the point), λ = share(1 − r) / (share(1 − r) + r): the dilation alone leaves −r(1 − share)/share of the miss per tick, a growing oscillation once the body goes with the limb. Every other body grab (a near joint, a trunk bone between two spine joints, the girdle) uses the measured offset, read off the pose each tick (loop gain: the offset over the lever that turns it, a fifth at most). No grab point (the harness by name, the bench, `selectBoneByName`) is the selected joint itself, as ever. **Ctrl+drag** is the SCOPED IK drag — the grabbed chain alone moves, see SCOPED IK below (until 2026-09-26 it was the single-joint FK rotate, `m_posingBone`, gone with the change); a press released without moving just selects the joint. **One joint alone is turned by B / S / T and the mouse** (THE JOINT MOUSE MODE, below the Transform tab's paragraph) or by the Transform tab's dials; the X/Y/Z hold-and-scroll rotate that did it until 2026-09-30 — and turned the grabbed joint in the middle of a drag — is gone.

**SCOPED IK — Ctrl+drag moves the grabbed CHAIN alone** (FOR THE TRUNK — the hips, the abdomen, the chest — SUPERSEDED 2026-09-28 by THE FIGURE MOVE below, and BACK SINCE 2026-10-28 for the abdomen and the chest — CTRL ON THE TORSO BENDS THE TORSO ALONE, further below: the HIPS alone move the figure under Ctrl, and a spine joint bends the trunk's chain down to the pelvis exactly as described here; a user pin below a scoped trunk drag holds since the same day) (2026-09-26; `IkScope` in armature.h, `Armature::beginIkDrag(scope)` → `Scene::beginBoneIkDrag(scope)` → `VulkanWindow::beginIkDrag(scoped)`; the user: "click and pull on a limb should only apply the IK to that limb ending at the body, same for the head, and some sort of logical limitation when ctrl posing the chest or torso"). ONE RULE gives every scope: the unknowns are the bones from the effector up to, not including, `IkRig::limbJunction(effector)` — the mass-based junction, so the chain IS what the plain drag already classes as the dragged limb — plus what hangs below the effector and is a dof anyway (a planted foot under a dragged knee, a dragged wrist's own channels); everything else is masked out of `dofBone` in `ikChooseUnknowns`, the root's rotation with it, and `ikPostureModel` pushes no root translation dofs. So: a hand takes the arm up to the chest with its collar (the collar box applies); a foot or a knee the leg to the pelvis bone (a knee swings over its planted foot, whose pin rows hold through the leg's dofs — with the hips frozen the heel lifts 2cm and the foot turns on it, as the plain knee drag's soft sole heading lets it); the head or a neck bone the neck chain to the chest (`limbJunction(head)` is the chest, where the collars and the neck branch); a spine joint its chain down to the root (every spine bone's junction is the root); a collar itself alone. A PELVIS grab (the girdle promotion included) is the one scope with the root's six degrees and every active bone — the legs, a planted hand's arm: the pelvis moves and what is planted stays planted. And nothing else happens: `ikTrunkPolicy` returns at once with every policy member off (no follow, hinge, sway, unfold or reach fold), `ikBalanceRow` returns (no balance), the tick's rise is 0 (nothing yields, nothing turns home), `m_jsWeightShift` and `m_jsTiptoeDrag` are cleared at the press, the post-steps (the arms' hang, the head's righting, the fingers' relax) are skipped, and the rig (`IkRig::setScoped`) reads no intent, tests no suspension and begins no step; the limits, the floor and the body volumes hold as ever, and the settle continues the drag under its scope. It replaced the single-joint FK rotate Ctrl+drag had been (`m_posingBone`, `endFkDrag` and the move handler's `nudgeSelectedBone` branch are gone; the Transform dials and the B / S / T mouse mode are how a joint is turned since 2026-09-30 — the X/Y/Z wheel until then — and the scripts' `fk`). Measured on all eight rigs (`--only scoped`, six phases): a hand pulled 20cm forward and 15cm up rests on the cursor within 3mm with the hips, feet, chest, head and other hand at 0.0000mm; pulled a metre up, beyond reach, the hand rises 87cm and the body stands still — no suspension, no lean, no tiptoe; the head pulled 15cm forward moves 46mm with the chest at 0.0000; the upper chest pulled 20cm forward moves 18cm with the pelvis and feet at 0.0000; the hips taken 10cm down land on the cursor to 0.001mm with the feet within 0.03mm and the chest riding down 100mm; a knee swung 12cm out moves 10cm with the ankle's place within 5mm. In-app `gallery_scoped.txt` (six shots, 27 expectations; `cpress` is the Ctrl press) asserts the same and was looked at; a hand pulled 30cm forward, at the arm's full length, rests 15mm short — the plain drag leans the body for those centimetres, the scoped one rightly rests (8mm with the collar box off: the rest is the shoulder's limits). Every plain drag is bit-identical: the scope's guards all read `IkScope::Chain`.

**THE FIGURE MOVE — Ctrl+drag of the BODY ITSELF moves the figure as she is posed** (2026-09-28; `scene/armatureikfigure.cpp`, `IkScope::Figure` / `IkScope::Part` in armature.h, `Armature::isBodyGrab` / `beginFigureMove` / `dragFigureTo`; the user: "when ctrl + pulling anywhere on the body should disable any ik pinning to the floor, lifting the figure in its current position. This should not affect user added pins"). THIS SUPERSEDED the SCOPED IK paragraph above FOR THE TRUNK from 2026-09-28 to 2026-10-28: a Ctrl+drag of the hips, the abdomen or the chest MOVED THE WHOLE FIGURE. SINCE 2026-10-28 THE HIPS ALONE DO (CTRL ON THE TORSO BENDS THE TORSO ALONE, below): a Ctrl+drag of the abdomen or the chest bends the spine over a still pelvis again, the scoped chain's; a limb, the head and the neck under Ctrl are the scoped chain's as described there, a finger or a toe the digit's. The window asks for `IkScope::Part` (Ctrl), which `Armature::beginIkDrag` RESOLVES by what the rig's drag turns out to have hold of: it begins the rig's drag as for Chain, reads the effector after the rig's promotions, and when that is the body's (`isBodyGrab`: the solve root — a pelvis-girdle grab is promoted to it, so the upper third of a thigh grabbed by its socket counts — or a bone whose `limbJunction` is the root that is neither a leg's joint (`isLegJointAboveFoot`: on a rig whose thighs hang straight off the hips a leg's junction is the root too) nor foot-class, i.e. a bone of the SPINE; the neck's and the head's junction is the chest) it ENDS the rig's drag and begins the move. `IkScope::Chain` itself is untouched, and still reachable for the trunk in code: THE LANDING BOUNCE drives `Chain` on the root (the pelvis over planted feet), which is why the move is a scope of its own and not a change to Chain. LIKE THE DIGIT DRAG the move is a solve of its own that the rig has no part in: no floor contact is planted — not the feet, not a knee or hand the pose has on the floor — nothing balances, steps, lifts off or is re-posed, the settle returns at once. Unknowns: the solve root's POSE TRANSLATION, three dofs at stiffness 1 with their reference where she IS (a move has no home) — the figure's place, what a hip walk writes and a pose file carries as its `@trans:` row, so the move is an ordinary pose edit and one undo step — and, for each USER PIN, the channels of its limb's chain from the pinned joint up to `limbJunction` (exclusive; `localChannelDofs`, shared with the digit drag). Rows: ONE cursor row on the grabbed POINT (the bone and the point in its frame; the pelvis drag's fivefold pull), and per held pin its place at `kPinWeight` × `kUserPinScale` and its rotation at the pins' orientation scale — so a pinned hand stays to microns while its arm folds or straightens, and when the arm runs out the pin STOPS THE BODY (lifted 30cm with a hanging hand pinned she rises 9-16cm on the eight rigs and rests). A pin on the grabbed bone sits out (dragging a pinned joint moves the pin); a pin on the BODY itself — the root, a girdle joint, a spine bone — holds the whole figure where she is (`m_moveHeld`). THE FLOOR STILL STOPS HER on the way down, as a bound on the TARGET rather than rows (the body is one rigid piece): each joint that rides the body may come down to its floor clearance — or to where it stood at the press, when that was lower: a pose's own penetration is not pushed out by a move — and the least room any of them has left is how far the target may go below the grabbed point; a pinned limb's joints are out of it. No joint of an unpinned figure turns: every channel reads 0.0000° of change and every joint of the body travels with the grab to 0.01mm. Measured on all eight rigs (`--only scoped`: four `ctrl:` phases replace the two scoped trunk phases; the harness's `Scenario::scoped` is `IkScope::Part` now): the chest pulled 20cm forward and 30cm up rests 0.0001mm from its cursor with the feet 300.0mm up, mean drag lag 3.6mm (the follower's); the hips carried 40cm sideways take the feet 400.0mm along with no step; the hips pushed 20cm down from standing move 0.0mm; with a hand pinned and the chest carried 10cm toward it the hand moves 0.0001-0.0004mm and no leg, spine or other-arm channel turns. Every other phase bit-identical (146 phases). In-app `gallery_scoped.txt` (8 shots, 45 expectations) has the lift, the Ground button bringing her back down (with the landing's bounce), the carry, the floor's stop and the pinned hand. `POSESTUDIO_NO_FIGURE_MOVE` is the A/B (the scoped chain, as before); `POSESTUDIO_IK_TRACE` prints `[js-figure]` per tick. AND THE GROUND BUTTON'S DROP IS IN THE POSE TOO (the same day; `Armature::shiftPoseY`, `Model::translateY`, `VulkanWindow::commitGroundDrop`): it went into the model's TRANSFORM, which no pose operation touches — lift her in her pose, ground her, Reset Pose, and the lift left the pose while the drop stayed in the transform: she stood below the floor by the lift (and a pose saved after grounding loaded back in the air) — so for a figure the drop is the solve root's pose translation now (world → model → the root's parent frame), the model transform of a figure stays the identity, and GROUNDING IS A POSE EDIT: `groundFigure` snapshots the pose, the landing (after its bounce), the one-step lift-out and `finishGroundFall` commit ONE undo entry, the drop is saved with the pose, and Reset Pose takes it back with everything else. A boneless model has no pose and still moves by its transform. `gallery_landing.txt` section 4b asserts it (grounded, Undo puts her 300mm up again; lifted, grounded and reset she stands on the floor), `gallery_scoped.txt` that a Ctrl lift, the Ground button and Reset Pose end on the floor. SOFT SPOTS: (the spine is bent alone under Ctrl again since 2026-10-28; from 2026-09-28 until then there was no way to); the floor's bound reads joint clearances, so flesh between joints may touch down a few millimetres early or late; a figure carried INTO another figure or an object is not stopped.

**THE GROUP — several joints dragged together (Shift+click, Shift+drag)** (2026-10-27; `Armature::toggleGroupBone` / `makeGroupPrimary` / `removeFromGroup` / `restoreSelection` and `m_groupBones` in armature.h, `IkRig::beginDrag`'s `secondaries` → `secondaryEffectors()`, `Armature::updateGroupTargets`, `IkSolveScratch::secondaries`; the user: "ctrl+click allows selection of more than one joint, then a similar modified drag pulls on both joints together. The figure should react as a human would"). SHIFT, not Ctrl — Ctrl is the scoped drag and the figure move. **Shift+click** adds the joint under the cursor to the selection as its PRIMARY (the previous selection joining the group as a secondary) or, on a member, takes it out (a Shift+CLICK on the primary hands the selection to the first secondary, or, alone, deselects it); a plain click, and every plain selection (`setSelectedBone`, `selectBoneByName`), is of ONE joint and clears the group; a Shift press on another figure, on a digit (posed alone) or with nothing selected is a plain selection (`Scene::shiftPressBoneAt`, `VulkanWindow::beginGroupGesture`, the click's removal through `IkDragState::groupClickBone` at the release). **Shift+drag** of any member is a WHOLE-BODY drag through the pressed joint — the primary: its grab point, its drag plane and every policy of a plain drag (the rig's intents, suspension, steps, the limb-specific drags, the weight shift, the tiptoe, the reach-down fold, the hand lay) — with the SECONDARIES riding the cursor's displacement: each one's effector position at the press plus the cursor's move since (through a damped follower of its own, the primary's twin, so both trail the cursor by the same tick's travel, and the settle runs both to rest), bounded by the floor and the body volumes as the primary's target is, and — for a secondary KNEE over its planted foot, or an elbow over a hand on the floor (`IkRig::pinUnderJoint`) — projected onto its shin's reach of the ankle's pin as the primary knee goal is (unprojected, the second heel lifted 13mm where the first stayed, and both knees pushed forward came out lopsided). The rig promotes each secondary as it promotes the effector (a token-mass grab to its limb's real joint, a girdle joint to the root), marks its path active (`rebuildActiveSet`), lifts its own contacts (`liftedByDrag` = lifted by the effector OR any secondary, each with its ancestor chain: `pathLenToAnyEffector`, `liftedByEffector`, `belowInItsLimb`), keeps the user pins in its subtree out as the effector's are, never steps a foot under it (`pinUnderAnyEffector`) and never plants its limb as a live contact. The solve gives each secondary one cursor row at the cursor's weight and bounded pull (`ikCursorRows`), prices its limb as the dragged one (class 1 from the joint up to its junction, with no twist price unless the limb serves a pin below or the joint is a foot's; its OWN joint priced as the primary's own is — a foot's or a fold joint's at the limb's price, a WRIST's at the trunk's: priced as a limb, a second hand's wrist ran to its 70° limit under its fingers' body-volume rows, 14-18cm at a thumb on every rig), makes its wrist's channels unknowns as the primary's are (without them the two hands differed by a wrist turn, 19mm at a thumb on the newest generation), boxes its collar as a dragged arm's (`s.handCollars`, a list now), and keeps its arm out of the idle hang (turned back toward hanging every tick it would fight its own row). The foot below a secondary knee turns on its heel as the primary's does (`kneeFoot` in `ikPinRows`: the ankle's place hard in the floor's plane, the ball bearing in height only, the headings soft; the lift and hang rows stay the primary's). A Ctrl drag, the landing bounce's own pelvis drag and a digit's drag ignore the group (`IkScope::Body` only). THE HIGHLIGHT: the secondaries and their highlight twins light with the selection through the joint buffer's second half (`highlightedWithSelection`; the upload key carries `groupVersion()`), a secondary head lighting its face rig.
