Measured on all eight rigs (`--only group`, four phases; gates set from these numbers): both hands raised 40cm together — both on their targets to 0.0014mm, the hips 0.2-2mm, no step, no lift-off, worst tick 12-16mm; both hands pushed 35cm forward — on target to 0.04-0.1mm on five rigs, 15-17mm short on the main family (their hands hang 37cm out from the sockets: 35 forward is the arm's length, and balance stops the lean as it stops a single hand's; the gate reads 25), worst tick 9mm (40 on the male), no step; both hands brought 30cm down (the reach-down fold, the primary's share) — 0.016-0.027mm, worst tick 18-44; both knees pushed 12cm forward over planted feet — both travel 114-115mm, the feet 0.014-0.016mm, worst tick 5. Every symmetric gesture gains under 1mm of asymmetry, and EVERY PRE-EXISTING PHASE IS BIT-IDENTICAL on all eight rigs against a baseline built from the committed sources (the `s.secondaries` loops are empty for a single joint, and `pinUnderAnyEffector` is `pinUnderEffector` then). In the app, `gallery_group.txt` (5 shots, 26 expectations; `sclick` / `spress` are the Shift click and press, `group` / `ingroup.<bone>` the metrics — the runner's by-name pixel lookup SELECTS the named bone, which a plain press relies on, so the Shift commands save the selection and put it back around it, `Scene::restoreSelection`): both hands up to shoulder height with the elbows out and the body still, both forward in a lean, both down to the knees in the fold, both knees forward over flat feet, and the selection's bookkeeping; looked at. TRAPS met on the way: a patch script whose new text CONTAINS its needle is not idempotent (a second run duplicated three blocks of armature.h); a shell heredoc turns `\n` in a string literal into a real newline — patch scripts with escapes go through a file. SOFT SPOTS: a secondary gets no limb-specific policy beyond the knee's reach and its foot's heel — a secondary foot has no slide and no weight shift, a secondary knee no lift-off of its foot (pull both knees up and only the primary's foot lets go), a secondary elbow no hand hold; the rise, the tiptoe and the lift-off are read off the primary (both hands pulled beyond reach hang her from the primary's cursor, the other hand on its own target); a hand is laid on the body for the primary only; the group is a selection, not undo state (undo restores the pose, not the group); a right-click's selection of the joint under the cursor clears it.

**FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG — the bone classes and the digit drag** (2026-09-28; `scene/armatureikdigit.cpp`, `BoneClass` / `Armature::boneClass` / `posingBone` in armature.h, `kDigit*` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; the user: "the face rig joints are specifically for expressions and should not be part of the IK, and probably shouldn't even be selectable for posing. Only the head itself should be selectable. Also, fingers and toes should only have very limited IK functionality. IK in the fingers and toes should only apply to that digit, stopping at the hand or foot respectively"). Every bone has a CLASS, found by structure with the rig (`classifyBones`, called from `ensureIkRig`; `POSESTUDIO_BONE_CLASS_TRACE` prints it, `POSESTUDIO_POSE_ALL_BONES` makes every bone Body — the A/B to before): **Face** = every bone below the rig's head (the jaw, lips, brows, eyes, ears, tongue: 11 on the two oldest generations, 42 on the newest, 77 on the others); **Digit** = a finger's or a toe's joint; **Body** = the rest. THE FACE RIG IS NOT POSED: `Armature::setSelectedBone` / `selectBoneByName` / `setIkGrabPoint` map a Face bone to the HEAD (`posingBone`), so nothing can select one — a click on the face (`Scene::selectBoneAt`: no joint snap for a Face or a Digit hit, and no joint of either is a snap candidate for a hit beside it) selects the head with the hit point as its grab point, the drag is the head's with the offset measured (the head is no segment: `beginIkDrag` refuses a rigid segment whose far joint is a Face bone), and no solve ever has a face bone among its unknowns. THE SELECTED HEAD LIGHTS THE WHOLE FACE: the head bone's own skin weights cover the scalp, so a click on a cheek lit the top of the head — the joint storage buffer (set 2 binding 0) is now TWICE the joints, its second half carrying per joint whether its flesh is lit WITH the selection (`Armature::highlightedWithSelection`: a Face bone under a selected head) in the `.x` of one more entry each, written per frame slot when the selection changes (`Model::m_highlightUploaded`), and `mesh.vert` reads it at `joints.dq.length() / 2 + joint`; `shadow.vert` and the outline mask read the first half as ever. A DIGIT'S JOINT, all three: it hangs below a wrist (`ensureHandMaps`) or an ankle (the foot's most proximal foot-class joint) and BENDS — its widest LIMITED channel spans `kDigitMinRangeDeg` 30 or more, a carpal's spans 7-20, and a helper authored −180..180 on every channel (the newest generation's hand anchor) is no joint; it stands OUT in its hand or foot — `kDigitMinReachShare` 0.10 of the limb end's reach (its farthest joint at bind) from the wrist or ankle: the range alone took a mid-foot bone 3-9mm from the ankle (45-60° of range) and a heel bone for toes, 0.02-0.05 out, where a thumb's base, the digit joint nearest its limb's end, stands 0.15-0.17 out on every generation; and it carries ONE digit at most — the ball of the foot the toes fan from (100° of range, five children) is the FOOT's, a carpal with two fingers the hand's. What is left below a wrist or an ankle (carpals, the mid-foot, the ball, a heel) was Body — a grab there went to the rig, which promoted it to the hand or the foot — and is CARPAL since 2026-10-28: a click on it selects the hand or the foot (A CARPAL SELECTS THE HAND, below). **THE DIGIT DRAG** (`beginIkDrag` on a Digit bone → `beginDigitDrag`; `dragIkTo` → `dragDigitTo`; `Armature::ikDigitDrag()`): a solve of its own that the rig has no part in — the rig begins no drag, so nothing is planted, balanced, stepped or lifted, the settle returns at once, and the scope (plain or Ctrl) makes no difference. Unknowns: the unlocked channels of the digit's CHAIN — the grabbed bone (when the grabbed point is off its joint, which a surface pick's always is: its own channels turn the point) and the Digit bones above it — at the limb's price (1/range², fold channels bounded at straight as in `ikPostureModel`); ONE cursor row on the grabbed POINT (`JointPositionTask::offset`, the point in the bone's frame: no measured offset, no segment dilation) at `kEffectorWeight` with the body's bounded pull; floor rows on every joint below the chain's top bone; the damped follower (`followIkTarget`, extracted from `dragIkTick`: the body's drags are bit-identical). NO POSTURE EASING (`IK_DIGIT_EASE` brings it back): with it a finger held 57cm beyond its reach rested with a channel a hair inside its limit, the steps shrank tick by tick until the active set's hair rule let that channel go, and the finger turned 87° in one tick a third of a second into a still hold (one of the eight rigs; 27mm at its joint). A digit's base joint grabbed AT the joint (by name, or the 2D fallback) has nothing to move it and the drag is inert. Pulled beyond its reach a digit goes to its authored limits after the cursor (the base figure's knuckle extends 50°: the rig's statement, not the solver's). Measured on all eight rigs (`--only digit`, three phases, and `--only eye-grab`): a finger pulled 60cm up travels 27-58mm at its last joint with EVERYTHING outside the finger at 0.0000mm (it raised the arm overhead before), hold still to 0.003mm; a fingertip's pad curled toward the palm rests 0.005-0.013mm from its cursor with every joint of the finger bent; a toe's tip raised 2cm rises 10-19mm with the foot still and nothing through the floor; an eye grabbed selects the head, rests 0.95mm from its cursor, and the eye's own rotation is 0. Every other phase is bit-identical on all eight rigs (141 phases; the finger-grab raise went with the behaviour it tested). In-app `gallery_digits.txt` (7 shots, 37 expectations, looked at; the scripts' `closeup <bone> <radius>` frames the camera on a joint, and `click`/`press <bone>` aim at the NAMED bone's pixel whatever its selection selects). SOFT SPOTS: from a view of the whole figure a finger is a few pixels wide and the hit is as often the hand's skin — zoom in; the highlight lights the grabbed phalanx only, though the whole digit moves; the oldest generation's toes are two bones in one chain (the small toes as a block, the big toe below it), so its big toe dragged bends both; a digit drag keeps OUT of nothing but the floor (no body-volume rows: a finger's reach is its own length); the eyes cannot be aimed by clicking them (they are face-rig bones: a pose file or a future look-at control).

**THE LANDING BOUNCE — a figure dropped onto her feet absorbs the landing** (2026-09-28; `scene/armaturelanding.cpp`, `Armature::beginLandingBounce` / `landingBounceTick` / `endLandingBounce`, `kLanding*` in armatureiksolvetuning.h, `VulkanWindow::onFallTick`; the user: "if she lands on her feet ... add a natural bounce to her legs to add a sense of realism. This should be greater the more acceleration there is in the drop. This is purely aesthetic"). When the Ground button's fall lands (`GroundFall::bouncing`, on the fall's own timer and clock from the moment of the landing), the window hands the armature the IMPACT SPEED sqrt(2 g h) and, if a bounce begins, ticks it until it is over. The motion is not keyframed: it is a PELVIS DRAG made by the solve's own machinery — `IkScope::Chain` on the solve root, the scoped drag's "pelvis and legs alone over planted feet" (no balance, no step, no post-step, the trunk riding) — whose target is the hips' place at the landing lowered and brought back along one smooth dip; so the knees fold as this figure's knees fold, within her limits, on any body. THE DIP is the speed's: `kLandingDipPerSpeed` 0.020m per m/s, saturating by tanh toward `kLandingDipMax` 0.20 of the figure's scale, nothing under `kLandingDipMin` 3mm — 19mm for a 5cm drop, 60 for 50cm, 110 for 2m (at 0.030 a 5cm drop folded the knees 30°: near straight a leg's height goes as the SQUARE of the knee's angle, so a shallow dip is a deep bend — 6cm of dip is 40° of knee). ITS SHAPE is sin² down over the first `kLandingDownShare` 0.32 of its time and cos² back up, lasting `kLandingSeconds` 0.50s at the cap and 0.7 of that for a slight dip: the dip begins and ends as the square of the time, so the knee — whose angle goes as the dip's square root — starts and stops at a steady pace. The FIRST shape was a damped spring's half wave (ζ 0.5), which starts at full speed: the knee of a straight leg travelled 62-81mm in the landing's second tick on the rigs (27-34 now). SHE MUST HAVE LANDED ON HER FEET: every pin the rig planted is a foot-class one and none is LIVE (a knee, a hand, the seat, the head on the floor are the pose's own contacts, seeded live), not on her knees, no floor seat, not suspended, and no user pin on the root or the pelvis girdle (a seat; looked for on the armature's own pins — the rig leaves a pin on the dragged joint out of the drag, so `seatPin()` reads none). PURELY AESTHETIC: the pose the bounce ends in is the pose it began in, EXACTLY — `endLandingBounce` assigns the landing pose's channels back (`m_ikStartEuler` / `m_jsStartTranslation` as the drag captured them) and re-poses — so it is no pose edit, no undo entry, and a bounce cut short (`finishGroundFall`: any press, key or edit closes the fall and the bounce through `closeSettlingEdits`) ends in the same pose. Over the LAST of it (`kLandingBlendFrom` 0.85 of the duration) the pose is blended home in joint space, so what the solve leaves different at a dip of zero (a foot the rig healed) eases in rather than snaps; from 0.6 that blend slid the toes 4-7mm on the floor (a joint-space blend of poses slides planted feet), from 0.85 0.4-0.8. THE BOUNCE BORROWS THE SELECTION (the IK drags the selected bone): the user's is saved and given back, and meanwhile the highlight, the skeleton overlay's hot segments and the joint buffer's lit group read `Armature::highlightBone()` — the user's selection through a bounce — and `activeContactPins()` is empty (no cyan markers). A fall completed at once (a press mid-fall) has no bounce. Probes: `POSESTUDIO_NO_LANDING_BOUNCE`, `POSESTUDIO_LANDING_DIP` (metres per m/s), `POSESTUDIO_LANDING_TRACE` (impact, dip, feet planted, other contacts, the verdict). Measured on all eight rigs (`--only landing`, three phases): dropped 50cm the hips dip 0.985 of the 60mm given, a knee folds 38-42°, the toes move 0.4-0.8mm on the floor and an ankle 0.6-1.4, worst single-tick move 27-34mm, the pose at the last tick 1.4-2.6mm from the landing pose and after the end 0.0000 (degrees and millimetres), the selection given back; 5cm / 50cm / 2m dip 19 / 60 / 110mm; none with the hips pinned, none for a drop of half a millimetre. In-app `gallery_landing.txt` (9 shots, 15 expectations; the scripts' `lift <m>` raises the figure, `ground` drops her, `wait` counts ticks through the fall and the dip), looked at on the base figure and run on four others. SOFT SPOTS: the trunk, arms and head ride the pelvis rigidly through the dip (no forward give of the trunk, no arm swing); a pose with a foot in the air lands and dips on the standing leg alone; the bounce's first tick unlocks a straight knee in one 3cm move (the fold retry, as ever).

**The design in one sentence: the rig decides WHAT the drag's constraints are, one solver finds the pose, and the pose is applied in full.** (Until 2026-09-18 the solve was a position-space multi-chain FABRIK whose joint positions were converted back to Euler channels by a rotation extraction, held calm by damping — 0.5 under-relaxation, movement caps, a micro-damp, a settle-freeze — and patched exact afterwards by a joint-space pin refinement; it ran 45 rounds. The user asked whether tremble could go without lag; inside that design it could not — an iterative, redundant, ill-posed loop is only calm because it is damped, and damping IS lag — so a second, well-posed solver was staged beside it behind a switch, passed the user's in-app verdict ("feels great, much better movement than the old system"), and the old path was DELETED the same day: `fabriksolver`, `ikconstraints`, `armatureikextract.cpp`, `armatureikpins.cpp`, `armatureik_detail.h`, `IkRig::solveDrag`/`settleToPins`, the prior-weight stiffness model, the twist windows and witness, the rider points, the pelvis-tilt assist, the heel-lift policy. Releases through 0.3.14 carry its first 37 rounds; rounds 38-45 of it were never committed.) File map: `scene/ik/jointsolver.{h,cpp}` (THE solver), `scene/armatureik.cpp` (the drag lifecycle: rig + solver-skeleton build, `beginIkDrag`, `dragIkTo`'s target, `settleIkTick`, `endIkDrag`, the FK collision stop's measure, the pin API), **the per-tick solve, SIX FILES since the refactoring round of 2026-09-24 — and see THE IK REFACTORING ROUND OF 2026-10-29 below for the parts the long stages were split into since** (`Armature::solveIk` was ONE 3,800-line function; it is now an orchestrator of seventeen STAGE member functions over one `IkSolveScratch` — `scene/armatureiksolvestate.h`, the per-tick state the stages share, every member documented and listed by the stage that sets it, plus the balance polygon the solver's `Problem` POINTS at — each stage beginning by naming the members it reads and writes (`float& plant = s.plant;`) so its body reads as it did and the compiler catches an alias it forgot; split MECHANICALLY and verified bit-identical on all eight rigs, 122/122, twice — once as one TU, once spread over the files): `scene/armatureiksolve.cpp` (the map in its file comment; `solveIk`, `ikSolve` / `ikApply` / `ikSolveTraces`, `dragIkTick`, `settleIkSolveTick`, the damping level, `buildJointSolver`), `armatureiksolvedrag.cpp` (what the drag IS: `ikChooseUnknowns`, `ikTrunkPolicy`, `ikStiffnessClasses`, `ikFootDrag`, `ikKneeDrag`, `ikElbowDrag`; and `ensureHandMaps`), `armatureiksolveposture.cpp` (`ikPostureModel` = `ikPosturePolicy` / `ikPostureDofs` / `ikPostureRoot`, `ensureSpineChain`, `ikSpineCoupling`), `armatureiksolverows.cpp` (`ikPinRowsBegin` / `ikPinRows(p)` — the per-pin body, once a loop, since 2026-10-29 an orchestrator over `ikPinRowFlatFoot` / `ikPinRowLiveContact` / `ikPinRowFootFree` / `ikPinRowFootModel` / `ikPinRowHold` sharing one `IkPinRowState` — / `ikPinRowsEnd`, `ikCursorRows` with `ikHandSlideRows` / `ikHandLayRows`, `ikBalanceRow`, `ikPlaneRows`, and the knee's hanging-foot geometry `ikKneeHangOffset` / `ikKneeHangUnder`, member functions that were lambdas), `armatureikpost.cpp` (the POST-STEPS: `hangIdleArms` over its eight parts, `rightIdleHead`, `balancePositions`), and `armatureiksolvetuning.h` (EVERY tuning constant with its reasoning, `envOr`, the damping levels, the env probes more than one file reads — the numbers are the model; a stage-local `static const` env probe stays with its stage). Add a stage by writing the member function, declaring it in `armature.h`'s stage list and calling it from `solveIk` in order; a value two stages share goes into the scratch; a value a `JointSolver::Problem` pointer or a stored lambda refers to must be in the scratch too (the first split crashed on `problem.supportHull = &support` — a stage local the solve read after the stage returned). The same round removed the code of four rejected variants that had stayed behind probes (the knee friction hold `IK_JS_KNEEFRICTION`, the ceiling's fold band `IK_JS_CEILING_OFF_STRAIGHT` / `_FOLD_BAND`, the knee hang's lerp `IK_JS_KNEE_HANG_LERP`, the planted hand's volume exemption `IK_JS_PLANTED_HAND_VOLUMES`), replaced two copies of the knee-detection loop with `IkRig::isLegJointAboveFoot`, and added two DETERMINISM phases — `[unit] a zero Euler composes to the bind exactly` and `[real] a kneel repeated after a Reset Pose ends in the same pose, to the last bit` (gate 0.0: `Scenario::resetAfterBefore`) — for the exact-bind fix of round 89. **The same round's structural AUDIT** (a full read of the IK-side files) fixed, each verified bit-identical on the eight rigs: the SEAT PIN's index (`IkRig::m_seatPin`) was never re-based when the pins shrank — `removePin` re-based the step pin only, and the suspension rebuild kept the user pins at new indices with the seat's old one, so after a lift `seatPin()` named the wrong pin or none; `liveContactRise` was the one pin accessor with no bounds check; the dead PIN-STRAIN half of the step trigger went (`updateStepping`'s `entryPinErr` was 0 at both call sites since the solver replacement, `kStepPinErrThreshold` with it — the header had already said "there is no pin-strain trigger"); four public rig accessors nothing called (`thighRoot`, `volumeSegmentRadii`, `isHand`, `suspendPending`, `stepPending`) and seven never-read `IkScript` members went; the three copies of the foot-below scan are `isLegJointAboveFoot`, the three contact-trace probes one file-local, the two 15cm contact heights one `IkRig::kContactHeight`, the ground-heal band a named `kGroundHealBand`; `far` is no longer an identifier (a Windows SDK macro: a latent build break); `m_selectedBone` is range-checked in every branch of `dragIkTo`; the girdle walk's loop guard matches its result's; the promotion rule's token-mass boundary is the contact rule's (`>=`); in the harness `r.phases` is sized before any early return (a phase indexing `r.phases[0]` on a run that failed early read past an empty vector), the `IK_HARNESS_POSE_TRACE` step column no longer prints 0 (the previous positions were overwritten before the dump), and the 16 copies of `indexOf` are one-liners over `resolveBone`. And the four INTENT gates of `IkRig::updateIntent` / `updateStepPolicy` that were raw metres (2cm pelvis-down, 15cm limb-down, 10cm limb-up, 8cm crouch) are figure-scaled like every other geometric threshold (`kPelvisDownIntentDepth`, `kLimbDownIntentDepth`, `kLimbUpIntentHeight`, `kCrouchIntentDepth` in `ikrig_constants.h`; the base rigs sit at scale 1, so bit-identical there; measured on the sweep). Probes that were undocumented: `IK_CONTACT_PLANT_RISE` (a live contact's rise read over its PLANTED height, not its flesh's), `IK_KNEE_ANY_ANCESTOR` (the knee-landing rule's joint as any ancestor of the pinned node), `IK_HARNESS_ASYMMETRY_TRACE` (the pairs over 2cm lopsided at a phase's start; =2 the worst pair per tick once it passes 5mm), `IK_HARNESS_PENETRATION_TRACE` (every joint over 1cm through the floor, per tick). and the rig — `ikrig.{h,cpp}` (build, the per-node structure caches, shared metrics), `ikrigvolumes.cpp` (the body volumes: build, the drag-start mask, the target clamp), `ikrigdrag.cpp` (`beginDrag`/`plantContacts`), `ikrigpolicy.cpp` (intents, the lift-off test, the step policy's entry), `ikrigstepping.cpp`, `ikrigcontacts.cpp` (live contacts, landings), the private `ikrig_constants.h` — plus `bodyvolume.h` (the capsules + the ONE reading of their axes), `ikeffector.h` (a pin), `skeletongraph` (the body graph: active-path marking), `balancecontroller` (masses, CoM, support polygon), `bodymesh` (the mesh sample the volumes are fitted to), `ikmath` (`eulerMatrix`/`eulerFromMatrix`, `closestSegmentPoints`), `cursorfilter.h`, and since 2026-11-02 `rigroles.{h,cpp}` — THE RIG STANDARD'S ROLES, what each bone IS by structure (see that paragraph below) — and since 2026-11-03 `rigconformer.{h,cpp}` — the conformer that gives a rig with no anatomy (an FBX skeleton) the standard's frames, orders and limits by role (the FBX paragraph) — and `namerules.{h,cpp}`, THE ONE READING OF A BONE NAME (its side token, a digit's token, the twist and segment words; the refactoring round of 2026-11-03).

**THE IK REFACTORING ROUND OF 2026-10-29** (the user: "a full comprehensive refactor of the entire IK system code — clean up, fix any bugs, improve efficiency where possible, remove any dead items or code, properly modularized and commented"; five batches, EACH VERIFIED BIT-IDENTICAL on all eight rigs — the harness's reports carry no timings, so `diff -rq` of two runs' output folders is the test, and every batch read IDENTICAL against the baseline taken before the first; the in-app suite then read 512/512 (every expectation, and no shot changed but the landing gallery's wall-clock-timed dip frame) with no shot changed, and the 23-character sweep 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry of one rig), 22 of 23 with no real failure, as the round began). WHAT MOVED: (1) STRUCTURE — `ikPinRows` (1,686 lines, one per-pin body) is an orchestrator over five parts (`ikPinRowFlatFoot` / `ikPinRowLiveContact` / `ikPinRowFootFree` / `ikPinRowFootModel` / `ikPinRowHold`) sharing one `IkPinRowState`; `hangIdleArms` (909) over eight (`findHangArms`, `hangArmSwings`, `softenHangElbows`, `relaxLiftedWrists`, `relaxIdleFingers`, `findHangTurns` / `stopHangTurns` / `applyHangTurns`, with the turn's lambdas as members and `HangTurn` a private type); `ikPostureModel` (869) = `ikPosturePolicy` + `ikPostureDofs` + `ikPostureRoot`; `beginIkDrag` (526) hands its per-drag resets to `resetContactState` / `captureDragStart` / `findWeightShift` / `resetFootAndHandState` / `findTiptoeDrag`; `ikCursorRows`' two self-contained blocks are `ikHandSlideRows` and `ikHandLayRows`; the body volumes are their own TU, `ik/ikrigvolumes.cpp`; the spine chain is searched once per skeleton (`ensureSpineChain`, read by `ikChooseUnknowns` and `ikPosturePolicy`, which each rebuilt it per tick). (2) ONE DEFINITION of what was copied — the foot-class rule (`IkRig::isFootClass`, public; its 0.20 was typed at twenty sites), the fold test (`Armature::isFoldChannel` / `foldAxisOf`, eleven copies), the locked range (`kLockedRangeDeg`), `freeChannelCount`, `boneUnder` (the solver's O(1) ancestor test behind the nine `under` lambdas), `chainLength`, the heading's `lateral`/`fore` axes on the scratch, `axisIndexOf` and `rotationVector` in `ikmath.h`, `volumeAxisParam` in `bodyvolume.h`, `kPi` with its degree factors, every env probe two files read as one file-local const (the solver's loop-local statics hoisted to file scope), the rig's `kFleshRadius` / `kContactHeight` / `limbHangsAboveHips` for its unnamed literals. (3) EFFICIENCY, the same arithmetic in the same order — `JointSolver` keeps a `Workspace` (the descent's twenty vectors allocated per descent, up to thirteen a tick), swaps the accepted trial in, builds the balance row's INSET POLYGON once per descent (`BalanceController::insetPolygon`; `closestBalancedPoint` rebuilt it, an O(hull²) clip, at every residual), computes each row's shared terms once in `linearize` (not once per DoF per row), and assembles and factors the normal matrix over the ACTIVE ROWS alone with J·D⁻¹ formed once a pass (an off row was an identity row with a zero ask, which changes the active block's factorization by nothing — its pivot is 1 and every product with it +0.0 — at rows³ for the whole, where the plane rows are many and mostly out); `IkRig` caches the per-node structure the policy asked for every tick (`descendsFromPelvis`, `hasFootBelow`, `isLegJointAboveFoot`, `legOf`, `isLimbTip`: each an O(n × depth) walk), swaps the last-applied pose, takes its per-tick flags by reference; the Armature keeps its per-tick buffers, captures the plane source's exemption vectors by reference (four n-vectors were copied into the std::function each tick), computes the ride positions once a tick, caches `hangArmHeld` through the hang's parts, reads `fkVolumeDepth`'s subtree off the solver's ancestor test, copies the head righting's Euler once a pass. (4) DEAD: `Armature::ikScope` / `ikFigureMove` / `setIkDamping`, `shortestArc` / `signedAngleAround` (the position-space solver's), `JointSolver::Result::maxStep`, `descend`'s discarded out-parameter, `IkEffector::pinned` (never read), the start imbalance's double computation, a stale `stepPending` comment, headers naming the deleted FABRIK solver. KEPT ON PURPOSE: every rejected-variant A/B probe (the project's convention: a variant stays behind its probe with its reason), the two typed copies of `kDegenerateSupportRadius` (the double is not the float's promotion), `SkeletonGraph::traversalOrder` (harness API), the landing's accessors. NO BUG FOUND that changes a pose (the audit's one latent assumption — `beginIkDrag` reads the model transform as translation-only, which the app's transforms are — is noted at its site). THE METHOD, for the next such round: a baseline run before the first edit; one batch at a time, each proven by the folder diff before the next (five runs, r35-r42); patch scripts with asserted anchor counts (a script whose new text CONTAINS its needle is not idempotent — run once, or snapshot with `git stash create` first); the stage-extraction rule — a part re-binds the scratch members it USES at its top, filtered by use so the compiler flags an alias; and the two traps met: a nested type must be declared INSIDE its namespace (the first `IkPinRowState` landed after `} // namespace pose`), and a patch helper that cuts between two anchors cut a third function that stood between them (`closestSegmentPoints`, restored from HEAD — a link error, not a silent one). THE HARNESS FOLLOWED THE SAME DAY (`tools/ikharness/realphases.cpp`, one 4,988-line function of phases): it is the `RealPhases` class now — the state every phase shared as a local of that function (the report, the rig, the T-pose rest pre-pose, ONE rest-pose probe armature where 38 were built, the floor scenarios' sizing) and the helpers (`run`, `runIdle`, `limitBoundEnd`, `phaseLegs`, `indexOf`, `under`) as members, and sixteen phase FAMILIES (`handPhases` … `tailPhases`) holding the `if (report.wants("…"))` blocks VERBATIM in their original order; the lambdas the blocks each re-defined are one definition each (`indexOf` ×16, `moved` ×8, `under` ×5, `jump` ×4, `pitch` ×3, `holdOsc` ×2, `lopsided`), the two `under` variants named for what they are, and four lead comments that had drifted away from their phases (the girdle's, the hand lay's, the Ctrl+drag's, the lifted foot's) put back above them. THE TEST IS THE SAME: the harness's printed output on the eight rigs is byte-identical to the baseline's (r45 = r33), and the sweep's rows are the pre-refactor sweep's — a test-code refactor that changes what a gate prints is not a refactor (the first run differed in exactly one line: a word-level rename had reached a gate's LABEL, "carpals under the hand"; renames touch the definition and the calls, never a string or a comment). `simulator.cpp`'s `runScenario` (970 lines, its measurements as lambdas over some forty locals of the run) is the one long function left in the harness, documented by its comment outline; a class of its own is a round of its own.

**The solver** (`JointSolver`, all math in double): minimize `sum w_t |task_t(theta)|^2 + sum s_i (theta_i - reference_i)^2` directly over the engine's own unknowns — Euler channels and a pose translation — inside the authored limits (a box). The POSTURE term (every channel pulled toward its drag-start value) makes the minimizer unique, so the pose is a continuous FUNCTION of the targets: a still cursor gives the same pose every tick (nothing to freeze), a returning cursor brings the body back exactly, cursor noise passes about 1:1 (the old loop amplified it ~14×), and the converged result is applied in FULL — no relaxation, no caps, no extraction; the limits are exact because the unknowns ARE the limited channels. Method: Levenberg-Marquardt with an ANALYTIC Jacobian (a channel's world axis — the rotation-order prefix applied — crossed with the lever), solved in the DUAL form (the posture term is diagonal and positive, so a step is one rows×rows Cholesky, ~20-40 rows, whatever the DoF count), the box as an active set, a projected step accepted only if the TRUE cost fell (two halvings, then tenfold damping). Task kinds: a point on a bone held at a target (`JointPositionTask`: optional Huber saturation, per-axis `axisScale`), a bone's world orientation (`JointOrientationTask`: uniform, or a weight PER AXIS of a world frame), one-sided planes (`JointPlaneTask`: fixed in the world or RIDING a bone via `refBone`; regenerated at every linearization by `Problem::planeSource`, with the reference cost re-read under the fresh rows), one balance row (the CoM's distance outside the inset support polygon, with a `balanceSlack` ramp), and soft COUPLINGS in joint space (`JointCouplingTask`: scaleA(a − refA) held equal to scaleB(b − refB) for two of the problem's channels — linear rows, their two Jacobian entries constant; they come last, after the balance row). Five lessons, each from a measured stall: the damping must be ADDITIVE (mu × metric — scaled with the stiffness it never rivals J^T J, and a straight pinned leg asked for height buys millimetres with radians that 1e8 pin rows turn into metres of true error); each row's per-iteration ASK is bounded (5cm / 0.3rad / 3cm of balance) while the cost never is; one-sided rows are C1 penalties; a volume's plane must ride its bone (else a spine rotation that carries the arm AND the chest it is inside is credited with resolving the penetration); and a straight limb is a SADDLE — knees and elbows (`foldSign`: ≥100° of range, all but a fifth to one side, and since 2026-09-25 a HINGE — the other non-twist channel under `kFoldHingeOtherDeg` 60°: the two oldest generations' thigh abduction, −85..15, met the range test and could not adduct past straight; see THE WEIGHT SHIFT) are bounded at straight under IK, and a solve that ends with one within 3° of straight and a task still unmet retries from 6° of flexion, keeping the pose that costs at least 0.1% less (an all-fours descent once hyperextended both knees to their −11° limit and stalled 5cm down; a softly started hip drag on a perfectly straight-legged rig never crouched). The `[unit] joint-space solver` harness phase gates FK parity with `eulerMatrix`'s composition, the Jacobian against finite differences (every row kind, a bone-riding plane and a per-axis hold included), a reach with a pin held, zero pose change over still ticks, and an exact return. A SIXTH lesson came later, and it had hidden behind passing gates: **a per-axis orientation row must be swing/twist, not the rotation vector split into components** (`lateralAxisError` in jointsolver.cpp). A planted foot's pitch is nearly free (20) beside a hard heading (4e6); split along fixed axes, the rotation vector is only meaningful while the WHOLE error is small, and in a kneel the foot stands pitched 90° from the orientation it is held to — there one degree of roll reads as 0.8° of heading, and the rows' small-error Jacobian (d e/dθ = the axis) predicted the opposite of what a step did: a full step predicted to help raised the true cost from 57 to 137,000, only 0.0002-radian steps were accepted, and every kneel crawled 10-18cm behind the cursor, jumped 10cm when a step broke out, oscillated 3cm through its still hold and ended in a DIFFERENT pose at a different drag speed — the uniqueness the solver exists for, gone. The rows now hold the bone's LATERAL axis (heading about the vertical: hard; level: soft) and measure the pitch as an angle about it, with exact Jacobians; to first order they are the old rows. The unit phase had checked this hold at 0.0001 radians on the theory that "the rotation log's curvature is not the Jacobian's business"; it now also checks it 80° off its target. WHEN A GESTURE LAGS OR JUMPS, READ `IK_JS_LM_TRACE` FIRST: a wall of REJECTs at shrinking steps is a mis-linearized row, not a tuning problem — or a row the model CANNOT SEE, the SEVENTH lesson: **a one-sided row costs and asks nothing until its joint is through the surface, so the linear model is blind to a surface a joint rests exactly ON.** Under load that never happens (a pressed contact sits a micron inside, visible); but a joint that comes to rest AT a surface with nothing pressing it is a trap — every step the model proposes goes through, the true cost rises by 1e7 × depth² against the cursor's bounded pull, only steps far smaller than ten dampings reach are downhill, and the solve "converges" where it stands. A flat foot slid along the floor (its ankle and eight toe joints exactly at their clearances) stopped dead 4cm short of a cursor it could reach; a knee resting on the other thigh's capsule held a crossing foot 5cm short. So a row IN CONTACT (clear by under `kContactBand`, 0.5mm) comes INTO the model when the step would cross it, with the room it has as a negative residual, and goes back out if it ends up PULLING its joint toward the surface (its residual after the step is its multiplier) — one change per pass, drops first: dropped together, rows came straight back across, ran out of entries, and the step went through the floor. Rows further out stay out: a capsule's tangent plane centimetres from its surface is a poor stand-in for it, and brought in from the whole 2cm margin (tried first) it held joints off volumes they were sliding along — a hand laid on the other forearm trembled 20mm at the knees, and a strained pull rested 2cm short in a different basin. `IK_JS_PLANE_TRACE` prints every floor and volume row in contact (which joint, against what); `IK_JS_NO_ROW_ACTIVE` is the A/B. A TENTH, which had hidden behind every out-of-reach hold: **a bounded ASK must not be a weaker PULL** (`JointSolver::residuals` / `linearize`, `IK_JS_OLD_ASK`). A position row more than 5cm from its target asks a step for 5cm of it — and did so at its full weight, r = sqrt(w)·k·e with k = 5cm / miss, so the MODEL's gradient was k of the true cost's. Nothing beside a hard row, which wins either way; but the cursor's row is soft once its Huber pull saturates, and it rests against the posture terms: held 30cm out of reach the model pulled a sixth as hard as the cost it was descending. The model's resting pose lay well short of the cost's, every step back toward it was rejected — the "ten rejected dampings" of every out-of-reach hold were THIS — and the pose stood wherever it had been between the two: path-dependent, and the "bounded pull of 200" was 200 only within 5cm. The row now asks k of its miss at 1/k of its weight (r = sqrt(w/k)·k·e, J = sqrt(w/k)·J_geo): the ask is bounded as before, the gradient J^T r is the true w·J^T e, the curvature is over- not under-estimated. Pulled in full, two old bugs the weak pull had hidden came out the same day: the soft HEEL's "flat" target took its heading from the ankle-to-ball offset, which a foot on tucked toes past upright has pointing BACKWARDS (the oldest generation's flat ankle was asked 11cm behind its ball: she stood up out of a kneel on 3cm of heel — the heading is the foot bone's lateral axis now), and the arms' hang (below) went unstable under a deeper fold. `IK_JS_ROW_TRACE` prints, for a rejected step (the tenth damping's second halving; with `IK_JS_ROW_TRACE_FIRST` the FIRST rejected attempt, which a tick that accepts a step at the seventh never reaches), each row's true move against the model's, with the position rows' task and bone; `IK_JS_COST_TRACE=<n>` the rows that END a solve costing more than n (weight × miss², the orientation rows' an upper bound) — which row a solve is fighting when the far-rows trace shows nothing; `IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE=<bone index>` what every row and the posture term ask of that bone's channels at the end of a descent; `IK_JS_FAR_ROWS[=mm]` the rows that end a solve further than that from their targets; `IK_JS_JACOBIAN_CHECK` a numeric check of the Jacobian at a solve that did not converge. AN ELEVENTH, found on a custom character whose crouch locked solid: **a channel a HAIR inside its limit is not in the active set, and its step goes far past the limit** (`JointSolver`'s active-set pass; `IK_JS_EXACT_ACTIVE_SET` restores the exact test). A channel is blocked when it sits AT a bound and its step points out of the box; one that stands a thousandth of a degree inside is free, the linear solve gives it a step of several degrees, the projection onto the box eats all of it — and the rows that counted on that channel moving are mispredicted wholesale (`IK_JS_ROW_TRACE`: a planted ball row's true move 85 times the model's), so every attempt is a REJECT at any damping and the pose stands still with its cursor centimetres away; the Jacobian check is clean, since nothing is wrong with the rows. A channel is now blocked when the room it has left is under 2% of the step it is asked for. The same trap was the old "sat back up from lying she rests 3-10cm short" soft spot: both thighs at their flexion limit, the second half of the in-app sit-up did NOTHING (the pose at its end identical to the pose halfway). A TWELFTH, its cousin (2026-09-22; `JointSolver::descend`, `IK_JS_NO_RATIO_STEP`): **a channel the model counts on that the trial CLAMPS breaks the step by exactly the part it lost.** The trial clamps each channel to its limit alone and takes the rest of the step in full; a COMPENSATOR — the toes' pitch keeping the toe tips on the floor while a tucked foot rolls onto its ball, asked 20° with 7 left (more than the hair rule's 2%, so not blocked) — is then clamped, the rows that counted on it mispredicted, and every damping rejected: seven a tick, the solve out of iterations, a kneeling knee 13cm behind its goal (`IK_JS_ROW_TRACE` prints the clamped channels now: `clamped: bone 8 axis 0 asked -0.34 got -0.12`). The RATIO TEST of every active-set method: the largest fraction of the step that keeps every channel inside its box, the whole step scaled so the model holds for it, the channel blocked at the next pass — tried THIRD, after the full and the half step have failed clamped, since where the clamped channel was not load-bearing the full step is the faster one. Across the suite it moves little (a hand laid on the other forearm penetrates 0.4-0.6mm where it penetrated 4.4-5.9, a carried foot lands 26mm where it landed 31, the prone hold's hand twitch 88-98 on two rigs where it was 69). A THIRTEENTH, which sat under every gesture that brings a limb against the body (2026-09-28; `IK_JS_STALE_TRIAL_PLANES`, `IK_JS_TRIAL_PLANE_TRACE` and `_NEAR`): **a trial is judged by the rows IT makes.** The one-sided rows are regenerated per linearization, within a 2cm margin of each surface, and the accept test read a trial's cost under the rows of the pose it had LEFT — but a step of 20 degrees at a shoulder moves its elbow 9cm: from outside the margin, where there is no row, to 15mm inside the chest. That trial cost 211 against 245 and was taken; the next linearization read the same pose at 4,900 and threw the arm back out past the margin; the one after took it in again — a two-cycle, sixteen iterations a tick, the pose left wherever the count ran out. A custom character's prone descent, her upper arms wedged between the chest's capsule and hands whose holds had faded, threw her head 258mm when the cycle broke (the sweep's largest real miss). A trial's cost is now read under the rows generated AT the trial (an accepted trial's rows are handed on to the next linearization, so the plane source is called no more often than before unless a step is refused). And with it the ninth lesson applied to the volumes: **a limb SEGMENT's row comes in from each end** over `kSegmentRowRamp` 0.12 of the segment (its weight by the ramp squared; `IK_JS_SEGMENT_ROWS_CUT`): cut in at full strength 2% along — where the joints' own rows were to take over, which the socket of an upper arm against the chest does not have — the cost of a pose jumped by 19 as the closest point crossed that line, and judged truly no step across it is downhill however small: the male rig's prone descent stood still for twelve ticks under a leaving cursor and let go 13cm at once (the stale judgement had hidden the cliff by never looking at it). Of the 8,235 rows of the eight rigs' reports 222 moved, in the 31 phases that bring a limb to the body or the floor — a reclining sit's worst hold jump 44 → 8mm, the oldest rig's half kneel 76 → 44, the male's prone 74 → 42, the character's 258 → 22 with its solves converging — and everything else is bit-identical. THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 41 near-misses and FOUR real (42 and five): the prone's worst tick is 9-66mm on every body, where five read 100-258 (258, 148, 131, 128, 102); what is left is the known mirror asymmetry and the flat kneel's three (two long-footed characters' kneel hold at 34-38mm, the heaviest character's kneel-up resting 13mm from its cursor). In-app 310/0 on the twelve scripts, one shot changed and accepted after looking (the push-up off the belly ends a finger's breadth lower at the chest, its worst tick 15mm where it was 26); tick work on the in-app bench 3.8ms mean and 9.0 at worst where it was 3.8 and 7.7. REJECTED on the way, each measured: a faded live contact re-seated where its joint stands as its hold returns, its fingertip's height row eased back down (the first reading of the pop: it cut 258 to 190, and with the solver mended it made a reclining sit's hold jump 39mm where it jumps 8); and a hand's hold kept from fading under a descent (no pop, and no prone either: the hips 34cm up on strut arms — the hands' letting go IS how she lies down). WHEN A SOLVE RUNS OUT OF ITERATIONS TICK AFTER TICK, read `IK_JS_LM_TRACE` for costs that alternate between two levels: that is a row the accept test cannot see, or a cost that is not continuous. An EIGHTH, about SOFT rows: **two springs on one joint must be given as ONE row at their weighted mean.** Every row's ask of a step is bounded (5cm), so two opposed rows that are both further than that from their targets ask for equal and opposite steps and the model is stationary ANYWHERE between them — a foot held between its plant spot (100/m²) and its hang under a raised knee (100/m²) stood wherever it had been left, by the spot on the way up and by the knee on the way down, the solve crawled at 0.7mm an iteration into its cap four ticks running, and the foot jumped 9cm when one miss came under the bound. (And a soft hold of a bone's ORIENTATION far from where the bone is — a trailing shin is 30-40° off vertical — is a uniform orientation row a third wrong in its small-error Jacobian: the same wall of REJECTs as lesson six; hold a POINT instead.)

**The model** (`Armature::solveIk`, every number harness-adjudicated on the base rig, then on all eight). UNKNOWNS: the unlocked channels (range ≥ 2°) of every bone that moves an ACTIVE node (`IkRig::activeNodes`: the paths joining the effector and the pins to the solve root), each pin's own channels (its held orientation), the toes under a standing foot, the root's translation — and the root's ROTATION under a pelvis drag, and under ANY drag while the body rises (below) (80/rad² for its PITCH, `kRootSquareness` 5 times that for its heading and its roll — a pelvis pitches readily, a sit rolls it back and a kneel tips it forward, but it stays SQUARE to its stance: at the one price a lunge pressed down onto its back knee answered the last centimetres with 11° of pelvis roll, now 3° — 20 pitched a kneeling torso 56° forward and splayed the thighs 29°, rotating the pelvis being cheaper than anything the legs could do; 80 had once been rejected for "breaking the kneel", a verdict that was the mis-linearized foot rows', not the number's: a sit rolls the pelvis back and a deep fold pitches it with no special case; any other drag leaves the root unrotated, since nothing anatomical limits it). Everything else — fingers, the face, an uninvolved limb — keeps its local pose and rides. STIFFNESS = 1/range² per channel (the rig's own statement of what moves easily; floor 20°, unlimited = 360°) × class, the classes cut at `IkRig::limbJunction` (the MASS-based junction: a finger's first branching ancestor is the hand, and token pectoral bones branch the chest): the dragged limb 1, a limb serving a pin 0.25 with its twist channel ×256 — on the pinned joint and the segments ABOVE it only, never below the ankle: the twist axis is read off a bone's longest child, the ball bone's is a small toe out to its side, and the toes' BEND carried the ×256 (a spring of 42/rad² holding them bent as the drag found them); dropping it from the foot bone too knocked three rigs' knees 5-6cm inward and stopped a fourth crouching — (at ×4 a bent swinging leg carried its foot sideways on 50° of thigh twist), the trunk 40 (at 4 an arm raise moved the head 10cm), the root's translation 4500/m² sideways and 1000 vertically (at 20 a 10cm hand pull slid the hips 4cm; at 300 vertically a gentle SIDEWAYS pull sank them 2cm to lengthen its reach), 1 under a pelvis drag or while suspended — and the vertical price YIELDS to a push DOWN (`kRootYieldStiffness` 30): against the cursor's bounded pull, 1000 stops the hips at pull/(2×1000) = 10cm however far the chest or a hand is pushed (the user's first report on the new solver: "she crouches a bit and gets stuck"), so it blends geometrically down to 30 as the target goes below where the drag BEGAN (from 5cm, fully by 20cm) and mostly vertically (its downward share of the whole displacement from 60%, fully by 90%) — a smooth function of the target alone, so the pose stays a function of the target (no intent flipping under a held cursor), a sideways pull keeps the stiff pelvis, and bringing the cursor back up stands her back up; a 25cm chest push now lowers the hips 22-25cm on every rig (was 8), a 65cm one 64cm, a hand pushed 50cm down 35cm. The same going UP, for a drag that BEGINS crouched (the second report: "pulling back up does not cause her to stand back up" — within ONE drag she always did, exactly; but let go of in a crouch, the crouch is the next drag's posture reference, and pulling the chest or the head back up met the same cap from below: hips +10cm, the chest 19-29cm short of the cursor): the price yields upward by the same travel and share, scaled by the HEIGHT THE LEGS HAD LEFT when the drag began (`m_jsRootRiseRoom`, the root's standing height over the floor less its height then: nothing under 2cm, fully from 8cm, figure-scaled; `IK_JS_NO_RISE_YIELD=1` withholds it). A drag that begins standing has no room, so every such drag is BIT-IDENTICAL to before (an arm raise must not buy tiptoe with a cheap pelvis), and the room is a constant of the drag, so the pose is still a function of the target. From a released 40cm crouch: chest pulled up 40cm → hips +39cm, head back at its standing height; by the head the same; a hand raised 90cm takes the hips up 12cm. **RISING is a mode of the whole lower body — and TWO quantities**: `m_jsRootRise` says the body IS getting up (the upward travel alone, full after 20cm; NOT its share of the whole displacement — getting up out of a kneel travels a third of a metre sideways too, and the share gate, which is the push-down's, held the rise at 0.7 on the rigs whose kneel reaches furthest forward: she stood on bent knees with her heels 3cm up); it FREES things — the pelvis's prices, its rotation, balance. (The BODY's upward travel, that is: a trunk joint's or the pelvis's own is the cursor's, a LIMB's is not. A kneeling figure whose hand was raised 40cm — a reach the arm has by itself — read as 40cm of getting up, and with the pelvis freed, its references turned upright and the legs sent home she rose 28cm off her knees to wave; a knee drawn up for a half kneel stood her up the same way. For a limb's joint — one without a trunk joint's mass below it, or a fold joint, or a foot's — the rise is what the limb CANNOT give: how far the target lies beyond `kLimbReachShare` 0.95 of the limb's reach from its socket as the drag found it (`m_jsLimbSocket`: the child of `IkRig::limbJunction` on the way to the effector), the upward share of that; still a function of the target. A crouching figure's hand raised 90cm takes the hips up 7cm, raised 1.3m — out of reach — stands her up; `IK_JS_LIMB_RISES` is the A/B.) `m_jsRiseProgress` says how FAR ALONG she is (the height regained over the height the legs had left); it TURNS things home — the pelvis upright, the legs to the rest stance, the soles, toes and heels flat, the hips under a grabbed trunk joint. On one factor those all snapped home in the first 20cm: a foot asked flat with its knee still on the floor, and, pulled up out of a sit by the HEAD, hips asked directly under a head still reclined 30° behind them — backward, away from the planted feet: the whole pose locked. A drag that begins low begins in a pose whose every channel calls the low pose home: the pelvis's HORIZONTAL price yields with the vertical one (getting up out of a kneel carries the hips a third of a metre back over the feet, and 200/(2×4500) is 2cm — pulled up by the chest a kneeling figure did not rise at all); the root's ROTATION joins the unknowns under any drag, its reference turning from where it began to UPRIGHT with its heading kept (`m_jsRootUprightEuler`; its price comes down from frozen as the rise completes, so nothing pops); the planted legs and the bone they hang from go HOME (references to the rest stance, `kRiseHomeStiffness` 0.2/rad² on a STANDING FOOT's limb only — a TIE-BREAKER that must stay one: at 2 the legs straightened eagerly enough to push the hips up and forward ahead of the chest the user held, the spine was crushed between them, arched to its limits and twisted, while the final stance is as clean at 0.02 (the upright pelvis is what makes it); and applied to every pin-serving limb it swung the ARMS of a figure getting up off all fours toward the bind pose, the limb twist channels excepted — a turned-out stance is the user's); and the sole's and the toes' targets turn FLAT (heading kept). **The HIPS are drawn UNDER a rising trunk joint** (`trunkRise` in `solveIk`: a soft row on the root at cursor − mix(the offset as found, the standing offset turned to the figure's heading, progress), its pull bounded at 200 × rise, and BALANCE stands down by (1 − rise)² — exactly as it is off under a pelvis drag: the user is taking the BODY somewhere). Left to infer the hips from the cursor, the contacts and balance, the solve had no good answer, and every variant of support handling traded one artifact for another across the rigs: the knees' support gone in one tick, balance hauled the hips back 2.4cm a tick and the spine twisted; kept as supports, the hips drifted 15cm forward under an arched back; drawn in gradually, the chest was thrown 13cm behind the cursor; and on three generations she stopped 6-13cm short. Dragged by the HIP the same figure had always got up cleanly on every rig — so that is what a rising trunk drag now is (`IK_JS_NO_HIPS_UNDER`). The HEEL's soft target is where the ankle sits over the ball AS FOUND, turning to flat with progress — not the rig's pin, healed to standing height the moment the drag begins (from a tucked-toe kneel that was 5cm of pull per heel from the first tick, as strong as the whole cursor's: it distorted a kneel under any drag, and its horizontal part sat 5cm ahead of where a flat foot's ankle belongs, which is why she stood up on 2cm of heel). Before these a figure pulled up out of a kneel stood with her pelvis still pitched 37°, thighs flexed 48° against it, knees bent 25° and heels 4cm up. `IK_JS_NO_RISE_YIELD`, `IK_JS_NO_UPRIGHT`, `IK_JS_NO_CONTACT_FADE` are the probes. GENERAL RULE: any posture stiffness s that opposes the bounded pull caps that motion at pull/(2s) — check a new stiffness number against it in BOTH directions and from a NON-rest start. The CURSOR row is 1e6/m² with a BOUNDED PULL (Huber at pull/2w; 200, ×5 for a pelvis drag): a reachable cursor is met to 0.01mm, and an unreachable one draws each channel only until its posture gradient matches the pull — the arbiter of how far the body follows (at 1e4 an out-of-reach forward pull leaned to the joint limits and stepped; at 50 a reachable target needing trunk lean rested 2cm short). PINS are 1e8 (slip a quarter micron; user pins ×4, their orientation at 0.2 m/rad so place wins a limit-forced compromise), and a pin target the rig MOVES (ground healing, a step's swing, a slide, a landing) is approached at 2.5cm/tick. THE FOOT CONTACT MODEL: a standing foot is held hard at the BALL (the floor-level joint with children under the ankle, found by bind height level by level — of a level's candidates the one nearest their middle: one generation hangs every toe straight off the mid-foot, and first-found was the LITTLE toe), the heel (the rig's pin) only softly (2000/m²) and never below its height (a floor row), the sole hard in yaw, soft in roll (5/rad² — held hard, a wide lean paid the ankle's exhausted roll in thigh twist: knees rolling inward) and nearly free in pitch about the ball (20), the toes' own orientation held (4e4); the BALL has its own eased target at ITS floor height (carried rigidly under the ankle's pin it was driven under the floor by exactly the heel's lift whenever a drag BEGAN on lifted heels, the rig healing the ankle's pin back down to standing height), the ball is found by BIND heights alone (the model-space floor was once subtracted from them, and a figure the Ground button had raised 1.5cm lost its balls: rigid ankle pins, silently), AND ON THE GENERATION WHOSE TOES FAN STRAIGHT OFF THE MID-FOOT the ball's sibling toe bases are its TOE MATES (2026-09-23; `m_jsToeMates`, `IK_JS_NO_TOE_MATES`; `IK_JS_BALL_TRACE` prints each pin's ball): there the ball is ONE toe's base among five (the nearest their middle) — an unknown, held flat — and the other four rode the mid-foot rigidly, neither unknowns nor held, so in a kneel they pointed straight down with the foot and 26mm of skin went through the floor (the base rig's toes bend back 24°; the third generation's kneel was the only one of the eight that showed it, and it showed on every kneel, sit-back and all-fours of that generation). The mates are unknowns with the toes' flat hold, the ball and its mates together weighing what one ball does (at the full weight each, five level holds against a foot rolling onto its edge under a swung knee ran every toe to its roll limit and a toe joint 6.5mm through the floor): all five toes bend back 45° in the kneel, the skin rests ON the floor (0mm in-app), joint penetration 18.7 → 6.1mm in the kneel and 18 → 2.3 in every floor pose of that rig; the seven other rigs have no mates and are bit-identical. (A first cut looked for the joint the toes fan FROM — the mid-foot bone with six children — but it stands at 0.68 of the ankle's height, well over the ball's 0.45, and its 12° of range could not have kept the toes flat anyway; `IK_JS_BALL_FIRST_LEVEL` restores the plain first-level pick, which the fan preference leaves unchanged on every rig.), and the sole's frame is built from the foot bone's own LATERAL axis (from the ankle-to-ball offset's horizontal part it was no heading at all for a foot standing upright on tucked toes, and the foot's hardest row then twisted both ankles to their limits) — so a heel lift is not a policy but the cheapest pose when the ankle runs out in a deep crouch or kneel, a high reach goes on tiptoe, and a lean rolls onto the foot's edge. A LIVE contact (a hand or knee come down mid-drag, or on the floor when the drag began) is UNILATERAL, its hold on the joint's place falling by SIX DECADES over the first 2cm of the joint's rise (an unloading contact has less and less friction; a LINEAR fade does nothing — half of 1e8 is as rigid as all of it, and a kneeling shin cannot rise without its knee sliding, so held "half" it rose 1.5cm and deadlocked: a third of a second of stall under a leaving cursor, then a 5cm pop). It is UNILATERAL — held in x/z, its height left to the floor row, so a rising body lifts it off (held in full, a rising figure's hands stayed nailed down) — unless the rig made it HARD (a knee over its planted foot while the body goes down), which is held in full. **PLACING A FOOT — the SLIDE** (`slide` in `solveIk`: 1 with a dragged foot's target at the ankle's standing height, 0 from 12cm up; a function of the target alone). A foot in the AIR is placed by its leg — the foot rides its shin — and since 2026-09-25 the hips go OVER THE STANDING FOOT as it lifts (THE WEIGHT SHIFT, below; until then they stayed, balance forgiving the imbalance the drag began with as measured against the RIG's support with the dragged foot out of it, and she stood on one leg with her weight beside the standing foot). A foot ON THE FLOOR is a stance being reshaped, and by the slide: the root's REFERENCE goes with the stance — its centre (a foot moved d moves the middle of the supports d / their number) and its HEIGHT (as tall as the legs' length allows over the feet where they now are, less what the drag-start pose was short of that: `straightHeight`, the minimum over the dragged leg and every standing foot's) — at a price of 250/m² every way (`kLegDragRootStiffness`; the price alone cannot do this, it only ever prefers LESS travel: at any price the hips stayed by the standing foot and the reach was paid in its knee — a 40cm side step was a side lunge, the standing knee bent 53°, the head 11cm down — and a height simply made FREE let a lifted foot take the hips up onto the standing foot's toes for its first 3cm); the foot's own channels join the unknowns and its SOLE is held as the drag found it (flat, heading kept, for a foot that began in the air) at pitch 20 / heading 200 / level 20 per rad² × the slide — the hold's TARGET eased from the rotation the slide FOUND the foot at (the tick the target came within the slide's band) toward its flat target at `kSoleTurnStepDeg` 5°/tick, the ease only ever leading (2026-09-25; `IK_JS_NO_SOLE_EASE`): a foot lifted and carried on a taut leg rides its shin 50° from flat, and the hold, at a fiftieth of its weight already twenty times the ankle's posture term, flipped it flat in two ticks — 85mm at a toe on the newest generation and four of its characters, 46 eased — soft beside the cursor's pull, two decades over the ankle's posture term (riding its shin rigidly a foot slid sideways stood on its inner edge, and one taken back stopped 3cm short, its toes reaching the floor first); and its footprint joins the SUPPORT drawn out from the middle of the rig's support by the slide (arriving in one tick, a foot carried 35cm sideways and set down had the hips sink 12cm on the way down and spring 6cm back up as it landed). Under ANY foot drag the leg's segments carry the limb twist price (a leg placed by its foot has no business turning: free, a 30cm step forward spent 57° of thigh twist, a foot taken across the other leg 66°, crossed legs 75°). Before: a 55cm stride stopped 4cm short with the hips where they had been, a side step 7cm short; now on all eight rigs the foot is on the cursor to 0.08mm, the hips take half its travel, the sole stays on the floor to a millimetre, the knee twist is under 1° (5° on one rig), a wide stance brought back in stands her up exactly (head −0.05mm, knees 0.2° on the main family; 9° and 4mm on the oldest), and two steps forward end standing. Probes: `IK_JS_NO_LEG_DRAG`, `IK_JS_NO_SLIDE`, `IK_JS_NO_SLIDE_CENTRE`, `IK_JS_LEGROOT`, `IK_JS_SLIDEPITCH` / `_SLIDEHEADING` / `_SLIDEROLL`. **THE WEIGHT SHIFT — a foot lifted off a two-footed stance takes the hips over the standing foot** (2026-09-25; `m_jsWeightShift` in `beginIkDrag`, the root's home in `ikPostureModel`; `IK_JS_NO_WEIGHT_SHIFT`, `IK_JS_WEIGHTSHIFT`, `IK_JS_WEIGHT_TRACE`). Every knee raise, kick, step-up and foot lifted behind in the galleries stood on one leg with the pelvis and head a hand's width to the LIFTED side of the standing foot — the standing leg hanging plumb from its socket, a stance that would topple — because lifting a foot was designed to move nothing else: the hips stayed and balance forgave the imbalance the drag began with, measured with the lifted foot OUT of the support (10cm on the base rig's wide A-pose, so the row never spoke). Now, for a drag that begins on exactly TWO planted feet (no live contact, no seat, not on her knees) one of which it can lift — the dragged foot, or the foot under a dragged knee — the root's horizontal HOME moves from where the centre of mass stood at the press to over the standing foot's MIDFOOT (halfway from the ankle to its footprint's centroid: the footprint's points are mostly the toes', and on a rig whose toes splay outward the centroid alone sat 4cm outside the ankle), by the LIFT — a foot drag's 1 − slide, a knee drag's 1 − plant, functions of the target — measured from the lift the drag BEGAN with (a foot already in the air shifts nothing: the drag-start pose is the contract; a raise brought back down brings the hips back and the pose returns exactly), the home approached at the pins' pace (`kPinEaseStep`: the foot lets go over 7-9cm of the target's lift and the home went 24cm with it, a body sliding sideways in a dozen ticks under a quick lift; the settle waits for it as for a pin). The shift is a constant of the drag and cannot know where the lifted leg's own mass goes, so BALANCE POLICES THE REST: with the shift on, the balance row's start-imbalance is measured against the support AS IT STOOD at the press — both feet in it, so nothing (`IK_JS_WEIGHT_FORGIVEN` restores the old base) — and a knee held at its cursor 24cm outside its shifted socket, carrying the weight 7cm back toward the lifted side, is answered by the trunk (12cm from the standing ankle forgiven, 0-8mm outside the footprint policed). The shift IS the stance's: 22-25cm on the main family's wide A-pose (ankles 41cm apart), 12cm on the generations that stand with their feet under their hips; the standing leg adducts 5-11°, its knee softens up to 11°, its sole rolls onto its inner edge (soft), and a knee raised straight up stands that far outside its socket with a hanging shin lying in the abducted thigh's plane, its foot toward the midline (the harness's hang gate is fore-aft only now). Two things it uncovered: (1) **a fold joint must be a HINGE** — the fold rule (`foldSign`: ≥100° of range, all but a fifth to one side, bounded at straight) matched the two oldest generations' THIGH abduction channel, −85..15, so a standing thigh could not adduct past straight under IK and put the pelvis over its foot by TWISTING 12° instead, the foot pivoting a centimetre on its ball; a fold's other channel, the twist excepted, must now span ≤ `kFoldHingeOtherDeg` 60° (a knee's side channel spans 10, an elbow's is locked; `IK_JS_FOLD_RANGE_ONLY`) — the six other generations author 20-26° of adduction and were never caught; (2) the sliding sole's eased target above. Gated: `the weight over the standing foot: centre of mass outside its footprint (mm)` ≤ 25 (`RunResult::comEnd`, `comOutsideFootMm` in `realphases.cpp`) on the knee raise, the foot lift and the mid-thigh raise, where it read 200-240 before (the hips' displacement is informational there now); the gallery's knee raise expects the hips over 15cm and its knee within 35px (the thigh's lateral reach from a shifted socket: 24, 6 before with the body toppling). In the app: raise a knee, kick, put a foot on a step or lift one behind — she stands over the standing foot, the leg angled in under her. SOFT SPOTS: a knee dragged toward the LIFTED side while the body shifts the other way runs out of the thigh's lateral reach sooner than before; a foot carried far sideways on a taut leg comes down with its sole rolling flat over the descent (46-61mm at a toe in one tick, the carried-foot phase's gate 70); the pelvis stays level and the trunk upright (no contrapposto: a real one-leg stance often drops the lifted side's hip and tilts the shoulders back) — the next refinement if the shots ask for it. **REACHING DOWN — a hand pushed down folds the trunk at the hips** (2026-09-25; `m_jsReachDown` / `m_jsReachHinge` / `m_jsReachTravel`, set in `dragIkTick` and `ikTrunkPolicy`, priced in `ikPostureModel`; `IK_JS_NO_REACH_HINGE`, `IK_JS_REACHSQUAT`, `IK_JS_REACHHINGE`, `IK_JS_REACHFOLD`, `IK_JS_REACHFOLDMAX`, `IK_JS_REACHHINGERANGE`). A hand brought to the knee was a half squat with the trunk bolt upright, a hand taken to the floor a deep squat with the hands between the knees and the gaze level: the knee is the cheapest joint in the body, the hips' vertical price yielded to a hand's push exactly as to a chest's, and the pelvis could not pitch at all under a limb drag, so nothing ever folded. Now a HAND drag (the effector its wrist: `m_jsWristOf[e] == e`, a finger or forearm grab promoted to it) from a STANDING start (the bow's room gate; not suspended, no seat pin, not on her knees or the floor) gets the bow's hinge — the root's pitch an unknown, coupled into the spine chain — by `m_jsReachDown`, the push-down's own `yieldFor` of the hand's DOWNWARD travel (5-20cm, 60-90% vertical): the pitch's REFERENCE folds forward by `kReachFoldPerMetre` 125°/m of that travel (110 first: a hand at the knee then bent the knees 45-59°; 140 for an hour: the fold ramped faster and three short-armed characters' floor reaches landed in another basin, 11-37mm short at the same 55° cap — the sweep's catch), `kReachFoldMaxDeg` 55 at most (75 first: the head hung at the knees, a yoga fold, not a reach), at `kReachHingeStiffness` 300/rad² over the share (firm enough that the reference decides the fold, soft enough for the cursor's bounded pull to bend it ±20°); the spine follows over a `kReachHingeRangeDeg` 220 coupling range (two fifths of the bow's curl per degree of pelvis pitch: a reach folds from the hips first, the back rounding a little); the HEAD keeps looking at the floor ahead — `rightingOf`'s deep-tilt fade (70-110° of chest tilt, made for all fours and lying down) is withheld by the reach's share (its third parameter), where a chest near horizontal had let the head hang; and the reaching arm is OUT OF THE FOLD RETRY while its hand is being placed on the floor (the hand slide's share over 0.5 clears `foldRetry` on its bones, as a landed hand's arm is): kicked, a straight arm reaching the floor flipped its hand between the palm frame's basins every tick of the hold — 133mm at a thumb tip on a heavy character, the grab oscillating 1.8mm; 38 and 0.03 without (`IK_JS_NO_HAND_SLIDE` / `IK_JS_NO_FOLD_RETRY` were the A/B); the hips' horizontal price yields to the counter's 250 as under the bow so they go BACK as the trunk goes forward, and their vertical yield is `kReachSquatShare` 0.5 of a chest push's (the price bottoms at 173/m² instead of 30) so the fold can compete with the squat. THREE VERSIONS IN ONE DAY, each looked at: the bow's hinge price alone (20/rad²) folded nothing at 30cm and everything at 78 (pelvis 49°, spine 80°, the head hanging at the knees), because the spine followed the pelvis at the trunk's 40× price — a fold to the knee cost eight times the squat; the spine's flexion cheapened to the limb's price then let the price decide, and a hand to the knee ARCHED HER BACKWARD 9-23° (the balance row and the cursor spent the cheap channel both ways) while a hand to the floor folded 130° with one leg straight; the REFERENCE version is what stands — a price can only prefer less travel, and what a person does reaching down is given. On all eight rigs: a hand 30cm down folds the pelvis ~37° with the knees at 40-55° and the head 35-45cm ahead of the hips (a bow over softened knees, looking down), a hand to the floor ahead (72cm down, 45 forward) folds it 55-58° with the knees 31-72° and the head 49-58cm ahead, the hand on the cursor to 0.07mm, no step, worst tick 13-69mm; the 23-character sweep passes all three phases (34 near-misses and 6 real at the first cut — two short-armed characters' targets 2cm under the floor, two heavy builds' hands flipping in the hold — 32 and the one known real after the retune); a hand brought to the knee and back up leaves the head within 0.3mm and the pelvis within 0.13°. The in-app pick-up, toe touch and hand-to-floor are the fold now. Gated by the three REACH-DOWN phases (`--only reach-down`: the head's gain AHEAD of the hips ≥ 150 / 350mm — sign-free across rigs, where the pelvis's pitch channel is not — the deeper knee ≤ 70 / 80°, the return exact). SOFT SPOTS: a deep one-handed reach ends with one knee more bent than the other by 20-30° on most rigs (a staggered stance the side view reads as plausible; a soft coupling of the two knees' fold channels was tried at `kReachKneeCoupling` 30 and is OFF: a straight knee is a fold BOUND, so the coupling could only pull the bent knee straight to match it, and a character rig's floor reach missed by 24mm with both legs straight under a fold at its cap — `IK_JS_REACHKNEECOUPLING=30` is the A/B); the shallow reach's knees bend 45-59° for a hand at the knee (a real one bends them 20); a seated figure reaching down rocks its pelvis only as far as the pull takes it (the seat rocks under hand drags too since 2026-10-28, without the fold's reference); the floor gallery's OLD all-fours route (a hand to the floor ahead, the other, the hips down) is a fold with the hands down now, not a squat — all fours is made from a kneel by the chest, as documented. **CONTRAPPOSTO — standing on one leg, the pelvis rolls the free side down and the chest stays level** (2026-09-25; `s.contrapposto` / `s.weightLift` in `ikPostureModel`; `IK_JS_NO_CONTRAPPOSTO`, `IK_JS_CONTRAPPOSTO` = degrees). Since the weight shift a knee raise, a kick or a step-up stood balanced over its foot with a dead-level pelvis and a trunk stiff as a post. Now, under any drag that can lift a foot (the weight shift's own condition: two planted feet at the press, one of them the dragged foot or the foot under a dragged knee; not a pelvis drag or a rising body, whose root turns anyway), the pelvis's ROLL joins the unknowns with a REFERENCE that turns the free side down by `kContrappostoDeg` 8° × the lift share (the standing hip hikes) — built like the hip sway's, a roll about the figure's fore axis on top of the root's drag-start rotation, the sway's `swayEuler` — at the pelvis drag's squared price over the share (`kRootRotationStiffness` × `kRootSquareness` / share, the share floored at `kContrappostoFloor` 0.02 so the channel is frozen rather than absent while nothing is lifted), and the spine chain joins the unknowns with the sway's CHEST-LEVEL row (`swayChest`, 1000/rad²) — so the spine curves back between the rolled pelvis and the level chest: the S of a relaxed one-leg stance. FOUR LESSONS in one afternoon, each a pop: (1) marking the root an unknown sends its PITCH through the bow's hinge branch, which divides by a hinge share of ZERO under a knee drag — an infinite stiffness that silently inerted every lifted-foot drag (the pitch stays out of the unknowns without a hinge); (2) let through at the sway's prices (pitch cheap), the balance row pitched the pelvis 12° back under a knee raise: the ROLL channel alone; (3) the chain ENTERING the solve with the lift met a balance need the shrinking support had built up and leaned the trunk through it in one tick (62mm at the brow), and LEAVING it at the landing 131 — so roll, chain and row are in the solve from the drag's first tick, the reference simply 0 until the lift, and nothing appears or disappears; (4) with its flexion and twist in too, the spine was a lever for the balance row under a knee drag, and as the foot came back down and the support regrew it pitched the trunk 9° in one tick (131mm at the brow, 91 at a shoulder): under the contrapposto only the spine's SIDE-BEND channels (about the fore axis) are unknowns. On the base rig: pelvis roll 5-7° at the 8° reference (the price and the level row leave about two thirds; 3-5 at a 6° reference, first tried), the free hip 12-18mm below the standing one, a knee raise brought back down returns exactly (head 0.000mm), worst tick 58-72mm (46-68 before). All eight rigs 125/125; the sweep 37 near-misses / 1 real (the known mirror asymmetry) — the four new near-misses each within 5% of its gate: two heavy characters' knee raises stand the other foot 10.5-10.9mm (gate 10) and jump 71-76 (70), one rig's grab rests 20.02 at a gate of 20, one's standing foot moves 5.17 at 5 — the standing foot rolling a shade further onto its edge under the rolled pelvis; the in-app suite 152/0 with the four lifted-foot shots changed and accepted. Gated: `contrapposto: the free hip lower than the standing hip (mm)` ≥ 5 on the knee raise and the foot lift (the lifted leg's thigh socket below the standing one's: sign-free, where the root's roll channel is not). **THE TIPTOE — a hand pulled up beyond the arm's reach lifts the heels** (2026-09-25; `m_jsTiptoe` in `dragIkTick`, the root's home in `ikPostureModel`, each heel's target in `ikPinRows`, `m_jsTiptoeDrag` at `beginIkDrag`; `kTiptoeDeg` 35, `kTiptoeReachShare` 1.0; `IK_JS_NO_TIPTOE`, `IK_JS_TIPTOE_DEG`, `IK_JS_TIPTOE_TRACE`). Between the arm going straight and the lift-off (15cm beyond reach for a quarter of a second) nothing gave: the soft heel rows (2 × 2000/m²) and the root's vertical price (1000) against the cursor's bounded pull are 15mm of heel, and a hand pulled 10cm beyond reach rested 97mm short with the feet flat — where a person reaching a high shelf rises onto the balls of her feet. A price only prefers less travel: the root's HOME rises, and each heel's target with it (the ankle pitched about ITS ball by the same angle — up by d sin t + h0 (cos t − 1), toward the ball by d (1 − cos t) + h0 sin t, the foot's own d and h0 read off the target as it stands; left where it was, the heel rows pulled against the home and the body rose a fifth of the way), by what the limb cannot give upward — the limb rise's own measure, read at the limb's FULL length (`kTiptoeReachShare`: at the rise's 0.95 share a hand that could reach flat-footed went 4.6cm onto its toes) — less the height the legs had left (a crouched start stands up first), up to the heel lift `kTiptoeDeg` of plantar flexion about the ball gives, sized from the feet's own bind geometry once the first solve has found the balls (6.2cm on the base rig: d 12.4cm, h0 5.2cm). A function of the target: the hand brought back within reach puts the heels down exactly (0.000mm). Only a drag of a limb's joint from a two-footed stance — no live contact, no user pin, not on her knees or her seat, not suspended — never a foot's; the sole's pitch about the ball is nearly free and the toes' hold keeps them flat, so the tiptoe is the foot rolling onto its ball as a crouch's heel lift is, and the lift-off follows as before, a few centimetres later (she tiptoes, then hangs). On the base rig a hand pulled +100cm straight up: heel lift 65mm (15 before), hips +64mm, the hand 54mm short (97); +92cm, 28mm short before, is reached on 63mm of heel; the arm raise within reach (+60) is bit-identical; the suspension phases differ by millimetres. All eight rigs: heel 48-71mm, hips +48-69, the return exact, no step, no suspension; the 23 characters 55-81mm; the sweep unchanged at 37 near-misses and one real (the known asymmetry); in-app 156/0 with the session's two reach-up shots changed and accepted (she stands on her toes under the raised arm) and the new `27b_side_tiptoe_reach`. Gated: `[real] tiptoe` — its pull sized by the ARM, the shoulder's height over the hand plus 1.3 arm lengths (+100cm was WITHIN the three T-pose generations' reach, their pre-posed hand starting lower on a longer arm, and the +112cm they need lifts the newest generation off the floor; at 1.15 arms they rose 18-30mm, the target barely past the bones) — heel ≥ 35mm, hips ≥ 3cm, no suspension, no step, jump ≤ 60mm; and `[real] tiptoe, then the hand brought back within reach` (feet and hips within 5/8mm, the hand on the cursor). SOFT SPOTS: a TRUNK joint (the head, the chest) pulled up stretches 2cm and no more — 'what the trunk cannot give' has no limb reach to read it off; a one-legged tiptoe is not made; the tiptoe begins a little before the arm is dead straight (the bone-length reach ignores the trunk's stretch and the shoulder's shrug: +86cm on the base rig, reachable flat-footed, lifts the heels 23mm); the pitch is one number for every rig, the ankle's own limit the box. **THE COLLAR MOVES AS A CLAVICLE DOES** (2026-09-25; `s.handCollar` / `s.handCollarAxis` found in `ikStiffnessClasses`, the box in `ikPostureModel`; `kCollarElevationMaxDeg` 30, `kCollarOtherMaxDeg` 15; `IK_JS_COLLARMAX`, `IK_JS_COLLAROTHERMAX`, `IK_JS_COLLAR_TRACE`). An audit of everyday one-hand gestures placed by world targets (a hand on the hip, both hands on the hips, to the chin, to the opposite shoulder, to the nape, to the small of the back, on the chest, arms out in a T, a wave, hands clasped in front) read naturally in every shot but one: a hand dragged straight to the NAPE from the rest pose ran the shoulder to its extension limit and the collar to ALL THREE of its limits — twist 30, swing −17, elevation −55 — the socket hauled 17cm inward and 15cm up onto the neck, the trunk leaning 14cm at the head to help. At the limb's price (1/range²) a collar with a 65-degree range is the cheapest joint in the arm, and it did what the arm could not; a hand pulled straight up beyond the arm's reach had it at 55 too, the raised shoulder hunched up the neck in every tiptoe shot. Under a HAND drag the dragged arm's collar — the girdle bone its socket hangs from — now has its box narrowed to what a clavicle does, about the bind and never tighter than where it stands (the fold bound's idiom): its ELEVATION channel (the unlocked channel about the fore axis) ±30°, its swing and twist ±15°.
