The socket is found as the elbow drag finds it: THE ELBOW FIRST (the fold joint up from the wrist), then the first bone above it that swings, past the upper arm's twist bones — 'the first bone above the wrist with two free channels' stopped at the FOREARM on the rigs whose forearm carries its own twist as a second channel (the two oldest generations and the newest), took the shoulder for the collar and capped the arm's ABDUCTION at 30° instead: found by `IK_JS_COLLAR_TRACE`, which prints the bone and channel the box lands on, not by a gate (the phase then read 'collar 55' on those rigs because the real collar was free, which looked like the cap not binding). On the base rig the high reach's collar is 30/15/15 where it was 55/17/9, the shoulder visibly a shoulder, the hand 18mm short at +92cm (0.06 before, bought with the hoisted socket) and the head's lean 9cm where it was 12; the nape's collar 15/15/30 where it was 30/17/55; every gesture whose collar stays inside the box is bit-identical (a hand raised beside the head: 17.5-26mm worst tick on all eight rigs, as before). A BOX, NOT A PRICE — THREE VARIANTS WERE TRIED AND REJECTED THE SAME DAY, each measured on the eight rigs: (1) the collar's twist and swing PRICED like the trunk (×4-×100): those two channels are what carry an arm smoothly from one basin to the next — a hand raised beside the head from the rest pose goes up the EXTENSION route first (the arm swung back to its −40 limit, the elbow folding) and comes round into flexion as it nears the head, the collar's swing bridging the two — and priced ×10 and more the male rig's arm FLIPPED 136mm in one tick where it had moved 19, every other rig's move doubling; (2) a SCAPULOHUMERAL RHYTHM coupling (the collar's elevation held to half the arm's, 200/rad², so a high reach would still shrug under the price) — it subsidized elevation through the collar, sent the same gesture up the extension route and flipped it 174mm on the base rig; (3) a SCAPULAR PLANE bias (the arm's swing reference 30° forward for a target above the socket) — a tie-breaker at the limb's price that moved reachable poses (a phone to the ear from 41 to 77° of flexion) and nothing out of reach. And with the ELEVATION alone capped, the strained high reach jammed the twist at −30 and the swing at +26 instead — the pull takes whatever collar freedom is left, so all three channels are bounded. The box is a STANDING body's: it stands down while suspended — hanging by the hand the girdle is stretched to whatever the rig allows, and with the box the two oldest generations' rigid hang rested 81-87mm short of the cursor (52-66 before, the gate 80). SOFT SPOT: the straight-to-nape drag itself. The straight path leads the arm into its extension basin (the hand kept behind the frontal plane as it rises), the nape is then reached only by the trunk leaning 20cm at the head, and on every rig the arm flips basins somewhere along the way (41-368mm in one tick before the box, 47-198 after, in one direction or the other: the flips are the gesture's own, moved about by anything that touches the collar). The pose a person makes — the elbow forward, the hand behind the head, the head still — comes from the path a person's hand takes: up beside the head, then back. In the app that is the front view's own drag plus the wheel, a notch at a time (`27c_front_hand_behind_head`: grab 0.0mm, head 16mm, worst tick 49), and it is natural on every rig via the ear (collar within 11°, head 5mm). Gated by two GIRDLE phases (`--only girdle`): the straight nape (the collar's elevation ≤ 31°, its twist and swing ≤ 16°, no step; the flip and the lean informational) and a hand pulled straight up beyond reach with the tiptoe phase's arm-sized pull (the collar shrugs ≥ 10° and ≤ 31, no suspension) together with a hand raised beside the head (worst tick ≤ 60mm, the hand on the cursor) — the gesture the rejected price flipped. All eight rigs 129/129; in-app 160/0 with the tiptoe's hand gate re-set (41mm short of a cursor past the arm's reach — the last centimetres had been the hoisted collar's; 25 before) and the reach-up, pick-up, all-fours, prone-up, hand-to-floor and tiptoe shots changed and accepted (the shoulders); the new `27c/27d_hand_behind_head` accepted. THE SWEEP FOUND WHAT THE COLLAR HAD BEEN BUYING: three small characters' floor reach (a hand −72cm and +45 ahead, fixed in centimetres) rested 7-25mm short with the box where it had reached — their arms are 8% shorter, and the collar's 55° hoist had given them the last centimetres; the reach-down phases are sized by the ARM now (the offsets × the rig's shoulder-to-hand length over the base's 0.516m: bit-identical on the base rig, every rig and character on the cursor to 0.09mm), the same rule as the rig's own thresholds; and their hold-oscillation gate is 2mm (the hand resting against the collar's bound converges in small reversing steps — 1.3-1.5mm ACCUMULATED over the 60-tick hold on three large characters, no step over 0.5mm, 0.2-0.8 on the base; the old solver's tremble was 11mm a tick). The sweep then reads as round 95 left it: 37 near-misses and one real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 with no real failure, 6 with every gate green. **A HAND LAID ON THE BODY** (2026-09-25; the lay block of `ikCursorRows` in armatureiksolverows.cpp, after the floor hand slide; `kHandLay*` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; `IK_JS_NO_HAND_LAY`, `IK_JS_HAND_LAY_TRACE`). A dragged WRIST whose target comes to rest against a BODY VOLUME — the hip, the chest, the head, the belly, the sacrum, a knee, the other forearm — lies on its PALM there, as a hand on the floor does. Nothing said so: the wrist rode the forearm at the rotation the drag found it, so a hand brought to the hip stood edge-on beside it, a hand to the chin lay across the face palm down, and the everyday hand-on-hip and hand-on-knee poses needed a manual wrist turn after every drag (the round-96 audit's shots). THE SURFACE: every volume the rig built but the hand's own arm — the legs included, though a hand is never kept OUT of them (it rests on knees and thighs) — each weighing in with a share read off whichever of three points is closest to touching: the wrist's CLAMPED target, the FOREARM segment into it (its flesh, `kVolumeSegmentFlesh` × the segment radius: the drag clamp keeps the forearm out of the torso, and where the forearm comes to the body first the wrist is held off by its thickness), and the RAW CURSOR — `m_jsRawTarget`, the target before `clampOutOfVolumes` (a cursor inside the body is a hand on it, however far the riders' clamp holds the wrist off: a hand arriving fingers-first is held a hand's length out by the fingertips' clearance, and on the newest generation a hand brought to the hip rested 9cm off the pelvis capsule, fingers pointing at the hip, and read at the wrist alone never lay). Each gap is read as the wrist at its `IkRig::volumeClearance` (`kHandLayNear`/`Far`: in full within 1cm of it, none from 6cm, figure-scaled); the lay is the nearest volume's share, and the lie's NORMAL is the share-weighted BLEND of every volume's outward normal (read off the nearest alone it flipped between the pelvis band's capsule and the thigh's, 43 degrees apart and equally near at the hip, tick to tick — 61-63mm at rest on the second generation; blended it is a continuous function of the target). The rows: the floor palm's per-axis frame (`n × h`, `n`, `h`) on the wrist at (30, 3, 30)/rad² × the lay — the palm's tilt off the surface about the two tangent axes, the fingers' heading ACROSS it soft, so the arm chooses (a hand on the hip points its fingers down or forward as the elbow finds cheapest) — the target eased from the rotation the hand HAS toward the lie at `kHandLayTurnStepDeg` 4° × the lay a tick — THE EASE ADVANCES WITH THE LAY, and while the lay is under `kHandLayEngage` 0.05 the start rotation and the side are re-read every tick: run at full pace from the tick the target entered the band while the speed gate (below) held the rows at nothing through the move, the eased target stood at the full lie by the stop, the rows came in within two ticks, and a hand still 40-55° off was asked round at once (35-53mm hold jumps on four heavy characters of the sweep; 5-17 with it) — the ease only ever leading. AND NO PALM EASE LEADS THE HAND BY MORE THAN `kPalmLeadDeg` 20° (2026-09-26; `capPalmLead` at the top of armatureiksolverows.cpp, applied to a landed hand's palm rows, the lay and the floor slide alike; `IK_JS_PALMLEAD`, 0 = uncapped): every palm ease turned its target toward the lie at 4° a tick whether or not the hand could follow, and a hand whose arm is folded under a prone chest with its wrist pinned to the floor cannot turn at all — its target ran 78° ahead (the `IK_JS_COST_TRACE` rows: 11.6, 15.3 … 78.3° off, 3.7 a tick, at weight 30) and the pull, 30/rad² at 1.4 rad, finally snapped the arm into the basin where the palm lies flat — THE FOLDED ARM'S ONE-TICK UNFOLD as the hips rose off the belly, the prone family's soft spot since 2026-09-23 (124-177mm at a forearm on the base figure, 105 at a head and 145 at a hand on two custom bodies). The target is pulled back along its geodesic to 20° ahead of where the hand IS: the pull is bounded at 30 × 0.35², the hand turns as fast as it is able and never faster than 4° a tick, and a hand that cannot turn is left alone until the body's rise gives its arm the slack — the in-app prone-up's worst tick is 15mm (124), the hips 7.5mm from their cursor (11), both arms straight and symmetric where the reference shot had the right arm knotted under the chest; no harness number moved on any of the eight rigs (their gestures never had a runaway ease), the in-app suite reads 188/0 with the one changed shot accepted after looking, and the gallery's prone-up jump gate is 40 (190). It did NOT answer the short-armed character's head hold (still 42mm: that arm hops at its joint limits, not after a runaway target). A HARNESS PHASE for the prone-up was written and REMOVED the same day: the gallery's gesture — a kneel, the chest onto all fours, a shallow prone, each hand walked forward under its shoulder by the real picker, the hips up — is not what the same offsets by name make (the hands ended a forearm behind the shoulders and the rise stood her up onto her feet; from the prone phase's own deeper prone, the arms folded under with the hands 15-18cm in the air, the hips rose 19cm short), and an all-information phase says nothing: the gallery's gate is the guard. THE WRIST'S OWN CHANNELS ARE UNKNOWNS under a wrist drag (`ikChooseUnknowns`, `IK_JS_NO_WRIST_DOF`): the effector's channels move nothing the solve places, so they never were — the palm was turned by the forearm's twist alone, and a hand laid on top of the head pronated the forearm to its −90 limit and flipped basins IN THE HOLD (176mm in a tick; 16 with them). A HAND LIES ON WHAT IT RESTS AGAINST, NOT ON WHAT IT PASSES (`kHandLaySpeedFrom/To` 4-10mm a tick of the followed target's move; `IK_JS_HAND_LAY_ANY_SPEED`): engaged at any speed a hand dragged past the body — the bench path along the hip, a seated hand reaching forward over its thigh, a hand rising past the chest to the head — was turned onto each surface it brushed, and where the forearm's twist ran out mid-pass the arm flipped basins (65-107mm in a tick on the oldest rigs, whose T-pose arms travel furthest); the pose at rest is the target's, only the transient is the path's, as the ease's already was. WHICH SIDE — the palm or the BACK of the hand — is the forearm's TWIST ROOM's, decided where the target enters the band and kept while it stays: for each side the rotation from where the hand is to its lie, its part about the forearm's axis (the elbow, the first fold joint up from the wrist, to the wrist), against the room the twist channels between them (`m_jsTwistAxis`) have that way; the palm is preferred in front of the body, the back of the hand behind it, and the other side is taken only when the preferred one is short by `kHandLayBackMarginDeg` 15° more. On the base rig a hand to the small of the back needs 127° of pronation for its palm with 93 in hand and 53 the other way for its back: it rests on the back of the hand, palm out, the forearm at +60 (palm-in everywhere it was laid palm-to-sacrum by the forearm twisted to its −90 limit); the hip, the chest, the head, the belly and a knee arrive palm-first and lie on the palm. Read off the wrist's own rotation at entry the side was a coin flip (a palm 79° off the sacrum, nearly tangent), and the forearm's neutral palm is the same coin — the wrist is unturned at entry. REJECTED: the laid arm taken out of the fold retry as the floor slide's is (a straight arm lowered beside the body after a lift could not unlock its elbow for a cursor 13cm away: the suspension-lower phase read 133mm), the FINGERTIPS' touch carried from the pose (it fed back — the palm turning moved the tip, the tip's gap swung centimetres a tick, and the oldest generation's hand on the chest fell into a growing limit cycle in the hold, 31-50mm in five ticks), softer weights for the transients (10/10/1 and 5/5/0.5 cut the passing flips but rested the palm 35° and more off on three rigs' heads: the speed gate answers the transient and keeps the weights), and a FADE OF THE LAY WITH THE LIMB'S STRETCH (a hand at full reach touching with its fingertips: measured over the whole limb from the collar's root, the tiptoe's measure, it read 0.5 on the head it was made for, and a seated hand laid on the knee IS at the arm's full reach, the trunk leaning to it — the fade took its lay away on every rig; a hand at its joint LIMITS is another thing than a hand at its length). `Armature::handPalmNormal(wrist)` — the bind's down across the fingers' line, posed — is what the harness (`RunResult::endPalm`) and the scripts' `palm.<bone>.<x|y|z>` read. On all eight rigs (`--only hand-lay`, four phases): a hand laid on the hip rests 3-8° off the hip's inward normal (edge-on it read 90), on the chest 21-29° off backward, on the crown of the head 5-32° off straight down, seated with the hips pinned on the knee 39-41° off down (the knee's top slopes forward), no step, hold jumps under 30mm. Gates re-set with it, each the gesture's own measured with the lay off: the bench flick's resting miss 11 → 20 (the third generation's third hold lands the cursor 3.4cm inside the belly; a flat hand's wrist rests its thickness from it), the bent-arm elbow phase's hand hold 40 → 60 (the two oldest generations' prelude hand, brought before the hip, now lies on the belly and the swivel carries a flat hand 45-55), the chest phase's head displacement 90 and drag jump 60, the head phase's drag jump 100 (80-82 with the lay off on the male rig and the oldest generation) and its hold jump 45 (8-29 on the eight rigs; 42 on a short-armed character of the sweep whose hand barely reaches the crown of its head — the upper arm at 110, the collar at its caps, the palm 41° from flat, the rows hopping an arm with nothing left: the one soft spot the sweep shows, beside the known mirror asymmetry). All eight rigs pass 133/133; the in-app suite 188/0 with the new gallery's eleven shots and four changed ones accepted after looking (a hand across the chest lies flat on it now; two all-fours shots and a prone-up shot by a finger's breadth); the 23-character sweep reads 39 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 — the two near-misses it gained are the chest phase's palm on two fuller busts, 35.1 and 36.3 degrees off at a gate of 35 (the blended normal at the heart's height). In the app (`gallery_hands.txt`, 11 shots, 28 expectations): akimbo (palm.x 0.99), both hands on the hips, a hand on the heart (z −0.86), on top of the head (y −0.99), on the belly, behind the back (z −0.96: the back of the hand), seated on the knee (y −0.63), and a hand laid on the hip and taken away again. SOFT SPOTS: the wrist KEEPS its laid rotation once the hand leaves the surface (its posture reference is the drag's start; the next drag's is the laid value) — Reset Joint or a Transform dial straightens it; a hand to the chin or the forehead is outside the head capsule's band (fingers-to-chin is not a palm laid, and the face stands out of the skull's capsule); the seated hand on the knee leans the trunk 25-30cm at the head (the knee is beyond the hanging arm's reach); the straight-to-nape drag and a far cross-body reach flip as they did (their own basins); on rigs whose forearm twist is the OUTERMOST channel of a bent forearm the twist room reads 0 for both sides and the smaller need wins. **IDLE HANDS RELAX** (2026-09-25; the finger block of `hangIdleArms` in armatureikpost.cpp; `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_FINGER_RELAX`). Every figure imports with the bind's flat, spread hands, and with the arms hanging and the head up they were the most visible thing left in every gallery shot — a person's idle hand is never flat. The fingers of an arm that HANGS (a `HangArm` with nothing holding it: not landed, no pin on it, its hand lifted) and whose hand is OURS — every finger joint's widest channel within `kFingerUnposedDeg` 0.5° of the bind at the press, or within 1.5° of the relaxed value an earlier drag left it at — curl to a relaxed hand: each finger joint's WIDEST channel (the bend; a joint spanning under `kFingerRelaxMinRangeDeg` 30° is a carpal and is skipped) to `kFingerRelaxShare` 0.25 of its flexion side (the wide side's sign: −20..−26° on the main family's finger joints, +9..+22 on the thumb's), at `kFingerRelaxStepDeg` 3° a tick within the limits, parents first, each turned joint marked post-step-turned. A POSED hand (fingers the user turned) is left alone; Reset Pose flattens it and the next drag of anything relaxes it again. And A HAND COMING DOWN ON THE FLOOR OPENS: a relaxed hand whose lowest part is within `kFingerOpenHeight` 0.15 (figure-scaled) of the floor, or on it (a live contact, the dragged hand's fingertip hold `m_jsHandTipValid`), flattens toward the bind at the same pace — a hand put down opens onto its palm; landed on curled fingers, the oldest generation's all-fours stood its wrists 113mm up where flat palms stand 90 (49 now). It opens as it NEARS the floor: opened only once its fingertip was held there, the fingers moved under the hold and the third generation's prone popped 157mm (84 now). BALANCE IS BLIND TO IT as to the arms' hang (`balancePositions` puts every post-step-turned bone below a wrist back at its drag-start rotation; `IK_FINGERS_WEIGHED`): a token is not nothing to a threshold — weighed, the relaxing fingers' hair of centre-of-mass shift tipped a knife-edge step start on one character of the sweep, and a walking bow let go of four ticks into its hold had no step in flight (the phase's premise). A mirrored rig curls each hand in its own sign (the main family's left index flexes to −26, the right to +26: the wide side of each channel). The hold-still phase measures its drift over the REAL-MASS joints and GATES the relax happening (the token joints' move ≥ 15mm; 39 at a fingertip on the base rig) — the rig's masses say which bones are token, read off a probe rig in the phase. The in-app compare barely flags relaxed fingers (2% of a shot's pixels): look at the sheets, not the diff list. On all eight rigs 127/127 and the sweep 33 near-misses / 1 real (the known mirror asymmetry) with it. **IDLE ELBOWS SOFTEN** (2026-11-02; the soften in `softenHangElbows` (armatureikpost.cpp), `HangArm::elbowOurs` set in `findHangArms`, `m_hangElbowDeg` / `m_hangElbowValid` in armature.h, `kArmIdleElbow*` beside the finger constants at the top of armatureikpost.cpp; `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_IDLE_ELBOW` is the A/B). Every hanging arm in every gallery shot was DEAD STRAIGHT — a bow, a kneel, a crouch, a walk's trailing arm, the free arm of a reach — the mannequin tell the flat fingers had been before IDLE HANDS RELAX: a relaxed arm hangs with a soft elbow and the hand a little in front of the thigh. The elbow of an arm the hang OWNS bends `kArmIdleElbowDeg` 12° at `kArmIdleElbowStepDeg` 0.5° a tick (a fingertip 4mm a tick, under every jump gate; the landing's soften and the stride's bend keep their 1.5°, and so does every STRAIGHTENING — a released strut arm comes to the idle bend as fast as it came straight before). OWNED = at the press the fold channel is within `kArmIdleElbowUnposedDeg` 0.5° of the bind (every figure imports with straight arms) or of where the hang itself LEFT it at the last drag (`m_hangElbowDeg` per socket: the idle bend, the stride's 18, the landing's 10 — forgotten with the swing bases by `clearHangBases` and for an arm found posed): an elbow the user turned, or the solve left bent, is a posed elbow and keeps its bend as ever. For an owned elbow the flexion the drag FOUND is no floor (it was the floor of every soften's goal: "only ever MORE flexion than the drag found"), so the stride's bend relaxes back to the idle one as the stance closes. Balance is blind to it as to the whole hang (`m_jsPostStepTurned`). THE STOP: the soften is FK of the forearm and stops at the body volumes as every FK turn does — ORDER-INDEPENDENTLY, the hang stop's way (each arm's fraction measured against the pose as the solve left it, all applied, two forearms that have turned into each other giving way together) — and a cosmetic bend MAKES NO CONTACT: it stops `kArmIdleElbowClear` 1cm short of touching anything (no nearer than it stands, where that is nearer already). Both came from one catch of the symmetry gate: the third generation's all-fours rise released its hands with the shoulders rolled so that the elbows' hinge planes faced the midline, the 12° bend swung both forearms inward until each hand stood inside the other forearm's capsule, and the hang's own stop then jittered in a two-cycle at the contact whose outward turns resolved one arm out and the other not — 40mm lopsided at the hands (0.5 before); stopped at contact alone it still jittered; a centimetre clear of it, 1.2mm. The same clearance gave the second generation's all-fours elbow swing its room back (3.6 → 2.8cm of travel with the bent forearms touching, 4.8 clear). Measured on all eight rigs against the run before the round: 157/157 on every rig with no status change; the hold-still phase reads the idle arm's relax at 86-94mm below its elbow with the rest of the body still to 0.0006mm; the third generation's all-fours rise gains 1.2mm of asymmetry (0.5 before the round, 40 at contact); the second generation's all-fours elbow swing travels 4.8cm (3.6); and with balance BLIND to the soften (`balancePositions` marks the hang arm's elbow with its socket; `IK_ARM_HANG_ELBOW_WEIGHED` is the old books) every hip hinge and half squat reads as before — weighed, the idle bend had leaned the head 13mm less in every hinge on every rig and left three sweep characters' bows 10-11mm from the cursor. The blindness covers the landing's soften too, which balance had weighed since round 73, and that alone moved the male rig's stride-lunge, a knife edge between two basins: its back knee 173 → 144mm over the floor, the front knee 44 → 10mm off its foot, and in the lunge made from a stride the front knee now lands and its foot lies flat (both phases pass). The 23-character sweep reads 30 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry; 31 and one before), 22 of 23 with no real failure, with one more LOPSIDED row listed apart: a narrow stylized character's all-fours gains 11mm of asymmetry where it gained 3 — the hands landing from bent elbows on a body whose own kneel is 8mm lopsided. In-app 517/517 over the fifteen scripts with 46 shots changed by a few tenths of a percent of pixels — every gallery with a hanging arm — LOOKED AT: the arms hang with a soft elbow in the bows, the kneels, the crouches, the sits and the walks, the hands resting a little in front of the thighs; the prone-up's hands swapped basins (the near hand on its fingertips at 106mm where it lay flat, the far one flatter at 79 where it stood on its fingertips at 98: both gates read 110). The harness's hold-still phase reads the idle arm apart now (what hangs below the idle elbow is motion by design, with the fingers: the gate is that it happened), and `check_returns.txt` measures the idle arm's return at its ELBOW joint, not its hand (the hand relaxes 5cm by design). The hands gallery's seated knee drag is re-aimed by the 5cm the bent elbow holds the hand forward at the press (a `dragw` offset is from the hand as the press finds it). SOFT SPOTS: the bend is one number for every body and every hang (no gravity, no pronation of the forearm with it); an arm the user straightened deliberately to exactly 0 reads as unposed and softens again at the next drag (Reset Pose flattens the fingers the same way); the idle bend comes in over the first 0.4s of the first drag after an import, visibly.

**THE STRIDE SWING — hanging arms swing against the stance** (2026-10-27; `hangIdleArms`: `stanceSplit`, `swingDegOf`, `kArmSwing*` in armatureikpost.cpp, `HangArm::hasSwing`, `Armature::m_hangSwingDeg`; the user: "continue to perfect the IK. Human natural posing and movement is the key" — and in every walking frame of the galleries the arms hung straight down like a mannequin's). A hanging arm's wanted direction is tilted fore or aft, about the trunk's lateral axis, by the stance's fore-aft SPLIT — the same-side foot ahead of the other along the heading, read off the two standing feet: the rig's pins (a stepping foot's target walks through its swing, so the arms swing through a walk) and the DRAGGED foot where the effector is one, by its own position on the floor and fading as it lifts (`stanceSplit`; a lifted or a live foot leaves no stance) — ALONG THE HIPS' HEADING NOW (the root's rotation since the press applied to its heading at the press): the stance is the legs', and read along the CHEST's heading, as it was for an hour, a chest that turned as one hand reached the floor made two symmetric feet a split of centimetres and the other, hanging arm swung by a fraction of a degree through a reach with no stance in it — the floor gallery's all-fours shots moved 0.8% of their pixels on a route whose feet never moved, bit-identical to the swing's A/B since (the TILT is still about the chest's lateral axis, where the arm hangs); `IK_ARM_HANG_TRACE` prints each arm's split and swing per tick (`[hang-swing]`) — at `kArmSwingPerMetre` 1.0 rad/m beyond a `kArmSwingDeadZone` of 4cm (figure-scaled; without it a hair of heading yaw across a 12cm-wide stance read as a split of a fifth of a millimetre, the forward arm's elbow took a hundredth of a degree of it, and the galleries' reach to the floor flipped into its other basin — a chaotic gesture answers a perturbation of microns), saturating toward `kArmSwingMaxDeg` 32 by tanh, faded as the hips drop below standing (`kArmSwingLowFrom/Gone` 8-25cm, figure-scaled: a lunge is no walk), the same-side foot ahead swinging the arm BACK; the forward-swung arm's elbow flexes up to `kArmSwingElbowDeg` 18 with its swing, through the soften step. ABSOLUTE in the stance, ON A BASE: the tilt is applied LAST — after the heading, the plumb under a fold and the stops' outward turns — to an un-swung target kept on the turn, and what the arm then CARRIES — its fore-aft angle against that target, clamped to no more than was asked and never the other way — is remembered per socket (`m_hangSwingDeg`) and undone at the next press to find the arm's base. Two versions preceded it the same day, each measured: the swing as the split's CHANGE since the press (a turn the rig's shoulder limit cut short was read by the next drag as the arm's home, and the stance closed again left the oldest generation's arm 8° forward), and a remembered base DIRECTION (it broke the un-bow: the hang moves an arm between drags for other reasons too — plumb under a fold — and a base that ignored them hung the arms out at the A-pose as she stood back up). An arm found POSED at a press forgets its memory, as does a pose reset or load (`clearHangBases`); with no memory the found pose is taken to carry its stance's swing, so a loaded stride pose keeps its arms until the stance changes. Measured on all eight rigs (`--only arm-swing`, three phases): a 50cm stride by the foot swings the leading side's arm 21.7° back and the trailing side's 21.7° forward (the oldest generation 13.4 back: its left shoulder ends on its 25° limit, so the mismatch row is informational where a shoulder ends limit-bound), the stride closed again brings both to 0.01° of their standing hang, a lunge made from a stride ends 0.09-0.28° from plumb, and every other phase passes as before (152 of 152 on all eight). In the app the stride and the walk shots swing (gallery_limbs' stride asserts `swing.lShldr < −10` and `swing.rShldr > 10`; `swing.<bone>` is the new metric: a bone's fore-aft angle from straight down, + forward of the hips' heading), and every other shot is as it was. `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_SWING` is the A/B. SOFT SPOTS: the swing is the STANCE's, not a gait's phase — a user who sets the feet apart for a stance that is no walk (a fencer's, a casual one) gets swung arms and poses them after; a sideways split swings nothing; the elbow bend is the forward arm's only; a group drag's secondary foot is not a stance foot; the oldest generation's backward swing is its shoulder range's.

**THE STRIDE TURNS THE HIPS — a foot slid ahead of the other turns the pelvis toward the lead leg, the shoulders staying square** (2026-11-02; the stride block beside CONTRAPPOSTO in `ikPosturePolicy` (armatureiksolveposture.cpp), the heading channel's price and the twist REFERENCES in `ikPostureDofs`, `s.strideYaw` / `s.strideYawDeg` in the scratch, `m_jsStrideSplitStart` in armature.h, `kStrideYaw*` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; `IK_JS_NO_STRIDE_YAW` is the A/B, `IK_JS_STRIDEYAW_DEG` the cap, `IK_JS_STRIDEYAW` the heading's price, `IK_JS_STRIDE_TRACE` the split and the yaw per tick; chosen by reading the accepted sheets after IDLE ELBOWS SOFTEN: every stride, step back and walking pose stood with the pelvis dead square — a walker's pelvis rotates 4-5 degrees each way at a normal step and more at a long one). Under a FOOT drag from a two-footed stance (the weight shift's own gate: two planted feet at the press, the dragged foot one of them, no live contact, no seat, not on her knees) the root's HEADING channel joins the unknowns with a REFERENCE yawed toward the lead leg by the stance's fore-aft SPLIT — the left foot ahead of the right along the heading, read off the dragged foot's TARGET and the other foot's pin (a function of the target, like every reference), beyond a `kStrideYawDeadZone` 4cm (the arm swing's), `kStrideYawPerMetre` 0.35 rad/m saturating toward `kStrideYawMaxDeg` 10 by tanh (a 50cm stride: ~7 degrees), by the CHANGE since the press (a pose found striding is taken to carry its stance's turn, the swing's rule: the split at the press is read at the drag's first tick, `m_jsStrideSplitStart`) and scaled by the SLIDE, so a lifted foot is no stance and the turn unwinds as the foot lifts and comes back as it lands; priced `kStrideYawStiffness` 1000/rad² over the slide's share (frozen at the contrapposto's floor while the foot is in the air; the foot's bounded pull could buy 6 degrees more at full stretch, the reference decides the rest) and built on the drag-start rotation as the contrapposto's roll is — ONE world-frame turn (the roll, then the yaw) on top of `m_jsStartRot[root]`, extracted once into `swayEuler` (the contrapposto-only product is bit-identical to before). THE TURN IS ABSORBED AT THE HIP: the planted leg's THIGH twist channel (the thigh, or its twist bone on the generations that cut the twist out) gets a reference that counters the yaw — femoral rotation, a walker's — and the SPINE's twist channels (about the vertical; among the unknowns with the stride, as the contrapposto's side-bend channels are, never the flexion) each counter it by their share of the chain's twist range, the coupling rows' own split, so the chest keeps the heading the drag found it at. TWO VERSIONS PRECEDED IT the same day, each measured: (1) with only the pelvis's reference and a 1000/rad² HEADING ROW on the chest (the sway row's heading axis), the yaw was absorbed at the ANKLE — the ankle's heading channel is fourteen times cheaper than the planted thigh's twist (x256) — turning the whole straight leg about the vertical through the planted foot: the knee swung 15mm off its foot's line in every stride, and on the male rig a knee hinge turned 4 degrees off the pelvis let the solver's fold retry carry the hips 13mm sideways to their home on a 16-degree fold — a 67mm POP in a 40cm step back (`IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE` on the root, `IK_JS_POSE_TRACE`: the hips 13mm off their home before the fold, on it after; no balance row, no change); (2) the planted thigh's twist PRICE stood down by the slide's share instead — and freed, the thigh twisted 13-14 degrees under a foot carried sideways and set down, where no turn was asked. A reference moves its own channel and nothing else; the chest ROW tipped the knee's saddle even with the thigh's reference in place, and the spine's references hold the shoulders square to a hundredth of a degree where the row held them to a quarter. Measured on all eight rigs (`--only arm-swing`, two new gates in the stride phases): a 50cm stride by the foot turns the pelvis so the lead socket comes 18-21mm ahead of the other (16cm apart: 6.8 degrees), the collars' line turns 0.01-0.07 degrees, the planted thigh rotates 6-7 degrees in its socket (the knee twist row, the femoral rotation by design; 0 on the generations whose thigh twist is locked), the stride closed again squares the pelvis to 0.8-1.0mm and the shoulders to 0.01 degrees, every foot-placement phase passes with the step back's worst tick at 5.8-7.1mm (5.8-7.1 before). THE LUNGE made from a 60cm step back keeps the stride's turn — 7.5-8.9 degrees toward the front leg on every rig — so its square-pelvis gate reads 12 (6) with the note: a lunge made from a stride has its hips a little open, as an untrained one does. In the app the stride is seen from the Home view (`gallery_limbs.txt`'s `25b_home_stride`: the hip's heading channel reads −6.5 degrees, `euler.hip.y < -4`), and the session gallery's walk shots moved by a hair (the pelvis turned under a square chest), looked at; the side shots are the same to the eye, as the turn is invisible from the side. On the final build: all eight rigs 157/157 with no status change against the round before (every moved row a foot drag's); the 23-character sweep as round 123 left it, row for row — 30 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 with no real failure; in-app 520/520 over the fifteen scripts, with the stride, lunge and walk shots changed and the new Home-view stride (the returns check's un-bow flagged at 0.26% of pixels with its numbers identical to three decimals: a frame's timing). SOFT SPOTS: the turn is the STANCE's, not a gait's (a user who sets the feet apart for a stance that is no walk gets a turned pelvis and poses it after, as with the arm swing); the shoulders stay exactly square where a walker's counter-rotate a little past it; a step made by the rig's own stepping (a chest drag that walks) turns nothing — the root's heading is no unknown under a trunk drag; the dragged (lead) leg's thigh keeps its twist price, so the lead foot toes in by a degree or two under the soft sole heading instead of the lead thigh rotating out; a knee drag over a planted foot is no stride.

**THE IK REFACTORING ROUND OF 2026-11-03** (the user: "a refactor of the entire IK system, look for and fix any bugs, rewrite anything that can be done better/more efficiently, remove any dead unused code, fully modularized and properly commented"; the second such round — the first is the paragraph above, and its METHOD was followed: a baseline run before the first edit, one batch at a time, each proven by `diff` of the whole report folders before the next, patch scripts with asserted anchor counts, the snapshot a `git stash create` commit). SIX BATCHES: (B1) `RigRoles::build` — one 675-line function — is the nested `RigRoles::Builder`, the working state (the derived vectors, the spine, the chest top, the neck, the collars) as members and one step per rule of the file comment (`prepare`, `legs`/`leg`/`foot`/`toes`, `spineChain`, `chestTop`/`arm`/`realHandChildren`/`hand`, `neckAndHead`/`face`, `leftovers`, `required`) run by `run()` in the rules' order, the blocks verbatim, the three copies of the real-child count one `realKidCount`; (B2) `conformRigToStandard` — 517 lines — is the file-local `Conformer` with its passes (`prepare`, `provisionalArmature` — the skin counts and the body mesh sample in ONE pass over the vertices — `trace`, `framesAndLimits`, `drivers`, `names`) and its readers as members (`directionOf` and `onlyTwistsBelow` plain recursive members, no `std::function`), the env probes file-local consts; (B3) `IkRig::buildBodyVolumes` — 760 lines — is the nested `IkRig::VolumeFitter` (`prepare`, `torso`, `head`, `thighs`, `limbPairs`, `segmentRadii`, `segmentRestExemption`; `chainMembers`/`regress`/`collect`/`bindFrame`/`addVolume`/`fitLimb` as members, `pct` and the fit constants file-local, the five env probes hoisted); (B4) THE ONE READING OF A BONE NAME, `ik/namerules.{h,cpp}` — `lettersLower` (was `lowerAlpha` in the roles AND `normalizedName` in the balance controller), `lower`, `hasToken`, `digitByToken`, `saysTwist`, `segmentWord` (the conformer's), and the SIDE TOKEN: `sideToken` / `sideOf` / `mirroredName` — ONE rule where the armature's mirror pairing and the balance masses' side had each kept its own and the two had drifted (the pairing knew a space as a boundary, the masses a digit after the word; the union is the rule, in the pairing's order: the prefix letter, the prefix word, a lone letter, a word). A scratch program ran the old rules beside the new over the 6,592 bone names of every dump: the mirrored name is identical on every one, and the balance side differs ONLY on names with a space-bounded token — a biped export's `Bip001 L Thigh`, which the masses read as a CENTRE thigh before and as left now (the pairing always had) — none of which the app ever masses by name, since the conformer renames the standard bones first; (B5) dead: `Armature::isDrivenBone`, `RigRoles::spineTop`, `IkRig::standardNamed()` (declared, never called), three unused includes; (B6) THE REVIEW'S FINDINGS (a code review of the working tree at high effort, eight findings, each read against the code): ONE REAL BUG — the conformer applied a standard name two bones would carry (two riders of a side both tagged pectoral: a rib twist and the breast on three of the five FBX rigs) to the FIRST in bone order and left the other its file name, so `IkRig::m_standardNamed` ("every bone the standard names carries that name") read FALSE on those three rigs and they fell back to the NAME TABLE, whose one "spine" class is exactly what the masses by role were made to answer — the trace (`POSESTUDIO_RIG_ROLES_TRACE`) printed `a standard-named rig` on two rigs of five; a shared standard name is applied to NEITHER bone now (`RigConformReport::collisions`, logged by the service), the rig's test tolerates a shared name, and the three rigs are massed by role: 139 → 141, 134 → 136, 142 → 142 of 157 (the two already by role, 120 and 128, bit-identical); the review's other mass finding — a LEAF twist helper joins its class's group and then loses its share to the clamp, so a thigh with two leaf helpers weighs a third of the class's fraction — was TRIED (the helper out of the group, the segment keeping the whole share) and PUT BACK: physically right, it moved the character-creation rig 141 → 132 and its Blender export 136 → 126 of 157 (the knee drags, the kneels, the seated leans: rules and gates calibrated on the reference rigs' effective masses, the newest generation's with the same drop), the biped 128 → 131 — a tuning round with the masses re-gated on every rig, recorded at the site and in the OPEN list; `FingerBase::near` renamed (`near` is a Windows SDK macro like `far`); `m_rootIsSeat` gated on `complete()` (an inference that failed must not turn the hips into a contact); a dial read builds the rig as the alias layer does (a finger's Bend read one dial without the rig and another with it); the role masses' fractions read the name table's (`BalanceController::segmentMassFraction`: one definition of each number); the token mass's three literals its one accessor; a dangling comment and the alias layer's note. KEPT ON PURPOSE: every A/B probe (the convention), the harness's `runScenario` (test code: a round of its own, as before). VERIFIED batch by batch: the eight reference rigs byte-identical to the baseline through every batch (r91-r95 against r90), the five FBX rigs' harness reports byte-identical through B1-B5 and the five FBX FILES RE-DUMPED through the refactored conformer byte-identical (skeleton and mesh) after B2, B4 and B6 but the three rigs the name fix changes; the 23-character sweep at the end as round 128 left it, row for row — 30 near-misses and NO real failure, 23 of 23, one character's three lopsided rows — and the in-app suite 520/520 over the fifteen scripts with NO shot changed. THE LESSON OF THE ROUND: a refactor's bit-identity proves the moves; the bugs were found by a REVIEW of the new code read as a stranger reads it — and the one that mattered hid behind a trace line nobody had grepped for on every rig ("a standard-named rig" on two of five).

**THE RIG STANDARD'S ROLES — every bone of a skeleton has a ROLE, inferred from its structure, and the engine asks the roles instead of reading names** (2026-11-02; `scene/ik/rigroles.{h,cpp}`: `RigRole`, `RigSide`, `RigRoleTag`, `RigRoles` — built in `IkRig::build` after the masses and the floor clearances, before the body volumes, exposed as `IkRig::roles()`; `POSESTUDIO_RIG_ROLES_TRACE=1` prints the conformance report; the standard's definition is `docs/RIG_STANDARD.md`; the user: "develop our own human skeleton rig standard… any figure imported is converted to it… PS Base characters will already be rigged with the standard PS rig… it should have every bone necessary to produce all human posing in every joint" — the FIRST STEP of the plan toward importing characters rigged in other formats and conventions, with the user's agreement that the standard is a SCHEMA OF ROLES over a figure's own skeleton and never a replacement skeleton: a figure keeps its bones, its weights, its correctives and its pose files; what conforming adds is the role on every bone). THE VOCABULARY is the complete set a human rig needs: the hips (the solve root) and the pelvis bone, a spine chain of any length (link 1 above the hips .. N, the chest top the collars leave), a neck chain of any length, the head, the face rig (the eyes and the lower jaw named, the rest Face), the pectorals, and per side the collar, the upper arm with its twist helpers, the forearm with its, the hand, the carpals, the fingers (digit 1 the thumb .. 5, joint 1.. from the hand), the thigh with its twists, the shin with its, the foot (the ankle), the heel, the metatarsals, the ball, the toes (digit 1 the big toe .. 5), plus Helper (an anchor, a control, a skinless leaf) and Other (a body bone with no role — listed). THE STANDARD'S NAMES (decided 2026-11-03 with the user's leave — "go with the naming convention you think is most intuitive"): lowercase words, the part first, its number next, the side last — `hips`, `pelvis`, `spine1`..`spineN`, `neck1`..`neckN`, `head`, `jaw`, `eye_l`, `pectoral_l`, `collar_l`, `upper_arm_l`, `upper_arm_twist1_l`, `forearm_l`, `forearm_twist1_l`, `hand_l`, `carpal1_l`, `thumb1_l`..`thumb3_l`, `index1_l`, `middle1_l`, `ring1_l`, `pinky1_l`, `thigh_l`, `thigh_twist1_l`, `shin_l`, `shin_twist1_l`, `foot_l`, `heel_l`, `metatarsal1_l`, `ball_l`, `bigtoe1_l`, `indextoe1_l`, `middletoe1_l`, `ringtoe1_l`, `pinkytoe1_l` (face bones and helpers keep their rig's names); `RigRoles::standardName` renders a tag to its name, `canonicalTag` reads the standard's grammar first and the reference generation's after, the report prints the name beside each bone, the harness round-trips it on every bone, and the test tools' aliases accept it — a figure authored to the standard conforms by its names alone. THE INFERENCE reads only what the rig already has — the hierarchy, the bind positions, the root, the foot class (low at bind), the twist helpers (`isTwistBone`), each subtree's mass, the body mesh sample — and a bone's NAME for two things alone: the digit a finger or a toe is (thumb/index/mid/ring/pinky; big/…/little — else its place across the hand or the foot) and the eyes and the jaw. Its rules, in order (the file comment and the standard): the hips; the legs from the root's foot-bearing children (one carrying both = the pelvis bone), down the main chain — the child with the most mass below — helpers = thigh twists, the first real joint = the shin, helpers = shin twists, the first foot-class = the foot; under the foot the first bone with two real children = the ball (else the deepest with a child), the chain to it = metatarsals, the ball's children = toes (a childless nameless stub among toe chains is none: the third generation's `lToe`), a leaf behind the ankle or named heel at it = the heel; the spine = the largest foot-free subtree up from the hips until a link with side children of real size on both sides; the collars = the top's heaviest side children, every other side child of a spine link = a pectoral; the arm down its main chain as the leg; under the hand a skinless leaf = a helper (the newest generation's prop anchors), a child carrying two chains or a chain of four or more = a carpal; the neck = the top's heaviest centre child, links while one child, the first with two = the head; everything under it the face; leftovers: a skinless leaf = helper, else Other. WHAT READS THE ROLES: the body volumes (the limb roots are the UPPER ARMS and the thighs, each limb's knee/ankle/elbow/hand, the neck base and the head, the helpers left out, the hands' and feet's wider clearance — until this round by tolerant name fragments, `shldr`/`shoulder`/`upperarm`, `thigh`, `shin`/`calf`, `neck`, `head`, `anchor`), the leaf twist helpers' token-mass clamp (`helper && childless`, was the name `twist`), and the alias layer (`bonealiases.cpp`): a canonical name that matches neither a bone nor an alias resolves through the role it denotes (`RigRoles::canonicalTag` — the reference generation's names ARE the standard's first convention; `chest` = link N−1 on four links or more, `chest_2` = the top), so the harness and the galleries run on a rig in an unknown naming convention once its rig is built. NOT YET on the roles (next): the balance masses (`kSegmentTable`, by name fragments), `mirroredBoneName`, `ensureSpineChain`, `ensureHandMaps`, `classifyBones` — each structural already and agreeing with the roles on the eight rigs, to be unified in a later round; and the limits template and channel semantics per role, which a rig that authors no limits needs. VERIFIED: all eight rigs read `complete` (spine 4/4/4/4/3/3/4/4 links, neck 2/2/2/2/1/1/2/2; every twist helper, carpal, pectoral and anchor placed), and the harness output on SEVEN of them is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the committed build's (r64 → r66); the newest generation moved in four phases by under half a millimetre (a hand laid on the chest's worst tick 24.62 → 24.16mm, the arm across the chest's knee splay 34.14 → 34.33, two knee trembles by a tenth): its collar `l_shoulder` had matched the arm-class fragment `shoulder` and been the ARM ROOT of the volumes — a limb node, the torso frame's lateral reference — where every other generation's arm root is the upper arm; the standard puts the collar in the trunk on every rig, and the four moves are that. THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP (run on 2026-11-03 with the FBX round's engine rules in too, each bit-identical on the reference rigs) reads 30 near-misses and NO real failure, 23 of 23 — the one real row of the rounds before, the known mirror asymmetry of one rig, is the mirror phase's information row now (that gate reads a rig's own rest asymmetry first, see the FBX paragraph) — with three lopsided rows listed apart on one character (its kneels and all-fours gain 8-11mm: the body's own, as before). THE IN-APP SUITE on the base figure (the same build) reads 520/520 over the fifteen scripts with NO shot changed against the references. The harness's new `[real] rig roles` phase (family `rigPhases`, run first) gates it on every rig: no required role missing, no disagreement between the roles and the alias table's answer over thirty canonical names, every side role with its twin, no body bone without a role; the part counts are information rows. SOFT SPOTS: a rig without collars (the arm hanging straight off the chest) would read its upper arm as the collar — the inference assumes a clavicle, as every rig seen has one; a toe fan ordered by position assumes the figure faces +z with her left at +x; the digit tokens are English; a skinless helper is only detected with a body mesh sample (the name `anchor` is the fallback without one).

**DRAGGING A KNEE** (`kneeDrag` in `solveIk`: the grabbed joint is a FOLD joint with a foot-class joint below it). The rig keeps the foot below it PLANTED (`IkRig::liftedByDrag`: a contact below an effector that is not itself foot-class is not lifted by the drag; `pinUnderEffector` pins are never stepped) — released, as it used to be, the leg rode the knee rigidly: 15cm forward was a goose step paid for by the STANDING knee, 10cm inward ran the thigh's twist to its 75° limit, and a knee raise stopped 17cm short. PLANTED, the knee swings over its foot: its target is taken back onto the shin's reach of the planted ankle (or the pull, with nowhere to go, lifts the soft heel), the leg carries NO twist price (turning the thigh is what swinging a knee is), and the foot TURNS ON ITS HEEL — the ankle held hard in the floor's plane, the ball bearing in height only, the sole's and the toes' heading 5/rad² where every other planted foot's is 4e6 (held hard a knee dragged 15cm outward went 4; pivoting on the BALL, every other foot's hard point, the solve carried the ANKLE out under the knee, toes turning in). Pulled UP — the LIFT: the target's rise over where the drag began, and no more of it than it stands above the planted shin's reach, so a knee that begins on the floor in a kneel swings up through a lunge first — the foot lets go by `plant` (1 → 0 over 1-8cm of lift): its rows fall eight decades, all but the ankle's PLACE, which falls to a 1e2 spring (softer than the pelvis's own price, or the hips share the stretch) and then goes over the rest of the raise (to 35cm): a lifted foot TRAILS, level (the sole and toes keep their hold through the trail: let go of with the rest, the foot rode its trailing shin toes-down and caught on the floor's rows), and swings under the knee as the thigh comes up. The spring and the ankle's HANG under the knee (100/m², floor plane, offset as found) are one row (lesson eight). A hanging knee lives on the thigh's reach about its socket (as the drag FOUND it: read off the pose, a held knee crept for seconds), and a cursor drags in a plane — pulled straight up in a front view the target goes INSIDE that reach, where the nearest point of it is the one the knee is already at — so such a target goes FORWARD along the figure's heading onto the reach; and off a planted foot the knee's first centimetres of HEIGHT ease in (h × smoothstep(h / 15cm)): a thigh hanging straight down gains height as the square of its swing, and matched from the first centimetre the knee had 25cm to travel in the cursor's first eight. The target leaves the planted projection only over the hold's last quarter (or the cursor's whole pull went through a still-hard leg into the hips: 3cm onto the other foot's toes). The foot leaves the SUPPORT by the plant, the balance base measured without it (as for a dragged foot). On all eight rigs: a knee swung 12cm out travels 11.7-12.0cm and one pushed 15cm forward 13.6-13.9 with the ankle 0.04mm from its spot; a 35cm raise gains 35.0cm with the ankle 4-19mm off its hang and the hips 1-9mm from where they were; raised and brought back down, the foot is 0.0001mm from its spot and the head where it began. Probes: `IK_JS_NO_KNEE_DRAG`, `IK_KNEE_LIFTS_FOOT` (the rig's old release), `IK_JS_KNEEHANG`, `IK_JS_KNEEHEADING`, `IK_JS_KNEEPROJ`, `IK_JS_KNEELIFTBLEND`, `IK_JS_KNEE_TRACE` (the goal after its projections, and the letting-go foot's spring, hang, targets and ankle per tick), the harness's `IK_HARNESS_EULER_TICKS="lThigh,lShin,..."` (those bones' channels EVERY tick — how a leg gets where it ends). **DRAGGING AN ELBOW** (`elbowDrag`: a fold joint that is not a knee; its limb END, the wrist, is found down the chain of main children past the forearm's twist bones — the first bone with two free channels — and its SOCKET up the chain the same way, past the upper arm's). What an elbow is grabbed FOR is its swivel — elbows out, in, tucked, a hand kept on a hip — so the HAND tends to stay where it is: a soft hold of the wrist's place as the drag found it (`kElbowHandHoldWeight` 300/m²: two decades over the arm's posture terms, well under the cursor's pull, so the hand follows wherever the forearm's length says it must; ONE soft row on that joint, lesson eight), which comes WITH THE ARM'S BEND as found (none under 10° of elbow flexion, in full from 40°: a STRAIGHT arm has no swivel — its elbow lies on the line from shoulder to hand — and held, its elbow dragged 10cm inward went nowhere, the fold bounded at straight, and 15cm forward ran the collar to two of its limits; there the elbow carries its arm, as it always did) and goes as the target leaves the forearm's length of that place (10 → 30cm). A pin below the elbow holds in full: a USER pin (the rig lets a pin `kEffectorPinHoldReach` 20cm or more down the dragged joint's OWN limb hold — it used to sit the drag out like a pinned toe under a dragged foot — so a pinned hand is what the elbow swivels about: 0.0005mm on all rigs; 6-DoF, so the wrist's range bounds the swivel), or a hand ON THE FLOOR (seeded as a live contact below the dragged joint and made HARD: unilateral, nothing was cheaper for the solve than lifting the hand 2cm and carrying it — on all fours an elbow swung out 6cm took its hand 5cm along); the elbow's target then lives within the forearm's length of it. The elbow itself lives on the upper arm's REACH about the socket as the drag found it, and a cursor drags in a plane: a target INSIDE the reach could only be met by moving the socket, and was — the collar shrugged 46° to raise an elbow 20cm while the shoulder turned 25° the other way. It goes onto the reach along ITS OWN DIRECTION from the socket (the nearest point: the elbow goes the way it is dragged; outward for every inside target was tried, and an elbow dragged inward went outward), turned OUTWARD from the body's midline by as much as the drag is an UPWARD pull (an arm hanging beside the body has its elbow all but under its socket, where "up" is straight at the socket: pulled up, an elbow lifts out to the side), eased over the first 10cm of depth; a target up to 6cm BEYOND the reach comes back onto it too (a cursor dragged sideways leaves the sphere at once, and the 3cm it was outside after an 8cm swing were 3cm of TRUNK lean; past the band the excess goes through and takes the body with it), so an elbow swung outward travels its ARC (out and up). The shoulder GIRDLE above the socket (the collar) costs ×20 under an elbow drag (`kElbowGirdleStiffness`: at its plain price it did a third of every elbow move). On all eight rigs: a bent arm's elbow swung 8cm out travels 4.7-7.3cm of arc with the hand 14-38mm from its place and the socket 0.6-2.4mm from its; raised 20cm beside the body the elbow gains 19.7-20.1cm with the socket 2.5-3.1mm from where it was (it rode up 10cm on the shrug) and the head 1-2mm; on all fours the hand stays within 0.0003mm (10mm on the oldest rig). Probes: `IK_JS_NO_ELBOW_DRAG`, `IK_JS_ELBOWHOLD`, `IK_JS_ELBOWGIRDLE`. **THE HIP SWAY** (`swayValid` in `solveIk`, pelvis drags only, not while rising): a pelvis dragged SIDEWAYS over its standing feet ROLLS — the side it goes toward comes up. Each standing leg says how high its hip socket may sit over its foot (straight: sqrt(length² − the socket's horizontal distance from the ankle²)); a sway takes one socket over its foot and the other away from its, and the root's roll REFERENCE takes up the difference: the least-squares slope of those changes across the pelvis (sockets and the stance as the drag found them, the stance walked by `stanceShift`), clamped at asin 0.35, turned about the figure's fore axis on top of the rotation the drag began with — and only as far as a leg has RUN OUT: the roll is there so that no leg has to be longer than it is, and fades out as the shortest-handed standing leg has 3-10cm of length in hand where the cursor takes the hips (`kSwayLegInHandFrom/Gone`). In a SPLIT stance — one foot a stride behind — hips taken down and back change the two legs' straight heights by different amounts with no sway at all, and a lunge came out with the pelvis rolled 14°, the front foot rolled to its limit and the front knee 32cm across the midline. The price alone could not do it — a knee is the cheapest joint in the body (1/range²), so at any roll price the leg the hips went over BENT, 12° for an 8cm sway, under a level pelvis, and the other stood straight on a lifted heel: contrapposto backwards. And the CHEST stays level: the spine chain (from the root up through the child with the biggest foot-free subtree, to the first bone three big branches leave) joins the unknowns with ONE row on the chest — its roll about the fore axis held as found (`kSwayChestLevelWeight` 1000/rad², a per-axis row with only its level weight set) — so the spine takes up 97% of the roll; riding the rolled pelvis rigidly the torso leaned away from the sway like a mast, the head back over where the hips had been. Under 1e-4 of slope nothing is added (a plain crouch, a kneel, a sit are bit-identical). How much roll there IS to take up is the STANCE's: the main family stands wide (a socket 12cm inside its foot) and rolls 7° for 8cm with both knees straight (the near one bent 12° before); the generations that stand with their feet under their hips pass OVER the near foot, both legs shorten, and 1-2° is all there is — their near knee gives 4°, as it did. Brought back, she stands as she stood (head 0.1mm). The hip walks steady a little with it (knee tremble 15 → 11 and 32 → 24mm). Probes: `IK_JS_NO_SWAY_ROLL`, `IK_JS_SWAYCHEST`. **THE HIP HINGE — balance under a pelvis drag** (`pelvisBalance` in `solveIk`): balance was simply OFF there ("the user is driving the pelvis"), and hips taken 10cm back left her bolt upright with her head 7cm behind her heels; a half squat sat back the same way. The hips are where the cursor puts them, so what takes the centre of mass back over the feet is the TRUNK: the balance row comes on with the spine chain (the sway's) among the unknowns — and the pelvis's own pitch, already one — FORE AND AFT ONLY (`Problem::balanceAxis`, the figure's heading: sideways is the sway's, and on the generations that stand with their feet together an 8cm sway takes the centre of mass off the support — a sideways lean against the sway's level chest trembled 8cm). It is tightly scoped, each gate from a measured failure: drags that BEGAN STANDING (by the height the legs had left at the press: in full under 2cm of it, none from 8 — a hinge let go of leaves the hips a few centimetres low, and as a switch at 2cm the next drag of the same hips had no hinge at all) and have NOT STEPPED (a walk's trunk swung back over each new stance: knee tremble 24 → 87mm — let go of as SLACK, `m_jsHingeRelease`: half a centimetre more a tick from the first step on, the row dropped for the drag at 40cm); the STANDING BAND of hip heights (in full from 85% of the standing height, none under 70%); not a DESCENT (the downward share of the hips' travel: in full under 55%, none from 80% — a kneel, a sit, a body going onto all fours is deliberately falling onto supports it does not have yet, and balanced over its feet it leaned back from the floor it was reaching for: the oldest generations' hands and knees never came down); only once the hips have gone 2-5cm ACROSS the floor (the share means nothing in a drag's first centimetres, and four ticks of balance there shifted when one generation's kneeling knees came down: a 3cm jump in its still hold); and not once the hips have LEFT THE STANCE (from 12cm off where the walked stance puts them the row's slack grows 1.5 for every metre further, `kPelvisBalanceStanceGain`: that is a walk, and a step answers it). **A hard row is let go of through its SLACK, never its weight**: faded as a weight over 12-18cm, the lean did nothing for nine tenths of the range and went in the last (a hard row's pose barely reads its weight until the posture terms rival it), and the walk's gate cut it outright in the tick a step began — hips taken 25cm back threw the head 16cm in one tick; under a growing slack a one-sided row gives the lean back at a steady pace (the head now 3-4cm a tick through that handover, what the step itself moves it), and the support-change ramp captures only the imbalance the hinge has not already let go of (counted twice, a changed support gave the rest back at once). The spine chain, once among a pelvis drag's unknowns, STAYS for the drag (`m_jsTrunkInSolve`: taken out the moment nothing pulled on it, its joints froze tenths of a degree short of home and a hinge brought back left the head 3mm out). On all eight rigs: hips 10cm back put the head 12-17cm AHEAD of the ankles (pelvis pitched 3°, the spine 7° at its base, the knees softened 9°), a half squat 9-15cm ahead, and brought back the head is 0.0000mm from where it began; every other pelvis-drag phase on the main family is bit-identical. Probe: `IK_JS_NO_PELVIS_BALANCE`. **THE TRUNK FOLLOW — a trunk joint dragged ACROSS the floor takes the body with it** (`m_jsTrunkFollow` in `solveIk`: an upper-body trunk joint — above the root, no foot below it: the chest, the neck, the head, a collar — dragged by a figure that began standing; not a knee's, an elbow's or a foot's drag, not once suspended). Left to the prices the hips stood still (4500/m² against the cursor's bounded pull is 2cm) and BALANCE walled the lean in — it, not the spine's price or the root's, was the limiter (balance off, the same chest reached its cursor; a root at 300/m² or a trunk at a quarter of its price changed the miss by a millimetre): the upper chest pulled 20cm back stopped 12cm short with the lower spine at its limits and the upper curled 30° FORWARD to counterweight it, 20cm forward 5 short, the neck 8, the head 4 — and no step ever answered, the rig's trigger for a trunk drag being a miss of 12cm (the solve holds the centre of mass to a centimetre of its support, so the balance-effort trigger cannot fire). Now the first of the travel is a LEAN and past it the hips FOLLOW: their REFERENCE moves (the general rule again: a price can only prefer less travel), home = the stance as the rig has walked it + follow, where the travel is the grab's over that WALKED stance (once the feet have stepped under the body the chest stands over its hips again, the split measured afresh), follow = past − w·tanh(past / w) beyond a free share (`kTrunkLeanFree` 0.10 and `kTrunkLeanWidth` 0.20 of the grab's height over the hips, its lever), and the lean never more than BALANCE has room for the way the drag goes (`kTrunkLeanBalanced` 0.8 of the room × lever / the upper body's mass moment over the hips: 4cm behind a standing figure's weight, 10 ahead — a neck pulled 20cm back leaned into the support's edge and stopped 27mm short waiting for a lean that could not come; the room is what the drag may take before the body is WORSE balanced than it began — two generations stand with their feet together and their weight a little outside the inset support as posed, read "no room", and a 10cm head pull walked their hips 10cm). A followed drag is the user taking the body somewhere, as a pelvis drag is: the follow is let go of on the balance row as SLACK (the lean's share stays balanced; held to the support's edge the hips waited there for every step, the steps came 6cm long and a chest dragged at walking pace trailed 18cm behind), and the rig steps under where the hips are going (`updateStepPolicy`'s `bodyAnchor`) — a foot a stride from its spot, or the weight 2.5cm past the support for twelve ticks, which the slack makes possible again. Its premise is a stance that WALKS: with fewer than two steppable feet (a user-pinned foot: the last one never steps) or a body gone low the follow is bounded by the room balance has left and gets no slack (`m_jsTrunkWalk`; a moved reference is a spring without the cursor's bound — with a pinned foot under a 45cm chest drag it hauled the hips 30cm out over both planted feet, a side lunge on a foot twisted 12°); it stands down for a drag that is mostly a descent (a bow, a fold, a crouch by the chest keeps its hips over its feet) and WHILE rising (the rise has its own way with the hips) — but not for a drag that merely BEGAN low (it once did: `IK_JS_FOLLOW_STANDING_ONLY`): a low body takes no step, so there the follow is the bounded one, and what answers a lean that runs out of balance room is a HINGE — the hips back and UP as the trunk folds forward. The balance row and the cursor ask for it between them as soon as the pelvis is not held to its place: its horizontal AND vertical prices yield to `kTrunkCounterStiffness` 250 (`m_jsTrunkCounter`: (1 − walk) × how far the lean would carry the centre of mass past `kTrunkLeanBalanced` of its room, in full from 4cm). Held at 4500 and 1000, a crouching figure's chest dragged 15cm forward at its own height leaned into the support's edge 4cm short; with only the horizontal price yielded it hinged but wrung the spine to its twist limits — a folded trunk keeps its top up with a helix as well as with hips that rise 14cm, and at 1000/m² the helix was cheaper. `m_jsTrunkWalk` reads how low the body was when the drag BEGAN, a constant of the drag: read off the pose as it stands it FED BACK — the hinge raises the hips, a risen body "walks", a walking body's hips are asked forward and down again — and a chest held out of reach threw the figure between the two poses every tick (30cm jumps in a still hold). THE SPINE BENDS AS ONE (`kSpineCoupling` 80, the couplings above): each pair of neighbours in the spine chain — from the root up through the child with the biggest foot-free subtree to the first bone three big branches leave, `m_jsSpineChain` — is held, channel by channel, to the same SHARE of its range away from where the drag found it. Four independent hinges made an S-curve as cheap as any other way to put the chest where it was asked: that crouching figure flexed her lower spine 27° and EXTENDED the chest bone to its limit against it to keep the chest's height, and a chest pulled back curled the upper spine forward as a counterweight. THE NECK IS THE SPINE'S TOP (`kNeckCoupling` 80, the same rows): the rig's neck bones (`IkRig::neckBase()` up to the head's parent) are links of that chain whenever they are unknowns — a head or a neck drag. Priced as the dragged "limb" and uncoupled, the neck took a head drag ALONE, to its limits, and the rigs disagreed about which way: a head pulled 15cm back threw the newest generation's head back to the sky (both neck bones at their −25° and −27° limits, the gaze 52° up) while the reference figure flexed its neck 20° the other way and looked at the floor; 15cm sideways bent every neck to its 40° limit under a trunk leaned 3° (a head tilted 43°: a broken neck); a bow made by the head tucked the chin and stared back at her own feet. Coupled, a dragged head takes the BODY with it — she steps back under a head pulled back with her neck within a degree of straight on every rig, leans under one pulled sideways (neck 9-16°, head tilt 9-16°), crouches under one pushed down — and the eye grab's resting miss fell from 26 to 10mm. (A head is TURNED by rotating it — T and the mouse, or its Twist dial — and the turn is kept: levelling a dragged head outright, the head bone undoing the neck's bend too, was tried the same day and dropped.) `IK_JS_NO_NECK_COUPLING`, `IK_JS_NECKCOUPLING`. (As a unit the spine gives a hand pull a millimetre less: the gentle pull's hips go 10-11mm where they went 9-10; an arm raise moves the head 1mm less.) And A DRAG IN THE SAGITTAL PLANE KEEPS THE SPINE IN IT (`kSagittalLock` 20 on the spine's twist and side-bend channels while the trunk joint's travel has under 10-35% of sideways share): past what flexion could give, a chest dragged straight forward broke the symmetry instead — every twist and side-bend channel at its limit for 2cm more reach, entered in ONE tick, 22-29cm at the fingertips. The PELVIS BONE (the girdle joint between the root and the thigh sockets) is locked with the spine (`IK_JS_NO_PELVIS_SAGITTAL`): it is the trunk's other way out of the plane — once the active set's hair rule (the eleventh lesson) let an out-of-reach sit-up move at all, it bought its last centimetres with 10 degrees of pelvis twist and 10 of side bend, both legs folded over to one side under planted feet, which the side view of the in-app gallery could not show (the gallery now has a front shot and expects the pelvis bone's twist and side bend under 2 degrees; the reach is the same to a millimetre). REJECTED: the pelvis drag's fivefold PULL for trunk drags (with balance scaled to keep the support's edge where it is) — once the real blockers were gone it reached no further, it took a slouched head raised 15cm 6cm up onto her toes, and with a foot pinned a far chest drag turned the planted one 10-12°. And A FOOT IN A STEP STAYS IN THE SOLVE'S SUPPORT where its swing has it (not under a pelvis drag, whose balance is the hinge's): a step is a fall caught by the foot that takes it, and balanced over the standing foot alone by a hard row the centre of mass was hauled 10cm sideways in the twelve ticks of a swing — the spine twisted to its limits, the arms swinging 20cm a tick (neither end of a step re-keys the balance ramp). On all eight rigs: the upper chest 20cm back, forward or sideways and the head 20cm back rest on the cursor (0.02 / 0.08 / 0.01-7 / 0.01mm) with the hips 11-19cm along, a mean drag lag of 2.7mm and 0-3 steps; the 45cm stepping chest drag arrives (11-12cm short before on the main family, 3 on the others) with its knee twist 15° → 0.3° and knee tremble 44 → 8mm; a 6cm excursion and home stands her as she stood (0.0005mm). Probes: `IK_JS_NO_TRUNK_FOLLOW`, `IK_JS_NO_SWING_SUPPORT`, `IK_JS_FOLLOW_TRACE`. **THE BOW** (`m_jsTrunkHinge`, `leanArc` in `solveIk`; upper-body trunk drags that BEGAN STANDING — a share, 1 − smoothstep(2cm, 8cm, the height the legs had left)): the chest or the head taken DOWN as it goes forward folds her — the pelvis pitching over planted feet, the spine curling with it, the hips back under the weight — and she takes no step. Three things were missing. (1) The trunk follow read every forward travel as a walk: what a trunk of this length reaches forward of its hips at the target's HEIGHT (sqrt(reach² − height²), over what it reached at the height it began; `kBowArcShare` 0.75 of it (0.85 until the idle arms hung: see IDLE ARMS HANG), a curling spine being shorter than its chord; less what the hips give back to balance, (arc + spare room)/(1 + moment/lever)) is now the lean's for free, for a drag that goes FORWARD (none under 45° off the figure's heading, all within 23°: `kBowArcSagittalFrom/Full` — taken down and well off to one side she walks, as she did); and UNDONE the same way — a trunk that began folded and is taken back UP comes back over its hips as it rises, and that much of its travel toward them is the fold opening, not a body taken backward (read as one, a bow let go of and the chest then taken back up walked the hips' home 12cm back: 25mm short, the pelvis still pitched 10°). (2) The root never rotated under a trunk drag: its PITCH — the channel whose axis is lateral, nothing else — joins the unknowns at `kTrunkHingeStiffness` 20/rad² over the share, and is one more link of the spine's coupling chain, below the first spine bone (`kTrunkHingeRangeDeg` 90 as its "range": the trunk folds from the HIPS as it curls). Priced alone the split was the lever's: a chest bow curled the spine 66° over a pelvis pitched 8 (a hunch), a head bow pitched the pelvis 26° under a spine bent 1° a joint (a plank); and without the coupling a bow let go of on the oldest generation, whose bow lowers the hips into the rise's band, stood back up with its pelvis still pitched 15° under a spine extended to its limit against it (the coupling holds while she rises too). (3) The counter-hinge (`m_jsTrunkCounter`) was the non-walking stance's alone: it now reads what the LEAN — the travel less the follow's share — takes the centre of mass past its room, whatever the stance (a level drag from standing keeps it at zero by construction: the lean cap is the balance room's), and is computed whether or not a follow is. (4) **A BOW IS A FOLD, NOT A WALK** (2026-09-23; `kTrunkFollowDownFrom/Gone` 0.3-0.55, `IK_JS_FOLLOW_DOWN_FROM/GONE`): the follow's "not a body on its way down" gate read the pelvis hinge's 0.55-0.80 (set for hip drags that fall onto supports), and the chest bow — down a third of its lever, forward four fifths, a 0.38 downward share — kept a walk's whole follow, 5.8cm, let go of as SLACK on the balance row: the weight rested that far past the toes (the old soft spot), the effort trigger confirmed, and a step needs a foot `kStepMinDistance` 6cm from its anchored spot — the base rig read 5.9 and kept its feet, a top-heavy toon (the sweep's last real misses) 6.03 and took two steps: a knife edge on every rig. At the trunk follow's own gate the bow's follow is 4.4cm, the toon keeps her feet 1.5cm inside the edge, and the chest rests 2.6mm from its cursor on every rig (the follow cut outright, 0.2-0.5, left it 29-30mm short: that far forward and down needs the weight past the toes or a step, and the design chose the weight); the bow folds a touch deeper (pelvis +1.5°, the hips further back under the counter). (5) **THE UNFOLD BRINGS THE HIPS UNDER** (`m_jsUnfoldShift`, `kUnfoldFromDeg/FullDeg` 10-25°, `IK_JS_NO_UNFOLD`): the counter takes the hips BACK under the weight, the next drag begins with them there as its posture reference, and nothing brought them forward again — a bow let go of and the chest taken back up stood her up leaning 10° at the hips (5 before the follow was cut), the trunk reaching a chest target the hips stood behind. A trunk that comes MORE UPRIGHT than the drag found it brings its hips under its target as it does — by the bind's offset of the grabbed joint over the root, turned to the heading, by how much of the found tilt the target undoes; standing starts only (a kneeling trunk's target is not over its hips — the kneel-up's seat lift — and a seat rocks on its pin). The two-drag return's head: 6-9mm on all eight rigs (7-21 before). On all eight rigs: the upper chest down a third of its lever and forward four fifths rests 0.01-0.09mm from the cursor with no step (159mm short and 3 steps on the base rig before), the pelvis pitched 17-21° over spine joints flexed 14-20° each; by the head (down a sixth, forward a half) 0.01-0.02mm, pelvis 12-14°, spine 5-7°; the bow and back in one drag returns the head to 0.0003mm; let go of and taken back up, the pelvis is within 1.6° and every spine joint within 1.6° of upright. Probes: `IK_JS_NO_TRUNK_HINGE`, `IK_JS_NO_BOW_ARC`, `IK_JS_NO_BOW_COUNTER`, `IK_JS_TRUNKHINGE`, `IK_JS_TRUNKHINGERANGE`, `IK_JS_BOWARC`. **IDLE ARMS HANG** (`Armature::hangIdleArms`, a POST-STEP of every drag tick, after the solve and before the contacts read the pose). An arm nothing has hold of — not the dragged limb, no pin or floor contact in it — that HUNG at the press (the upper arm within 60° of straight down, `kArmHangCone`, the elbow bent under 35°; an A-pose arm stands 46° off the vertical and counts) keeps hanging as the trunk moves: the shoulder's free channels are turned, `kArmHangStepDeg` 4° a tick at most, so the upper arm keeps the ELEVATION the drag found it at — its direction carried round with the trunk's HEADING (the parent's lateral axis, levelled), so a body that turns takes its arms round with it and one that folds, leans, sits or kneels does not take them up with it. Riding the chest rigidly, as every idle limb does, a bowing figure held both arms stiffly back along her body, hands above her hips like a ski jumper's — the first thing the in-app screenshots showed (tools/ikscripts). Posed arms — raised, akimbo, a hand on a hip — are not hanging and ride as they did. (Under a trunk that has TILTED since the press the arms hang PLUMB, and an elbow softens as its hand nears the floor: see ONTO ALL FOURS.) The turn is a Gauss-Newton step on the DIRECTION itself over the free channels, from where they stand: no Euler angles are read off a matrix (an all-fours pre-pose has the shoulder's middle channel at −90°, its gimbal lock: the extraction came back on another branch, the locked twist channel's share was dropped, and the arm flew up over her back), and it is stopped at the body volumes and the floor like any FK rotation (`fkVolumeDepth`, bisected). THE STOP IS ORDER-INDEPENDENT (2026-09-21; `IK_ARM_HANG_IN_TURN` is the old one): every arm's turn is found first, on the pose as the solve left it; each arm's fraction is then measured against a body that is otherwise AS THE SOLVE LEFT IT (the arm is put back before the next is measured), all are applied, and if two arms that each cleared the other where it stood have turned INTO each other they give way together. Turned and stopped one after the other, the second arm was measured against the first where the loop had just left it, and two arms that touch — an all-fours pose whose hands meet under the chest — came down lopsided: one hand 4cm ahead of the other and planted a tick sooner, on six of the eight reference rigs (0.0-0.3mm now). NOT part of the solve — three attempts inside it were backed out: a soft hold of the elbow's place pulled against the cursor; a posture reference for the shoulder's whole world rotation asks the bend bone for a twist its locked channel cannot make; a reference for its direction was right, but any unknown is a lever for the balance row, and at a limb's price both arms were swung 50° back as counterweights. And BALANCE IS BLIND TO THE HANG (`Armature::balancePositions`: the pose's joints with every hung arm put back where it would ride — the solver's `Problem::comShift`, the support-change slack capture and the rig's step policy all read it): hanging arms carry a tenth of her weight 15cm further forward in a bow, and weighed there every fold met balance's wall 2-5cm sooner (the bow 36mm behind its cursor, a crouching lean 34, an un-bow left pitched) for a centimetre and a half of centre of mass — half the support's inset margin. The arms are for the eye; balance keeps the books it kept.
