The solve's VOLUME rows skip a hanging arm's joints (it is none of the solve's unknowns, so such a row could only move the BODY away from the arm: a chest pulled sideways on a narrow-stanced rig, the hanging arm brushing the hip, threw the head 10cm in a tick); its FLOOR rows stay (exempt, a body coming down took its hanging fingers 4-7cm through the floor before the hand's contact formed). What is left of the hang in the numbers: the chest bow rests 4-5mm from its cursor on the main family (the solve's gradients see the hung arms; the bow sat at nine tenths of the cursor's bounded pull before) — hence `kBowArcShare` 0.75 and an 8mm gate. Probes: `IK_NO_ARM_HANG`, `IK_ARM_HANG_TRACE`. **THE HEAD STAYS UP** (`Armature::rightIdleHead`, the second POST-STEP of a drag tick, after the arms' hang). The neck and the head rode the chest like any idle limb, so a figure leaned or hinged 20° forward looked at the floor, one walked forward by her chest arrived staring at her feet, and a kneel or a sit bowed her head with her back. The head is RIGHTED: the chest's tilt from upright (every bind is translation-only, so a bone's world rotation IS its rotation away from the bind) is given back by C·tanh(tilt / C), C = `kHeadRightDeg` 30° — all but a few degrees of a 20° lean, 27-29° of a deep bow (she looks at the floor ahead of her, as a bowing person does), fading out from 70° to 110° of tilt (on all fours or lying down a neck is not held up against the trunk). The head's TARGET is that righting applied to the chest's rotation, times the head's rotation in the chest's righted frame AS THE DRAG FOUND IT (`m_jsHeadIntrinsic`: what the user posed the head to, over and above the righting) — a function of the trunk's pose alone, so nothing pops at the press, a drag brought home brings the head home (gaze 0.0000° off), TWO DRAGS COMPOSE (a bow let go of and undone in another drag ends with a level head), and a head the user turned down 20° looks 24° down in a lean and 20° again when she is brought back. The chain's channels (`IkRig::neckBase()` … `headNode()`, found by name with the body volumes) come from a small regularized least-squares of their own — the head's rotation held at the target (1e4), each channel pulled to its drag-start value by 1/range² (range floored at 20°, capped at 60°) — solved in its 3×3 dual, limits as an active set, so the split between the neck's bones is their ranges' and the answer does not depend on the path; the move is rate-limited (`kHeadRightStepDeg` 3° a tick). It works with the chain bones the SOLVE does not have (`m_jsHeadFree`: a bone is one of the solve's unknowns when a child of it is ACTIVE) — under a head drag that is the head bone alone, whose own channels do nothing for the grabbed joint's place — and what it gives back there is the TRUNK's tilt, never the neck's own bend: the target is the righted chest times the chain as it would RIDE, the solve's bones as the solve has them and the righting's own at their drag-start values (`m_jsHeadIntrinsic` is the drag-start righting undone, in the chest's frame), so a bow made BY THE HEAD looks at the floor ahead of her (gaze −46°; riding a neck flexed 30° she stared back at her own feet, −70°). A pin or a floor contact in the head stands it down. BALANCE IS BLIND to it as to the hang — to the bones the righting HAS, and only those (`balancePositions` puts them back at their drag-start rotations; marked by the whole chain, as it was for a day, a head drag's balance weighed the head where the neck had NOT taken it; `balanceIsBlind()` says when either post-step has the pose away from riding). On all eight rigs: hips 10cm back leave the gaze 4-7° under the horizon (23-29° riding), a bow by the upper chest has the neck give back 27-29° with no sideways tilt. Probes: `IK_NO_HEAD_RIGHT`, `IK_HEAD_RIGHT_DEG`, `IK_HEAD_RIGHT_NO_FADE`, `IK_HEAD_RIGHT_NOT_BLIND`, `IK_HEAD_RIGHT_TRACE`. (And the arms' hang no longer takes FINGERS for arms: a finger's middle joint is a fold joint too, and until the trace showed them each finger was found "hanging" and turned at its knuckle — `kArmHangMinLength` 12cm, socket to fold joint.) **THE FLOOR STOPS HER FLESH** (`IkRig::fitFloorClearances`, `setBoneRotations`, `floorClearanceAlong`; `IK_FLOOR_NO_MESH`, `IK_FLOOR_TRACE`). The joints above the feet had ONE floor clearance, 1.5cm — "a flesh radius" — for a pelvis whose joint sits 20cm over the seat of the buttocks, a chest 13cm inside the back, a skull, a knee: a figure sat down on the floor went on down until her hip JOINT was 1.5cm over it, 8cm and more of flesh through it (the in-app floor gallery's `meshlow`, the skin's lowest point: no joint reading shows skin), and — since a joint is a floor CONTACT only within a centimetre of its clearance — her pelvis never was one. A joint's clearance is now how far ITS OWN FLESH reaches below it as the bone is turned: the SUPPORT FUNCTION of the skin its bone dominates (the body mesh sample the volumes are fitted to), about the joint, along the world's down in the bone's frame — max over the bone's skin of (v − joint)·down; every bind is translation-only, so the bind frame is the world's. Kept per bone are the skin's EXTREME points along 128 directions; the Armature hands the rig the bones' rotations before a drag and at the top of every tick (`syncRigRotations`), so a clearance is a constant of the tick and every reader — the solve's floor rows, the contact detection, the target clamp, the hang's stop — reads `floorClearance(i)` as before. Direction matters: from the pelvis the flesh reaches 10cm forward, 14 back and 21 straight down. Three lessons, each from a version the harness threw out the same day: (1) ONE number (the thinnest of 26 cones) either sinks a sit 10cm or hovers a figure on her back; (2) the joint's own AND its parent's skin read in the CHILD's frame is wrong the moment the joint bends (a sit held 6cm in the air); (3) "rigid with its bone" is false of the skin a bone dominates PAST its far joint and useless for CONTACTS along its length — a standing shin's flesh reaches the ankle, and answered for whole it made the knee a floor "contact" 49cm up, planted hard the moment the hips came down (no kneel at all on the one-bone thighs): a bone with exactly one child that carries real skin is a SEGMENT and answers for the near `kSegmentNear` 0.6 of its span, the child and the limb's end for the rest; the pelvis, chest, head and hands keep all of theirs; a joint with no skin of its own beside one that has (the root, 2cm from the pelvis bone) takes its neighbour's. Foot-class joints keep their bind heights — the standing contact model is what it was — and nothing is less than the old radius. WITH IT: **the seat is a contact** — a joint of the pelvis girdle resting on the floor is seeded, and detected, as a live contact under any drag but the pelvis's own, whatever the lift reach of the dragged joint says (the chest has the pelvis inside its "own limb's" reach); under a PELVIS drag the root and its girdle are never contacts (they go where the cursor takes them: planted, a thigh socket's hold fought the cursor and one generation's sit tipped the pelvis 72° back); and **balance stands down while the pelvis rests on the floor** (`IkRig::floorSeat`, `IK_NO_FLOOR_SEAT`: there is nowhere to fall from a sit on the floor, and balanced she would not recline at all — the chest 49cm from its cursor). Before: a sitting figure stood, to the rig, on her feet alone, 40cm out of balance, and reclined by the chest her arms were flung out as counterweights the tick her hands touched down — 70cm at the fingertips in ONE tick, 48, 24 and 11 in the next three. Now she leans back onto her hands (worst single-tick move 38-48mm; 99 at a fingertip on the newest generation), her skin 4-9mm OVER the floor in a sit, a recline and a kneel where it was 9-77mm under. Two smaller things the same audit found: **a bone a POST-STEP has turned is referred to where the post-step LEFT it** when the solve takes it over (`m_jsPostStepEuler`: a hanging arm whose hand lands, a righted neck under a lift — referred to its drag-start value it was snapped back), balance being blind only to the arms the hang HAS (`hangArmHeld`); and **a rising body UNLOADS its floor contacts with the rise** (`m_jsRootRise` joins the live contact's six-decade fade, `IK_JS_NO_RISE_UNLOAD`): read off the joint's own rise the hold is a switch with a memory — it kept a kneeling knee down until the rising hips left it no way to stay within 2cm of the floor, then let go of all of it at once: the knee swung 16cm in one tick on a character rig getting up out of a kneel (2-9cm on the others); now 2-7cm on all eight. **THE SEAT ROLLS — laying her down and sitting her up** (`seatRollDeg` / `seatRollLift` in `solveIk`, `m_jsFloorSeatStart`, `m_jsSeatNode`, `m_jsSeatRolls`; 2026-09-21, the user: "her hip stays pointed up"). A figure sitting on the floor, leaned back by the chest, arched her spine to its limits over a pelvis still 40° from the floor and stopped with her chest 35cm up. A price can only prefer less travel — at the bow's price the pelvis gave a recline a third of it and the spine, a tenth the price joint for joint, the rest — so, for a drag that BEGAN with the pelvis resting on the floor (a constant of the drag, read where the rig seeds the pose's contacts: never a mode off the pose it changes) and has hold of an upper-body trunk joint, the root's pitch REFERENCE turns with the cursor about the seat: by the angle the line from the hips to the target has swept, in the figure's sagittal plane, since the drag began — swept about the seat AS ROLLED (the hip joint rests 14cm over the floor lying on her back, 24 sitting: a fixed point over `IkRig::floorClearanceAlong` of the SEAT NODE, the pelvis bone, three rounds), none of it for a target within a third of the lever of the seat, all from two thirds; the root's HEIGHT reference goes with it (`rootHome.y += seatRollLift`: left at the height found, the vertical price of a body that stands held a figure sitting up 7cm down against the floor's own rows), and the spine's coupling to the pelvis is measured from the ROLLED pitch, so the trunk goes down all of a piece. A function of the target alone. With it: "upper body" is the BIND's statement for such a drag (a lying figure's chest is level with her hips: no hinge, no roll, and she was brought up by a spine curled to its limits); **a trunk joint is to the seat what a hand is to its shoulder** — taken up, the body RISES only by what the trunk cannot give about the seat at its sitting height (`IK_JS_SEAT_RISES`), a figure found more than 45° from upright is SAT UP, never stood up, by that drag (out of a lying pose the rise freed the pelvis and drew the hips in under a chest still behind them: 16-20cm pops at the head), and an UPRIGHT sit taken up gets no roll at all (getting up is the rise's from its first centimetre; a roll that came and went with the handover stalled one generation's getting-up three ticks and let go of it in one, 14cm). **HER ARMS ARE NO PROPS in such a drag** (`IkRig::m_seatedTrunkDrag`: no arm joint is seeded or detected as a contact — a hand held to its spot under a shoulder on its way to the floor gripped and let go on alternate ticks, 10-20cm at the fingertips; a hand that must stay is PINNED — and a contact that formed by itself on an arm that hung is remembered across drags, `m_incidentalContact` / `IkRig::setIncidentalContacts`); EVERY idle arm is the hang's, bent or not, and keeps OFF THE FLOOR: its wanted direction is turned from the hang toward the one it would RIDE the chest at — along the floor toward her feet as she goes down — by just as much as clears it, each bone's clearance read AS TURNED (`kArmHangFloorMargin` 2cm, `IK_NO_ARM_HANG_FLOOR`), and an arm FOUND lying beside her wants to hang by her side again (`HangArm::wants`: down, a little out — kept as found, a figure sat up from her back held both arms straight out in front of her, which no number showed and the first rendered frame did); the hang's arms and the righted head are exempt from the solve's floor rows meanwhile (rows only the body can answer). On all eight rigs: laid down, the pelvis ends 72-92° back with no spine joint arched a degree, the chest 3-26mm over where its back rests, worst single-tick move 15-53mm, hold 0.2-2.6mm; sat back up, the pelvis rolls 64-87° up again and the chest comes up at least three quarters of the way. Dragged past the trunk's reach of the seat she SLIDES along the floor on it (a live contact slips at 2.5cm), her feet staying. Probes: `IK_JS_NO_SEAT_ROLL`, `IK_JS_SEATROLL`, `IK_NO_FLOOR_SEAT_ROCK`, `IK_JS_SEAT_TRACE`, `IK_SEED_HANGING_HANDS`. **AND THE FLOOR'S ROWS ARE ON THE FLESH ITSELF** (`IkRig::fleshPoints`, the plane source in `solveIk`; `IK_JS_NO_FLESH_ROWS`): a clearance is a constant of the tick — right for a pelvis, a limit cycle for a HAND, whose wrist turned 40° in a tick under a forearm coming down, had another clearance the next, was thrown back up and turned back (solves out of iterations, 12-16cm at the fingertips). A real-mass bone's rows are now on up to four of its flesh's lowest points, well apart (one row is a pivot; the lowest ALONE of a pelvis's two buttocks is a rounding error's choice, a symmetric sit solved askew), MIRROR-CLOSED (2026-09-21: the directions a bone's extreme skin points are taken along are a Fibonacci lattice of 64 AND ITS MIRROR IMAGE across the sagittal plane — a lattice alone is not mirror-symmetric, so neither were the points it found on a symmetric pelvis, nor a left thigh's the mirror of the right's — and on a centre bone a point taken brings its mirror TWIN with it: four taken lowest-first from a symmetric set are left, right, a midline point and then a LEFT one whose twin no longer fits, and pressed hard into the floor the odd row out is a roll — a sit pushed 12cm past the seat ended with both knees 5-7cm to one side on two generations, 0.0000mm now; `IK_JS_FLESH_NO_TWINS`), rigid with the bone, so the model knows what turning it does; the per-tick clearance stays for every other reader and is re-read off the SOLVED pose before the contacts are (`syncRigRotations` twice a tick). The SEAT's rise is over where its flesh rests NOW (`IkRig::liveContactRise`: rolled over the buttocks the hip joint goes 3.5cm up, resting all the while — read as a rise its hold came and went on alternate ticks), and every live contact's fade is EASED over the ticks (`m_jsContactGone`, 0.35 a tick, `IK_JS_NO_FADE_EASE`): read off the last solve's pose it is a switch thrown a tick late, a relaxation oscillator under a held sit. The arms' hang no longer turns a three-channel shoulder about ITSELF (the twist channel is out of its step, which is damped at 3e-5: under a deep fold one generation's arm was twisted 78°, jammed a swing channel into its limit and stood out sideways, stepping ±4° a tick; `IK_ARM_HANG_TWISTS`), and its floor stop reads clearances as turned. **A KNEE LANDS WHERE IT LANDS** (2026-09-21; `kKneeLandingHold`, `kKneelFootHold`, `kKneelTiltScale`, `IkRig::reseatLivePin`). The in-app galleries' last POP, in two of them: a kneel dragged the way a user drags it — a few centimetres deeper and shorter than the rig's own — threw the hips 20cm down and back in the ONE tick its knees landed, and left them there. The rig's HARD knee pin (a knee over its planted foot, going down) was nailed, in full, to the spot the knee HOVERED over when it crossed the 5cm landing band, and brought straight down there — which a tucked foot already at its ankle's limits cannot follow: the knee's pin and the ball's, two 1e8 rows a shin apart, pulled the leg apart with 47,000 against the cursor's 1,000 (`IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE`), and the body gave way to relieve it. Three things, each from that trace: (1) a knee COMING DOWN is held hard in its HEIGHT — what brings it onto the floor — and only softly (1e4/m²) in its place until it has landed, then planted where it IS (free altogether it drifted 12cm inward on 25° of thigh twist); (2) **the foot below a planted knee keeps its place softly** (1e4; its height in full): the knee is that leg's support, a kneeling person's toes are not nailed down, and the two hard pins' residual was leaking into the one thing that moves both — the pelvis's pitch (the ball's rows asked +195 of it, the knee's −148); (3) **on planted knees the pelvis's TILT is dear** — the root's rotation and the PELVIS BONE's (`IkRig::isPelvisBone`: the girdle joint, other than the root, that BOTH LEGS hang from — one anatomical thing with the root. NOT "a girdle joint with two children", which is what it was taken for until 2026-09-21: the newest generation's THIGHS carry their twist helpers as children beside the shin, were priced like a pelvis on planted knees — hip flexion at 800 beside the pelvis's 800, `IK_JS_POSE_TRACE` — and a kneeling figure of that whole generation, sat back onto her heels, reclined 41 degrees with her head tipped up instead of folding at the hips: root −20, pelvis bone −21, hips flexed 9 where every other generation flexes 50. Found by LOOKING at the in-app floor gallery on a custom character; the `kneel-heels` phase gates it on every rig: the pelvis 4-8 degrees back, the hips folding 15-27. The sagittal lock's and the seat rock's pelvis tests are the same query) cost `kKneelTiltScale` 5 times the root's price under a pelvis drag, eased in a seventh a tick: hips pushed lower than rigid thighs allow over knees that are ON THE FLOOR cannot go there (a kneeling body gets lower by sitting BACK), and against a pull five times a limb's the missing 7cm were found by tipping the pelvis 27° at the root and 25 more, its limit, at the pelvis bone, under an arched back — which no gate measured and the first rendered frame showed. (A GLOBAL pitch price was tried first: at 240 every generation's SIT, whose pelvis must roll back, stopped 20cm short; a reach clamp on the hips' target and a friction-like knee hold did nothing — the tilt was never about reach or sliding.) On all eight rigs the user-sized kneel lands with a worst single-tick move of 38-77mm (it read 209), a still hold, and 7-16° of pelvis tilt (52); sitting back onto the heels from it no longer pops (161 → 7mm in-app) or splays the thighs 75°. Probes: `IK_JS_KNEE_LANDING_HELD`, `IK_JS_KNEELANDHOLD`, `IK_JS_KNEEL_FOOT_HARD`, `IK_JS_KNEELFOOTHOLD`, `IK_JS_KNEELTILT`, `IK_JS_NO_PELVIS_BONE_PRICE` (the rejected friction hold's probe, `IK_JS_KNEEFRICTION`, went with its code in the 2026-09-24 refactoring round). **FIGURES STAND ON THE FLOOR — and the rig's thresholds are the BODY's, not centimetres** (2026-09-21; the user: a custom character "can't kneel, and her hip goes into an unnatural position"). The rig reads the floor off the figure's own bind: a foot-class joint's floor clearance IS its bind height, the ball is the joint under `kBallHeightFraction` 0.45 of the ankle's height, foot-class is low at bind. A character whose morphs leave her soles 3cm over y = 0 has toes at 0.56 of her ankle's height: NO BALL was found, her ankles were pinned rigidly (no heel lift, no tucked toes), her kneel stopped with the knees in the air and the pelvis tipped to find the missing depth — 23 harness failures on her dump. Every figure is now grounded at import (the figure-import paragraph), and her dump passes with 4 near-misses; she kneels, knees on the floor and torso upright, and sits. Three more of the same kind, each found by the character sweep (the Build section): **(1) a pelvis drag's DOWN intent ends when the target comes back up** (`IkRig::updateIntent`, `m_lowestPelvisTarget`: down while the target is 2cm under its drag-start height AND within `kRiseAgain` 3cm of the lowest it has been; `IK_DOWN_INTENT_STICKS`) — it was the first test alone, so a short character brought down onto all fours and taken back up in ONE drag kept her hard knee pins (made while going down) all the way up and could not rise; **(2) the knee-landing rule is the KNEE's** (`IkRig::kneeOver(joint, pinned)`: the joint is the real joint the pinned node hangs from, the pin below is a standing FOOT's and not itself a live contact, and the joint is COMING DOWN — `m_lastApplied`, last tick's pose): on a grounded figure a thigh-twist bone, and the knee of a figure sitting back onto her heels, met the old test — any joint over a planted node within the 5cm band — and were planted hard in mid-air (a 120mm pop at the shin in the in-app floor gallery); **(3) a user pin below the dragged joint holds from 20cm OR `kEffectorPinHoldShare` 0.28 of its limb's length, whichever is less** (`IK_PIN_HOLD_FIXED_REACH`): a toon's forearm is shorter than 20cm, and her pinned hand sat out every elbow drag — 24mm of "slip" that was the pin being ignored; on ordinary proportions nothing changes (a hand under its elbow is 0.37 of the limb, a fingertip under its hand 0.20, a toe under its foot 0.14). THE RULE: a length in the rig's policy is a statement about ONE body; say it as a share of the limb, the stance or the figure it is about, and check it on the sweep. **ONTO ALL FOURS — a kneel, then the chest taken forward and down** (2026-09-22; `IkRig::onKnees`, `m_jsKneelStart`, `kKneelFoldStiffness`, `kKneelFoldRangeDeg`, the landed-hand rows in `solveIk`, `m_jsContactRise`, the contact settle in `IkRig::updateContacts`). Until this round there was no gesture for it: hands pushed to the floor ahead of her were answered by a deep squat, and from a kneel the chest stalled 26-30cm short of the cursor — the spine curled to its 35° limit over a pelvis that never pitched, the hands 19cm up. Seven things, each from a trace or a rendered frame, and the last four from the same all-fours drag failing four different ways: (1) **A drag that BEGINS on her knees folds at the HIPS** (`onKnees`: a live pin on a leg joint above its foot, read at the press): the bow's hinge with the pelvis's pitch at `kKneelFoldStiffness` 5 (the bow's 20) and coupled into the spine's chain over a `kKneelFoldRangeDeg` 300 "range" — planted knees are as good a base to fold over as standing feet. The fold is SHARED: on the base rigs the pelvis pitches 45-66° and three spine joints 8-22° each — the in-app shot reads as a natural all-fours, the back a little rounded; lowering the pelvis's price to 0.5 moves the split by half a degree (the coupling's pull on the pelvis is 1/range, weak), and it is left. (2) **The idle arms hang PLUMB under a tilted trunk** (`hangIdleArms`: blended in by the tilt the trunk has GAINED since the press, over 25-70°, keeping 0.12 of the outward lean; `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_PLUMB`): kept at the elevation the drag found them, arms hanging beside an upright kneeling trunk pointed forward-down under a trunk folded 90°, and the hands landed a forearm ahead of the shoulders; plumb, they land under them. (3) **The elbow SOFTENS as the hand nears the floor** (`kArmLandSoftenDeg` 10, eased in over the fingertip's last `kArmLandSoftenFrom` 15cm at `kArmLandSoftenStepDeg` 1.5°/tick; `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_SOFTEN`): an arm that lands dead straight with its hand straight below it is a fully extended chain — the straight limb's saddle — and the tick such hands touched down the chest lost its cursor by 3-5cm, the solver's fold retry kicked both elbows and the pose hopped 15cm at the brow to the basin it found. A landed hand's arm is also out of the fold retry altogether (`JointSolverDof::foldRetry`), exempt from the ×256 limb twist price (pronating a hand onto its palm IS a forearm twist; `IK_JS_HAND_TWIST_PRICED`), and balance stays blind to a hang arm that lands mid-drag (`HangArm::landed`; `IK_ARM_LANDED_WEIGHED`). (4) **A HAND ON THE FLOOR LIES ON ITS PALM** (`ensureHandMaps`: per wrist — found by structure, an elbow's main-child chain to the first bone with two free channels — the hand's farthest bind descendant is its TIP, `m_jsHandTip`, and every joint of the hand maps to its wrist, `m_jsWristOf`, so a generation whose CARPALS are real joints and land gets ONE model a hand, not two (two gave a 309mm jump)). A hanging hand comes down fingers first, the wrist a hand's length up, and with nothing to say which way a loaded hand lies the solve bent the ELBOW as the body came on down — a figure on her fingertips, back rounding, head tucking under. Two rows a hand: the FINGERTIP is held to the floor in its HEIGHT at the contact's own weight (`kFingertipHold` 1e8, `axisScale` (0,1,0), faded with the contact), and a soft per-axis pull turns the hand palm down (`kPalmPitchWeight` / `kPalmLevelWeight` 30, `kPalmHeadingWeight` 3 per rad²), fingers along the heading it landed with — FORWARD for a hand that lands more than 10cm ahead of the hips, whatever its fingers did on the way down — eased from the rotation it landed with at `kPalmTurnStepDeg` 4°/tick, and the ease only ever LEADS (a hand already further along is met there). So the hand rolls down exactly as the shoulder lets the wrist down, the fingers sliding forward under it. Three variants were tried and thrown out the same day: the fingertip held in PLACE too (rolling about it carried the wrist a hand's length BACK while the body came forward — the arm went taut, the chest 3-6cm off its cursor, the fold retry kicking every tick); the wrist held with a pitch target derived from its height and no height row (the arm swung UP, wrists 45-62cm off the floor, 260-360mm jumps); and the fingertip row softened to 2000/m² (it let the hands slide and cross under a body coming down: the male rig's landed 20cm lopsided, the chest 16cm off its cursor — the softening had been a workaround for the next item). (5) **A contact SETTLES** (`IkRig::updateContacts`; `IK_CONTACT_NO_SETTLE`): once its joint rests on the floor — within the contact band of its clearance — THAT is the height it is planted at. A hand touches down at wrist height 17cm and lies down 13cm lower, and its rise, for the fade of its hold and for the lift-off, was read over the height it TOUCHED at: taken all the way back up in the same drag, a figure on all fours kept both hands on the floor, her trunk folding 19° further at the hips to leave them there. (Only AT REST: a planted height that followed the wrist all the way down read every centimetre it bobbed back up mid-roll as a rise — the hold let go, an arm swung 38cm up and came down again.) (6) **A drag that dips and comes back UNLOADS the contacts it made on the way down** (`m_jsContactRise`: the body's rise over the LOWEST its target has been in THIS drag, with the same limb and seat reductions as `m_jsRootRise` and no room factor, joining the six-decade fade; `IK_JS_NO_DIP_UNLOAD`). `m_jsRootRise` is scaled by the height the legs had left when the drag BEGAN — none for a drag that began standing — so a hip taken down onto all fours and back up in one drag never read as a rise, and on the five rigs whose arms could reach, the hands stayed on the floor with the hips at standing height: a forward fold. (Held by their own rise alone, the hard hold is a switch with a memory, as the knees' was.) (7) **Balance stands down for a kneeling figure's TRUNK drag** (`seated` in `solveIk`, with the seat pin and the floor seat; `IK_JS_KNEEL_BALANCED`): a kneeling body cannot step, and her trunk taken forward past her knees is going onto her hands, which is what the knees are for. Balanced over knees and toes, the row's answer to a chest taken forward was to haul the hips back and UP — the knees, unilateral contacts, lifted 7-8cm off the floor as the shins came up onto the toes — and when the hands landed the body dropped back onto them: 104-144mm at the head in one tick. And the lean's ROOM (the trunk follow's counter-hinge) is measured against the FEET as the rig has stepped them and the contacts the drag FOUND (`m_jsStartStanceNodes`), never a contact the drag itself makes (`IK_JS_COUNTER_LIVE_STANCE`): read off every contact it grew by 23cm in the tick the hands touched down, the counter fell from 1 to 0, the pelvis's price snapped from 250 back to 4500 and hauled the hips 11cm forward to the reference the hinge had left — the chest, on its arms over the planted hands, ran 6cm ahead of its cursor and could not be brought back. (The drag-start polygon walked by the steps was tried first: a walking bow's split stance has more room fore and aft than the stance it began with, and read short, the counter stayed on after the step — the settle moved the head 27mm where it moves 9.) With them, on all eight rigs (`--only kneel-allfours`: a kneel made and let go of, then the upper chest taken forward four fifths of its lever and down to the shoulders' height on all fours — an arm's length and a palm over the floor): the chest rests 0.02-2mm from its cursor, both hands and both knees on the floor, the wrists 34-64mm up (flat palms; informational on the oldest generation, whose shoulder flexion ends at −75° and whose hands land on their fingertips close together), the pelvis 45-66°, no spine joint over 22°, no step, worst single-tick move 17-62mm (it read 88-144 with the knees lifting, 230-360 in the variants), and the pose symmetric to 0.01mm. The older all-fours phases (`--only hands`, the pre-posed hip drag) read the wrists at 35-50mm where they stood 150-160 on fingertips, and a full rise in the same drag lets both hands go on every rig. In the app: kneel her (the hips down and forward), then drag the chest forward and down until the hands touch — `gallery_floor.txt` section A2, with the wrists, the knees, the pelvis, the skin and the jump asserted; pushed on LOWER than that the elbows bend and the back rounds (a push-up's bottom), and the head tucks under as the righting fades past 70° of chest tilt. **And pushed on DOWN from all fours the body LOWERS** (`m_jsKneelYield`, 2026-09-22; `IK_JS_NO_KNEEL_HORIZONTAL_YIELD`): under a kneel-start trunk drag BOTH pelvis prices yield to the push by its downward travel alone — the share gate is a standing body's, so that a sideways pull keeps its hips, and a kneeling body's hips go where the trunk takes them: pushed toward the floor from all fours the chest goes as far forward as down, and share-gated the yield was nil, the hips stayed at 4500/m², and the chest stopped 39cm short with the hips UP on the toes (a head-stand). `IkRig::onKnees` excludes the girdle (the pelvis bone rests on the floor in a sit with both legs below it, and read as a knee the yield loosened a reclining seat: hold jumps of 46-58mm on two rigs). Also found on the way: the solver's fold-retry trigger counts SOFT rows too (a hard-only trigger, `IK_JS_RETRY_HARD_ONLY`, let a foot carried and set down jump 125mm where it jumps 49); `IK_JS_RETRY_TRACE` prints the unmet rows and the kicked bones, `IK_JS_NO_FOLD_RETRY` is its A/B, `IK_JS_HINGE_TRACE=2` prints the balance trace under every drag, and `IK_HARNESS_POSE_TRACE=<n>` widens the harness's jump dump (`IK_HARNESS_POSE_TRACE_MM=<mm>` lowers its 30mm threshold: a hold-still gate that fails at 27 names its joint that way — the oldest generation's kneel-up hold was the fingertips of its hanging arms, 2026-09-25). **THE BALANCE POINT IS THE INSET POLYGON'S** (`BalanceController::closestBalancedPoint`, a NINTH solver lesson: **a row's cost must be CONTINUOUS in the pose**). Until 2026-09-20 the balanced point nearest a centre of mass was the nearest BOUNDARY point pulled the margin toward the hull's CENTROID — the inset polygon only where the centroid lies square behind the edge. Off to one side the pull is oblique, and a centre of mass crossing into the margin band found its balanced point a finite distance away ALONG the edge: the need stepped from zero to a centimetre and more, a cliff of ~2 cost units in the balance row. A pose resting on it refused every step across, whatever its size (`IK_JS_LM_TRACE`: a wall of REJECTs from steps of 1e-2 down to 1e-6, the cost jumping by the same 1.95 each time) — a chest bowed 23° off the heading stalled six ticks and then twisted the spine 10° in one to get round (a 24cm jump at the fingertips), and every diagonal lean had been living on it: the strained hand pull (55cm, forward and across) rested 20-36mm short on the main family and now rests 0.04-0.08mm from the cursor with its knees straight; single-foot poses trembled against it (a foot lifted and carried: knee tremble 7-41 → 2-6mm; a foot lift 4-22 → 0.1-3; a knee raise's hips 1-9 → 0.2-1.6mm). It is now the nearest point of the hull SHRUNK by the margin (each edge moved inward, the hull clipped by it), the margin never more than `kMarginWidthShare` 0.2 of the hull's narrowest width (all 3cm inside a two-footed stance; 1.5cm inside a single foot, whose 5cm heel a 3cm band swallowed whole — the weight of a body mid-step was asked 9cm further forward than the old point had it), halved until it fits. And what the old point's oblique pull had been doing BY ACCIDENT — leaving a need standing after a step landed — is now done on purpose: **a support that GROWS is grown into** (`m_jsGrowFrom`, `kSupportGrowStep` 1cm a tick: each new vertex drawn out from the NEAREST point of the polygon it joins — from the polygon's MIDDLE until 2026-09-22, and a hand landed 26cm ahead of the knees was then inside the old polygon for its first 22 ticks, the support not moving at all while the drag took the weight on forward: see ONTO ALL FOURS; `IK_JS_GROW_FROM_MIDDLE` — the slack's ramp held meanwhile). A one-sided row has nothing to say once it is met, so a body that leaned to keep its balance over one foot (the hip hinge through a step) was given its whole lean back the tick the other foot landed: hips taken 25cm back, the head went 9-10cm in that tick; it now goes 29-34mm at worst on all eight rigs (33-40 before the change that exposed it). Probe: `IK_JS_NO_SUPPORT_GROW`; `IK_JS_HINGE_TRACE` prints the centre of mass, the support's extent and the need. **THE SEAT — a pinned pelvis is a body something else holds up** (`IkRig::seatPin`: a USER pin on the solve root or a joint of the pelvis girdle, `IkRig::isGirdleJoint` — the pelvis bone, the first two spine bones, a thigh socket: the joints a click on the hips finds, and so the ones P pins). The app knows nothing of props, so this is the one way to say "a chair holds her here": sit her down by the hips, pin them, pose the rest. Held as any user pin is (six degrees) and balanced over her feet as any body is, she could not be posed: a seated figure's weight is a forearm's length behind her feet AS POSED, every centimetre of recline made that "worse", and the chest pulled 10cm back along its own arc went 2 (77mm short); leaned forward the fold was the spine's alone under a bolt-upright pelvis (27mm short, trailing all the way). With a seat pin: BALANCE is off (what holds her up is not her feet), the rig takes NO STEP (`updateStepPolicy` returns — a standing figure with pinned hips used to step after a chest it could not follow), she does not RISE (`dragIkTick`: rose = 0, so nothing is freed or turned home), and THE SEAT ROCKS: under an upper-body trunk drag `m_jsTrunkHinge` is 1 however low she sits (the root's pitch an unknown, coupled into the spine's chain as in the bow), and the pin's orientation hold becomes a per-axis row with its PITCH weight zero — heading and level kept (the pelvis itself, a spine bone of the girdle, or the pelvis bone: root, `inSpine`, or two children; a pinned THIGH is a thigh resting on the seat and is held in full, the pelvis rocking over it). On all eight rigs: the chest leaned 30° forward along its arc rests 0.6-5.6mm from the cursor with the pelvis pitched 11-14° (the spine shares the fold, and a curling trunk is a few millimetres shorter than its chord: a target even 3% INSIDE the arc rests 5-8mm short — shortening a trunk a centimetre costs 50° of curl, which the bounded pull does not buy), reclined 20° 0.07-2.1mm with the pelvis 7-9° back, the same lean with the PELVIS BONE pinned 0.2-9mm, a hand reached a forearm past its arm's length 0.03-0.06mm and a foot put out 0.0004-0.02mm with the hips where they were (0.0001mm). Every phase without a girdle pin is bit-identical. Probes: `IK_NO_SEAT`, `IK_JS_NO_SEAT_ROCK`. The rig's step policy reads the target the solve WORKED TO (`m_jsSolveGoal`: a knee's or an elbow's is on its reach, centimetres from the cursor by design — read off the cursor, that distance was a body the stance could not follow, and she took a step sideways after her own elbow). BALANCE is 1e4 on the distance outside the inset polygon (its pull meets the cursor's at a centimetre: an out-of-reach hand pull leans to the edge of the support and no further; steps are for where the user takes the BODY), under a pelvis drag only as the hip hinge above, RAMPED in (1cm/tick of slack after the support changes) and never asking back the imbalance the drag STARTED with (a still cursor re-balanced two pre-posed rigs by 16mm). The FLOOR and the BODY VOLUMES are 1e7 plane rows raised within 2cm of a surface: every body joint against its floor clearance (a pin's subtree exempt, a soft heel not), and `IkRig::dragVolumes()` read directly — joints, limb segments (the closest point rides the parent bone) and the fingers, each plane riding its capsule's first joint. SUSPENDED (`IkRig::suspended`), a 1e4 row hangs the root `suspendHang()` below the cursor so the body dangles under the raised arm (without it the posture term floated the figure up with its arm at its side); the hang was RIGID until 2026-11-02 — THE DANGLE, below the akimbo paragraph, hangs the legs and the free arm. THE LIFT-OFF IS EASED IN (`dragIkTick`: `m_jsLiftDeficit`, `m_jsHangDeficit`, `kLiftRiseStep` 2.5cm a tick, `IK_JS_NO_LIFT_EASE`): by the time the rig decides she hangs from the grabbed joint the cursor is 30-40cm beyond her reach, and the hang is straight below it — half a metre to the side of where she stands, for a hand raised out at the body's side — so solved in full the first suspended tick took the whole body 42cm up and 40 across at once (59-71cm at a toe, on every rig and whatever was grabbed). The solve is given the cursor less the deficit the lift began with and the hang's target starts where the root IS, both paid off at that pace: she comes up off the floor and swings in under the hand over a quarter of a second (worst single-tick move through a lift 39-53mm). A cursor jump is walked in 4cm sub-targets within the tick (continuation: iterations, never lag) — as long as the target's own MOVE since the last tick, not as the miss: a target held out of reach is a miss that stays, and walked afresh every tick it was twelve solves a tick, each ending in ten rejected dampings — 18ms of tick work, over the tick's whole budget, for a hand held half a metre beyond its reach with the mouse still (now 2.7ms) — and the piece count is taken a hundredth of a piece SHORT of its ceiling: under a still cursor the follower's last micron of travel made the move 1e-7 and the ceiling TWO pieces, so every tick of an out-of-reach hold solved first to a target halfway back and then to the real one from wherever that left the pose (on the newest rig a folded-over crouch answered with a 2-tick limit cycle, 45mm at the face). DAMPING, for feel only (the first verdict was "slightly too snappy"; `Armature::ikDamping`, level 1 of 0-3, `IK_JS_DAMPING`): (1) POSTURE EASING inside the solve — each channel's posture term also pulls toward the PREVIOUS tick's value, which is one quadratic (a blended reference, stiffness × (1+κ), κ = 1), so every constraint row stays exact on every tick, the resting pose is unchanged, and only the body's redundant follow-through eases — constraint-preserving relaxation, where blending whole poses in joint space (the old path) slid planted feet; (2) a critically damped FOLLOWER on the target (ω = 120 rad/s, integrated exactly per 60 Hz tick) that rounds the grabbed joint's starts and stops and trails a steady cursor by one tick of its travel. Measured on the base rig, old path → this: bench idle-joint tremble 11.6 → 0.0mm, hand at rest 1.2 → 0.01mm, feet drift 0.7 → 0.2mm, a pinned-hand drag's knee tremble 41 → 0.4mm, finger dip on a cross-body reach 102 → 0.0mm, a lifted figure lowered back landing with its hip 0.14mm from its start height (was 25mm), the release settle gone (the pose already satisfies its pins); hand lag 3.3mm at moderate speed and 6mm on a 1.2 m/s flick, all of it the follower's (0.8 / 0.001mm undamped). Probes: `POSESTUDIO_IK_TRACE` (`[js]` rows: dofs, rows, iterations, cost, miss, pin error), `IK_JS_POSE_TRACE` (the posture bill), `IK_JS_LM_TRACE` (every step attempt); A/B levers `IK_JS_PULL`, `_ROOTPULL`, `_TRUNK`, `_ROOT`, `_ROOTY`, `_ROOTYIELD`, `_ROOTROT`, `_PINLIMB`, `_PINTWIST`, `_BALANCE`, `_HEEL`, `_SOLEROLL`, `_DAMPING`, `_NO_PLANES`, `_NO_BALL`, `_NO_RISE_YIELD`, `_NO_UPRIGHT`, `_NO_CONTACT_FADE`.

**The rig's policy** (`IkRig`; built lazily per figure from the bind skeleton, `Armature::ensureIkRig`). **The FBIK root is `rootNode()` — the first multi-child DESCENDANT of the anatomical root**: real figures root at an origin-level FIGURE NODE with the hip as its lone child a metre up, and that virtual chain is excluded WHOLESALE (no graph edge, no contact eligibility, no balance mass — planted as a contact it once pinned the whole figure to the origin); a merged follower's scene node beside the pelvis is excluded the same way (contacts must DESCEND from the pelvis). `sizeScale()` (pelvis bind height, dead zone [0.85, 1.15]) scales every geometric threshold, so a 0.54-scale child crouches and walks. AT DRAG START (`beginDrag`/`plantContacts`): joints under `kIkContactHeight` (15cm world) are contacts, minus those within a limb's LENGTH of the effector by tree path (`kEffectorLiftReach`, metric not hops: dragging a foot lifts its whole toe subtree, a hip drag keeps both feet) — unless they hang BELOW an effector that is not itself foot-class (`liftedByDrag` → `belowEffectorInItsLimb`: a dragged KNEE keeps its foot, a dragged elbow or shoulder the hand it finds on the floor — seeded below it as a HARD live contact; the effector must be a joint of that limb, never a TRUNK joint, which has every limb below it: a chest drag that held the hands it found on the floor in full could not get a sitting figure up on the two short-armed generations); and only FOOT-CLASS joints (`kFootBindHeight`: low AT BIND — the ankle, the toes) count: a joint that is low because of the POSE (a kneeling knee, a hand on the floor) is seeded as a LIVE contact instead (`seedPoseContacts`, `IK_NO_POSE_CONTACTS=1` withholds it), so a drag begins in the state the last one ended in — planted like feet, a figure left kneeling could not be stood up again (pulled by the chest she was read as beyond her leashes' reach and LIFTED off the floor dangling; dragged by the hip the stepper "stepped" her knees); each limb's MOST PROXIMAL foot-class JOINT becomes the pin, planted or not (a foot standing on its toes — tucked under a kneel, on tiptoe — is still a foot and its model hangs off the ankle: on four generations a tucked foot's ankle rises past the contact height, the pin went to the TOES joint, with no ball, no heel row, nothing to bring the heel down) (the ankle — pinning every toe made feet curl) at its GROUND-HEALED height (a hovering figure is pulled back onto the floor, never preserved — but A FOOT THE USER LIFTED STAYS LIFTED, `kFootLiftedHeight`: at a drag's start a foot whose LOWEST joint stands more than 3cm over its own floor height, while another foot of the figure stands, is not planted at all. The contact band is 15cm deep and the healing reaches 12, made for millimetres of residue, so a foot raised 5-10cm by one drag — a step being posed, a pose file with a foot up — was planted by the NEXT drag of anything, a hand included, and hauled 98mm back down to the floor on every rig. A foot on its toes or its heel has a joint down and stands; with no foot standing the whole figure hovers and healing it is the point; a landing out of a lift still plants whatever comes down; a lifted foot rides its leg, lands as a live contact when a crouch brings it to the floor, and becomes the support if the standing foot is the one dragged; `IK_PLANT_LIFTED_FEET` is the A/B) with a socket-centered reach LEASH (now read only by the lift-off test); user pins become HARD pins (never healed, stepped or released; a pin on the effector or in its subtree sits out — except under a pelvis drag, and except 20cm or more down the dragged joint's OWN limb, `kEffectorPinHoldReach` + `belowEffectorInItsLimb`: a pinned hand under a dragged elbow, a pinned foot under a dragged knee, HOLD); nothing planted and nothing pinned → the root is pinned in place; a TOKEN-MASS grab (a carpal, a mid-foot bone, a pectoral — and until 2026-09-28 a finger, a toe or a face bone, which no longer reach the rig: FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG above — own mass token AND subtree under 1%) is PROMOTED to the limb's real end joint (`dragEffector()`), the Armature shifting the target by the grab offset — carried through the effector's rotation since drag start for a limb, MEASURED off the pose every tick for a small bone on a TRUNK joint — a pectoral bone on the chest, which is what a click on a figure's chest finds as often as the chest itself (its joint stands nearest the pixel; on a fuller bust, always), or a face bone on the head (`m_ikGrabMeasured`; `IK_GRAB_TRUNK_FIXED` / `IK_GRAB_HEAD_FIXED` restore the fixed offset). Held FIXED, as it was until 2026-09-21, the offset did not turn with a chest that LEANS as it is dragged: pulled 12-18cm sideways by a pectoral the grabbed point ended 23-52mm from the cursor and trailed it by 14-38mm on every rig (by the chest itself: 0.04 and 3); measured, 4-21 and 3-9 — what is left is the lean's own (a point beside the spine drops as the chest rolls, and a standing body cannot be asked up). The fixed offset had been the old position-space solver's, which passed cursor noise on fourteen-fold and trembled a grabbed head through this loop; here an eye grab rests 0.95mm from its cursor where it rested 9.9, trails it by 1.3mm where it trailed 9.7, and under ±2mm of cursor noise wobbles 15mm where it wobbled 14; and a grab of the PELVIS GIRDLE is a PELVIS drag (`IkRig::girdleGrab`, `kGirdleGrabReach`): a trunk-class joint within two bones and 20cm of the solve root — the pelvis bone and the first spine bone (2-3cm from the root on every generation), the second spine bone (10-12cm), the thigh sockets (15-16) — goes where the pelvis goes and nowhere else, and these are the joints a click on the hips FINDS (the root's own body is two 2cm segments; the pelvic flesh is the pelvis bone's, the hip joints the thighs'). As ordinary trunk drags they met the root's horizontal price — 2.2cm of hips for a 10cm drag, the grab 5-8cm short, no sway, no hinge, no walk: the user's "dragging the hip forward and back doesn't really do anything". The effector is promoted to the root and `Armature::dragIkTo` MEASURES the grab's offset from the root on the pose as it stands, every tick (a pelvis drag rolls and pitches the root and turns the bones between, so no frame captured at the press carries it): the grabbed joint rests on the cursor to 0.1mm; a user pin on the grabbed joint sits out; `IK_NO_GIRDLE_GRAB` is the A/B; the support polygon spans ALL contacts (toes give the real footprint); pairs of limbs already overlapping are masked out of the volumes for the drag. PER TICK around the solve: `updateIntent` (DOWN = a pelvis drag 2cm below its start height, or a limb pushed 15cm below itself while TAUT toward the target — a folded arm below its cursor is not pushing the body down; UP = a limb pulled above itself; then `boundLivePins` and, for non-pelvis drags, the LIFT-OFF test), then on the solved pose `updateContacts` and `updateStepPolicy`. SUSPENSION: a sustained (~0.25s), mostly vertical pull on a target GEOMETRICALLY beyond the leash-bound body's reach (never the drag error: a reachable up-pull must simply raise the arm) releases the contact pins (user pins stay) and activates every real-mass segment (token bones ride: gravity once pulled a jaw open); LANDING: once every foot has cleared the floor by 5cm, a foot back within the contact band re-plants every joint under the contact height through `plantContacts`, moves the drag-start reference under the landed body (`stanceShift()` — the solve's root reference follows, so the figure stands back up where it landed) and sets `takeLanded()` → `captureFlatHold(true)` (the soles roll flat from the rest orientation). LIVE CONTACTS: a real-mass joint whose lowest RIDING part (fingers under a hand, toes under a foot, the face under the head) comes within 1cm of the floor becomes a unilateral pin there — SLIDING when dragged past 2.5cm, LIFTING OFF when the limb is taut and the joint 2cm up for 3 ticks (but while the centre of mass still stands outside the support WITHOUT it, under any drag but the pelvis's, a lifted contact stays a SHADOW of a support — holding nothing, its footprint following the joint — so the support never collapses in one tick under a body that is leaning on it; `IK_NO_SHADOW_SUPPORT`), re-arming 3cm up; never the drag's own limb except under a pelvis drag; a knee over its still-planted foot plants from 5cm while the body goes DOWN and is HARD there, so a kneel brings it onto the floor (its geometry otherwise leaves the knees hovering 3-4cm up on the balls of the feet). STEPPING (`updateStepping`): when the body goes where the stance cannot follow, the steppable foot farthest from its stance spot re-plants there — the drag-start stance SHAPE re-centered on the CoM, or, for a pelvis drag and a trunk drag that takes the hips along (the Armature's trunk follow hands `updateStepPolicy` its `bodyAnchor`: where the solve's reference has the hips; without one, a trunk drag missing its target by 12cm still anchors on the residual), on where the user is TAKING the body (offset by the stance's drag-start offset from the hip) — through an eased swing with a lift arc, reach-clamped (0.98 of the leg's path), side-preserving against the other foot (legs never scissor), only standing feet and never the last one; triggers are sustained (12 ticks — the MISPLACED signal's confirmation shrinking with the distance, none at all from `kStepPromptFactor` 2.5 × the 12cm, since 2026-09-22: the confirmation is for the balance effort, which blips, and a foot a stride from its spot is no blip — confirmed for the flat 12 a fast chest drag's first step began as the cursor STOPPED, 20 ticks of drag then 1.4s of walking after it, each step's 12 ticks waited out after the last one landed; now the first step begins mid-drag (tick 13 of 20) and the hold's mean lag falls 37 → 13mm; on the main family the sideways 45cm chest drag's first step then goes to the OTHER foot, 16cm from its spot against the leading foot's 17, and its gathering step lands 6cm shorter — the trailing heel rests 21mm up (9), the hip walks' 2cm, its gate 25; `IK_STEP_SLOW_CONFIRM`): the low-passed balance EFFORT — measured OVER the imbalance the drag BEGAN with (`m_startImbalance`, taken at `plantContacts` on the drag's first pose and dropped once a step has landed: the drag-start pose is the contract for the rig as it is for the solve's balance row, which never asks that imbalance back. Until 2026-09-20 the trigger read the raw need: a pose that leans — loaded from a file, made with FK, or a bow let go of, whose follow leaves the weight 6cm out by the row's measure — was 6cm of "effort" from the first tick, and grabbing her HAND with the cursor held still walked her two or three steps, the head 19-29cm, on every rig in one scenario or the other; `IK_NO_START_IMBALANCE` is the A/B) — or a foot 12cm from its anchor-centred spot (what makes a hip walk step proactively and take its final gathering step); a crouch (8cm below the start pelvis) keeps its feet, and NO step begins while the pelvis — or where a pelvis drag is taking it — sits 12cm below its STANDING height (`kStepLowBody`: a figure dragged up out of a kneel stepped a foot forward under its own knees while still kneeling), the stance's offset from the hip blending to the BIND stance's for a drag that begins low (a kneel's or a sit's offset is the pose's, not a stance); a step in flight always finishes (`landPendingStep` at mouse-up). There is no pin-strain trigger any more: the solve never drags a foot off its plant.

**Self-collision** (`bodyvolume.h`, `IkRig::buildBodyVolumes`): tapered capsules fitted to the figure's own mesh — the Model hands the Armature a BODY MESH SAMPLE at import (every skinned vertex's bind position + dominant bone; the skeleton dump writes it as `<dump>.mesh` for the harness; `IK_VOLUME_NO_MESH=1` keeps skeleton sizing). The TORSO is ONE banded profile along the spine (5cm bands: half-width 85th percentile, front 90th, back 10th, the pectoral riders included — the breasts belong to them) sampled at each spine joint into a centre capsule offset ahead of the spine plus two side capsules (a stadium; a per-bone fit extrapolated nonsense from thin slabs); LIMBS taper by the median radial distance in four bands; the LEGS stand for their INNER surface (80th percentile toward the other leg — an all-round thigh held crossed legs 15cm apart at the crotch); the HEAD from the skull. Each volume applies (`BodyVolume::applies`) to the OTHER limbs only: 1 = a joint, 3 = a joint AND the segment into it (limb capsules test segments — limbs cross at their middles; the torso tests the UPPER ARM's segment only: the forearm's held the cross-body reach off the chest), 2 = a hand's RIDERS (fingers, thumbs, carpals) against the torso and head with 1cm clearance; never a limb's own joints, its root, anything overlapping at BIND, or hands against legs (hands rest on knees; the capsule stops at the wrist so planted hands can sit side by side). `volumeAxis()` is the one reading of a capsule in a pose (solve rows, target clamp, FK stop, harness). **A joint that ENDS a fleshy segment is as wide as the segment's end** (2026-09-22; `IK_JS_BARE_JOINT_CAPS`): the solve's row on such a joint — a knee against the other thigh, an elbow against the torso — keeps the segment's flesh margin (`kVolumeSegmentFlesh` 0.6 of its own capsule radius), not the bare 1.5cm joint clearance; the segment row stands down within 2% of its ends, and held there at the joint's clearance the model's margin dropped 2cm the moment two crossed thighs touched at the knee — on three characters with fuller thighs the legs crossed with the knees 13-14mm inside each other by the harness's measure (which counts the segment's flesh to its end), 0.0mm now, every reference rig bit-for-bit or within a pixel. The Armature clamps the drag TARGET out of the volumes and the floor (`clampOutOfVolumes`), and **the FK collision stop** (`nudgeBone`: the Transform dials, the B / S / T mouse mode, and the scripts' `fk`) applies a rotation in full, measures `fkVolumeDepth` over the pairs that MOVE relative to each other, and bisects back to the largest fraction that penetrates no deeper than before or than 2mm — a limb stops at the body on the way in and can always rotate OUT of a loaded pose's penetration; pose loads, undo and the utilities apply as authored. `IK_VOLUME_TRACE=1|2`, `IK_VOLUME_NO_LIMBS`, `IK_VOLUME_TORSO_NO_SEGMENTS`.

**The window's side** (`vulkanwindow_ik.cpp`): a ~60 Hz timer is the SOLE solve driver while the button is held (mouse moves only record the target), running the raw cursor target through the adaptive `IkCursorFilter` (`scene/ik/cursorfilter.h`, shared with the harness: alpha 0.3 near-still → 1.0 from 7mm of lag — kept from the old path, where it shielded a noise-amplifying solve; with the solve's own follower it is a candidate for removal, and doing so changes the approved feel, so measure first); it redraws only when `dragIkTo` reports a change. On release `settleIkTick` runs until false — there is nothing to land unless a pin target is still being approached (a healing hover, a step that was in flight), which finishes with a placed hand or foot held where it was let go — then `finalizePose` and the undo commit (`finishIkSettle`). **THE SETTLE IS THE DRAG, CONTINUED UNDER A STILL CURSOR** (`Armature::settleIkSolveTick` → `dragIkTick(m_jsSettleTarget)`, the followed target the last drag tick solved to, with `m_jsSettling` keeping the rig from reading new intents or beginning new steps): every reference and row the pose was made with, the arms' hang and the head's righting included. Until 2026-09-20 it was a solve of its own — with NO target at all for a trunk drag ("trunk effectors are free in the settle", from the position-space solver's landing round) — and every posture term pulls to the drag-start pose: a bow let go of while a step was in the air SPRANG BACK as the foot came down (the chest 17cm, the head 44cm, the gaze 62° — on the base rig), her arms and head riding since the post-steps did not run, and the NEXT drag then found arms pointing forward that no longer counted as hanging: taken back up, she stood with both arms straight out in front of her and her head tipped back 13°. No harness phase saw it (they let go after a hold, when no step is in flight); the in-app two-drag script did, in its first run (`IK_JS_OLD_SETTLE` is the A/B); any overlapping interaction finishes it first (`closeOpenPoseEdits`). Correctives blend live on the GPU throughout.

**A KNEELING FOOT LIES FLAT** (2026-09-26; the user, with a photo of a kneel-sit — the shins flat, the feet lying on their tops with the toes pointing back, sitting on her heels: "when kneeling, feet get unrealistically stretched and looks wrong, it is way more common to flatten feet out against the floor"; the flat block at the top of `ikPinRows`, `kKneelFlat*` / `kLyingFootHangClear*` / `kKneeUnrollLift` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; `IK_JS_NO_KNEEL_FLAT` is the A/B, `IK_JS_KNEEL_FLAT_TRACE` prints each lying foot's state per tick). Every kneel stood the foot on TUCKED TOES — the ball pinned at the floor, the toes held level, the ankle at its dorsiflexion limit: the standing foot's model, which the kneel had no other — and it was the first thing wrong in every kneel shot. The foot behind a knee that is ON the floor now lies on its top, toes back. Its SHARE (`m_jsFootFlat`, eased 0.1 a tick) rises once the knee above it has LANDED — a live pin that is not a shadow, on a leg joint above the foot (`IkRig::isLegJointAboveFoot`: a floor-sitter's SEAT, the pelvis bone, was taken for a knee and flattened both her feet), within 0.5-2cm of its planted height — and a foot found within 20° of its plantar limit under a planted knee at the press is flat from the first tick (no planted knee at the press: 0, whatever a kneel let go of and Reset left in the share). Over the share the standing foot's rows go: the ball row and the toes' hold by decades (10^(−8·flat), 10^(−6·flat)), the sole's hold too (its HEADING is the standing foot's as the drag found it, and held to it the lying foot twisted 18° and rolled 25° against its shin); the floor's rows come onto the foot (footFreed) except the ankle's and the mid-foot's (`footLying`: their clearances are the standing foot's bind heights, and the foot floated level 5cm off the floor); the heel row holds the ankle at its planted PLACE (a lying shin is held by friction — with the ball's pin gone the thigh's twist relaxed and swung the shin 10cm inward through a still hold) and lets it DOWN to the LYING height, the knee's planted height (the shin's radius; at its standing 7.5cm the foot hovered), keeping the height the roll found it at through the roll's first half — the foot rolls IN THE AIR: rolled over its resting ball and toe tips the toes were folded to their limits under it, the floor's rows locked the roll, and the foot flipped 8cm in the tick the active set gave (131mm at a toe in a still hold). The pose is GIVEN, not priced: the ankle's PITCH channel (the one along the foot's lateral axis, `m_jsFootFlatAxis`, its plantar direction the one that takes the ball down: `m_jsFootFlatLimit`, and the other limit `m_jsFootFlatTuck`) gets a REFERENCE that eases from where it stands to the plantar limit at `kKneelFlatTurnStepDeg` 2.5° a tick (150°/s), the two other channels to 0 (in line with the shin: asked there at once, they sprang 78mm at the toes when the sole's hold went), and EVERY toe joint below the ankle to straight (`m_jsToeFlatRef`: the ball's chain alone left the toe segments to the floor, a big toe bent 32° through a kneel's hold on the generation whose toes fan from the mid-foot), at `kKneelFlatStiffness` 300/rad² over the share in `ikPostureModel`. The roll is REAL MOTION, 16-20mm a tick at a toe tip, and on the rig whose knees land last it runs on into the still hold: the kneel phases' hold-jump gates read 25 (15), and the SETTLE waits for a rolling foot as it waits for an easing pin (`IkSolveScratch::flatEasing` → `m_jsPinsEasing`; ended when no pin eased, it would have left the foot half-rolled). THE UNROLL — getting up: let go of by its knee (the knee's hold fading with the body's RISE; a knee held down by its ceiling, `kneeBelowHips` — a body pushed up off its belly — keeps its foot LYING, so from prone onto all fours the feet stay flat, as a person's do: read off the rise alone the push-up rolled both feet onto their toes under held knees and the returning rows brought them down 107-117mm in a tick), the foot rolls back onto its TOES in place — the tuck, the ankle's other limit: the tucked kneel's own foot, whose rise every rig already gets up by; unrolled to STANDING it hung in the air on a shin the rise had lifted and landing it snapped the knee straight, 267mm — at `kKneelFlatUnrollStepDeg` 4° a tick, its ball held by a soft spring (`kKneelFootHold`, in the floor's plane) where it lay and every other row of the foot off (the toes' references let go for the tuck: a tucked foot's toes are folded back against the floor, and held straight at the lying stiffness the foot could not tuck — 133-140mm on two rigs), and THE KNEE ABOVE IT GETS A CEILING THAT RISES WITH THE ROLL (`kKneeUnrollLift` 25cm × the roll's progress, `m_jsFootUnrollProgress`): the knee comes up as the toes tuck under, no faster. Held down outright until the handover the rise stalled — the grab 47mm behind its cursor — and let go of everything in one tick, 240-260mm on every rig; free, it lifted with the hips while the foot still hung in the air on its soft spring, and where the foot hung when its rows returned decided the pop: 52 to 275mm, rig by rig and variant by variant (hard in place the tuck was infeasible, the toes at their curl limits against a nailed ball and the hips collapsing back onto the knees; the ball slid under the ankle, the rig's pin re-seated at the ankle, the ball's target tracking it after the handover, the ball held down softly — each tried the same day and taken out again). At the HANDOVER (the tuck reached, `m_jsFootUnrolled`) the rig's ankle pin is re-seated a heel's length behind the ball that stays where it lay (`IkRig::reseatPin`; left at the spot the ankle LAY at, a foot's length ahead, the support polygon stood 24cm ahead of the feet and every rise ended on tiptoe with the hips 12.5cm short), the sole's and toes' held orientations and the posture references are re-read off the tucked foot, the share fades 0.1 a tick with the ball's target eased to its spot at `kKneelFlatLandStep` 1cm a tick, and the tucked kneel's rise takes over. A DRAGGED KNEE's foot found lying (the half kneel out of a flat kneel; `lyingKneeFoot` in the else branch) RIDES ITS SHIN instead: its ankle keeps its lying height (`footLying`), its ball row is off, its toes are held straight at the lying stiffness, and the sole's hold comes in over the trail's second half toward LEVEL while the ankle's pitch reference eases toward the value that levels the sole — read off the shin's orientation each tick — once the knee lifts; and the hang's target stands `kLyingFootHangClear` 3cm clear of what puts the foot's lowest joint on the floor, fading as the foot comes level, so the trailing toes swing free (hung exactly at the touching height the floor's rows bent the toes up to their limits tick by tick — a ratchet, the hang read off the shape they had just bent — and they sprang back 100mm at a tip when the turning foot lifted them off; given the standing foot's model outright, the sole's world-frame hold stood the lying foot up on its toe pads and the hang then brought the ankle down onto them, 147mm at a toe). Three rig changes came with the round: a shadow support EXPIRES once its joint is `kShadowMaxRise` 10cm off the floor (kept while the body would be unbalanced without it, the knees of a figure pulled up by the chest out of a flat kneel — her feet a foot's length further back than a tucked kneel's, her weight 27cm ahead of them — stayed supports half a metre up, no step ever came, and she stood leaning on straight legs on tiptoe; the whole pin goes: the footprint alone withdrawn, at once or at a centimetre a tick with the pin standing, left the base rig's and the male's kneel-up jumping 97-105mm where the removal jumps 52), the HEALING flag clears when no planted pin's joint stands over 1cm above its target (`IK_HEALING_STICKS`; it never cleared, and no step could come), and the START IMBALANCE is re-measured against the full hull after the pose's contacts are seeded (`IK_START_IMBALANCE_FEET_ONLY`; against the feet alone a kneeler's every step trigger was forgiven). A taut-leg exception to the low-body step gate was tried for a day and removed: it made two generations step three and four times through a sit-up. The harness's floor-penetration measure exempts a lying foot's ankle and mid-foot (`Armature::footLyingExempt`), and the getting-up-out-of-a-kneel gates read 100mm of jump (85) and 25 of mean lag (15): the flat kneel's getting-up rolls the feet onto their toes first, so the hips wait on the roll (15-22mm of mean lag where the tucked kneel's read 3-8) and the handed-over foot's rows come back over the share's fade (58-97mm at a toe or shin in the tick they land). On all eight rigs 139/139; in-app 234/0 with the floor gallery's eleven shots and the mixed gallery's kneel changed and accepted after looking — the kneel, the kneel onto all fours, the sit back onto the heels (the user's photo), the half kneel with its rear foot lying and its drawn-up foot landing flat, the prone with straight legs and flat feet, and the push-up off the belly, whose hands the gallery now pulls straight DOWN under the shoulders (walked 14% forward, as the tucked-foot prone had them, they stood behind the shoulders once the trunk pivoted up and both lifted off: she kneeled up with her hands in the air). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 42 near-misses and FIVE real (40 and one before the round): the known mirror asymmetry; two long-footed characters' kneel still-hold at 34-38mm a tick at a toe (the roll onto the top continuing into the hold, at their foot's lever — the gate reads the base's 16-20); the heaviest newest-generation character's kneel-up by the chest resting 13mm from its cursor (10); and one character's prone descent popping 258mm at the brow as her arms fold under the flatter body (84 with the flat feet off, `IK_JS_NO_KNEEL_FLAT`: the lying feet keep her shins down, the legs straighten, and the hips come further forward onto arms that fold and flip) — ANSWERED 2026-09-28, and it was the SOLVER's: a trial judged by the rows of the pose it had left (the thirteenth lesson, in the solver's paragraph); 22mm now. AND THE OTHER THREE, the same day, each a rule that read the wrong thing: (1) **THE LYING ANKLE'S PLACE IS ON ITS SHIN'S REACH OF THE PLANTED KNEE** (the heel row's lying target in `ikPinRows`; `IK_JS_LYING_ANKLE_AT_SPOT` is the A/B): toward the spot the foot stood on, a shin's length — at the height asked — from where the knee landed. It was the spot itself, which a lying shin does not reach: the knee comes down over a foot standing on its tucked toes, the ankle up over its ball, a palm ahead of where it stood, so the drag that MAKES a kneel ended with that soft row 2-9cm from its target on every rig (23mm on the base, 87 on the oldest). A soft row that can never be met is a standing pull on the leg, through a hold the solve creeps under (its descent gaining 1e-3 an iteration along the leg's swing about the knee): on two long-footed characters both shins went from one side-bend limit to the other in ONE tick of the still hold, the thighs twisting 5 degrees — 34-38mm at a toe, 9-11 now, and the ankle rests on its place within a centimetre of the height asked on every rig. Only while the foot lies or rolls onto its top (`toLying`): the unroll's and the handover's targets are what they were, and a drag that BEGINS kneeling had no such miss (its pins are planted where the joints are). (2) **A HOVER IS A FOOT OFF THE FLOOR** (`IkRig::updateStepPolicy`; `IK_HEALING_BY_ANKLE`): the healing flag — no step while a hovering figure is being pulled down — stood while a planted foot's ANKLE was a centimetre over its target. On a heavy character the lying ankle rests 13mm over its standing height (a thicker foot under it), so the flag stood from the press; the foot then came up onto its toes, the ankle higher still, and the flag never cleared: pulled up out of a kneel by the chest she ended on tiptoe, heels 37mm up and the chest 13mm short, where her base figure takes two steps and arrives. It now also needs the foot's LOWEST part (`contactHeight`) a centimetre off the floor: two steps, the chest 0.02mm from its cursor, heels 11mm. (3) **THE SLACK HAND'S CEILING IS LET GO OF THROUGH ITS SLACK** (`kHandCeilingSlack` 6cm; `IK_JS_HAND_CEILING_FADES` is the weight's fade): under a descent the ceiling was faded by WEIGHT over the target's 2-8cm of downward travel — at a sixteenth of 1e7 it still held a wrist 12mm under where it was going, 85 of a solve's cost of 135 (`IK_JS_TRIAL_PLANE_TRACE` names it: a world plane on the wrist at 6e5), and the last of the fade let the hand go at once. Found when (1) moved one character's prone descent into it: the freed arm turned 28 degrees at the shoulder in that tick, 130mm at a fingertip; 46 with the ceiling RISING over that travel instead, gone once it stands 6cm up — the hip hinge's lesson, a hard row is let go of through its slack, never its weight. All eight rigs 146/146 (the 19 kneel-start phases moved; nothing lost a tenth of its gate but the oldest rig's kneel hold, 5.5 → 8.3mm of 25, and the ceiling's change moved the prone on three rigs by millimetres); THE SWEEP reads 40 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 with no real failure, 6 with every gate green; in-app 310/0 on the twelve scripts with NO shot changed on the base figure (its kneel's ankle was 2cm from the spot), and the floor gallery run on the two long-footed characters with the changes on and off: the same poses to a millimetre in those gestures, looked at (their prone-up ends with the hands in the air either way — the gallery's base-sized drags, a known miss of that gallery on those bodies). `IK_JS_UNROLL_BALL_DOWN` (the held-down ball) is the one rejected variant kept behind a probe.

**Verified in the in-repo harness** (`tools/ikharness/`, target `PoseStudioIkHarness`, CTest `ik-harness`; `PoseStudioIkHarness <dump> [--verbose] [--only <phase-key>]`, or `--custom <bone> <dx> <dy> <dz> <moveTicks> <holdTicks> [pins...] [--pose <bone> x y z]... [--hover m] [--scale s] [--return ticks] [--before <bone> <dx> <dy> <dz>]... [--to-rest] [--share <0..1>] [--pin-after]`; `--pin-after` / `Scenario::pinsAfterBefore` sets the pins AFTER the `--before` drags ("sit her down, THEN pin the hips": set first, a pin on the pelvis holds the prelude's own hip drag where it stands); `--share` / `Scenario::grabShare` grabs the bone's BODY that share of the way along its rigid limb segment (`Armature::setIkGrabOnSegment`; every grab metric then reads the grab POINT); `--to-rest` / `Scenario::toRest` takes the grabbed joint back to where it sat before the preludes ("the kneel undone" is NOT the standing pose: a kneel pitches the torso, and the chest's kneeling position plus the hip's reversed travel ends 13cm behind where a balanced standing chest sits — balance rightly refused it); `--before` / `Scenario::before` is a drag made and RELEASED before the measured one, so a phase can start from the pose a real session would have): it drives the REAL loop (`beginIkDrag/dragIkTo/settleIkTick/endIkDrag` through the shared cursor filter, at the viewport's tick order — its `bench` phase reproduces the in-app `POSESTUDIO_IK_BENCH=lHand` numbers exactly, the parity proof) on a **dumped figure skeleton** (`POSESTUDIO_DUMP_SKELETON=<path>`; vendor rig data, kept OUT of the repo; re-dump after any format change) — eight of them, every figure generation, with a bone-alias layer and a shoulder pre-pose for the T-pose rigs. Phases: the hand gestures (gentle pull, bench path, wheel depth, arm raise, strained pull + release), the digits (a finger pulled far beyond its reach, a fingertip curled, a toe raised: each alone), the trunk (chest push-down, standing back up from a released crouch, head pull-up on a slouch, a grab on the face — the head's — noisy pulls), the floor (hip crouch, deep crouch, all fours + rise, kneel, sit, hover heal, foot lift, child crouch/walk), walking (hip walks, stepping chest drag), lifting (suspension — which also gates the worst single-tick move through the lift-off, 80mm: it read 380-620 — and lift-then-lower), pins (hand/foot pinned under far drags and a crouch), self-collision (arm across the chest, hand onto forearm, foot across thigh / onto shin, legs crossed, the FK limb sweeps), pose utilities, hold-still. Gates are regression guards set from measured behaviour, not specs; all eight rigs pass 120/120. **Name a bone by its ID in `--custom`** (`chest_2`, not `chestUpper` — see the node id/name note in the follower-addons paragraph; an ad-hoc run once grabbed a follower's duplicate `chestUpper` and measured a phantom). The gates re-set when the old solver went, each with its reason in `realphases.cpp`: heel RISE counts as feet drift (the slouched head pull-up goes 11-15mm onto the balls of the feet: 20), a walk's trailing heel may rest lifted ~2cm with the hip exactly on the cursor (30), the stepping chest drag's thigh rotates 14° mid-step to keep the foot's heading (16), the slow noisy pull's ACCUMULATED idle reversal travel is 20mm over ~600 ticks at 0.03mm a tick (30), the flick-stop reads the follower's one-tick trail (8), a strained release moves the soft heel 2mm (5), the chest push-down must take the hips at least 20cm of its 25 (it read 8 while the crouch stalled against the bounded pull, so the gate is the stall's guard) and its planted feet may turn 12° (the oldest generation lifts its heels at that depth), the stand-back-up phase (a 30cm chest crouch made and released, then the chest pulled 30cm up) must raise the hips 24cm with the chest on the cursor (it read 10cm and 16-19cm short on all eight rigs before the upward yield), and the all-fours' contact gates are informational on the oldest generation, whose pelvis tilts back as the hip goes down (its thighs stop at −100°) so the folded trunk comes up and the hands stop 20cm above the floor. Leg diagnostics (`IK_HARNESS_LEG_TRACE`), `IK_HARNESS_ARM_TRACE`, `IK_HARNESS_VOLUME_TRACE`, `IK_CONTACT_TRACE`, `IK_STEP_TRACE`, `IK_SUSPEND_TRACE`. The six FOOT-PLACEMENT phases (`--only foot-place`: a 50cm stride, a 40cm step back and side step along the floor; a side step let go of and brought back in; two steps forward, one foot then the other; a foot lifted 20cm, carried 35cm and set down in one drag) gate the foot on the cursor (3mm), the hips' share of the travel (40%), the placed sole on the floor (12mm, any joint of the foot), no step, the knee twist (8°), the worst single-tick move (40mm; 50 where a STRAIGHT knee unlocks — the solver's fold retry moves it 6° in one tick, 44-49mm at the knee) and, after the multi-drag ones, the head back at its standing height and the knees straight; `RunResult::restPos` is the standing figure, before any prelude. The kneel also gates its STILL HOLD (worst jump 15mm, oscillation 3mm: they read 98mm and 27mm behind gates that only looked at where the knees ended), and the two getting-up phases (a kneel made and released, then the hip or the chest dragged back up) gate no lift-off, no step, the hips all the way up and nothing through the floor. `IK_HARNESS_EULER_TRACE="lFoot,lToe,…"` prints those bones' channels at a drag's start and end with their limits — which joint is stuck against what; `IK_HARNESS_POSE_TRACE` names the joint that jumped. The four getting-up phases (a kneel or a sit made and released, then the hip, the chest or the head taken back to rest) measure, on all eight rigs: the grab on the cursor to 0.08mm, heels 0-4mm over their standing height (11 once — measured against the ANKLE's own bind height: against the lowest pin's it read "7cm" on the rigs whose pin was the toes joint), mean lag 3-8mm, worst single-tick move of any joint 16-90mm, no lift-off, at most the oldest generation's two gathering steps; `IK_HARNESS_TRUNK_TRACE` prints cursor, grab, hip, knee, pin counts and the spine's channels per tick (stdout is unbuffered, so the rig's stderr events interleave in order). The four KNEE-DRAG phases (`--only knee-drag`: the knee swung 12cm out and pushed 15cm forward over its foot, a 35cm raise held, the raise brought back down) gate the knee's travel, the ankle on its spot (5mm; 2mm after the return, with the head and hips where they began), the ankle's hang under a raised knee fore-aft (60mm; the whole hang and the hips are informational since the weight shift, 2026-09-25), the weight over the standing foot (the centre of mass outside its footprint, 25mm), the other foot, no step, no lift-off, and the worst single-tick move (45mm planted — the male rig's knee stands a little behind straight and leaves it in one fold retry — and 70 through a raise, whose first 8cm of height are 25cm of swing, the toes half a metre further out on that lever). The four ELBOW-DRAG phases (`--only elbow-drag`: a bent arm's elbow swung out — the arm bent first by a hand drag made and let go of — the same with the hand PINNED, a straight arm's elbow raised 20cm beside the body, and on all fours an elbow swung out) gate the elbow's travel along its arc, the hand on its place (40mm soft / 1mm pinned / 30mm on the floor), the shoulder socket where it was (25mm; 50 for the raise, which used to ride 10cm up on a shrugged collar), the head, no step, jumps and a still hold. The two CROUCH-LEAN phases (`--only crouch-lean`: from a 30cm crouch made and let go of, the upper chest dragged forward by half its lever — its height over the hips: the three-bone spines carry it 21cm up, the others 29 — and by its whole lever, beyond reach) gate the chest on the cursor where it can be (3mm; it reads 0.02-1.2, and 41 before), the spine's worst twist or side-bend channel (3°; it reads 0.00-0.02, and 20 at its limit before), the LEAST-flexed spine joint below the grab (≥ 3°: an S-curve has one extended; it reads 7-30), at most the two steps one generation takes once the hinge has raised her hips, jumps, and a still hold (it read 30cm a tick).
