The four TRUNK-BOW phases (`--only trunk-bow`: the upper chest down a third of its lever and forward four fifths; the head down a sixth and forward a half; the bow and back up in one drag; the bow let go of and the chest then taken back to where it sits at rest) gate the grab on the cursor (3mm), no step, the fold SHARED — the pelvis's pitch at least 10° (7 by the head), every spine joint below the grab at least 8° (3) and none over 26 — no spine twist, the feet, jumps, a still hold, an exact one-drag return (head 1.5mm, pelvis 0.2°) and a two-drag one (head 30mm, pelvis and spine 3°). The eight HEAD-RIGHT phases (`--only head-right`: hips 10cm back; a bow by the upper chest; the hinge and home; a bow let go of and the chest taken back up; the head itself dragged down and forward; the head pulled 15cm back and a little down — the most-bent neck bone under 8°, it read 20-27, and the gaze between −10° and 12° — and 15cm sideways — the neck under 20°, the head's tilt under 25°, they read 40 and 43-50; and a walking bow LET GO OF MID-STEP, four ticks into its hold; every phase of the suite now also REPORTS where the head looks, `gaze over the horizon at the end`, as an information row) gate the gaze over the horizon (`RunResult::gazeStartDeg` / `gazeDragEndDeg` / `gazeEndDeg`, `headRollEndDeg`: −10° at worst after the hinge, where riding read −23 to −29), what the NECK gives back in a bow (20-32°: how deep the chest tilts is the rig's, so the gaze itself is not gated there), an exact return (gaze 0.3°, head 1.5mm), a level head after two drags (±5°), a dragged head that goes where it is taken, and — the settle's guard — that the chest moves under 12mm, the head and an elbow under 25mm and the gaze under 4° between mouse-up and the end of the settle (they read 168mm, 443mm, 240mm and 62°). The two FLOOR-SEAT phases (`--only floor-seat`: the hips dragged 12cm DEEPER than the seat — the hip joint must rest within 25mm of its seat's clearance, where it sank 18cm — and a sit made and let go of, then reclined by the chest: the seat a live contact, the head 20cm and more back, no step, and the worst single-tick move under 120mm, where it read 680) — and the floor scenarios are sized from the RIG's clearances now (`floorClearanceAlong`: a kneeling shin lies front-down, a seat rests on what is under the pelvis), the kneel's knee gate and the lunge's depth with them. Two more FLOOR-SEAT phases since 2026-09-21: a sit LAID DOWN ON HER BACK by the chest (the pelvis's pitch at the end under −65° — it read −35 to −45 — no spine joint arched 10°, the chest within 40mm of where its back rests, worst single-tick move 80mm — it read 157 — hold 25mm and 2mm of oscillation, nothing through the floor) and, let go of, SAT BACK UP in a second drag (the pelvis rolled 55° up again, the chest up three quarters of the way, no lift-off; where she SAT is not always where she can sit again — the seat slid a few centimetres on the way down and the thighs meet their flexion limit sooner from there — so the grab's distance from the old spot is informational). And the USER-SIZED KNEEL (`--only kneel`: the rig's kneel 2cm deeper and 4cm shorter): worst single-tick move 80mm (it read 209), a still hold, the pelvis's tilt — root and pelvis bone together — under 25° (52), no step. Two gates re-set with the solver's pull: the over-deep lunge's pelvis roll (8°; 7.3 on the character rig) and the kneel-limb's hips (75mm). The three POSED-START phases (`--only posed-start`: a pose that leans — the spine flexed with FK — grabbed by the hand and held still; a bow let go of, then a hand raised; and the leaning pose's chest taken 25cm further forward) gate no step and a still head (2mm; it read 78-253 with 1-3 steps), no step and the head within 9cm under the raised hand (it read 177-352), and that the last one DOES step (the rule forgives the start, not the drag). The five SEATED phases (`--only seated`: a chair-height sit made by the hips and let go of, then the hips — or the pelvis bone, what a click finds — PINNED: the chest leaned 30° forward and reclined 20° along its own arc about the hips, a hand reached a forearm past the arm's length, a foot put out) gate the grab on the cursor (7 / 4 / 10mm for the leans — they read 27 and 77 before — 2mm for the limbs), the pelvis ROCKING the way of the lean (4°; it read 0), the hips where they were, no step, jumps and a still hold. The two KNEEL-LIMB phases (`--only kneel-limb`: from a kneel made and let go of, a hand raised 40cm — hips and both knees within 2cm; they read 0.3mm, and 283 before — and a knee drawn up 25cm with the hips within 4cm and the OTHER knee within 3) and the LUNGE (`--only lunge`: a foot slid 60cm back and let go of, then the hip taken 38cm down: the pelvis square to 6° of roll and of heading — it reads 1.4-3.3 — no step, nothing through the floor; how far down the back knee comes is the rig's legs' and informational). The two LIFTED-FOOT phases (`--only lifted-foot`: a foot lifted 10cm by one drag and let go of, then a hand dragged — the foot must stay where it was, 5mm; it read 97-99 — and then a 25cm crouch, which must land it as a second pin with nothing through the floor). The six LIMB-GRAB phases (`--only limb-grab`: a thigh grabbed by the socket and taken 8cm sideways; mid-thigh raised 15cm and swung 8cm out; the upper arm raised 15cm; the forearm raised 12cm; the spine grabbed between two joints) gate the point on the cursor where it can be (1-2mm; it reads 0.0005-0.06), the HEIGHT gained where the cursor leaves the point's sphere (150mm ± 10-15: it reads 150.0 for the thigh, 150-157 for the arm), the hips going with a socket grab by the cursor's own travel (6-10cm: never the thigh's length), the shoulder socket under a raised upper arm (30mm; it reads 4 — no shrug), the foot under a swung thigh, steps and jumps. The five TRUNK-FOLLOW phases (`--only trunk-follow`: the upper chest pulled 20cm back, forward and sideways, the head 20cm back, and a 6cm excursion brought home) gate the grab on the cursor (3mm; 12 sideways, where the spine's side bend runs into its limits), the hips along (4cm), steps (4; 2 sideways), the feet (40mm: the pelvis stays level under a trunk drag, so a sideways weight shift stands the far foot 3cm up on its toes), the head's worst single-tick move (60mm), the mean drag lag (25mm; it reads 2.7), a still hold, and the excursion's exact return with no step; the stepping chest drag's arrival gate came down from 150mm to 5 with it. The three GIRDLE-GRAB phases (`--only girdle-grab`: the pelvis bone 10cm back, the first spine bone 8cm sideways, a thigh socket 10cm forward — found by structure, the generations name them differently) gate the GRABBED joint on the cursor (1mm; it reads 0.02-0.10), the hips' share of the drag (80%; it read 22%), the feet (15mm: hips forward stand 12mm up on the balls of the feet), no step, jumps and a still hold; the HIPS-BACK WALK (`--only hip-back-walk`: the hip 25cm back, through the hinge into steps) gates the head's worst single-tick move (50mm; `RunResult::headJumpMax`; it read 158). The three HIP-HINGE phases (`--only hip-hinge`: hips 10cm back; a half squat, 10cm down and 12 back; the hinge and home again) gate the hip on the cursor, the head AHEAD of the ankles, the feet, no step, jumps, a still hold and an exact return (head 1.5mm). The two HIP-SWAY phases (`--only hip-sway`: the hip 8cm sideways; the same and back) gate the hip on the cursor, both knees' bend (8°), the head's place over the hips (35mm), a lifted heel (15mm), no step, and an exact return (head 1mm, roll 0.2°); the roll itself is informational (it is the stance's). **Known soft spots:** a held BOW rests with its weight about 2cm past its toes (the 4.4cm of follow the trunk cannot give is slack on the balance row, under the 6cm a step needs — the weight or a step, and the design chose the weight); a bow taken well off to one side (23-45° off the heading) is part bow, part walk, with the spine's twist near its limit; a SEATED figure must be PINNED by the hips to be one (no props: unpinned, a lean from a sit is a crouch's — the hips hinge back and up off the seat); a STANDING figure with pinned hips slips a planted foot 4mm under a 12° pelvis rock (straight legs have no length to give; the ball rows and the hip pin split the difference); a seated reach by the HAND hinges at the hips since 2026-10-28 (THE SEAT ROCKS UNDER A HAND DRAG TOO) but without the reach-down fold's reference; dragged straight forward beyond reach it rests short with its flexion unspent (the bounded pull, by design); a mid-thigh point pushed straight FORWARD is a knee pushed over its planted foot (the point rests 6cm short and sinks 5cm: pull it up a little and the foot lets go); a followed trunk drag keeps the pelvis LEVEL (the sway's roll and the root's rotation are the pelvis drag's), so a sideways weight shift stands the far foot 3cm up on its toes (the sway WAS tried there, the root's rotation brought in at 800/rad² for the roll alone: the heel came down to 2cm, the grabbed chest went 5mm further from its cursor — the spine's side bend spent undoing the roll — and a stepping chest drag's knee tremble rose from 8mm to 20, the roll's reference jumping at every landing: rejected); backward the support has 4cm of room behind the weight, so a chest pulled 10cm back already takes two small steps — and that is RIGHT: a lean-back allowance that kept her feet still was built (2026-09-20, harness-green on all eight rigs) and thrown out the same day by the first in-app screenshots, which showed her tilted back like a plank with her weight behind her heels where the two small steps leave her upright and balanced; a chest brought HOME after the stance has walked leans back over it rather than walking home; a chest dragged 45cm sideways trails its cursor by up to 9cm mid-drag (side steps are clamped at the other foot) and arrives after; a STRAIGHT arm's elbow still carries its arm rigidly (there is no swivel to hold the hand through); a user-pinned hand's ORIENTATION is held too, so the wrist's range bounds how far its elbow swivels; **THE HALF KNEEL** (2026-09-22; `--only kneel-limb`'s third phase, `gallery_floor.txt` B2): a knee drawn up out of a kneel — to hip height and forward, past what its shin reaches over the tucked foot, so the foot lets go — swings its foot UNDER it and lands it ahead, flat, the shin coming down from the knee, the other knee down. The hang held the ankle at its offset from the knee AS THE DRAG FOUND IT (`kneeHangOffset` in `solveIk`) — under it for a standing leg, BEHIND it for a kneeling one whose shin lies on the floor — so the foot trailed at knee height behind, the shin folded to its 155° limit and the foot up against the thigh. Four things: **the hang's target is where a FULL-LENGTH shin from the knee's goal meets the ankle's floor height** (`kneeHangOffset` since 2026-09-22: behind the knee by sqrt(shin² − height²) in the direction the drag found the ankle, straight under it once the knee is a shin's length up; a standing leg's shin is vertical and its knee a shin's length up — bit-for-bit what it was). Until then it SWUNG the found offset to the vertical one with `glm::mix` over `kKneeUnderLift` — a LERP between two 33cm vectors 100° apart, whose midpoint is 21cm long: mid-swing the ankle's spring (1e4-1e5 through the release) asked the ankle CLOSER to the knee than the shin is long, and the one answer is to FOLD the knee. Every rig folded (the base rig's knee flexion rose 103 → 122° and recovered as its foot slid out flat, and the 25cm draw raised the knee 35-48cm for 25 asked, the under-target hauling it up to make room for the shin); a half-size character's — kneeling with its foot tucked under the shin, the ankle and metatarsals at their limits — ran its knee to its 155° limit, twisted the leg 33° to get the folded foot's toes level, and flung the foot up over the knee (the thigh at its 85° ABDUCTION limit at the end, a gimbal-lock trap of the YZX order — flexion and twist axes near parallel, no knee-preserving path back and the posture pull too weak to pay the knee row for a detour: the foot 55cm up, the round-82 sweep's dwarf). (The lerp's code and its probe `IK_JS_KNEE_HANG_LERP` were removed in the 2026-09-24 refactoring round.) THE HANG PULLS DOWN TOO (`place.axisScale.y` = the hang's share of the merged row; `IK_JS_KNEE_HANG_FLAT` is the flat one): it held the ankle in the floor's plane only, and a foot lifting off behind a rising knee had nothing to bring it down — the x/z target met as well from above as from the floor, and the same character's shin folded up along the thigh; the target never lies under the floor now, so its pull down is the leg's gravity. AND THE LAST CENTIMETRE IS THE FOOT'S: a knee at hip height is a centimetre short of a shin's length over the floor on every rig (7cm on the oldest generation, whose shin is that much longer than its thigh), the hang asks the foot under the knee over the last of the reach (`kKneeUnderReachFrom/To` 0.25-0.9 of the shin's length), and the ankle's floor contact should lift the knee that centimetre — but the knee sits inside its cursor row's Huber knee (0.1mm), where the model sees the row's whole 1e6 and never proposes the rise the true cost (2 against the hang's 7) would take: the foot rested 9-14cm behind the knee with its toes curled against the ankle's limit. The knee's GOAL rises instead, to where a vertical shin stands on the floor, the WHOLE deficit under a cap ramped in by the cursor's reach (`kKneeGoalRaiseShare` 0.3 of the shin; a share of the deficit is not self-consistent — it leaves the foot on the floor behind a knee the model will not lift): the knee ends 1-7cm over the cursor, the foot under it. And the foot's orientation holds keep `kKneeTrailLevelShare` 0.05 at the trail's end (the toes' 4e4 → 2000): faded to exactly zero with the spring, the male rig's foot landed flat and flipped onto its heel in the next tick, toes 18cm up, the ankle's posture pulling it back to the kneel's tucked value (a 164mm pop; `IK_JS_KNEE_TRAIL_LEVEL`); the ankle's PLACE spring — the foot's floor spot, still strong at 20cm of lift — swings with it by as much as the found shin was NOT vertical, and such a foot's hang pulls `kKneeSwingHangScale` 3 times harder (at the standing hang's 100/m² it rested 12cm behind the knee); the foot's sole and toes turn FLAT as the foot TRAILS — over `kKneeLiftFull`..`kKneeTrailFull` of lift, once it is off the floor — as they do with the rise (held tucked, toes-down, a foot asked forward along the floor ran its toe tips into the floor's rows and folding up was cheaper; turned by the lift's FIRST centimetres, as it was for a round, a foot still standing on its tucked toes was asked flat and its toes pushed 18mm into the floor's 1e7 rows — a solve starting at a cost of 6000 that rejected every step, and the kneeling knee shot 8-10cm out and back, 135-139mm in a tick on the three oldest generations, whose kneel lies flattest: THE OLDER GENERATIONS' KNEE SHOOT of round 80's soft spots, 60-71mm now on all eight and gated at 90 in the 25cm draw); and the floor's rows come onto the letting-go foot WITH its release (`floorScale` in `solveIk`, at 1 − plant; `IK_JS_KNEE_FLOOR_SWITCH`) — exempt while planted and switched on all at once at the lift's first centimetre, a dozen 1e7 rows landed on toes that stand 2-5mm "through" the floor by their flesh's measure. Tried and reverted the same day, each measured across the rigs: the knee's reach measured from the ankle as it STANDS rather than its ground-healed pin (right in principle, 10-15mm worse in the 25cm draw and mixed in the half kneel), the knee's goal leaving the planted reach at the pin's pace (no effect), the dragged knee's ball row softened to 1e4 (the half kneel 239-258), and removing the place spring's spot swing (the foot folded up behind and flipped 25cm; it is the swing that keeps it out of the knee's-limit basin). and **the leg's twist price comes back as the foot lets go** (geometrically with `plant`; `IK_JS_KNEE_LIFT_TWIST_FREE`): free — turning the thigh is what swinging a knee over its PLANTED foot is — a lifted leg twisted 63° and stood its knee 10cm out with the foot behind and in, a frog's leg. On all eight rigs: the knee rises 30-42cm, the foot lands within 6cm of under the knee (−300 behind before) and under 12cm over the floor (250), the shin under 110° (155), the other knee within 3cm, twist 3-5°, worst tick 56-69mm (69-128 before the reach target; the 25cm draw's knee step 18-57 where it was 60-71, its hips 6-10mm where they were 11-16, and it rises the 25cm asked); the phase's drag is sized by the THIGH (0.93 up, 0.67 forward — 42cm on a half-size character pulled the knee above its hip and the leg into the air). (the 2026-09-22 sweep's dwarf, its foot 55cm up, and the character whose other knee moved 56mm, both land it now: 5-6mm ahead of the knee, shins 89-98°). **THE DRAW'S POPS AND DRIFTS** (2026-09-23; the in-app 42cm draw popped 95-97mm at a toe on two custom characters, the male rig's leg flipped 88mm at the top of its draw, a wide-kneeling character's knee drifted through its still hold, and — a sweep regression of the same round — the KNEELING knee slid 38-68mm across the floor on four characters). Five things, each measured across the eight rigs and the 23 characters: (1) **the foot's hang target is EASED** at the pins' pace (`kneeHangUnder`, `m_jsKneeUnderEased`, `kPinEaseStep`; `IK_JS_KNEE_UNDER_NO_EASE`): where the knee's goal reaches a shin's length up, the reach point sqrt(shin² − height²) collapses fastest and the goal's raise took the knee its last centimetres in two ticks — the ankle's target jumped 5cm in one, and the foot with it (112mm at a toe on a short-legged character); the reach point is now the hang's WHOLE offset (no mix with the offset as found: `kneeHangOffset` returns the on-floor point alone). (2) **THE THIGH'S LATERAL REACH — the YZX gimbal** (the lateral clamp in the `lifted` block; `IK_JS_NO_KNEE_LATERAL_REACH`): a thigh's rotation order puts its abduction channel about the fore axis INSIDE its flexion, so a thigh flexed to horizontal has NO abduction left — that channel ROLLS it — and its own twist is locked: a knee drawn up to hip height comes in front of its socket whatever the cursor asks sideways (lateral = thigh × cos(flexion) × sin(abduction limit) — the cos(flexion) gone since 2026-10-27, when the thigh's abduction stopped being a roll: THE ROTATION ORDER, below, the limit read off the thigh's own channel by side and direction; the plain geometry — a 0.25 floor under cos(flexion), tried as a hedge, left the foot 21mm behind its knee on a wide-kneeling character and 48 on the oldest generation, where the geometry lands it 6mm ahead and 8 behind). Asked where the kneel had it (9cm outboard on the base rig, 18 on a wide-kneeling character) the knee's row went unmet by that much at the top, its bounded pull leaned on the pelvis and the shin's side channel through the hold, and the pose flipped basins — the shin's side channel from one limit to the other, the pelvis 7° in a tick, a toe 68-74mm; the character's knee drifted 74mm through its still hold (0.1 now). (3) **the lifted leg's twist price is the MOTION's, not the channel's name** (the stiffness loop, `kTwistAlongFrom/Full` 0.8-0.95; `IK_JS_TWIST_BY_NAME`): with the thigh flexed to horizontal the abduction channel IS a twist, at a swing channel's price, and rolling the thigh 22° in one tick — the shin's side channel crossing its whole range to keep the foot — was the cheapest way to a knee goal 3cm inboard at the top of the male rig's draw (a toe 88mm; 30 now). A channel whose axis, as it stands (the rotation-order prefix applied), lies within that cosine band of the bone's own direction carries the twist price, geometrically with how far along it lies (a 0.6-0.9 band took the third generation's 25cm draw to 60.07mm against its 60 gate). (4) **THE KNEELING KNEE IS A HARD CONTACT under a leg drag** (`IkRig::boundLivePins`, `isLegJointAboveFoot`, `legOf`; `IK_KNEE_DRAG_KNEES_SLIDE`): soft — unilateral, sliding once dragged past 2.5cm, its hold fading with the joint's 2cm rise — it was where the drawn knee's unmet pull went once the lifted thigh could no longer roll toward a goal the cursor put out of its reach (the sweep found it: the planted knee slid 38-68mm on four characters, 22 on the male). TRAP: `effectorIsTrunk()` is a MASS test (subtree > 5%) and a dragged SHIN passes it — the rule as first written never fired; by the LEG (`legOf(effector) >= 0`, the thigh socket a joint hangs from) it holds the knee to a hundredth of a millimetre on every rig and character; `IK_BOUND_TRACE` prints the rule's inputs, `IK_JS_CONTACT_FADE_TRACE` each live contact's rise, its `gone` and which unload term drives it. (5) **A ROW THAT COMES IN ON A JOINT ALREADY THROUGH ITS SURFACE ASKS ONLY THAT IT GO NO DEEPER** (`floorAllow`, `m_jsFootAllow`; `IK_JS_KNEE_FLOOR_NO_ALLOW`): the release's floor rows come in gently by their SHARE (`floorScale`, round 82), but a row's residual is the whole depth from its first tick, and the third generation's tucked toe tips stand 15-18mm through the floor by their flesh's measure (2-5mm on the others): at a fiftieth of 1e7 they were already pushed out, the foot rolled and yawed to its limits, the shin's side channel too, the thigh abducted 15° in one tick — the knee shot 8cm out and back (84mm at the shin, 68 before the hard knee took away the 3mm the other knee used to give). The depth at the release's first tick is the row's allowance, a constant of the drag, given back over the trail (the foot in the air and level by then): 31mm, and its 25cm draw's knee step 55 → 25; the other seven rigs bit-identical. On all eight rigs the half kneel's worst tick is 27-42mm (57-69 before this round), the other knee 0.0001mm, the foot 8-31mm behind its knee (−1 to −59: the oldest generation's −59 is −8); the in-app draw 60 → 26mm; the two characters' toe pops 95-97 → 27-40; the sweep unchanged at 34 near-misses and one real (the asymmetric rig's own), 22 of 23. `IK_HARNESS_EULER_TICKS="lThigh,lShin,…"` prints those bones' channels EVERY tick (which channel crosses its range in the tick a joint jumps), `IK_JS_KNEE_TRACE` the knee's goal after each projection and the foot's place/ankle/under targets with the spring and hang weights; `IK_JS_ROW_TRACE` now names the channels the trial CLAMPED (asked/got). SOFT SPOT: the in-app gallery's 42cm knee draw, sized for the base figure, takes a shorter-legged character's knee above a shin's length up — the foot hangs 18cm up under it, rightly; a knee drawn up only as far as its shin reaches over the tucked foot swings over that foot, by design, and does not make a half kneel; a lunge pressed LOWER than its landed back knee allows folds the front knee sideways (the front thigh at its flexion limit, the leg with nowhere else to go), and a wide-stance lunge's front knee tracks 8-12cm inside its foot (the zero-twist leg plane: the crouches' mild valgus); a knee whose foot is USER-pinned never lets go of it (pulled up, the knee stays over its foot); a knee taken 30cm across the other leg is simply out of reach (thigh and shin at their limits) and its first fast tick works 11ms; a straight knee unlocks in one 4-5cm move; a figure sat back up from lying and dragged PAST upright leans on after the cursor into a forward tuck (what stopped her short before was the active set's hair trap, not her thighs); a small bone grabbed beside the spine (a pectoral) and dragged far sideways rests 1-2cm under its cursor (it drops as the chest rolls into the lean, and a standing body cannot be asked up); on a slightly asymmetric CHARACTER a symmetric gesture ends a centimetre or two lopsided (one knee 4 degrees more bent than the other for a leg a millimetre longer: the body's own, amplified by straight-leg geometry); on custom characters (the sweep) every real miss is answered but one rig's own 6mm mirror asymmetry (34 near-misses remain); a lying figure cannot be STOOD up in one drag (she is sat up first, by design); dragged past the trunk's reach a seated figure slides along the floor on her seat; hands PLACED on the floor do not prop a seated figure whose trunk is dragged unless they are pinned; a kneeling figure's hips stop where her rigid thighs stop them over her planted knees (pushed lower she rests above the cursor, pelvis upright: drag the hips BACK to sit onto the heels), and on the newest generation the head still moves 8cm in the tick its knees land; all fours is made from a KNEEL by the chest (hands pushed to the floor ahead of a standing figure are still answered by a deep squat), and pushed lower than the shoulders' natural height it is a push-up's bottom, elbows bent and the back rounded; a landed hand rolls onto its palm through the rig's 2.5cm slip, so its wrist creeps forward over a few ticks; the oldest generation's hands land on their fingertips close together under the chest (its shoulder flexion ends at −75°); PRONE is made BY THE HIPS (2026-09-22; `--only prone`, `gallery_floor.txt` section A3; broken by round 77's hand ceiling until 2026-09-23 — see AND THE PRONE DESCENT HAD BEEN BROKEN BY IT SINCE): from all fours drag the hips far forward and down — a thigh's length and more ahead of the planted knees — and she lies on her belly, thighs forward from the knees, shins folded up behind, hands where they were planted, face down (turn the head after). Three rules made it: **the head is no prop under a pelvis drag** (`IkRig::updateContacts`, `IK_HEAD_PROPS`: planted as a live contact when the face touched the floor ahead of her, it stood her on her head, a tripod of knees and face with the hips frozen 40cm from their cursor); **the pelvis's kneel-tilt price fades as the hips go AHEAD of a planted knee** (`m_jsProneShare`, `kProneFootFreeFrom/Full` 8-25cm of travel ahead, `IK_JS_NO_PRONE`: dear on her knees, the thigh met its 35° extension limit with the pelvis 22° from upright and the hips stopped 30cm over the floor, 30cm from the cursor, on every rig — a body lowered onto its belly pitches face-down); and **the foot behind such a knee gives up its height rows** (the ball's height and the ankle's place, let go of outright — its sole's and toes' orientation holds stay, and the ankle turns under them as the shin comes up; let go of too the foot, held at its dorsiflexion limit under the tucked toes, sprang 10cm in a tick; faded by weight the feet flipped 16cm: a hard row goes in the last of its fade; its sole's hold is two decades softer, and — a hole this uncovered — **a HARD live contact exempts only its own joint from the floor rows, not its subtree**: a planted knee had exempted its whole leg, and a foot let go of behind it had no floor at all, 36-45mm through it on two characters). The base rigs lie with the hip joint 14-20cm up (on the belly's flesh), symmetric to 0.03mm; the newest generation's long thighs and the third generation are not flat at a thigh's length forward (informational), and the oldest rig's arms fold under and meet (317mm lopsided: the symmetry gate is off there); under the still cursor at the end the hands twitch 5-12cm as the body settles forward (the jump gate is 150; a toon character reads 223) — the arms fold under the torso and the torso's volume rows push the hands off their 1e8 pins by 4cm, the rig's slide then moves the pin in one go and the hand re-places itself; NOT a taut-arm effect (a taut-arm slide of the hand's pin was tried on 2026-09-22 and did nothing here — the arms are folded, not taut — while it slid the hands in under a rising figure and took an elbow drag's hand along: removed) — a soft spot. **GETTING UP OFF THE BELLY** (2026-09-22; `gallery_floor.txt` section A3's second half): drag the HIPS up — straight up, or up and back — and she comes up onto all fours, hands and knees planted; from there the hips again, or the chest, stands her up. It works with the hands still folded under her, and better with each hand first dragged forward and down onto the floor ahead of her shoulders (a push-up's hand placement: 0.4mm on the cursor, the hips 29cm up, worst tick 58mm). Three rules (`solveIk`, the live-contact block): **the rise does not unload a HAND whose arm has length to give** (`armSlack`: the wrist's contact node's reach from its limb junction under 0.95 of `IkRig::liveSpan` — the arm's span as the contact formed; `IK_JS_RISE_UNLOADS_HANDS`), and such a hand gets a one-sided CEILING at its planted height (`handCeilings` in the plane source, `IK_JS_HANDS_UNILATERAL`; a ceiling, not a hold in y — a hand that lands on its fingertips still rolls onto its palm 13cm lower; and the WRIST's own contact only, `m_jsWristOf[node] == node`: on a carpal's contact it blocked one generation's palm roll): unloaded by the rise from its first centimetre, as every other contact is, the hands left the floor as the trunk swung up with the pelvis and she got up off her belly into a kneeling plank with both hands in the air — a body pushed up off its belly keeps its hands down while the arms straighten, and lets go of them taut (the lift-off); **nor a KNEE the hips were LOW over when the drag began** (`kneeBelowHips`: the knee's SOCKET — its parent, not the root: the hip joint sits a hand above the thigh's socket, and read from it a kneeling thigh was already 'past' its length — stood under `kKneeUnloadHeight` 0.6 of the thigh's length over the knee in `m_jsStartPos`, a constant of the drag: read live, a kneeling figure's hips passed the thigh's height, the knees unloaded and came back the tick they dipped, and she could not get up out of a kneel at all; `IK_JS_RISE_UNLOADS_KNEES`, `IK_JS_KNEE_CEILING_TRACE`), which keeps its own CEILING too, fading with its hold (`kneeCeilings` → the plane source at 10^(−6·gone)): unloaded from the rise's first 20cm the knees let go and the shins swung 14-16cm in a tick, free in its height the knee rose 13cm toward the rising hips, lifted off and re-planted (a 15cm pop at the shins), and held to the floor until the rise was complete the knees of a figure getting up out of a KNEEL jumped 21cm when let go of at once — a knee a drag found its hips UP over (a kneel) keeps the free height it always had (a ceiling on it, even one fading with the hold, lagged the hips 22mm and let go 9cm at once); and **the head chain is never SEEDED as a contact under a pelvis drag** either (`seedPoseContacts`, the same `IK_HEAD_PROPS`: a face resting on the floor at the press was a prop the next drag stood on). A drag that begins with the hips low over the knees keeps its knees for the whole drag — it comes up onto all fours; to stand from there is the next drag's. On all eight rigs the suite is unchanged (118/118); the toon character's prone-hold jump went from 223-246mm to 31 with the knee's ceiling. **AND ON A HALF-FLAT PRONE THE LIFTED HAND LETS GO AND THE KNEES STAY** (2026-09-26; `m_jsContactRiseUnloaded` → `IkRig::setRiseUnloadedContacts` / `riseUnloaded`, `kRiseUnloadedShare` 0.9, `IK_LIFT_TAUT_ONLY`; `kKneeUnloadHeight` 0.6 → 0.8): the newest generation's heaviest character does not lie flat — his arms as struts hold the shoulders up, the hip joint 26cm up, the thighs 46° from vertical — and the gallery's prone-up on him POPPED 144mm at a finger and stood him up into a BEAR CRAWL, hands and feet. Two rules, each a mismatch between two measures of one thing. (1) The solve unloads a hand contact with the body's rise when its arm has no length to give by EITHER measure — the arm's own stretch (`IkRig::liveContactStretch`) or the junction's reach over the span — while the rig's lift-off read the junction's reach ALONE: his right arm was straight from the shoulder (stretch 0.98) with its collar shrugged forward (reach/span 0.81), so its hold faded to nothing while the hand lay on the floor, and the weightless hand then rode the rising chest 17cm into the air with its pin standing — neither the solve's (no hold) nor the hang's (`hangArmHeld`: the pin stands) — until the torso's volume rows swung the free arm 144mm in one tick. A hand the rise has unloaded (the rise term past 0.9 of the six-decade fade, a hold under 400/m²; its arm not slack) is TAUT to the lift-off now: 2cm up for three ticks it lets go, and its arm is the hang's — turned plumb, its fingertips brushing the floor. A landing hand is never one (no rise unloads it), which is what the round-79 objection to the stretch as a lift-off test was about. Set at 0.999 the flag came two ticks before the pop and the three-tick confirmation put the release IN the pop tick — as had an 8cm free lift-off, taut or not, tried first (`kLiveContactFreeLift`, removed). (2) A knee stays a support under a rise while the socket stands under `kKneeUnloadHeight` of the thigh over it AS THE DRAG FOUND IT — the cosine of the thigh's lean from vertical: all fours puts it at 0.94-0.97 on every reference rig (a hip drag up from there STANDS her; `IK_JS_KNEE_CEILING_TRACE` prints it), the base rig's flat prone at 0.44, his half-flat prone at 0.70 — past the 0.6 it was, so his knees lifted with the hips; 0.8, a thigh 37° from vertical, is a tenth of the thigh clear of both. His prone-up: pop 144 → 54mm (a foot, as the knees settle), knees down (68mm), the hips 49mm short of a base-sized cursor (his thighs' length over planted knees); the base figure BIT-IDENTICAL throughout (its prone reads 0.44 either way, its hands stay slack); all eight rigs 133/133; the 23-character sweep reads as round 98 left it, character for character — 40 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23; in-app 188/0 with NO shot changed. An ELBOW RELAX for a lifted hand's arm (toward 10° at 3°/tick, collision-stopped) was written and REMOVED the same day: nothing exercised it — the lifted arm was already straight (its fold channel at 10°; the 57° beside it was the forearm's twist), and the oldest generation's kneel-up is bit-identical with and without it. SOFT SPOT: the lifted hand hangs with its fingertips brushing the floor (7mm into their clearance) and never re-plants — a released contact re-arms only 3cm clear of the floor, which a hand hanging fingers-down from a shoulder at all-fours height never is. **AND THE PRONE DESCENT HAD BEEN BROKEN BY IT SINCE — FOR NINE ROUNDS** (2026-09-23; `m_jsTargetDown`, `kHandCeilingDownFrom/Full` 2-8cm, `IK_JS_HAND_CEILING_ALWAYS`): the slack hand's CEILING (this round's, above: a hand held DOWN at its planted height while its arm has length to give) also stops a body coming DOWN onto its arms — from all fours the hips taken a thigh's length forward and down need the arms to FOLD under the body, the wrists pitching up off the palm-flat height on their planted fingertips, and capped at 3.5cm they could not: the hips stood 45cm up, 43cm from their cursor, on EVERY rig (150mm on the belly, 114 from the cursor, before the ceiling came), and nobody saw it — the prone phase's rows were ALL INFORMATIONAL (two generations never lie flat), and the in-app gallery kept passing on the base figure only because its shorter drag made a sphinx-like pose that its assertions accepted; it was found when the gallery was run on four custom characters (the hips 20-24cm from the cursor, the head tucked under). The ceiling is a rise-time rule: it now stands down with the target's DOWNWARD travel since the drag began (none under 2cm, all from 8, figure-scaled — a function of the target) — for EVERY arm: a fold-only release (`kCeilingFoldReach/StrutReach` 0.80-0.92 of the wrist's reach over the arm's span, its probe `IK_JS_CEILING_FOLD_BAND` and code removed 2026-09-24) was tried for a day and dropped — both kinds of arm end taut, and from the half-released band two characters' hands rose and let go, a 41cm collapse. What stops the collapse is the RIG: **NO LIFT-OFF UNDER A DESCENT** (`IkRig::updateContacts`, `m_lastDownIntent`; `IK_LIFT_OFF_DESCENDING`): a body the user takes DOWN by the hips does not let go of the hands and knees it comes down onto. Released, the strut-armed rigs' hands rose 14cm with the rearing trunk while still counted as contacts, and the moment the arms read taut they lifted off and the body came down 27-41cm in a tick (a heavy character's prone in the sweep: a 270mm jump and 38mm through the floor). Kept, the body comes down ON them: that character lies with the hip joint 203mm up (443 before, the arms as struts), two more characters read 53 and 112mm at worst, and the NEWEST GENERATION LIES DOWN TOO — the hip joint 225mm up where it stood 348 on straight arms, the pelvis 108° forward, a 33mm worst tick (its hands, held, slide under it at the rig's slip). The third generation alone still stands on its arms (418: its forearm ends dead straight with the wrist at its limits — a hand walk this design has no gesture for); the folding rigs are bit-identical. A STRUT HAND'S FRICTION was tried the same day and reverted: a taut hand under a descent given a 1e4 place hold (the pelvis pull rests it 5cm out, past the slip, so the pin walks) slid the FOLDING rigs' hands too — their arms end taut as well, extended behind them as the body slides over — and the base rig's hip stayed 360mm up. Two contact rules came with it, each from the in-app push-up that followed: **a HAND under an ELBOW on the floor is its own contact** (`IkRig::servedByPinAbove`, `isLimbTip`; `IK_ELBOW_SERVES_HAND`): lying flat with the arms folded under the chest, the elbows come first in the node order and were the arms' only contacts — a joint under a pin on its own path was 'served' by it, as a planted foot's toes are — and the elbow's contact is taut the moment the shoulder rises an upper arm's length, so a body pushed up off its belly lost its arms in the first centimetres and came up onto its knees with both hands folded at the chest, 40cm in the air; a limb's TIP (a real-mass joint with no real-mass joint below it) under a pin that is neither a tip nor foot-class is a contact of its own; **and an ELBOW over a hand on the floor is no contact at all** (`IkRig::servedByHandBelow`; `IK_ELBOW_CONTACTS`): unilateral, held in its place until the arm read taut, it slid along the floor as the body rose and let go in one tick when the arm straightened (the forearm 110mm, the hand 200) — a hand on the floor is its arm's contact, an elbow resting beside it incidental; for LEGS the rule stays the other way (a knee over its planted foot is a second support: a kneel). With them the prone descent is back on the six rigs that lie flat (the hip joint 134-201mm over the floor, the pelvis 88-114° forward, the base 150/99 as in round 76) and, in-app, on four of five custom characters (the grab 31-76mm from the cursor where it was 200-243; the heavy male still 202 — his hips stay up and his hands lift off, so the sweep's gate reads him informational), and the gallery's push-up off the belly passes as written (hips on the cursor to 2.7mm, hands 22-30mm up, no pop). THE THIRD GENERATION AND THE NEWEST STILL DO NOT LIE FLAT: the drag's travel outruns what the arm gives — the third's forearm ends dead straight with the wrist at its limits, a strut holding the shoulders up (hip 415), the newest's hands go taut and lift off (348) — a hand walk this design has no gesture for. **THE PRONE HIP HEIGHT IS GATED NOW** (`realphases.cpp`: ≤ its seat's clearance + 12cm) on every rig whose hands stay down and whose wrist does not end limit-bound (`RunResult::liveLiftOffs`, the live contacts lost tick to tick, is new; `limitBoundEnd(r, "lHand")`), informational on the others (the third, the newest, the oldest and the base's variant, whose wrists end bound though the last two lie flat) — a gate that cannot fire says nothing, and this one could not for nine rounds. SOFT SPOT (ANSWERED 2026-10-27, THE HAND LETS GO below): a hand dragged out from under a prone body had been a WEAK GESTURE — dragged far ahead it knotted the arm at its limits (elbow 135°, wrist 80°) and the push-up then threw it up 30-50cm, and the gallery's 20cm drags landed in one of TWO BASINS (the arms push straight and she is up on all fours, or they stay folded under and the chest stays down with the hips up) on differences of 1e-5 in the pose they began from; what threw the arm was the hand's hold going from everything to nothing in one solve once the arm was straight, and the gallery's gesture is the hands walked FORWARD under the shoulders now. A REPEATED DRAG IS BIT-IDENTICAL NOW (2026-09-23; `Armature::recomposePoseLocal`; `IK_DRAG_STATE_TRACE` prints, at every press, the incidental-contact flags, the hang arms, the post-step memory and a POSE CHECKSUM, and at every tick the target and the checksum; since 2026-09-26 also the pins the rig planted at the press — node, target, live/user/hard, the effector — and a `[drag-map]` row per tick: the cursor in model space, the grab offset, the target handed to the rig, the effector's position, and whether the offset was measured or the target mapped — which is what showed the pelvis snap's 2cm first-tick target): a kneel made, Reset Pose, and the same kneel again differed by 2e-5 in the checksum after the FIRST tick — same pose, same target — and the row trace showed every starting residual a few 1e-7 apart with the posture cost at the drag's very start 1.5e-7 off: the POSE was not the same. A zero Euler composed as `localBind · OR · I · OR⁻¹` is the bind to a float rounding, and a reset — or any joint brought back to exactly zero — left every world matrix 1e-7 off the freshly built figure's (`poseLocal = localBind` at build); a solve that runs out of iterations grows that to 2e-5 in a tick and, on a chaotic gesture, to another basin. A zero Euler is the bind itself, exactly, now: 130 ticks of the second kneel match the first to the last bit, and the eight rigs read 120/120 with one harness phase moved (the oldest generation's prone, by millimetres). A HAND SLID ALONG THE FLOOR LIES ON ITS PALM (2026-09-23; the hand slide in `solveIk`, `m_jsHandSlideEase`; `IK_JS_NO_HAND_SLIDE`): a dragged WRIST whose target is at the hand's floor height gets the landed hand's palm-flat frame (the bind's fingers line and palm normal), headed along the drag, at the foot slide's weights by the same share of the target's height, eased in from the rotation the drag found it at (`kPalmTurnStepDeg` a tick — asked flat at once, a hand folded under the chest swung its forearm 149mm in the drag's second tick). Nothing had said which way a dragged hand lies, and from a hand folded under a prone chest the arm's cheapest way forward was as often the elbow swung out sideways with the forearm pivoting (the hand ahead, the elbow out on the floor, the wrist at its limits) as the arm extending; the push-up that followed the folded one left the chest down, 23mm off its cursor. The gallery's push-up off the belly now places the hands UNDER THE SHOULDERS (14% of the view: dragged 20% they land ahead of the shoulders, and as the trunk pivots up over the knees the arms go taut before the hips arrive — a hand unloads and lifts by the round-77 rule, one arm straight and the other in the air) and reads 8mm on the cursor with both hands down and the arms straight. SOFT SPOTS LEFT: (the folded arm's ONE-TICK UNFOLD as the hips rise off the belly — 147mm at the forearm, `IK_JS_RETRY_TRACE` showing the soft cost climbing for 25 ticks and letting go at once — was the landed hand's PALM TARGET running 78° ahead of a hand that could not turn, and is gone since 2026-09-26: the palm eases' LEAD CAP in the hand-lay paragraph; 15mm now, the gallery's gate 40); the prone hold's hand twitch stays (exempting a planted hand's riders from the torso volumes was tried — behind `IK_JS_PLANTED_HAND_VOLUMES`, removed with its code 2026-09-24 — and let a finger jump 126mm mid-drag on the base rig, the torso then lying through the hand); a hand dragged well ahead of the shoulders knots the arm at its limits and the push-up throws it up. `IK_JS_CEILING_TRACE` prints the hand and knee ceilings and where their joints stand. **THE ARMS HANG AS SHE GETS UP OFF ALL FOURS** (2026-09-22; `gallery_floor.txt` A2's second half, `hang.<bone>` in the scripts — a bone's direction from straight down): got up by the CHEST from all fours she ended with both arms held straight out in front of her, tick after tick. The hang DID have the arms (a hand that lifts off the floor mid-drag leaves its arm to the hang, `hangArmHeld` false once the pin is gone) and wanted them plumb; what stopped them was the hang's own collision STOP, twice over (`IK_FK_DEPTH_TRACE`, a probe on `fkVolumeDepth` naming the pair): the collars shrugged forward on all fours put the mid-upper-arm point 2mm inside the chest's volume, the plumb's 'a little out' (0.12) asked the arm IN from the 0.27 it stood at, and a turn is ONE rotation — blocked inward, blocked downward. Three things (`hangIdleArms`): an arm RESTING AGAINST THE BODY is never asked further in (its outward share is kept as it stands, `IK_ARM_HANG_ASKS_IN`); the stop lets an arm SETTLE `kArmHangSettle` 1.5mm deeper than the drag FOUND it (`HangArm::depthAtPress`; read off the press, not the tick — a tolerance a tick is a creep, an arm hung into a hip by a side bend going in 0.3mm a tick for as long as the bend is held); and, since a chest is wider lower down (2mm a degree of swing: the allowance is spent 20° from plumb), a hang blocked by a volume it is inside asks for the same hang turned OUTWARD — the least angle up to `kArmHangOutMost` 12° whose turn goes `kArmHangOutThrough` 0.9 through, found by BISECTION (`kArmHangOutBisections` 8; in steps of 0.6° one arm took a step the other did not, and the male rig's un-bow ended 12mm lopsided at the hands — the symmetry gate's third catch), `IK_ARM_HANG_NO_OUT` — so the arm slides down the chest's surface and hangs where the chest lets it (coarse 3° steps taken at half a turn ratcheted it out to 35°). The `together` check of the order-independent stop reads the same allowance (`allowOf`; with the old one it zeroed every turn the per-arm stop had passed). AND A HAND HANGS FROM ITS STRAIGHT ARM (`kHandSlackShare` 0.97): a hand contact is held down, a ceiling on it, while the wrist's reach from its limb junction is under 0.97 of the arm's span, and the rise unloads it above — so as the shoulder rises it follows at the arm's length, stays on the floor under a push-up whose shoulders stop at the arm's length, and lifts off under a chest that goes on up. The share sits ABOVE the rig's taut test (`kLiveContactTaut` 0.95): at the same 0.95 a hand following its shoulder read taut and slack on alternate ticks, never three taut ticks running, never lifted off, and a figure stood up off all fours by the chest with one hand still a contact — the arm the solve's, the wrist flipped under the palm's rows, 27cm at a fingertip in one tick. (A hand TAUT FOR THE DRAG once taut was tried instead: it left the push-up in a plank, the hands in the air the moment the arms were straight.) On the base figure the upper arms hang 5-8° from plumb after the lift (67° before, one of them), no pop (54mm), and the prone push-up is unchanged (hands 19mm, hips +29cm). **OFF ALL FOURS BY THE CHEST SHE KNEELS UP** (2026-09-22; `--only allfours-up`, `gallery_floor.txt` A2's second half): a chest lifted off all fours came up toward her FEET — read as the cursor's whole upward travel the lift was getting up from its first centimetre, the knees unloaded and 6cm off the floor under a trunk still folded, the legs sent home — where a person comes up onto her knees first. A kneeling figure's trunk is to her hips over the planted knees what a seated one's is to her seat: **the rise of a kneel-start trunk drag is what the trunk cannot give about the hips at their KNEELING height** (`IkRig::kneelSeatHeight` — the root's bind height over the highest planted knee's, from that knee's contact; all fours has the hips there already, a child's pose 15cm lower; `m_jsKneelSeatY`, a constant of the drag; `IK_JS_KNEEL_RISES`), and a trunk found more than 45° from upright is kneeled up, not stood up, by that drag (the next one, from the kneel, stands her: the getting-up phases are unchanged). With the rise off nothing held a unilateral knee contact DOWN, and straightening the legs off the toes was the solve's cheap way to a chest asked 40cm up (the knees 4.5cm in the air): **the knees are held down by a ceiling** (`m_jsKneelHold`, 1 while the trunk can give what is asked — the target within its reach about the seat, let go of over the 5cm beyond — the UPWARD excess only, since 2026-09-25: a chest taken forward and down past a kneeling trunk's reach is a body going onto its hands and on toward the floor, whose knees stay where they are; let go of on ANY excess, a toon character's shorter trunk (0.28m about her seat, the base's 0.40) ran out 13cm before the in-app gallery's base-sized chest drag did, her knees came 11cm off the floor and she stood in a PLANK on her hands and toes, and the chest lifted off it afterwards threw a finger 424mm in a tick (27 now, the knees on the floor and the palms flat; the kneel-start phases are bit-identical on the eight rigs, and the base's four floor shots moved by 0.2-1.8% of pixels, its own chest drag having nudged the hold by a hair, accepted after looking; all eight rigs 129/129, in-app 160/0, the sweep unchanged at 37 near-misses and one real, 22 of 23) — and for the whole drag when the trunk began folded; `IK_JS_NO_KNEEL_HOLD`). **The trunk LEAVING its hands unloads them** (`m_jsKneelUpRise`: the hand contacts of a kneel-start trunk drag fade by its upward travel over the first `kKneelUpUnloadCm` 1.5cm, while the shoulder still stands over the hand — over the rise's 5-20cm a hand stayed nailed until the last decade of its rows and sprang to hang under a shoulder that had gone 20cm, 137-303mm at the fingertips in one tick; `IK_JS_NO_KNEELUP_UNLOAD`), and an unloaded contact's arm is the hang's (`m_jsContactUnloaded`, read by `hangArmHeld`: its pin stands until the rig's lift-off, and riding the trunk the hands lay on the floor ahead of her, arms straight). The kneel-up has no rise of its own to unload the hands with, and their own rise cannot always be made: on the oldest generation the hands stand on their fingertips, the carpals their contact joints 13cm up with no pitch left in the wrist — a DEADLOCK, the fingertip row that would let the wrist rise waiting on the rise (the trunk held down 17cm short; 23 on the two generations whose collars run sideways from the chest, where a dead-straight arm's reach over its chain's span reads 0.85 and the slack-arm ceiling never let go). **A hand is SLACK by BOTH measures now** — the limb's own stretch (`IkRig::liveContactStretch`: the best, over the path's joints with `kStretchChainShare` 0.6 of the span below them, of the joint's distance from the target over the chain between; 1 for a straight limb) AND the junction's reach over the span (the lift-off's test, which asks whether the body has left the limb's reach and is kept for that: a hand landing from a hanging arm is straight too, and on the stretch alone the male rig's landing hands lifted off as they landed); by the stretch alone the main family's hands, landed with 30° of elbow, were held under a hip rising to standing while the solve folded the trunk rather than straighten the arms (the reach had released them at 1.05). THREE MORE THINGS the phase found: **lift-offs of one tick are judged TOGETHER** (`IkRig::updateContacts`, `IK_LIFT_ONE_BY_ONE`: the shadow test asks whether the body still leans on a lifting contact with the support drawn without it, and pin by pin the first hand of a pair to lift saw the other still in the support and let go while the second, checked against a support with no hand in it, was kept as a shadow — one hand hanging, the other nailed to the floor, 341-411mm lopsided at the fingertips on three symmetric rigs, the symmetry gate's fourth catch); **a SHADOW gets no ceiling and leaves its arm to the hang** (`IkRig::pinIsShadow`, `IK_SHADOW_HOLDS_ARM`; set to gone outright instead, its rows went in one tick: 88mm at a fingertip — the fade of the joint's own rise has them by then); **an arm whose hand is on the floor at the press hangs once it lifts, whatever its elbow reads** (`HangArm::handLandedAtPress`, `IK_ARM_HANG_BENT_LANDED_RIDES`: the 35° bend test is a standing start's, a hand on a hip; the oldest generation's elbows are bent 45° on all fours and its arms rode the trunk up, 60° from plumb), **and its WRIST RELAXES toward neutral once the hand has really lifted** — the pin gone or a shadow, never the fade, which goes to zero for a tick as a planted hand bobs under a held prone (a wrist turned 4° then re-planted fought its palm rows: 224mm on the newest rig) — at `kWristRelaxStepDeg` 4°/tick (`IK_ARM_HANG_NO_WRIST_RELAX`: riding its posture reference, the all-fours hand bent back 80° to lie flat under a vertical forearm hung flat, held out like a paw). On all eight rigs (the chest taken back up and back to where the kneel had it): the chest 10-24mm from its cursor (20-34 before the seat lift), the knees within 0.3mm of their all-fours height, the pelvis −7..9° (1-19 forward before; 45-66 on all fours), the upper arms 3-14° from plumb (60-70 riding), no step, no lift-off, worst tick 35-47mm, symmetric to 0.4mm (the oldest rig excepted, whose shoulders' TWIST the all-fours landing on its limit-bound arms left 2° apart — invisible while the arms stood on the floor, 50mm at the fingertips once they hang; the kneel-allfours exemption). The sweep then found the hinge itself missing on nine custom bodies (the chest 50-89mm short, the pelvis still at 46°, the spine arched to its limits): the trunk hinge's `upperBody` test reads the grab's height over the root AS THE DRAG FOUND IT (> 5cm), and on those bodies the all-fours chest lies level with the hips or below — a kneel start now reads the BIND's heights, as the floor seat does for a lying figure (18-22mm, pelvis 4-7° on them after). **AND THE HIPS COME UP TO THE SEAT** (2026-09-23; `m_jsKneelSeatShift` in `dragIkTick`, the root's home in `solveIk`; `IK_JS_NO_KNEEL_SEAT_LIFT`): on all fours the hips sit 9-11cm BELOW the kneeling seat height (the thighs lean: 13-15cm behind the knees on most rigs, 24cm ahead on the oldest), and the kneel-up's rise reads nil — the kneeling chest is within reach about the seat AT the seat's height — so nothing freed the root's vertical price and the hips came up only as far as the cursor's bounded pull dragged them against 2000/m², 2-3cm: the chest rested 20-34mm short of its cursor on the reference rigs and 50-59 on two heavy custom characters (the round-83 sweep's real misses). The root's vertical HOME rises to the seat by the target's uprightness about it (`kKneelSeatUprightDeg`/`kKneelSeatLevelDeg` 30-80°: none while the target lies level with the seat, all once it stands near straight up — a function of the target, so a chest taken back down keeps its hips low): the chest rests 10-24mm from its cursor on all eight rigs with the pelvis upright (−7..9°, where it stood 1-18° forward), 36 on the half-size character, 46 on the toon — the thighs' length stops the hips 3-6cm under the seat where they stand, the kneel's own geometry. THREE HORIZONTAL HOMES were tried the same day and put back, each measured on six rigs: OVER THE KNEES (`IkRig::kneelSeat`, the seat as a point — the trunk leaned forward to keep the chest at its target, which is where the KNEEL had it and not over the knees either: pelvis 26-39° forward, the chest 38-166mm short, the half-size character's knees 32cm in the air); UNDER THE TRUNK'S TARGET by the bind's chest-over-root offset, as the standing rise draws them (a kneeling trunk's target sits well behind an upright trunk's: pelvis 22-28° forward, 167mm short on the newest generation, 429 on the half-size one); and the horizontal price YIELDED to the uprightness, `IK_JS_KNEEL_UP_YIELD` (the hips went where the pull took them, back and down on the oldest: 78mm short). The hips' horizontal home under a kneel-up stays where the drag found them; the remaining miss on a heavy body is theirs (the toon's 46mm: her hips 15cm behind her knees on all fours, and a rise over planted knees is a move along the thigh's arc). `IK_JS_KNEELUP_TRACE` prints the seat, the target's tilt and the lift per tick. In the app (a side view, where screen-left is her FRONT): from all fours drag the chest up and back over the hips — she kneels tall with her arms hanging (0.0mm on the cursor, pelvis 25°, gaze −11°); dragged up and FORWARD instead she leans up onto her knees with the arms hanging and the hands an arm's length below the shoulders, an inch off the floor. By the CHEST prone is still not a gesture: the hands are planted contacts that never walk, the shoulders go on past them and the arms fold back under the belly (a hand walk to under the sockets was tried and thrown out — the two arms met under the chest and the mirror-image volume rows between them broke the pose to one side by 26-340mm on five rigs; and a neck let into the solve so the face's floor rows could lift the head did nothing, the head cannot lift until the chest does); (the top-heavy toon's two steps in a bow are gone with the follow's gate), a dwarf's short legs twist 23° at the knee mid-step in the child walk (the rig sizes its thresholds by the pelvis's height, which is a full-size figure's on him); flesh is tested as each bone's own, rigid with it, so a prone chest rests on its breasts and a head on the back of its skull, but skin near a bent joint is only roughly where the rows think; a POSED idle limb rides (an arm bent more than 35° at the press, or raised: only arms that hung at the press hang); a bow made with FK, or loaded from a pose made before the head was righted, has a neutral neck that the righting reads as the user's own 29° of look-down, so undone by IK she stands looking at the floor until the head is reset (the price of two drags composing); a dragged head no longer TILTS much by itself (the neck bends with the spine: 6cm sideways is a sway of the whole body with the head all but level) — a head is turned by rotating it; a chest dragged far enough forward to walk begins its first step during a fast drag but the walk still takes a step's swing (0.4-0.5s at 0.9m/s) per stride after the cursor stops, and the second step waits its 12-tick confirmation once the foot is within 30cm of its spot; a body that moves INTO a hanging arm is not stopped by it (the arm's own stop only limits its own turn); the trunk's hang out of a lift is rigid (the legs and the free arm dangle since 2026-11-02); an out-of-reach pull rests 1-2cm short of where unlimited leaning would reach (the bounded pull, by design); kneeling is a hip drag down-and-forward and plants the knees only once they are within 5cm of the floor; the cursor filter's near-still smoothing is still upstream of the follower. **In-app benchmark:** `POSESTUDIO_IK_BENCH=<bone>` (with a figure on the command line; `<bone>:depth` also fires wheel-depth notches; `<bone>@<share>` CLICKS the bone's body instead of selecting the joint by name — it projects the point that share of the way along the limb segment to the screen and runs the real pick at that pixel, so picker, grab point and drag plane are exercised with no desktop input, and prints which bone the pick found and how far from the aimed point it took hold) runs a scripted drag through the real timer/solve/render/present chain with NO desktop input and exits; `POSESTUDIO_IK_PERF=1` prints the same report per second of a real drag. Measured on the dev box: ticks every 16.0ms, tick work 0.4-4.2ms (3.5 mean through a move on 2026-09-21, 7 at worst: the budget is 16), lag 3.3 / 6.0mm, oscillation 0. **In-app SCRIPTED TEST (`POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT=<file>`, `vulkanwindow_script.cpp` + `ikscript.h`, the eighth TU of VulkanWindow; `tools/ikscripts/` holds the scripts, `run.sh`, `montage.py` and the command reference):** where the harness drives the Armature alone, this drives the APP — `press <bone>[@share]` is the real picker at the projected pixel followed by the real press path (`cpress` the Ctrl press: a SCOPED drag), and when the pick lands on ANOTHER PART of the body than the script names — the rig would drag a different joint: a broad character's hanging arm covers her chest from the side, one hand covers the other — the bone is grabbed by NAME instead, at the same point, and the log says so (a small bone OF the named one, a pectoral for the chest, is what a user's click finds too, and stands; before 2026-09-21 a whole gallery of "chest" drags hauled one character's upper arm about, and on the base figure a `press rHand` had been posing the LEFT hand all along), `drag`/`dragv` move a scripted cursor in SCREEN space through the real camera-parallel drag plane (`dragw` moves the world target, the harness's gestures), the 60 Hz timer solves, `release` settles, `pin`/`fk`/`pose`/`reset`/`view`/`orbit` do what their UI does, `report` prints what a user would see as numbers (grab-to-cursor, hips/head travel, pelvis angles, steps, worst single-tick joint move, the posed channels) and `shot <name>` saves the rendered frame as a PNG. Nothing is injected into the OS (the window's own gesture functions are called), so it runs on a desktop in use — AND NOTHING COMES IN (2026-09-30; `VulkanWindow::event`): while a script runs the window swallows real mouse, wheel and key events. A wheel notch from the user's own scrolling, the cursor merely over the window, dollied the camera under a scripted foot slide — the drag's target jumped with the view's scale (`POP at tick 70: rShin moved 142 mm`, the pelvis rolled 8°, every later shot of that gallery framed differently) and read as a regression of the change under test until a rerun came out clean: A POP THAT A RERUN DOES NOT REPRODUCE, WITH LATER PRESSES AT OTHER PIXELS FOR THE SAME GRAB POINT, WAS THE CAMERA (the app-wide View shortcuts, QActions of the main window, are not blocked); and the frames are READ BACK FROM THE GPU (`VulkanRenderer::requestCapture`/`takeCapture`: the next frame's composited swapchain image is copied into a host buffer after the present pass and waited for — the swapchain images carry `TRANSFER_SRC` where the surface allows, `VulkanSwapchain::readable`), so an occluded window captures as well as a visible one. `run.sh` builds a CONTACT SHEET of a script's shots (`montage.py`, Pillow) — one image to look at per gallery. **The scripts are TESTS** (2026-09-20): `expect <metric> <op> <value>` asserts on what `report` prints and more (`VulkanWindow::scriptMetric`: `grab`, `grabpx` — on screen, `hips`, `head`, `steps`, `jump`, `lowest`, `gaze`, `headroll`, `tilt.<bone>`, `hang.<bone>` — a bone's direction from straight down, `y.<bone>`, `moved.<bone>`, `euler.<bone>.<axis>`), the app's exit code is the number that failed, and `run_all.sh <out>` runs every script in the folder — six galleries (`gallery_side`, `gallery_mixed`, `gallery_limbs`, `gallery_scoped` (the Ctrl-drags: a hand, a hand beyond reach, the head, the chest — the trunk alone since 2026-10-28 — the hips (the figure move), a pinned hand under a Ctrl chest drag, a knee — what did NOT move asserted at 0.5mm), `gallery_hands` (hands laid on the body: the hip, both hips, the heart, the crown of the head, the belly, the small of the back, a seated knee, and a hand laid and taken away), and `gallery_perspective`: the Home and three-quarter views a user actually poses in, where "down" on screen is down AND toward the camera), `gallery_floor.txt` (floor poses made the way a user makes them — down toward all fours by the hands, ONTO all fours by a kneel and the chest, up off all fours by the chest onto her knees with the arms hanging, a HALF KNEEL by a knee drawn up out of a kneel, onto her BELLY by the hips from all fours and back UP off it onto all fours, a kneel sat back onto the heels, a sit reclined by the chest, a sit with a leg stretched out — with `meshlow`, the SKIN's lowest point over the floor, the Ground button's CPU-skinned measure, asserted: flesh through the floor is invisible to every joint reading), `session_poses.txt` (whole poses built from several drags in a row — a walk, a sit with a hand brought to the knee, a high reach, picking something up — looked at from a three-quarter view) `check_returns.txt` (numbers only: out-and-home in one drag, a bow undone in a second drag, a walking drag let go of mid-step, a posed head) and `check_picking.txt` (numbers only: what a click at a joint's pixel SELECTS, from the side where half the body is hidden and from the front — see A CLICK PICKS WHAT THE CURSOR IS ON) and `gallery_digits.txt` (fingers, toes and the face in close-up — see FINGERS, TOES AND THE FACE RIG) and `gallery_landing.txt` (the Ground button's landing bounce — see THE LANDING BOUNCE) and `gallery_group.txt` (several joints dragged together — see THE GROUP) and `check_transform.txt` (numbers only: the Transform tab's dials — `dial <forward|sideways|twist> <value>` sets one the way the tab does, `dial.<kind>` / `dialmin.<kind>` / `dialmax.<kind>` / `dialjoint.<bone>` read them; and `uishot <name>` saves a picture of the application's WIDGETS as `ui/<name>.png` — `VulkanWindow::uiShotRequested` → `ViewportWidget` grabs the main window; the native viewport is not in it — with `POSESTUDIO_TAB=<title>` starting the app on that side tab: how a properties panel is LOOKED AT with its state driven by a script; and the joint mouse mode's `modal` / `modalmove` / `modalkeep` / `modaldrop` and `app`; and `check_project.txt` — the `.pss` save / New / open round trip through `saveproject` / `openproject` / `new` and the `figures` / `dirty` metrics), 517 expectations (on the base figure; the limbs gallery's front-view lift and its five since 2026-11-02 — see THE DANGLE; the scoped gallery's abdomen, collar, neck-base and pelvis-bone sections' twenty-three and the chest's rewrite since 2026-10-28 — see CTRL ON THE TORSO; the hand-by-its-carpals section's eight since 2026-10-28 — see A CARPAL SELECTS THE HAND; the group gallery's 26 and the stride's two since 2026-10-27, the stand-up from the heels' four and the two stand-ups by the hips — off all fours and out of a sit, sixteen — since 2026-10-28; the galleries are run on custom characters too — the prone descent, the getting up off it, the kneel-up off all fours and the half kneel included; the half kneel's drag goes to HIP height since 2026-09-22 — the 20cm draw it was made with had relied on the hang's lerp hauling the knee 20cm above the cursor) — the floor gallery lays her down on her back and sits her up again (pelvis pitch, chest tilt, skin over the floor, worst single-tick move); the runner logs every single-tick POP over 10cm with the joint and the tick (`POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT_TICKS=1` prints the worst joint's move EVERY tick with the grab and the cursor, `POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT_EULERS="lThigh,lShin"` those bones' channels every tick — the app's twins of the harness's `IK_HARNESS_POSE_TRACE` / `IK_HARNESS_EULER_TICKS`, for a pop only a scripted click makes) and keeps the app's whole output and exit code as `unfinished.txt` when a script does not reach its end (twice in one round's batch runs a script's app went away in its first drag — no crash record, never reproduced in 27 standalone runs; the evidence is kept now), and `report` names the joint that moved most; scripts name bones as the reference figure does and the runner maps them onto the other generations' names (`figureBoneName`, the harness's alias table), so the same galleries run on any figure — which is how a rig-specific flaw no gate measured was found (the newest generation's head thrown back under a head pull) — prints a pass/fail summary, and with `POSESTUDIO_IKSHOTS_REF=<folder>` compares every shot with its REFERENCE (`compare.py`: mean difference and changed-pixel share) and lays the ones that LOOK different out beside their references in `changes.png`: after a change there is one image per script to look at, holding only the poses that moved; `--accept` makes the run's shots the new references. The references are renders of a vendor figure: they live OUTSIDE the repository, like the skeleton dumps. THE RULE IT EXISTS FOR: a posing change is LOOKED AT before it is called an improvement. Its first run (2026-09-20) overturned a harness-green round the same day (the lean back: numbers better, picture worse) and found the most visible flaw in the app that no gate had ever measured (the ski-jumper arms). Run `gallery_side.txt` and `gallery_mixed.txt` after any change to the trunk, balance or idle-limb behaviour, and READ THE SHEETS.

**Pose utilities:** Edit → **Reset Selected Joint** / **Reset Limb** (the joint and everything below it) / **Reset Pose**, and **Mirror Pose** (swap the body's sides; centre bones flip in place) / **Mirror Limb to Other Side** (copy the selected subtree, mirrored; from a centre bone like the chest it mirrors both arms and the head); the joint context menu offers Reset Joint / Reset Limb / Mirror Limb too. In the Armature (`resetBone`/`resetPose`/`mirrorPose`/`mirrorSubtreeToOpposite`): bones pair by NAME — if the name's twin stands at the bone's MIRRORED bind position (the main generation's `…Carpal2` is one bone of the palm on the left and another on the right, 2cm apart: such a pair is dissolved) — then by PLACE (a bone left without a name twin that stands off the sagittal plane pairs with the unpaired bone at its mirrored bind position under the mirrored parent: the main figure generation's base file spells some thirty left/right ids differently on its two sides — a big toe's second joint, four carpals, the lip corners, the eyelids — and until 2026-09-21 Mirror Pose flipped each of those in place like a centre bone; found by the harness's symmetry measure) (`Armature::mirrorBone` — `l`/`r` before an uppercase letter or underscore, or `Left`/`Right`), the mirror rule is per-channel **(x, −y, −z)** on the Euler pose plus a negated x on the pose translation — exact for the sagittal reflection because the figure's left/right orientation frames are themselves mirrored, verified geometrically in the harness (sub-millimetre, mirror twice is the identity). Pins are NOT mirrored or reset. Each utility is one undo entry through `VulkanWindow::runPoseUtility` (refused mid-drag; a pending release and an open dial edit are closed first). **Manual joint pins:** select a joint and press **P**, or right-click → **Pin Joint** / **Unpin Joint** / **Unpin All Joints**; `Armature::m_bonePinned` is persistent and **part of the pose snapshot** (`@pin:<bone>` rows beside `@trans:`; older files and builds ignore them), so a pin toggle is an undoable pose edit, undoing a drag restores the pins of that moment, and a `.pose` file reproduces its pins. `Scene::record` draws user pins as **orange octahedra** always and the drag's contact pins as smaller **cyan** ones while a drag is live. In the solve a user pin is simply the hardest rows there are (position and orientation, six degrees): it holds to microns under any drag of another joint, a pelvis crouch stops where the pinned arm runs out, and none of the old path's machinery for making pins hold (back-off policy, polish rounds, prior exemptions, the refinement) exists any more. **Overlays removed 2026-09-07** at the user's request, in git history as the design record: the rotate GIZMO (three axis rings with their authored-range arcs) and the balance SUPPORT-POLYGON floor overlay.

**The Transform tab** (2026-09-30; `src/properties/transformpanel.{h,cpp}`, `scene/armaturedials.cpp`, `scene/jointtransform.h`; the user: "when any joint is selected, the transform tab should have slider bars to control Bend Forward (the natural direction for the joint), Bend Sideways (the secondary direction), Twist ... from 0 to 100, and 0 to -x for inverse bending ... in proportion to the forward bend, and stop when the limit is hit"). The side panel's second tab (Asset Manager, **Transform**, Environment) shows the selected joint's name and three `DragNumberBox` rows — labelled **Bend**, **Side-Side** and **Twist** since the user's first look (the code's `JointDialKind` keeps BendForward / BendSideways / Twist; the button under them is **Reset All**) — each on ONE scale whatever the joint: 0 = the rest pose, 100 = the channel's limit in the dial's direction, a negative value the other way IN THE SAME PROPORTION (value / 100 × `fullDeg`) until the other limit stops it — a knee authored −11..155° reads −7.1..100, an even channel −100..100, a head authored −30..25 (its long side is BACKWARD) −120..100, an unlimited channel (the hips) ±100 = ±180°. **A DIAL IS ONE EULER CHANNEL, FOUND BY STRUCTURE** (`Armature::jointDial(bone, JointDialKind)` → `JointDial{bone, axis, fullDeg, minValue, maxValue}`; never by bone names, so it holds on every generation): (1) the joint a selection PRESENTS is `dialJoint` — a selected TWIST bone (what a click on the lower thigh finds) shows its bend bone; (2) **TWIST** is the bone's length axis = the ROTATION ORDER'S FIRST AXIS, the format's own statement (checked against geometry on the eight rigs: they disagree only where the joints mislead — toes fanning sideways from the ball, a mid-foot bone millimetres from its ankle); where the bend bone's twist channel is locked (the generations that cut a limb's twist out into a twist bone) the dial turns that twist bone's free channel (`mainChild` if it `isTwistBone`, else any twist child — one generation hangs leaf twist helpers); (3) of the two other channels **BEND FORWARD** is the WIDER range (a knee's 166° beside 10, a wrist's 150 beside 58, a collar's shrug) — unless the narrower spans `kDialNearEqualShare` 0.75 of the wider or more, where ranges say nothing (a shoulder's 150/130, the oldest generation's chest 40/50 and shoulder 100/110, where range alone picked the SIDE bend) and the channel that swings the limb fore and aft is it (|sweep.z|, `sweep` = the channel's bind axis × the limb's direction); **BEND SIDEWAYS** is the other; (4) each dial's POSITIVE direction: a ONE-SIDED channel's long side (short ≤ `kDialOneSidedShare` 0.4 of long: a knee, an elbow, a thigh's 115/35, a collar's 55/10 — the threshold sits in a measured gap, 0.30-0.36 below it, a head's 20/40 and a neck's 12/27 at 0.44-0.50 above it, whose long side is backward); else, for the forward bend, the way that takes what the joint carries FORWARD, or where it moves up and down, DOWN (a wrist flexes, a finger and a toe curl, a foot points); for the side bend and the twist the longer side, and on an even channel OUTWARD for a side's bone / toward her left for a centre one (twist: + on the left and centre, the (x, −y, −z) mirror on the right) — so THE SAME VALUE ON A LEFT AND A RIGHT JOINT GIVES MIRRORED POSES. `Armature::limbDirection` is the limb's direction: ± the length axis, the sign read off the heaviest children (a mirrored pair together: the thighs under the pelvis, the collars under the chest), walking down past children that sit at the joint, the head by the way it stands on the neck (its children are the face rig's). THREE d's were tried in the Python prototype over the dumps before this one: the far joint (the mid-foot bone sits 1cm behind the ankle: the foot read backward), the centroid of all descendants (finger and face bones outvote the trunk: the newest generation's chest read forward-DOWN), the heaviest children alone (the toes fan sideways). On the base rig: hip ±100 every way; thigh forward −30..100 (= −115°), sideways −31..100 (abduction), twist ±100 on the twist bone; shin −7..100; foot −69..100 (100 = pointed); spine joints forward = flexion; collar forward = the shrug, sideways = fore-aft; shoulder forward = forward, sideways = raised (LOWERED on the T-pose generations, whose long side is down); forearm −15..100 with no side bend; hand −114..100 (100 = flexed). **Setting** (`Armature::setJointDial` → `nudgeBone`, the body of `nudgeSelectedBone` generalized to any bone): an FK rotation through the limits and the FK COLLISION STOP — so the pose may rest short of the value and the panel reads it back after every set. **The window** (`vulkanwindow_pose.cpp`): `jointTransform()` (the `JointTransform` POD: name + three `{enabled, value, min, max}`, via `Scene::selectedJointTransform`, which reads `highlightBone()` — the user's selection through a landing bounce), `beginJointTransformEdit` / `setJointTransformDial` / `endJointTransformEdit` — ONE undo entry per bracket (begin is refused while a drag owns the pose and closes every other open edit; a set without a begin opens the bracket; `closeSettlingEdits` closes an open one, so a viewport press, undo or a utility can never overwrite `m_preEditPose` under it; `poseEditInFlight` includes it) — and the signal `jointTransformChanged`, emitted from **`renderFrame`** (`notifyJointTransform`: the state compared with the one last announced): rendering is event-driven and EVERY selection or pose change already requests a frame, so the frame is the one place that sees them all — an IK drag, the B / S / T mouse mode, undo, a pose load, a reset, a delete — and no gesture has to remember the tab (do not add per-site emits). `ViewportWidget` forwards all of it. **The panel** brackets with the box's `editingStarted`/`editingFinished` (a scrub or a type-in = one entry), poses live on `valueChanged` and refreshes itself at once, mirrors the state with signals blocked, disables a row the joint does not have (a hinge elbow's side bend) and all three with nothing selected; the per-row button returns that dial to 0 and **Reset All** all three in one bracket (unlike Edit → Reset Selected Joint it also returns the limb's twist bone). The group, restore button and row builders are SHARED with the Environment tab (`src/properties/propertyrows.{h,cpp}`; object names `#PropertyGroup` / `#PropertyRowRestore` / `#PropertyResetButton`, styled once in `_environment.qss`, which now covers both tabs) — build a third properties tab from them. Verified: the harness's `transform-dials` phase on all eight rigs (a fold's 100 is its long limit and its low end the short one in proportion; set/read-back; a value past the range stops at the limit; seven direction gates — trunk forward and left, thigh forward and out, arm forward, knee back, elbow forward; twist dials on thigh, upper arm and forearm whichever bone carries them; and EVERY dial of EVERY paired body joint at the same value, each side posed alone, mirrored to 0.0001mm — posed together the second side's collision stop would meet the first), and in-app `tools/ikscripts/check_transform.txt` (80 expectations with the mouse mode's: the knee's range and undo, a twist-bone click showing the thigh, mirrored thighs, the head's forward = chin down, the dials reading an IK drag's elbow), with the panel LOOKED AT through the new `uishot`. SOFT SPOTS: the dials are FK — no balance, no planted feet (the Ground button after); "Bend Forward" on a collar is the shrug on five generations and fore-aft on the oldest (the wider range wins, and the ranges differ); a joint whose rest value lies outside a one-sided range reads a positive low end; the scale is per joint, so 50 on a knee is 77° and 50 on a chest bone 7°; translation and scale are not on the tab yet; the dials compose in the figure's rotation order — the format's way since 2026-10-27 (THE ROTATION ORDER, below).
