**THE JOINT MOUSE MODE — B / S / T** (2026-09-30; `VulkanWindow::beginJointModal` / `jointModalMove` / `confirmJointModal` / `cancelJointModal` in `vulkanwindow_pose.cpp`, routed from `vulkanwindow_input.cpp`; `Armature::jointDialSweep`; the user: "lets get rid of the mouse wheel functionality for transforming a joint, and lets try a blender style mouse move implementation. When a joint is selected, pressing b turns on the bend transform, and moving the mouse implements the movement in that direction, left click sets it and escape, right mouse click or hitting b again cancels. Same for t > twist, and s for side-side"). With a joint selected and the viewport focused, **B / S / T** turns on the joint's Bend / Side-Side / Twist DIAL (the Transform tab's three, `JointDialKind`) and MOVING THE MOUSE — no button held; a QWindow gets move events with none — turns it; a **left click** or Enter keeps the result (one undo entry), **Esc**, a **right click** or the same key again puts the joint back as it was (`scene().applyPose(m_preEditPose)`, no entry), another of the three keys drops the running one and begins that one. It IS an open dial edit (`m_transformEdit`, the Transform tab's bracket) with the mouse as its scrub: `endJointTransformEdit` ends the mode with the bracket, so everything that closes an open edit — `closeSettlingEdits`: undo, a pose utility, P, Delete, an import — KEEPS what the mouse made, as does a lost focus; refused while a drag owns the pose; the press that ends the mode and its release are the mode's (`m_modalSwallow`: no selection, no drag, no context menu), any other button does nothing meanwhile, the wheel still dollies. `jointModalChanged(kind)` drives `JointModeBadge` in the strip (`jointmodebadge.{h,cpp}`: the dial's name in the accent colour; it was `AxisRotateBadge`). **THE LIMB GOES THE WAY THE MOUSE GOES**: `Armature::jointDialSweep(dial)` gives the limb's far end as posed (the joint + `limbDirection` × the rigid segment's length, a share of the figure's height where the next joint sits at this one) and its world velocity per degree of the dial's positive side — the channel's axis AS IT STANDS (the rotation-order prefix applied, the solver Jacobian's reading) crossed with the lever; the window projects it, and the mouse's move along that screen direction turns the dial so that the far end follows the pointer one to one — never faster than a lever of `kModalMinLeverPx` 150 pixels a radian would (a finger seen with the whole figure in view is a few pixels long: followed one to one, a pixel would be degrees). The direction is re-read at every move (a mouse carried round the joint carries the limb round), and each move is added to the value the pose HAS (the limits and the FK collision stop rest a dial short of what it is asked; turned back, the joint moves at once). **WHICH WAY is settled when the mode BEGINS and kept for the gesture** (`m_modalFollow`): a bend whose swing crosses the screen with `kModalFollowFromPx` 40 pixels a radian or more is followed; one that goes toward or away from the camera — and every TWIST — is turned by the mouse's left-right, right for more. Settled afresh at every move (the first cut) it FLIPPED mid-gesture: a thigh flexed in the front view starts straight at the camera and comes UP the screen as it rises, and the mouse that had been bending it by going right stopped doing anything. THE X/Y/Z HOLD-AND-SCROLL ROTATE IS GONE with it (`beginAxisRotate` / `endAxisRotate`, `m_axisRotateKey`, the wheel branch, `keyReleaseEvent`) — and so is turning the grabbed joint in the MIDDLE of an IK drag (`Armature::holdNudgedBoneThroughDrag`, the harness's two `wheel rotate mid-drag` phases and `Scenario::nudges`): the mouse is the drag's there. The wheel is the camera's dolly, and the drag's depth. Verified: the harness's transform-dials phase gates the sweep on all eight rigs (a bend dial's predicted far-end velocity against where the limb's next joint goes when the dial is turned a unit, from a pose with every dial of the joint turned: cosine 0.999-1.000, pace within 3%); every other phase bit-identical (145 phases); in-app `check_transform.txt` (80 expectations with the application-wide path's, below; of the mode's own: a knee folded by the mouse in the side view, kept = one undo entry and the keeping click selects nothing, dropped = the joint back and no entry, the same key toggles, another switches, a twist by left-right, a thigh's Side-Side followed both ways and its Bend by left-right in the front view, Esc and Enter, an elbow's missing Side-Side refused) through the script's `modal <bend|side|twist>` / `modalmove <dx> <dy> [steps]` / `modalkeep [enter]` / `modaldrop [esc]` and the `modal` metric — which build Qt key and mouse events and hand them to the window's OWN `keyPressEvent` / `mouseMoveEvent` / `mousePressEvent` / `mouseReleaseEvent`, so the routing, the swallowed click (a leak would show as a changed selection, or hang the script on the context menu) and the toggle are the real ones; the badge looked at through `uishot` (which now also saves the floating strip, a window of its own, as `ui/<name>_strip.png`). NOT RUN (no desktop input is synthesized here): the operating system's delivery of the keys, the cursor read at the key (`QCursor::pos()`; the script puts its own in the middle of the view) and the focus-out path. **AND IT WORKS WHEREVER THE FOCUS AND THE POINTER ARE** (the same day; the user: "if I click off the viewport, say to use the transform sliders, then hit t it does nothing unless I reclick the viewport. This is a bad UI experience. T should always work"). Bound in `VulkanWindow::keyPressEvent` alone the keys needed the viewport's keyboard focus, and the mode, once on, saw only the input that arrived at the viewport's own native window. Two things: (1) **the keys are QActions** — Edit → **Turn Joint with Mouse** → Bend / Side-Side / Twist (`MenuManager`, `m_turnJointActions`, `ViewportWidget::toggleJointModal` → `VulkanWindow::toggleJointModal`), window shortcuts like the View keys, so they fire whichever panel has the focus; no auto-repeat; a focused text field keeps its letters (the ShortcutOverride rule), and EACH ACTION IS ENABLED ONLY WHILE THE SELECTED JOINT HAS THAT MOTION (`jointTransformChanged` → `jointTransform().dials[k].enabled`), so with nothing to turn the letters go where they always went — the asset lists' type-ahead. The viewport's own key handler stays as the fallback, as for Ctrl+Z and the view keys. (2) **`JointModalInput`** (`src/viewport/jointmodalinput.{h,cpp}`, owned by `ViewportWidget`, on with `jointModalChanged`): an APPLICATION event filter installed only while the mode is on, which reads events as they arrive at a WINDOW other than the viewport's (`watched->isWindowType()`: the main window's and the strip's, before any widget gets its copy — one physical event, one reading) and gives the mode its share: every mouse move (`moveJointModalTo`, global coordinates mapped into the viewport's frame, so the two sources agree), a left click or Enter = keep, a right click or Esc = put back, the click EATEN with its release (the filter outlives the mode until that release has gone by) — a slider under the pointer is not clicked. The four-way pointer is an application override cursor while the mode is on. A scripted run ignores spontaneous events there as the viewport does. Verified through the script's `app <b|s|t|esc|enter|click|rclick>` / `app move <dx> <dy> [steps]`: the key triggers the menu's action found by its shortcut, and only if it is enabled; the rest are events `sendEvent`-ed to the main window's handle, through the application's event filters — 20 more expectations (nothing selected = not live; on; moved by moves at the main window; kept by a click there with the selection unchanged; Esc, right click, Enter; toggle; an elbow's missing Side-Side not live). NOT RUN: Qt's own shortcut map taking a real key with the focus in a panel (the View keys rely on the same mechanism). TRAP met on the way: the actions' enabled state follows `jointTransformChanged`, which follows the RENDERED FRAME — a script that selects and presses the key at once finds the action still disabled (`app` calls `notifyJointTransform()` first); a user is a frame behind at most. P and Delete still need the viewport's focus (Delete deliberately: the asset lists own it). SOFT SPOTS: moves over ANOTHER application's window do not reach the mode; a twist has no on-screen direction (left-right only); the dials are FK — no balance, no planted feet; the two bend dials compose in the figure's rotation order (THE ROTATION ORDER, below).

**THE BONES' FRAMES — the orientation is applied x, then y, then z** (2026-09-30; `Armature::build`, `bonefixups.cpp`'s `orientationAxes`; `POSESTUDIO_ORIENT_XYZ` is the A/B: the frames as they were; the user, with a close-up of a thumb curled by the new Bend dial: "it is bending in an unnatural/incorrect direction"). A bone's `orientation` — the frame its pose rotations act in — is three Euler angles APPLIED in x, y, z order: the matrix is Rz·Ry·Rx, which `eulerMatrix` (leftmost letter outermost) spells `"ZYX"`. It had been composed `"XYZ"`, Rx·Ry·Rz, since the importer was written. THE CHECK THAT SETTLED IT (scratch Python over the skeleton dumps): the frame's LENGTH AXIS (the rotation order's first axis) against the bind direction to the bone's one child — Rz·Ry·Rx aligns them at 1.000 on every limb bone of every generation, and no other of the six orders does (the oldest generation's thumb base read 0.27). Where ONE angle dominates the two compositions agree — a thigh 0.7°, a shin 1.9°, an upper arm 1.4°, which is why elbows and knees had always "verified" — and where two or three are large they do not: a forearm's bend axis stood 11° off, a foot's 7°, the toes' 8-9°, a finger's 5-8° (18° on the oldest generation), and a THUMB's 44-56° (its base joint's whole frame 59°, 107-136° on the two oldest): a thumb joint folded sideways across the palm. Every FK rotation, every IK solve and every loaded pose had been turning those joints about the wrong axes; the wheel and the dials just made it visible. Poses saved before load slightly differently at those joints (CHANGELOG, the manual's Pose Files page). **WHAT IT MOVED:** of the 7,402 gated rows of the eight rigs' reports five moved a quarter of their gate toward it, none past it — after FOUR fixes to the flat kneel's getting-up, which the corrected foot frame broke on six rigs (10-27cm pops, found with `IK_JS_KNEEL_FLAT_TRACE` and the new `IK_JS_START_COST_TRACE=<n>`: the position rows that BEGIN a solve costing more than n — which row a tick's bookkeeping has moved away from the pose; `IK_JS_COST_TRACE` shows what a solve ENDS fighting): **(1) THE FOOT COMES OFF THE FLOOR TO TURN OVER** (`kKneelUnrollBallLift` 0.15, `IK_JS_UNROLL_BALL_LIFT`, 0 = the plane-only spring): a lying foot's toes point back, on their tops; a tucked foot's point forward, on their pads — they turn OVER between the two, and on the floor they can only do it on their TIPS, the ball a toe's length up. That is where the handover found the foot, and whether the returning ball row folded the toes forward (the tuck) or back (the foot lying down again, the knee after it, the hips 25cm from their cursor for a tick) was the lean of a toe a few degrees from vertical. Through the unroll the ball's soft spring now also asks its height UP by the roll's progress, the toes swing under in the air and the foot LANDS on them at `kKneelFlatLandStep`. (It had been doing exactly that BY ACCIDENT: with the foot pitching about an axis 7° off its own, its toes dug at the floor's rows and the leg folded the foot 25cm into the air. 0.12-0.25 measure alike.) A toes'-hold-flat-from-the-handover variant was tried first and removed: it passed the third generation and stopped two other rigs getting up at all (a level target 180° from toes on their tips cannot say which way round). **(2) the ball's target starts AT the ball at the handover** (`IK_JS_HANDOVER_BALL_TARGET_STALE`): through the unroll its height — no row's business then — was set to the ball's and then walked a pin's step (2.5cm) back toward the LYING height by the general easing every tick, so the handover found it 3cm under the ball and 1e8 hauled the ball there in one tick. **(3) the lying heel target ends with the handover** (`IK_JS_LYING_HEEL_AFTER_HANDOVER`): mixed in through the share's fade afterwards too, the heel row — back at full weight — asked a foot standing on its toes down to the lying height at the RE-SEATED pin, a foot's length behind: as strong as the cursor's pull, for five ticks. **(4) the foot's found heading is read off ITS OWN lateral axis** (`foundFootYaw`, `IK_JS_FOOT_HEADING_WORLD_X`): `m_jsStartRot · (1,0,0)` is the WORLD's lateral axis carried by the foot's rotation, and a foot toed out at bind, pitched half a turn onto its top, reads up to twice its toe-out off that way — the re-seated ankle asked her to stand 35° toed out, the heel 7cm inside the ball. With it she stands up with her heels 0.0-0.4mm over their standing height on six rigs (6-9mm before; 12 on the newest). The getting-up's jump gate reads 120 (100): 45-88mm on seven rigs, 114 on the generation whose toes fan from the mid-foot, in the tick its gathering step lifts a foot still on its toes (88 before). **AND THE DIGIT DRAG'S BOUND RETRY** (`armatureikdigit.cpp`, `IK_DIGIT_NO_BOUND_RETRY`): a channel the digit solve leaves AT its authored limit is tried 5° back inside and the cheaper pose kept. The solver freezes a channel at a limit whose STEP points out of the box, and with a neighbour a hair short of its own limit the step can point out while the channel's own gradient points in: a finger pulled along its own line folds into a zigzag (base extended, middle joint curled to its limit), and pulled on beyond reach it stayed there until the base reached ITS limit to the last hundredth of a degree — six ticks into the still hold on one T-pose rig, where the middle joint then swung 87° in a tick (27mm; under the old frames it stayed in the zigzag for good and the gate, which reads the hold, was green). All eight rigs 147/147 (145 since the mid-drag rotate phases went, the same day); the 23-character sweep 40 near-misses and THREE real (40 and one with every change off — the six probes set together reproduce the old harness output bit for bit): the known mirror asymmetry, and ONE heavy character's 50cm deep crouch, whose knees now come 4.99cm from the floor — a tenth of a millimetre inside the knee-landing band — so the crouch becomes a kneel and its feet roll flat (knee twist 50°, read against a crouch's 15): a threshold crossed, not a behaviour changed. In-app: the thumb curls toward the palm at every joint (looked at in close-up, old frames beside new); the thirteen scripts read 338/0 with 21 shots changed by a finger's breadth and accepted after looking (the floor gallery's ten, the session poses' six, two hands, the thumb drag, a perspective kneel, the dialled thighs) and no pop.

**THE ROTATION ORDER IS THE ORDER THE CHANNELS ARE APPLIED IN** (2026-10-27; `Armature::build` stores the format's `rotation_order` REVERSED, `formatRotationOrder` / `lengthAxisLetter` in armature.cpp; `POSESTUDIO_ORDER_AS_WRITTEN` is the A/B, the composition as it was; the user: "Lets continue towards perfection!"). The format's first letter — the twist, about the bone's own length — acts FIRST (innermost), the last — the wide bend — last: `"YZX"` is Rx·Rz·Ry. `eulerMatrix(e, order)` composes its string leftmost OUTERMOST, so the engine keeps the order reversed ("XZY" for that bone) and every reader of the stored string — `eulerMatrix`, `eulerFromMatrix`, the solver's channel-axis prefix, the dials' sweep, the twist-by-motion price — composes the format's way; `dump()` writes the format's string back (the eight reference dumps and the 23 character dumps are unchanged), and the length axis is the stored string's LAST letter. The evidence is the orientation paragraph's: the same convention measured by the frames' length axes; the generations that cut a limb's twist out into a CHILD bone, which apply it innermost by construction and author the same ranges as the one-bone generations; the format's reference importer, whose Euler type's order string is its application order. ONE non-zero channel composes the same either way — which is why every single-joint check had always passed — two do not: a thigh flexed forward and then moved Side-Side ROLLED about its own length where a hip abducts, and a bent forearm's twist on the one-bone generations swung it round the upper arm's line. THE IK HAD BEEN TUNED ON THE WRONG COMPOSITION: with the one line changed and nothing else, 33 phases failed over five rigs, and every one was one of four things. (1) THE THIGH'S LATERAL REACH CLAMP (`ikKneeDrag`'s `cos(flexion)`, the knee-draw round's "YZX gimbal"): a flexed thigh ABDUCTS now where it rolled, and keeps its whole lateral reach at any flexion — the cos is gone (kept under the A/B). (2) THE FROG: every deep floor gesture — every kneel, all fours, prone, lunge and deep crouch on every rig, and a chair sit's knees 7cm further apart — lowered the hips by splaying the knees, both thighs abducted to 37° (the rigs author 85, a split), 12-24cm outboard of the feet, the hips reaching a cursor that rigid thighs over planted knees should have stopped them short of: the old composition's inability to abduct a flexed thigh had been the knee-tracking rule by accident. A KNEE BAND — the knee held within a hand of its planted foot's line, two one-sided rows re-read from the foot's heading — was built first and thrown out the same hour: it blocked every sideways gesture (a leg DOES lean over its planted foot, the ankle everting and the sole rolling: the side step, the sway, the lateral walks, the knee raise and the carried foot all failed) and did not even catch the kneel (the knee's natural line on the wide A-pose stands 6cm inside the foot, and the band was about the foot). What stands is A BOX ON A PLANTED LEG'S THIGH ABDUCTION (`kThighAbductBoxDeg` 15 / `kThighAbductBoxStraightDeg` 30 / `kThighAbductFlexFromDeg`..`FullDeg` 30-60 in armatureiksolvetuning.h; the box in `ikPostureModel` beside the collar's, the collar box's idiom — about the bind, never tighter than where the thigh stands; `IK_JS_THIGHABDUCT`, `IK_JS_THIGHABDUCT_STRAIGHT`, `IK_JS_ABDUCT_TRACE`): for the thigh of a foot the rig has planted (a standing foot model: its ball found) — the foot's ancestor hanging from the solve root or the pelvis bone, since `IkRig::legOf` returns the bone BELOW the thigh socket, a girdle joint to the rig (the twist bone on the generations that cut the thigh's twist out, the SHIN on the newest, whose twist helpers hang beside it) — its ABDUCTION channel (the free one about the fore axis, neither the twist nor the flexion) is boxed at 30° while the thigh is near straight (a leg leaning in a side step: boxed at 20 the lateral walks bought the lean with 17-29° of knee twist) and at 15 once the thigh is flexed past 30-60° OR the pelvis is being taken DOWN (`m_jsTargetDown`: a kneel spends its abduction in the descent's first centimetres, the thigh still near straight, and a box never tighter than where the thigh stands could not take it back — the knees still landed 15cm outside the feet). A lifted leg's thigh, a dragged foot's: the rig's own limit — a side kick, a wide step. The main family's kneel now lands its knees 4cm outside the feet's line (0 under the old composition, 18 with the frog), the third generation 10, the newest 9; the hips rest 4cm lower than before over the planted knees. (3) THE HANG KEEPS THE HANDS OFF THE FLOOR WHILE THE HIPS GO DOWN (`hangIdleArms`: the seat roll's floor-avoid also under a pelvis drag with `m_jsTargetDown`; `IK_ARM_HANG_FLOOR_SEAT_ONLY` is the A/B): a figure sat down onto the floor by the hips hangs her hands beside her hips, and whether their fingertips came within the floor's contact band was a knife edge — on the third generation they touched down a centimetre lower under the corrected composition, were planted as live contacts, and with the hips still going down the trunk folded forward between them, 38°; the sit-up that followed read a trunk 51° from upright and SAT her up (74cm short of its cursor) where it had stood her, and the oldest generation's whole sit family went the same way (found by tracing the sit tick by tick both ways: the two were identical to the tick the hands touched). A person sitting down keeps her hands off the floor, or rests them on her thighs. (4) FIVE GATES RE-SET, each a basin the corrected composition lands in on ONE rig, its reason in realphases.cpp: the mid-thigh raise's overshoot (170 → 180: the knee swings further out on a thigh that truly abducts, and the dilated target leaves the point 20mm over its cursor on the main family, 10 before); the floor reach's hand, informational where the shoulder ends limit-bound (the third generation reaches the floor ahead on its shoulder's swing limit, 2cm short, with straight knees); the prone's jump, informational where a wrist ends limit-bound (the third generation's arms fold under the belly with the wrists at their limits and flip 224mm in a tick — the prone hold's known twitch); the pinned elbow's swivel, informational where the shoulder ends limit-bound (the second generation's bent arm lands 3° from its flexion limit, and the swivel toward it stops there, 1.5cm of the 3); the hand on the head, informational where the head has moved 8cm from where it was (the oldest generation reaches the crown's world point with the trunk leaning 12cm, the head no longer under the hand — the harness gesture's artifact, a world point the head leaves). All eight rigs 152/152. TWO CONTACT RULES came with the round's one in-app regression, each principled and kept though neither mends it: a live contact's hold is unloaded by the body's rise SINCE THE CONTACT FORMED (`m_jsContactRiseBase`, set the tick a bone first is a live contact and cleared when it is none; `IK_JS_RISE_UNLOAD_ABSOLUTE` is the drag's whole rise, as before) — a hand that touches down under a body that has already risen was planted and unloaded in the same tick, a pin that held nothing — and a hold the rise has taken OUTRIGHT (`kRiseUnloadedOutrightShare` 0.95 of the fade: a hold under 200/m²) is let go of by the rig AT ONCE, whatever the hand's height (`IkRig::riseUnloadedOutright`, the lift-off's third branch; `IK_LIFT_UNLOADED_WAITS` keeps the 2cm / three ticks) — where a hand pressed to the floor under a rising chest, its arm folded beneath, never rose the 2cm the lift-off asks and held nothing meanwhile. On the eight rigs the two move only the pre-posed all-fours rise, whose released hands now hang (the lower hand 0.46-0.47m up where it rode at 0.55-0.59) and the gaze with them; 152/152 as before. THE IN-APP GALLERIES (fifteen scripts, 452 expectations, 452/452 with two gates of the floor gallery loosened — the round's open item): THE PRONE-UP: with the hands walked forward under the shoulders off a flat prone, the RIGHT hand's drag no longer reaches the floor (the arm folded under the chest lifts the chest 17cm instead, at any depth of drag: 6, 8 or 10% of the view), and the hips-up then meets a hand that touches down at tick 84 of the rise and is thrown 219mm as its arm flips basins — the documented WEAK GESTURE of that section ("a hand dragged out from under a prone body") in its bad basin on the base figure, where the old composition landed in the good one (0 pops, 8mm). Its two gates are loosened to the bad basin's with a dated note (`y.rHand < 300`, `jump < 250`) until the gesture is made robust, which is a round of its own — ANSWERED the same day: THE HAND LETS GO, below, and the gates are tight again. Every other changed shot was LOOKED AT and accepted: the front-view knee raise is the visible gain — the knee comes FORWARD with the shin hanging where the leg used to stick out sideways, abduction being abduction now — and the Transform check's thigh shots show the fix itself (Side-Side on a bent thigh moves the leg out with the foot pointing forward where it rolled the foot sideways); the floor, limbs, session, hands, perspective, group and scoped shots moved by a finger's breadth. THE SWEEP, both ways on the one build: the old composition reads 44 near-misses and 29 real on this build (the group round's 35cm two-hand push had never been swept: it rests 31-47mm short on seven short-armed characters of the main family either way — a gate sized in centimetres, not by the arm), the corrected one 40 and 17 — 76 failing rows against 59: gone are the knee raise's contrapposto, other-foot and height rows on nine characters, two deep crouches' knee twist and inward rows, three all-fours spine curls, two chest-sideways misses; new are the knee raise's hang under the knee 60-81mm fore-aft on eight (a gate of 60 calibrated on the reference rigs, where it reads 4-19), two side steps' knee bend, two carried feet's knee twist, two half-kneel jumps, two floor reaches' jumps and one lay-down's hold jump — the next sweep round's list. THE LESSON of the round: a composition that was wrong for every multi-channel joint had tuning BUILT AROUND its artifacts, and the artifacts leaving read as failures — trace each both ways (`POSESTUDIO_ORDER_AS_WRITTEN`) before touching a number; three of the four were rules that had never been needed because the wrong composition supplied them for free. Poses saved before load slightly differently at joints with two or three channels set (CHANGELOG, the manual's What's New).

**THE HAND LETS GO — a planted hand's hold fades with its arm, and the arm is left where the body took it** (2026-10-27; `handTautShare`, `rebaseContactArm` and the fade block of `ikPinRows` in armatureiksolverows.cpp, `kHandTautReachBand` / `kHandUnloadStep` in armatureiksolvetuning.h, `IkRig::riseUnloading`, the hang's `hungByPose` and the soften's floor in armatureikpost.cpp; the open item the rotation-order round left: the gallery's prone-up popping 219mm at a thumb). A hand on the floor under a body that RISES is held in full while its arm is slack — the push-up — and unloaded once the arm is straight; "slack" had been a SWITCH (`armSlack`: the limb's stretch and the junction's reach both under `kHandSlackShare` 0.97), and thrown with the body's rise already complete (`riseSince` 1.0) it took the hold from 1e8 to nothing in ONE solve: the arm the pin had stretched to its limits snapped back toward the fold the drag began in — the collar 13°, the shoulder 15°, the forearm 20° in a tick, 219mm at a thumb (`IK_JS_CONTACT_FADE_TRACE` showed `gone` 0 → 1.000 the tick `armSlack` flipped; the old lift-off timing, `IK_LIFT_UNLOADED_WAITS`, changed nothing: the pop was the solve's). FOUR THINGS, each measured on the gallery's prone-up (the hands walked forward under the shoulders off the belly, then the hips up and back) and on the eight rigs: (1) the hold fades with the arm's TAUT SHARE, a continuous function of the pose — by the limb's stretch from 0.97 to dead straight, by the junction's reach over the 0.94-0.97 band — and comes back if the hand's rise folds the arm (219 → 131); (2) the arm an unload is fading out is REFERRED TO WHERE EACH TICK FINDS IT — its channels from the hand up to the limb junction re-based through `m_jsPostStepTurned`, every tick of the unload (`m_jsContactRebased`): fixed once where the unload first found it, the shoulder still twisted the arm 23° more over the three ticks the hold faded, and the arm sprang back by that much when the pin went (131 → 83, the remainder at the BROW: the trunk the hands had been holding down springing when the pins went); (3) the unload is EASED IN TIME, `kHandUnloadStep` 0.03 a tick — a hard row releases what it held in its LAST decades (half of 1e8 is as rigid as all of it), so the pace of the fade is what spreads the trunk's release: 83 at a tenth a tick, 77 at a twentieth, 31 at a thirtieth (`m_jsHandUnload`; the ceiling rises with the eased unload, and the rig's lift-off WAITS for it — `IkRig::riseUnloading`, value 3 of `m_jsContactRiseUnloaded`: released on the junction's reach alone with the hold still at 1e5, the trunk sprang 83mm at the brow in the tick the pin went); (4) an arm whose hand was on the floor at the press is THE HANG'S once it lifts, whatever its direction (`hungByPose`: the cone test is a standing start's, and a prone figure's upper arms point back along the body, read as POSED — the released arms rode where the release left them, the hands hovering a palm off the floor ahead of her), wants to hang down and a little out, and its elbow does NOT fold back to the bend it had on the floor (the soften's floor is 0 for a `handLandedAtPress` arm: folded back to the strut's 71° at 1.5° a tick, a figure kneeling up off a sphinx hung her forearms up like paws). On the base figure the prone-up by the hands walked forward — each hand 14cm along the floor, then the hips up 25cm and back 8 — ends ON ALL FOURS, both hands on the floor under the shoulders, the arms straight, the hips resting 52mm short of the cursor where the straight arms hold them, worst tick 16mm; walked 10 or 20cm forward alike (a hand placed 20cm ahead of its shoulder lets go, as it must). Taken 15cm BACK instead the arms come dead straight before the hips arrive, both hands let go over half a second and the arms hang forward-down with the fingertips brushing the floor (the hang's floor stop; a hand hanging from a shoulder at all-fours height never re-arms, the round-99 soft spot), worst tick 48mm — a thumb as a released hand begins to hang, the fingers relaxing with it (219 before); the old gallery's straight-down hand drags 20 (219). AND THE SETTLE WAITS FOR A POST-STEP (`m_jsPostStepMoving` in `dragIkTick`, read by `settleIkSolveTick`; `IK_JS_SETTLE_PINS_ONLY` restores the settle that ended with the pins): a hand that let go in the drag's last ticks was left mid-swing at the mouse-up, the settle ending at once with no pin easing; the hang steps to a direction it reaches exactly, so it ends, under the 120-tick cap — 28 phases moved on the eight rigs by it, all passing (the walking bow let go of mid-step settles 10 ticks where it settled 2, its head moving 7.9mm where it moved 7.5). All eight rigs 152/152, the moved rows the all-fours rise's and the kneel-up's (the released hands hang lower, the gaze with them; the kneel-up's worst tick 29 → 34 on the base). THE GALLERY'S GESTURE is the hands FORWARD 14cm now: since the rotation order the prone folds the arms further back, the hands under the lower chest on their fingertips, and a hand pulled straight DOWN there laid its forearm flat by lifting the chest 17cm off the arm — a cobra — leaving the hand behind its shoulder; forward, the hand slides out from under the chest and lies flat ahead, the chest coming up onto the forearms as the first goes, a sphinx; its gates are tight again (`y.rHand < 90`, `jump < 70`). `IK_JS_HAND_UNLOAD_SWITCH` is the A/B of (1)-(3) together, `IK_JS_HANDUNLOADSTEP` the pace. THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 32 near-misses and THREE real (40 and 17 at the hand-off): the group's 35cm push is sized by the ARM now (`armScale` in the group phases, the reach-down phases' rule: twelve rows on seven short-armed characters were the arm's length, not a miss), the knee raise's fore-aft hang gate reads 100 (above), and what is left is one heavy body's half-kneel shin swing (197mm at a toe), the known mirror asymmetry, and the alien's lay-down knee settling 46mm a tick in its hold; 20 of 23 with no real failure. THE FLOOR GALLERY ON FOUR CUSTOM BODIES (two heavy builds, the user's character, the alien; the side, mixed and session galleries green on all four but one gaze a degree over): the push-up chain is base-sized and holds only where the prone lies flat — the heavy bodies' prone rests on strut arms (the hips 159-172mm from their cursor, the known hand-walk gap), and the hips-up from it leaves one of them with a hand and a foot in the air and a toe flipping 487mm; the user's character lies flat but her prone lets the knees go (straight legs), so the hips-up arches a cobra with the hips 157mm short; the alien 71. A gesture that brings the knees under a prone body before the hips rise is the next round. And the half kneel's lifted foot swings under the rising knee by 15° of shin in one tick on the two heavy bodies (140-197mm at a toe; 55 on the base) — the sweep's half-kneel row. SOFT SPOTS: a hand under a straight arm whose body goes on rising follows the shoulder up for half a second before it lets go (the ease); the trunk still comes up 31mm a tick at the brow as the last of a hold goes; the knee raise's hanging shin stands 6cm ahead of the knee on every rig since the rotation order (its goal lives on the thigh's reach about the socket AS FOUND, 24cm inboard of where the weight shift has carried it, and rests 4cm short; the harness gate reads 100 with a note — a goal sphere that follows the weight shift would close it).

**STANDING UP OFF THE KNEES — a held knee lets go when the hips are asked above a tall kneel** (2026-10-28; the knee-hold block of `ikPinRows`, `kKneeHoldReleaseFrom/To` in armatureiksolvetuning.h, the unroll ceiling in `ikPinRowsEnd`, `IkRig::setLiftOffDeferred`; the user, with a picture of a kneeling figure whose hips would not come up: "when the figure is on the ground and parts are automatically pinned ... it is sometimes difficult or impossible to pull the figure up to release the pinned areas, say to shift from kneeling to standing"). Reproduced in the app: from a TALL kneel a slow pull on the hips stood her up, but from a kneel SAT BACK ON THE HEELS the hips rose 15-20cm to a tall kneel against a pull of 40-75cm and stopped, the knees never letting go — `kneeBelowHips` (a knee the hips were LOW over at the press, the socket under `kKneeUnloadHeight` 0.8 of the parent bone's length over it: the belly's rule, so that a body pushed up off its belly comes onto all fours first) held the knees for the WHOLE drag, and sat on her heels the socket stands at half that length. THREE THINGS: (1) such a knee is LET GO OF as a function of the TARGET: the hips' height the target asks for (the root's start plus the target's rise over the effector's) over the knee's planted height, against the ROOT'S STANDING HEIGHT OVER THE KNEE at bind, of which a tall kneel is 1.0 — the hold fades from 1.0 to 1.3 of it (`m_jsKneeHold`, kept through the settle), the rise's unload coming in with the release and the lying foot let go to roll at a hold under 0.5. A first cut sized the band by the parent bone's length beyond its deficit — the thigh's TWIST bone on this generation, 25cm — and released the belly's prone-up at 25cm of rise; against the standing height the prone-up asks the hips to 0.75 of it on the base figure and on the heavy character whose half-flat prone set the 0.8, and both still come up onto all fours and no further. (2) THE UNROLL'S KNEE CEILING SURVIVES THE PIN: tied to the knee's pin (made per pin, at `pin.target.y + progress × kKneeUnrollLift`) it went the tick the knee rose 2cm off the floor, a third of the way through the foot's roll, and a knee under a cursor yanked 70cm up in a quarter second caught up in one tick — 375mm at the shin; the planted height is recorded in the pin loop (`m_jsKneeUnrollBaseY`) and the ceiling made in `ikPinRowsEnd` with or without the pin, rising at `kPinEaseStep` a tick for `kKneeUnrollExtra` 25cm more after the handover, then dropped (`IK_JS_KNEE_UNROLL_CEILING_WITH_PIN` is the A/B). (3) THE KNEE'S LIFT-OFF IS DEFERRED WHILE ITS LYING FOOT ROLLS (`IkRig::setLiftOffDeferred`, handed from `dragIkTick`; `IK_LIFT_NO_DEFER`): with the pin went the foot's soft hold in place (`kKneelFootHold`), the foot slid 10cm and the thigh splayed to its abduction box in the tick the pin went — `lThigh.z` 0.9 → −15.0 — 122mm at the knee; kept until the handover, the tucked foot's standing model takes the leg over. On the base figure: stood up from the heels by a 75cm pull, the hips rise 69.5cm (standing), worst tick 46mm (impossible before; 122 with the release alone); the tall kneel's slow stand-up 43 (73 before), the 70cm yank 92 (375); the belly's prone-up bit-identical. All eight rigs 152/152, 30 phases moved and all passing — the getting-up-by-the-hip phases calmer (worst tick 87 → 24 on the oldest generation, 77 → 41 on the base), the lunges, the prones and the kneel-ups by millimetres. `gallery_floor.txt` has the gesture (section B's `54d_stood_up_from_heels`, from the Home view: she arrives standing with one gathering step, the stepping knee's swing its worst tick at 91mm, the gate 120). SOFT SPOTS: the hips stop 5-6cm short of a cursor pulled far above standing (the legs' length, as a standing figure's do); a knee held down by the belly's rule still lets go all at once at 1.3 of the standing height if the target is yanked straight through the band (the fade is over the target's travel, not time).

**THE TRUNK COMES UP WITH THE RISE — standing up by the hips from a folded trunk ends upright, looking ahead** (2026-10-28; `m_jsTrunkHomeShare` / `m_jsHandsPlanted` / `m_jsHeadIntrinsicHang` in armature.h, the share's update and the `homeBase` references in `ikPostureModel` and `ikSpineCoupling`, the trunk rebase in `rebaseContactArm` and the freed foot's spring in the `bear` block of `ikPinRows` (armatureiksolverows.cpp), `kTrunkHomeStep` in armatureiksolvetuning.h, the intrinsic's relax in `rightIdleHead`, the low-body step gate in `IkRig::updateStepPolicy`; the user: "Lets continue getting this perfect"). Stood up by the hips off all fours, or out of a sit-up, she stood bent double with her gaze 57° under the horizon: the ROOT's reference turned upright with the rise (RISING, above), the SPINE kept its drag-start fold (its references are the drag-start values) and the HEAD kept the hang the all fours had given it (`m_jsHeadIntrinsic` captures the drag-start righting UNDONE, and on all fours the righting is faded past 70° of chest tilt, so the head's hang was captured as if the user had posed it). FIVE THINGS: (1) while the body RISES (`m_jsRootRise`) the spine chain is among the unknowns (`m_jsTrunkInSolve`) and its references turn from the fold to the bind by `m_jsTrunkHomeShare` — the rise's progress followed at no more than `kTrunkHomeStep` 0.03 a tick (half a second for a whole fold; at 0.05 the brow moved 60-93mm a tick through the unfold, at 0.025-0.035 under 85) and FROZEN while a hand is still planted (`m_jsHandsPlanted`: a live, non-shadow wrist contact) — the root's upright reference and the coupling's references turning with it (`IK_JS_NO_RISE_TRUNK_HOME` is the round's A/B, `IK_JS_TRUNKHOMESTEP` the pace, `IK_JS_TRUNK_HOME_TRACE` the share per tick); (2) the share of the head's intrinsic that was the righting's FADE at the press (`m_jsHeadIntrinsicHang` = smoothstep(70°, 110°, the chest's tilt at the press)) relaxes with the same share, so the head comes up to look ahead while a head the user turned on an upright trunk keeps its turn; (3) THE TRUNK A NAILED HAND CURLS IS RE-BASED AS THE ARM IS (`rebaseContactArm`, pelvis drags with the body rising; `IK_JS_HAND_UNLOAD_NO_TRUNK_REBASE`): under a pelvis drag the hand's path to the root runs through the spine, which is among the unknowns while the hand is pinned, and the nailed hand curled the spine against its drag-start references as the hips rose — 5-10° over three joints priced 87/rad², a spring weaker than a 100/m² row at a 2cm miss on the arm's lever, which no fade of the hold can release (the six decades end at 100/m², `gone` easing toward 1 and never reaching it) — and the pin's going let it go in ONE tick: 144mm at a fingertip, 101 at the brow, whatever the pace of the trunk's home (0.05, 0.025 and 0.012 a tick read 144, 129, 119: the pace was never the spring). The spine chain and the root's rotation are re-based through `m_jsPostStepEuler` every tick a hand's hold is fading, and the trunk's home is measured from the re-based value (`homeBase`), so nothing springs and the trunk comes home at its pace from where the hands left it; a drag that began STANDING and dipped has no rise mode and no home, so its trunk is NOT re-based (re-based there it kept whatever the nailed hands had made of it — the pre-posed all-fours rise ended with the trunk still curled and the hands 18cm up where they hang at 40 — and un-rebased it springs back to the drag-start fold as it always did); (4) A FREED FOOT KEEPS THE UNROLL'S SPRING THROUGH THE HANDOVER (the `bear` block; `IK_JS_FREED_FOOT_NO_SPRING`): a foot the prone rule has freed — the hips ahead of its planted knee, `footFree` — has no ball row after the flat foot's handover (the row returns at 1 − footFree of its weight: nothing) and the spring that held it through the unroll ended with the unroll; until the knee's pin lifted off the foot was held by nothing but the floor, and a stand-up by the hips off the PRONE-UP's all fours — a sphinx, the hips a thigh's length ahead of the knees, held 18cm behind the cursor by the knee ceilings — flung the free leg 36cm forward through that gap and yanked it back when the pin went and the row returned at 1e8 with its target 8cm from the ball: 528mm at a toe, the body lurching 10cm toward the cursor and back (58 now); (5) A LOW BODY WITH NOTHING BUT ITS FEET ON THE FLOOR MAY STEP under a pelvis drag whose target is above the gate (`IK_STEP_LOW_BODY_ALWAYS`): the low-body gate (`kStepLowBody`, no step while the pelvis sits 12cm under standing) was a KNEEL's rule, and off that sphinx the knees lift, the legs reach full extension with the feet 63cm behind the hips, the pelvis rests 13cm under standing and no step could come that would let it rise — a deadlock the gate itself made, a figure held up by the hips with her feet trailing on their toes; with no live contact left (no knee, hand or seat: `pinIsLive` and not a shadow) the gate stands down, she takes two steps forward and arrives on the cursor (0.0mm) (the step gate and the landing's pace are in the next paragraph). TRIED AND REJECTED the same day, each measured: the trunk's home by `risen` alone (the pelvis asked fully upright while the knees held it: 302mm at a finger when the hands let go); the share paced but the spine entering the solve only once no hand is pinned (271: the hand's path makes the spine an unknown regardless); the root's reference paced with the share (144: the spring was the hands'); and A HOLD TAIL — a live contact's hold taken on down to nothing by the joint's rise beyond its 2cm band (the 100/m² floor is a phantom spring a contact kept past its band drifts from at a growing miss) — which made a hand pinned-but-weightless (341mm: the round-99 trap) and, let go of at once instead, threw the arm of a hand rolling onto its FINGERTIPS in the prone (its wrist 6cm up with the tips still down, 140-238mm on four rigs): the phantom's strain is answered where it is STORED (the rebase), not by its weight. On the base figure: stood up by the hips off all fours (a kneel, the chest onto all fours) the hips +58cm, the head +131cm, gaze 3.8°, worst tick 66mm (341 / 144 before); off the prone-up's all fours 125mm mid-step with two steps and a flat standing end (528); out of a sit-up gaze 0.0°, worst tick 44-59; off a half kneel 63; off a floor sit 48. All eight rigs 152/152 with 38 rows of three phases moved (the suspension's and the sit-up-by-the-head's gaze a degree or two higher: the root's paced reference); the step gate moves no phase. In-app 472/472 over the fifteen scripts (the floor gallery's `53d_stood_up_off_all_fours` after the prone-up and `63_stood_up_from_sitting` after the lay-down and sit-up, both looked at: she stands upright, looking ahead, arms hanging; the prone-up shot changed by a hair at the feet and was accepted). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads as round 113 left it, row for row: 32 near-misses and THREE real (the heavy male's half-kneel shin swing, the known mirror asymmetry, the alien's lay-down hold), 20 of 23 with no real failure. The script runner's `POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT_POS="rHand,rMid3"` prints named bones' world positions every tick — which joint a pop is in when no channel of its chain moved (the 341mm "finger" was the collar: the trunk unfolding 16° in one tick at the end of a 0.9m lever). SOFT SPOTS: the trunk's unfold is a constant pace, not the rise's (a fast yank stands her up with the trunk still coming up for half a second after); a chest-dragged rise keeps its references (the rebase is the pelvis drag's); off the sphinx-like all fours the first step's mid-swing snap (the next paragraph); a figure whose all fours has the feet well behind her stands up by STEPPING, which a user who wanted her feet where they were will not expect.

**THE HANGING FOOT'S TARGET WAITS FOR A FOOT THE FLOOR HOLDS BACK — the half kneel on heavy bodies** (2026-10-28; the lead cap in `ikKneeHangUnder` and the eased clearing height in `ikPinRowsEnd` (armatureiksolverows.cpp), `m_jsLyingHangY` in armature.h; the sweep's one real half-kneel row, the heavy male's 197mm toe swing, and the user's "heading towards perfection"). A knee drawn up out of a flat kneel hangs its lying foot from the shin, and the hang's REACH POINT was where a full-length shin from the knee's goal meets the ANKLE's floor height — straight under a knee a shin's length up — but a lying foot still points DOWN, and under the knee its toes would be through the floor: the floor's rows on the toes held the foot 16cm BEHIND the target while the hang row (300-400/m²) pulled, and the tick the sole's hold levelled the foot enough, the whole leg swung forward under the knee — 22° of shin in one tick, the ankle 12cm forward and 10cm down, 158mm at a toe on the heavy male and 173 on a heavy female (55 on the base, whose smaller foot cleared sooner). TWO THINGS: (1) THE HANG TARGET NEVER LEADS THE ANKLE BY MORE THAN TWO EASE STEPS (`ikKneeHangUnder`, 2 × `kPinEaseStep` figure-scaled; `IK_JS_KNEE_UNDER_NO_LEAD_CAP`) — the palm lead cap's lesson (round 99) again: eased from where it WAS, the target walked on while the foot stood held, and when the foot could follow it followed all at once; capped, the target waits for a foot that cannot follow and leads it on at the pins' pace once it can; (2) the height at which the foot, shaped as it is, clears the floor (`lyingFootHangY`) is EASED at the pins' pace (`m_jsLyingHangY`, `IK_JS_LYING_HANG_NO_EASE`): read fresh each tick it dropped 7cm a tick as the foot levelled and the row pulled the ankle down by that much — a feedback with the levelling (158 → 141 alone). On the three bodies the half kneel's worst tick is 44-45mm (158 / 173 / 50), no pop, the foot landing ahead under the knee as before (the harness's larger draw lands it 10-18mm ahead with the shin at 93-97°, as it did); a knee drawn higher than a shin's length up (the gallery's base-sized draw on the heavy male) hangs its foot in the air under a vertical shin with the toe tips brushing the floor, rightly. REJECTED the same day, each measured: the hang's reach point taken at the clearing height (behind the knee while the foot points down, under it as it levels — 37mm on the three bodies in the app, but under the harness's larger draw a foot that never levelled never came under: 100-151mm behind the knee on three rigs, the shin at 114°), and the lying foot's sole target eased from its found rotation at `kSoleTurnStepDeg` (the slide's rule) — the levelling was not the driver, and lagging the target behind a foot the floor's rows were levelling anyway made it worse (161 / 173). AND TWO SMALLER THINGS from the same session: THE FLAT FOOT'S GENTLE LANDING ENDS WHEN IT HAS LANDED (`m_jsBallLanded`; `IK_JS_LAND_GENTLY_ALWAYS`): after the handover the ball's target eased at `kKneelFlatLandStep` 1cm a tick for the rest of the drag, so a STEP the rig then took — standing up off the prone-up's sphinx-like all fours, 93cm forward — walked the foot after its pin at a third of the swing's pace, both feet landing 20-50cm behind their spots with the stance error reading 0 (the pins were there) and her standing on her toes with her legs angled back; the pace is the pins' once the target has met its spot since the handover (gentle in the height alone, with the ball's few centimetres of travel at the handover taken at the pins' pace, moved the kneel-ups' knee steps 37-44 → 74-83 on three rigs and was dropped; a 'while the target is above its spot' gate read a step's lift arc as a landing, 210 at the brow). And A LOW BODY WITH NOTHING BUT ITS FEET ON THE FLOOR MAY STEP (`IkRig::updateStepPolicy`, `IK_STEP_LOW_BODY_ALWAYS`): the low-body step gate (`kStepLowBody`, no step while the pelvis sits 12cm under standing) was a kneel's rule, and off that sphinx the knees lift, the legs reach full extension with the feet 63cm behind the hips, the pelvis rests 13cm under standing, and no step could come that would let it rise — a deadlock the gate itself made, a figure held up by the hips with her feet trailing on their toes; with no live contact left (no knee, hand or seat: `pinIsLive` and not a shadow) the gate stands down, she takes two steps and arrives on the cursor (0.0mm), standing flat. All eight rigs 152/152: the half kneel's worst tick 52 → 42 on the main family, the knee raise's hanging ankle fore-aft of its hang 56 → 31 (round 112's soft spot, half answered: its lateral part 59 → 91, the capped target no longer hauling the foot sideways under a shifted socket), and the kneel-ups' knee steps 36-41 → 54-61 — the gathering steps at the pins' pace, no longer slowed to a third by the forever-gentle landing, which had left the feet behind their pins. In-app 473/473 on the fifteen scripts with the knee raise's two shots moved by a hair (the hanging foot a few millimetres lower under the knee) and the new `53d_stood_up_off_all_fours` accepted after looking: she stands flat on both feet where the reference had her mid-step. THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 31 near-misses and TWO real (32 and three): the heavy male's half-kneel row is GONE, and what is left is the known mirror asymmetry and the alien's lay-down hold (46mm a tick at a knee); 21 of 23 with no real failure. SOFT SPOT: the first of those two steps begins with the hips at a straight planted leg's full reach, 35cm short of the cursor (the hips OUTSIDE the planted foot laterally), the fold retry kicking both straight knees every tick and finding nothing, until mid-swing the knee folds and the hips come 20cm in one tick — 125mm at the shin (the gallery's gate 350; a landing deficit paid off at `kLiftRiseStep`, the lift-off's rule, was tried and did nothing: the snap is mid-swing, not at the landing).

**THE HANG'S FLOOR-AVOID TURN IS CONTINUOUS** (2026-10-28; the bisection in `hangIdleArms`' floor block, armatureikpost.cpp; `IK_ARM_HANG_FLOOR_NO_BISECT` is the A/B; the sweep's lay-down real row — the alien's 46mm hold jump). An idle arm kept OFF THE FLOOR while the seat rolls or the hips go down (the floor block above) had its wanted direction turned from the hang toward the ride direction by the FIRST OF SIXTEEN FIXED STEPS along the arc that cleared the floor — a direction quantized in steps of between/16, 4.7° of a 75° arc. As the chest rolled down the clearing step's index changed once, the wanted direction jumped a whole step in one tick and the hang took its full `kArmHangStepDeg` 4° at once: 46mm at a fingertip on a long-armed character, one tick into her still hold (`IK_ARM_HANG_TRACE`: `wanted dir` y −0.22 → −0.14 between two ticks, the euler's middle channel 11.7 → 7.7; the base rigs had the same jump in every lay-down, recline and sit-up, hidden inside the move's 80mm gate). The boundary is BISECTED now — the sixteen-step scan brackets it, eight halvings bring it to 0.02° — so the wanted direction is a continuous function of the pose and the arm follows it in small steps. On all eight rigs the floor-seat family's worst ticks halved: the lay-down 44-48 → 21-32mm, the recline 32-37 → 14-19, the sit-up 39-47 → 16-25, the base rig's recline hold 35 → 10 and the male's lay-down hold 11 → 2.3; the alien's lay-down hold 46 → 2.2. Nothing else moved (the phases that begin from a floor sit gain under a millimetre of start asymmetry, the sit's arms now resting a hair differently); the lying arms rest at the exact clearance, so the floor-penetration row of two rigs' lay-downs reads 3.5mm where it read 0 (the margin is 2cm; the row's gate 30). AND THE HANG'S FLOOR MARGIN STANDS CLEAR OF THE RIG'S SEED BAND (`kArmHangFloorMargin` 3cm, was 2): the first in-app suite on the bisected build read 469/473 — the stand-up out of the sit by the hips ended FOLDED IN HALF with her hands on the floor (the hips 300mm short, the pelvis pitched 48°, no step), while the same four drags in isolation stood her up with either variant. Two measures of one fact had met at the edge: the hang keeps an idle arm `kArmHangFloorMargin` over its floor clearances, and the rig seeds a joint within `kSeedContactBand` of its clearance as a live contact at the next press — both 2cm — and with the turn continuous the arm rests EXACTLY at the margin, on the band's edge: in the gallery's sit-up the right fingertip came to rest at 41mm where the quantized turn had left it at 45 (`POSESTUDIO_IK_SCRIPT_POS` on both builds), and at the stand's press that hand was planted (its wrist held, the other hand rising free), the trunk's home froze on the planted hand (THE TRUNK COMES UP WITH THE RISE) and the low-body step gate saw a live contact. At 3cm the arm hangs a centimetre clear of the band — no higher than the quantized turn had left it — and the suite reads 473/473 with the recline, the sit-up and the stand changed by a hair and accepted. The margin moved the SECOND GENERATION's pre-posed all-fours too: its hands had never come down on that hip drag since round 111's floor-avoid under a descent (the hands 15cm up, the phase's contact rows informational there), and they land now (0.045m, four pins) — which turned the elbow-swing phase's gates on for that rig, whose hands land on their RING-FINGER BASES 4.6cm apart under the chest and whose wrist then rolls 32mm about the planted finger under the swing (the wrist-contact rigs read 0.005): its gate is 40 with the note. All eight rigs 152/152; the oldest generation's lay-down and sit-up penetration rows fell 22 → 11 and 14 → 3mm with the wider margin, and two rigs' 3.5mm went back to 0. THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry; the alien's lay-down row is gone), 22 of 23 with no real failure.

**A TWIST HELPER IS NO CONTACT — getting up off the belly on a body that lies flatter than the base** (2026-10-28; `IkRigBone::helper` set from `Armature::isTwistBone` in `ensureIkRig`, `IkRig::helperNode` read by `seedPoseContacts` and `updateContacts`' DETECT (ikrigcontacts.cpp); `IK_HELPER_CONTACTS` is the A/B; the open list's push-up chain on custom bodies). The floor gallery's prone-up on the user's character ended in a COBRA — the hips 157mm short of their cursor, the trunk hinged up, the legs flat — and on the heavy male it flung a toe 480mm; on the base figure the same chain comes up onto all fours. The rig's contact rules take the FIRST real-mass joint whose flesh reaches the floor, in node order, and the main family's THIGH TWIST bone comes before its shin: a body whose prone lies flatter than the base's (her hip joint 16cm up where the base's is 23, the thighs level instead of sloping down from the hips) brings the twist bone's flesh — the lower thigh — within the seed band before the knee's, and it was PLANTED, 12cm up, mid-thigh (`[drag-state] pin lThighTwist ... live=1`), the knee itself 'served by the pin above' and free. Under the hips-up that pin was the belly's rule's KNEE: held DOWN by the knee ceiling for the whole drag (`kneeBelowHips`, socket 7cm over a 20cm 'thigh'), so the hips could lift the thigh only about its middle — the cursor's row asked +479 of the root's height and the two ceilings −708 (`IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE=1`): 106mm of 250, the trunk hinging for the rest — and the lying foot's model took the pin's height for its knee's and asked the ankle 7cm UP (the heel rows 46mm far, `IK_JS_FAR_ROWS`). A twist helper — two locked channels, the free one along the bone — splits a rigid segment and has no joint a body rests on: the segment's far joint (the knee, the hand) is the contact. The rig is told which bones are helpers at build and never plants one; the floor's rows on their flesh stay (a thigh's middle is still kept out of the floor). On the user's character the prone-up now comes up onto all fours — hands under the shoulders on straight arms, knees down, feet flat — the hips 243mm up (106), 46mm from their cursor (157), worst tick 23mm (83); the heavy male's toe flip is gone (worst tick 38, was 480) and he kneels up off his half-flat prone with one hand planted (his other hand never reached the floor in the hands-forward drag: the strut-armed prone's own gap, below). On the eight rigs only the main family's PRONE phase moved — its live contacts at mouse-up 8 → 6 (the twist bones had been counted there too, planted under the sloping thighs at the end of the descent), the hip joint resting 134 → 128 and 133 → 119mm over the floor, the pelvis 105 → 98°: she lies a little flatter — 152/152 on all eight. In-app 473/473 with no shot changed on the base figure (its gallery's prone never planted the twist bones: the reports are bit-identical). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP reads as round 116 left it, row for row: 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 with no real failure. AND TWO MORE RULES THE SAME TRACE LED TO, on the heavy female's prone-up (the twist pins had been holding her thighs down by accident, and without them she PLANKED — hips on the cursor, knees 22cm up, hands in the air): (1) A KNEE ON THE FLOOR KEEPS IT UNDER A LIMB DRAG (the live contact's unilateral hold in `ikPinRows`; `IK_JS_KNEE_RISES_UNDER_LIMB_DRAG`): the rise the unilateral hold is for is the BODY's, and a hand slid forward from under a prone chest lifts nothing — yet on her the sphinx it made arched the trunk and carried both knees 3cm up, past the hold's fade. Under a hand's or an elbow's drag (`m_jsWristOf[effector] == effector`, `s.elbowDrag`; not a trunk effector), the body neither rising nor suspended, a live knee that is not the drag's own gets a CEILING at its planted height at full weight through `handCeilings` — it may settle, not rise; on the eight rigs two rows of the kneeling hand raise moved (a planted foot's rotation by half a degree on two generations). (2) A SEAT SERVES NO KNEE (`IkRig::servedByPinAbove`; `IK_SEAT_SERVES_LEGS`): a joint under a pin on its own path is 'served' by it and is no contact of its own — a planted foot's toes, a hand's carpals — and a pin on a joint of the PELVIS GIRDLE (the pelvis bone resting on the floor: her belly; a thigh socket whose flesh does) is above every leg joint on its path, so while her belly rested on the floor no knee of hers could be seeded under a hand drag at all (`[drag-state] pin pelvis ... live=1`, the knees absent; the base figure's belly rides clear of the floor in its prone, so its knees were always its own). A girdle pin serves nothing below it now: bit-identical on all eight rigs. WITH THE THREE: the user's character comes up onto all fours; the heavy female's knees stay on the floor through the hand drags (49mm, the pins `lShin`/`rShin` at the hips-up press) and she comes up ONTO HER KNEES, the hands letting go as the arms go taut — the gallery's base-sized hand drags put her hands too far ahead for her shorter arms — and hanging forward-down with the fingertips brushing the floor, the documented end of a far-handed prone-up; the heavy male likewise, with one arm riding bent (the gallery's hand drag ends 7cm in the air on his framing: the wrist and the fingertip both at 66-68mm at its end, never a contact). The floor gallery on the four custom bodies (`chars117_*`): the user's character 0 failures of 72 (3 before), the heavy female's and male's remaining rows the gallery's sizing — the prone-up's hands and knees (above), the two stand-ups' `hips > 600/700` on bodies that stand lower, the half kneel's foot in the air — with no pop left on the male (4 before, 487mm at a toe); the alien as before (3, the sit-up's pelvis rows). In-app 473/473 with no shot changed on the base figure (its belly rides clear of the floor in its prone, and its hand drags never lifted a knee). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP is unchanged by the two: 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23. SOFT SPOTS: the heavy male's prone still rests on strut arms (the hip joint 15cm from its cursor; the known hand-walk gap), and a hand dragged forward off such a prone does not reach the floor, so his hips-up stands him up on his knees with that arm riding bent, held up like a paw (a POSED idle limb rides, by design); the twist helpers of the forearm are no contacts either, so a forearm lying flat is held at the elbow and the hand, which is what a forearm on the floor is held at.

**THE SEAT ROCKS UNDER A HAND DRAG TOO** (2026-10-28; the block after the reach hinge in `ikTrunkPolicy`, armatureiksolvedrag.cpp; `IK_JS_SEAT_ROCK_TRUNK_ONLY` is the A/B; the documented soft spot 'a seated reach by the hand bends the spine alone'). A pinned pelvis — the seat, THE SEAT paragraph above — rocked under a TRUNK drag alone (`m_jsTrunkHinge` 1: the root's pitch an unknown at the bow's price, coupled into the spine's chain, the pin's orientation hold per-axis with its pitch weight zero), and a seated figure reaching with a HAND bent her spine over a pelvis held bolt upright: the gallery's hand laid on the knee leaned the trunk 32cm at the head with the pelvis at 0° and the lower spine curled 10°. A person reaching forward or down from a chair hinges at the hips. A hand drag (`m_jsWristOf[effector] == effector`) under a seat pin now sets the trunk drag's hinge — the reference where she sits, so the arm's reach pulls the pelvis over only as far as the cursor's bounded pull buys against 20/rad²: the root pitches 12-16° where it pitched 0, the lower spine 6° where it curled 10, the hand on the knee to 0.02mm, the head 35cm from where it was (32: the hinge reaches further). The reach-down fold (REACHING DOWN) stays a standing start's — a seated reach down rocks on its pin instead. All eight rigs 152/152 with the two seated hand phases moved on each (the knees 1-2cm less inward under the rocked pelvis, the gaze a degree higher) and ONE gate re-set: the seated hand's palm on the knee 45 → 60° off straight down (50-55 on the eight rigs, 39-41 before: the hand lies on the knee's sloping top from a trunk hinged at the hips rather than curled over it). In-app 473/473 with the seated hand-on-knee's two shots changed and accepted after looking (she leans to the knee from the hips with a straighter back where she curled over it). THE 23-CHARACTER SWEEP is unchanged: 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23. SOFT SPOTS: a seated hand reaching DOWN past the knees hinges forward without the reach-down fold's reference, so the pelvis goes only where the pull takes it (no 'fold to reach' gesture from a chair); the thighs follow the rocked pelvis (the app knows no seat surface for them), so the knees move a centimetre or two with the lean.

**A CARPAL SELECTS THE HAND** (2026-10-28; `BoneClass::Carpal`, `Armature::m_boneLimbEnd`, `classifyBones` / `posingBone` / `knownPosingBone` / `highlightedWithSelection` in armatureikdigit.cpp, the segment test in `beginIkDrag`; the user: "selecting the hand node can be difficult, and posing carpals in IK mode is not practical — selecting carpals in IK mode should not happen, making the hand selection area much larger and easier to target, similar to how we handled the head"). What is below a wrist or an ankle and is neither the limb end nor a digit — the carpals (four on the main family's right hand, three on its left, where `lCarpal2-1` is a follower-renamed id), a hand's prop anchor, the newest generation's metacarpals, the mid-foot, the ball of the foot, a heel — is a CARPAL now: the hand's or the foot's own flesh, not a joint to pose. Until this round they were Body bones — selectable, the hand's flesh split among them, a click on the palm as often a carpal's as the hand's (the rig promoted the DRAG to the hand, but the selection, the highlight sliver and the Transform tab were the carpal's), and a hand grabbed on its palm had a 'segment' ending at a carpal, so a click near that end dragged the carpal by dilation. A click on one selects its LIMB END (`posingBone`: the wrist, the ankle — `m_boneLimbEnd`, the `limbEnd` the classifier already carried for the digit test) with the hit point as the grab point, as a click on the face selects the head; its flesh lights with the selected hand or foot (`highlightedWithSelection`, through the joint buffer's second half, the face rig's path); no joint of one snaps or is a snap candidate (the pick's `== Body` tests); the hand and the foot are no segment (`boneClass(far) == Body`), so a palm grab is dragged by the hand's own joint with its offset measured. The scripts' `lit.<bone>` reads `highlightedWithSelection` too. Verified: the digit phase gates that every Carpal under the hand selects it (2-5 per rig); the eight rigs are otherwise bit-identical (the harness grabs by name); `gallery_digits.txt` section 7b clicks the three left carpals and the mid-foot and the ball (`selected.lHand` / `selected.lFoot`, `lit.lCarpal1/2`, `lit.lMetatarsals`). In-app 481/481 (the fifteen scripts; eight expectations new) with one shot changed and accepted: the hand drag's close-up, where the whole hand lights now instead of the wrist's sliver. SOFT SPOT: a carpal cannot be turned by the Transform dials or B / S / T any more (nothing selects it); a pose file still carries whatever a carpal was posed to.

**CTRL ON THE TORSO BENDS THE TORSO ALONE** (2026-10-28; the `IkScope::Part` resolution in `Armature::beginIkDrag`, armatureik.cpp — `POSESTUDIO_CTRL_TRUNK_MOVES` is the A/B, the figure move for every body grab as from 2026-09-28; the user-pin rule in `IkRig::plantContacts` (beginDrag's), ikrigdrag.cpp; the user: "ctrl+click grab the chest or any other part of the torso should still propagate the IK, but in a limited fashion like when ctrl+click dragging a limb — ctrl+click dragging the upper chest should continue to IK pose through the torso, but stop before the legs. This will allow posing and manipulating of the torso region"). THE FIGURE MOVE (above) had taken every grab of the body under Ctrl — the hips, the abdomen, the chest — and there was no way left to bend the spine alone under Ctrl (its soft spot). Now `Part` resolves to `Figure` only when the rig's drag effector IS the solve root (the hips; a grab of the pelvis girdle is promoted to it); a spine joint resolves to `Chain` as every limb does — the SCOPED IK paragraph's rule, which had always covered the trunk (a spine joint's chain is the bones from it down to, not including, the root; `limbJunction` of every spine bone is the root): the pelvis and the legs stay at 0.0000, no root translation or rotation, no balance, no step, no follow or hinge, the arms and the head ride the chest, and the spine's coupling rows (`ikSpineCoupling` reads whichever links of `m_jsSpineChain` are unknowns) bend the chain as one. `isBodyGrab` stays as the figure move's own test of which pin holds the whole figure. AND A USER PIN BELOW A SCOPED TRUNK DRAG HOLDS: the rig's pin rule leaves a user pin in the effector's subtree out under a plain trunk drag (a pinned hand would fight the body's follow), and the Armature now sets the rig's scoped flag BEFORE `beginDrag` (cleared on a failed begin; the figure move's `endDrag` clears it too) so that under a scoped trunk drag the rule keeps it — the pinned limb's bones hang below the effector on the pin's active path and are unknowns by the chain rule's own "what hangs below the effector" clause, so the hand stays to microns while the trunk bends toward it and the arm folds, and when the arm runs out the pin stops the chest; a pin outside the chain's subtree (a planted foot) is a constant row, as ever. AND A SPINE JOINT OF THE PELVIS GIRDLE KEEPS ITS OWN EFFECTOR UNDER A SCOPED DRAG (the same evening; the user: "ctrl+click is not performing limited IK for chest_2 and abdomen_2"): the first and second spine bones are GIRDLE grabs — `IkRig::beginDrag`'s pelvis-girdle promotion takes them to the root, and under Ctrl that was the figure move still (`[ik] beginDrag FIGURE by abdomen2` in the app's trace, from every view). The promotion now stands down for a girdle joint with NO foot-class joint below it (`IkRig::hasFootBelow`) while the rig is scoped — the pelvis bone and the thigh sockets, a foot below them, still go to the root: a Ctrl grab of the hips moves the figure — so the lower abdomen under Ctrl bends its one or two spine joints down to the pelvis (`IK_SCOPED_SPINE_GIRDLE_PROMOTES` is the A/B). The upper chest had resolved to the chain from the first (from the front; from the SIDE a click on the chest lands on the hanging arm's skin and grabs the arm, by design). AND A COLLAR UNDER A SCOPED DRAG IS THE TRUNK'S (the same night; the user again: "ctrl+click drag on chest_2 still does nothing"): a pixel scan over the chest (`click chest_2 dx dy` on a 7×7 grid, home and front views) showed the whole band above the breast line — where a user grabs "the upper chest" — picking a COLLAR bone in both views, and a collar's scoped chain is one bone (its limb junction is its parent, the upper chest) that cannot move its own joint: a true "does nothing". `IkRig::beginDrag` now promotes a collar to the spine joint it hangs from under a scoped drag (`isSpineJoint`, new: above the root, junction the root, no foot below) — AND THE NECK'S LOWEST JOINT with it (the user's third report, the same night, with a screenshot of the lit upper chest: "I ctrl+click and drag chest_2 and nothing happens; the other chest and abdomen joints work" — the chest_2 highlight runs up the throat, a click there picks the neck's base (`click chest_2 0 -50` picks `chest_2`, higher `neck`), and a Ctrl drag of it read `head 0.0mm`: its chain was that one bone; the rule is simply "a bone hanging directly off a spine joint, whose limb junction is that joint" — the upper neck and the head keep their chains, which run down through the neck base, so Ctrl on them bends the neck alone, round 100's design; its phase: the neck's lowest joint pulled 15cm forward under Ctrl moves 146-149mm with the upper chest 80-87mm along (60-62 on the two T-pose generations; the gate 50 with the note), the hips and feet at 0.0000, worst tick 5-7mm, 157 on every rig with everything else bit-identical) as a pectoral is promoted by its token mass, so the Armature measures the grab's offset off the pose each tick and the trunk bends through wherever on the upper chest the click landed. `IK_SCOPED_COLLAR_STAYS` is the A/B. Its phase (156 on every rig, everything else bit-identical): a collar pulled 15cm forward under Ctrl moves 145-149mm with the upper chest 98-102mm along (70-71 on the two T-pose generations, whose collars run sideways from the chest), the hips and feet at 0.0000, no leg channel turned, worst tick 5-9mm; in the app the collar's press resolves to the upper chest on the base figure and the user's character alike (`[ik] beginDrag effector=lCollar solved=chest_2`, the head riding 207-213mm under a 15% sideways drag), and `62e_scoped_collar_front` is the shot, looked at. Two phases came with it (155 on every rig, every other phase bit-identical): the second spine bone pulled 10cm forward under Ctrl moves 47-52mm — what its two joints' flexion gives — with the hips and feet at 0.0000, no leg channel turned, worst tick 13-16mm; the pelvis bone pulled 20cm up lifts the feet 200.0mm with no joint turned and the body rigid to 0.0002mm. In-app `62d_scoped_abdomen_front` (the upper abdomen Ctrl-dragged sideways in the front view: the lower spine side-bends, the head rides 140mm, the hips and feet still) and a pelvis-bone lift (`hips > 199`, no joint turned), looked at. MEASURED on all eight rigs (`--only scoped`): the upper chest pulled 20cm forward under Ctrl moves 165-183mm with the hips and feet at 0.0000, no leg channel turned, no step, worst tick 11-14mm, a still hold (on the base figure the three abdomen and chest joints flex 18-20° each — the coupling's even split); with a hand PINNED and the chest carried 10cm toward it and 5cm up, the chest moves 73-76mm (49-51 on the two T-pose generations, whose pinned hand stands out at arm's length), the hand 0.0002-0.0004mm, the hips, feet, legs and other arm at 0.0000, worst tick 7-15mm; pulled 30cm straight up instead the hand stays to 0.0004mm and the hold jumps 0.12-0.24mm; the hips pulled 30cm up under Ctrl (the figure move, the hips' own phase now) lift the feet 300.0mm with no joint turned and every joint rigid to 0.0001mm. EVERY OTHER PHASE IS BIT-IDENTICAL on all eight rigs (153 phases: the hips' lift is one more), and the 23-character sweep reads as round 119 left it — 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), 22 of 23 with no real failure — with the seven Ctrl phases (the girdle's two, the collar's and the neck base's included) passing on every character. Verified: the harness's four `ctrl:` phases are the hips' lift (the chest's lift of 2026-09-28 moved to the hips: feet +300, no joint turned, rigid to 0.01mm), the hips carried and pushed down as before, and the two chest phases rewritten — the upper chest pulled 20cm forward (the trunk alone: the chest ≥ 80mm, the hips and feet ≤ 0.01, no leg channel turned, no step) and the chest carried 10cm toward a pinned hand (the chest ≥ 50mm, the hand ≤ 0.05mm, the hips, feet, legs and other arm still; then pulled 30cm up, beyond the trunk's reach: the hand ≤ 0.5mm, the legs still, a still hold); every other phase bit-identical on all eight rigs (a plain drag and every limb's Ctrl drag read nothing of this). In-app `gallery_scoped.txt` has the chest bent forward under Ctrl (`62_scoped_chest_side`: `moved.chest_2 > 80`, the hips and a foot under 0.5mm, the thigh and shin channels 0), the hips' lift and its grounding (`62c_ctrl_hips_lifted_side`, `62b`), and the pinned hand under a Ctrl chest drag (`63b`: the chest over 50mm, the hand under 0.1, the hips and feet still) — looked at; in-app 512/512 over the fifteen scripts with only that gallery's shots changed (`62b`, `63b`) or new (`62c`, `62d`, `62e`, `62f`), accepted after looking (the landing gallery's dip shots differ by wall-clock timing every run, as documented), and the same gallery reads 76/76 on the user's own character. The manual's Posing page and the shortcut table say the torso bends alone under Ctrl and the hips move the figure. SOFT SPOTS: the pelvis cannot be rocked under Ctrl (the root is frozen: a Ctrl drag of the hips is the figure move) — a seated figure's trunk lean under Ctrl bends the spine alone, where the plain drag with a seat pin rocks the pelvis; the arms ride the chest rigidly (no hang, by the scope's design), so a chest leaned far forward under Ctrl holds its arms back along the body as the pre-hang bow did; a Ctrl head drag with a pinned hand lets the hand's path through the chest give too (the chest is below the effector on the pin's path), which is the chain rule's reading of "what hangs below".

**THE PRONE LIES DOWN — the hips taken forward and down from all fours, on any body** (2026-10-29; `m_jsProneLie` in `ikChooseUnknowns` (armatureiksolvedrag.cpp), the lying references in `ikPostureDofs`, the trailing-hand block of `ikPinRowLiveContact`, the floor-avoid of `findHangTurns` (armatureikpost.cpp); `kProneLieFrom/Full`, `kHandTrailPull` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; the open item the refactor left: the prone on heavy and long-armed bodies rested with the hips 19-22cm up on their arms as struts, the pelvis diving past flat (142°), the face propping the chest). A prone is a KNEEL-START pelvis drag whose TARGET goes AHEAD of the planted knees (kProneFootFreeFrom/Full past the knee's pin, along the heading) and DOWN toward the belly (kProneLieFrom/Full 0.2-0.5 of the way from the hips at the press to the hip joint's clearance lying prone — the pelvis's flesh along its fore axis); its share is a function of the target alone, kept through the settle, and it does FIVE things: (1) the neck chain and the spine join the unknowns (the trunk from the hips to the face had been one rigid piece pivoting about the root — the face on the floor held the chest up by the neck's length, and nothing in the solve could turn either; the righting leaves the neck-lift bones to the solve, `m_jsNeckLift`); (2) the trunk's HOME turns to LYING at the paced share — the spine's references to the bind, the pelvis bone's to 0 (it bent to its 25° limit chasing the cursor otherwise), the root's pitch to upright plus a right angle forward — and does NOT freeze on planted hands (the prone's hands are leashes, below); (3) the pelvis's kneel-tilt price keeps its weight under the lying share (`kKneelTiltScale`'s fade let the pelvis DIVE 30° past flat to chase a cursor beyond the thighs' reach from the planted knees: the whole 35° of hip hyperextension and the pelvis bone's 25 for a few centimetres forward, the spine arched off it); (4) `m_jsProneShare` — the hips-ahead-of-the-knee share that frees the kneeling feet and fades the tilt — reads the target's share too (a DEADLOCK on the third generation otherwise: its kneel lies the feet flat with the ankle held at its place, the hips go ahead of the knees only as the shins straighten, the shins straighten only once the feet are let go, and the feet are let go only once the hips are 8-25cm ahead — she stood dead at 13cm with the knees bent 89° until the hands' release broke it, 112mm in a tick); (5) THE HANDS ARE LEASHES: a hand's hold is unloaded by the share through the eased hand unload (`kHandUnloadStep`), as a trailing hand's is (kHandTrailFrom/Full of the arm's span behind the shoulder, the earlier rule, kept), and a trailing hand is a FRICTION CONTACT — its x/z hold and its fingertip height row are Huber-bounded at `kHandTrailPull` 300 × (1 − the fade): released by weight alone the leash let go GEOMETRICALLY (the body stood dead against 1e8 rows while the cursor ran 29cm ahead and paid the backlog off in one 112mm tick when the weight fell under what the pull could stretch; bounded but unfading, the leash was as strong at 0.99 of the fade as at 0.1 and went in the one tick the weight reached zero); its own-rise fade is replaced by the eased unload (held in x/z and free in y, a hand behind a shoulder moving away is lifted the moment the arm is taut, and the joint's two-centimetre fade let the leash go in three ticks: 90mm at the brow).
