THREE THINGS FOUND ON THE WAY, each a trace's: the share had been read off the CHEST-TOP's live TILT (70-110°) — a mode read off the thing it changes: it rose to 1 on the way down, the root's reference was left at its dive, the spine arched to its extension limits to lift the chest off a pelvis asked nose-down, the tilt fell back under the band and the share went to zero (the trunk arched, the head 34cm up); the trunk RE-BASE of `rebaseContactArm` overwrote this tick's REFERENCE with the current value as well as the home (`IK_JS_LYING_TRACE` printed a lying reference of 90 while `IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE` showed the solver's at the value: the root had no reference at all for as long as a hand's hold was fading, which under the prone is the whole drag), and its first tick moved the home 56 → 77° (93mm at the brow): it sets the home alone now, under the RISE alone; and the hang's floor-avoid — `clears` carried the arm rigidly to a candidate direction, and the real turn (through the shoulder's channels) differed by a twist, a thumb 5mm into the floor, the stop refusing every step: it PROBES THE TURN (hangTurnFor at 10° steps, hangTurnFloorDepth), for the FLOOR and the VOLUMES (a direction inside the hip's capsule was one the stop blocked outright — the lying body's arm, left where the sinking trunk took it, propped the hips on its fingertips' floor rows, 24mm), and the arc's end is ALWAYS of the trunk-axis family (the chest top's −up — down for a seated trunk, backward-horizontal for a prone one, turned OUT by the least share from the hang direction's own outward share up to 0.60 that clears, scanned and bisected): the ride direction taken when it cleared flipped the end by 27° the tick it did, as a lying trunk settled, and the arm swung in at the hang's pace through the lay-down's hold (46mm a tick on two rigs); an in-step clamp from the share the arm HAD was tried and gained the sit-up 14mm of left/right asymmetry (each arm keeping its own history). THE HIP HEIGHTS ARE THE BODIES' OWN: the base rig rests on the thigh twist's flesh and the nose (`IK_JS_PLANE_TRACE`), the heaviest character on 124mm thighs — hip joint over the floor 117 / 137 / 155 on three generations, 177 and 215 on two heavy builds, 110 on a slight one (128 / 19-22cm with struts, before); pelvis 95-107° on every rig; the face down; worst single-tick move 42-98 (the third generation's 98: its arms fold back to the collar's and the wrist's limits, a strut the fade releases). The harness's prone phase GATES the hip height on every rig now (the hands let go by design); `IK_HARNESS_PRONE_SHARE` scales its travel; `IK_JS_TASK_TRACE` prints the problem's rows by task and bone name, `IK_JS_BLOCKED_TRACE` the solver's active set, `IK_ARM_HANG_AVOID_TRACE` the avoid's search, and the `[fade]` trace the trail with the socket, hand and hips; `IK_JS_PRONELIE_FROM/FULL` and `IK_JS_PRONE_TILT_SHARE` are the A/B levers. All eight rigs 157/157 (60 phases moved against the baseline, all in the all-fours, floor-seat and prone families: the all-fours rise's asymmetry 25 → 0.3mm on the second generation, its elbow swing 144 → 33, a lay-down's penetration 11 → 0); the 23-character sweep as before, 31 near-misses and one real (the known mirror asymmetry); the in-app suite 512/512 on the fifteen scripts with the floor gallery's thirteen shots changed and looked at (the prone, the prone-up, the kneel-up, the recline, the lay-down, the sit-up, the stand-ups: the arms along her sides where they folded under the chest) and the landing gallery's wall-clock-timed dip frame. In the app the prone looked at: she lies on her belly with the legs straight, the feet pointed, the arms along her sides and the face down; `gallery_floor.txt`'s prone-up walks each hand forward 28% now (the hands lie beside the hips, a trunk's length from under the shoulders) and its two gates were re-set after looking (the far hand lands on its fingertips under its shoulder, 98mm; the stand-up off that all fours snaps its first step mid-swing, 131, the known soft spot). SOFT SPOTS: the recline's and lay-down's worst tick rose 19-32 → 50-58mm — the hang turning the arms at its own 4°/tick toward the lying place (looked at: the arms come to rest along her sides); the third generation's prone pops 98mm as its jammed arm lets go; a hand released beside the hips stays there (the hang's), so a prone-up must walk the hands forward first. THE FLOOR GALLERY ON FOUR CUSTOM BODIES with the final build (`chars121/`): the heavy male, the user's character and the heaviest newest-generation build LIE DOWN now (pelvis 86 / 79 / 96°, where the first two rested on strut arms before the round), and their prone-up's misses are the gallery's base sizing — each hand walked 28% of the view forward from beside the hip lands ahead of a shorter body's shoulder, and the hips-up then lifts the hands off (184-431mm up on two of them) or leaves one planted (205 on the male); the heavy female's prone stops at a sphinx (pelvis 57°, the grab 187mm from its cursor, a 170mm tick on the way) and the hips press that follows it begins no drag at all — the one custom-body case the round leaves open, with the gallery's resizing by the body. TWO MORE ON THE HEAVY FEMALE, 2026-11-02 (her in-app prone had stopped at a sphinx, pelvis 57°, skewed 12° to one side with a thigh twisted 40, a 170mm tick on the way, and the hips press that followed moved nothing): (1) THE HIP SWAY IS A STANDING STANCE'S — `ikTrunkPolicy`'s sway reads the standing feet's legs for their straight heights, and a kneeling figure's feet are planted standing-foot pins too, so under her prone descent it rolled the pelvis to "keep a leg from being longer than it is"; it stands down for a drag that began on her knees (`IK_JS_SWAY_ON_KNEES` is the A/B; bit-identical on the eight rigs — the base rigs' prones never met it); with it she lies flat (pelvis 91°) — but her trunk then reared into a cobra, the spine at its extension limits and the head 45cm up, and dropped 40cm in one tick: (2) A RELEASED ARM JOINS THE SOLVE UNDER THE PRONE (`s.hangArmDof` in `ikChooseUnknowns`, the limb's price in `ikPostureDofs`; `IK_JS_NO_HANG_ARM_DOFS`) — a hanging arm whose hand has let go is none of the solve's unknowns, and its floor rows are rows only the BODY can answer: the released arms propped her chest until the hang found a clearing direction, and on three reference rigs they held the hips 2-3cm up (the second generation 166 → 130mm, the oldest 183 → 150, the newest 155 → 133). Exempting the rows (tried first) put the arms 3-8cm through the floor on four rigs — the hang alone cannot keep an arm clear of a floor the trunk is carrying it down onto. The arm's channels, socket to hand, are unknowns at the limb's price while the prone's share is up, referred to where the hang left them (`m_jsPostStepEuler`), so the floor's rows bend the ARM: penetration 0.3-6.7mm on the nine rigs, and the heavy female's chain in the app lies flat at 91° with a worst tick of 43mm, walks the hands forward and comes back up onto all fours (pelvis 24°, the grab 53mm). Three LESSONS for the next round of this kind: a mode read off the pose it changes oscillates — read the TARGET; a hard row released by WEIGHT releases geometrically — bound its PULL and fade the bound; a boolean choice flips — stay in one continuous family.

**AKIMBO — A HAND LAID ON THE HIP TAKES ITS ELBOW OUT** (2026-11-02; the akimbo block of `ikHandLayRows` in armatureiksolverows.cpp, `m_jsAkimbo*` in armature.h, the reference in `ikPostureDofs`; `kHandLayElbowOutDeg` 45, `kHandLayElbowStiffness` 20, `kHandLayHipBand` 0.15 in armatureiksolvetuning.h; `IK_JS_NO_AKIMBO`; found by reading the hands gallery's sheet after the prone round: every hand laid on a hip hung from a STRAIGHT arm, the hand pressed to the hip's side, which nobody does). The hand lay turns the palm to the hip and left the arm's redundancy — the elbow — where the posture terms had it, hanging. Where the laid surface's normal is LATERAL (the hip's side, |n·lateral| > 0.7; the belly's normal is forward, so a hand on the belly is untouched) and the hand lies within `kHandLayHipBand` of the hips' height, the SHOULDER'S ABDUCTION channel — the socket's Side-Side dial, `jointDial(socket, BendSideways)` — gets a REFERENCE that stands the upper arm kHandLayElbowOutDeg from straight down on the side that takes the elbow OUT (`jointDialSweep` says which way the dial's positive side moves the elbow; a degree of channel is a degree of arm), at kHandLayElbowStiffness × the lay's share (× the lateral share × the hips' band), stored by the lay and applied by the posture model the next tick. A soft POSITION ROW on the elbow was the first cut and REJECTED the same hour: the trunk answered it as readily as the shoulder — at 300/m² the head moved 90-185mm on four rigs as the body leaned toward the elbow's target, at 100 the arm came out only 23-32° — where a reference moves its own channel and nothing else. THE REFERENCE ASKS WHAT THE ARM CAN REACH (the same day; `kHandLayElbowReachMarginDeg` 5): a fixed 45° asked more abduction than the forearm could give with the hand on the hip — on a broad-shouldered, narrow-hipped body (two heavy characters of the sweep) the hand lies 2cm INBOARD of the socket and 39cm below it, an elbow 45° out puts the hand 3cm beyond the forearm's length, and the solve paid the difference with the TRUNK: the body leaned and sank toward the hand, the head 53-71mm, the hips 18mm down, the knees 25° (`IK_JS_GRAD_TRACE` on the shoulder: the cursor row's 9.9 against the reference's 7.6, the hand unreachable at the ask), and on the reference rigs the same ask leaned the head 17-25mm. The reference is now the LEAST of 45° and the abduction at which the forearm just reaches the hand's target — the elbow on the upper arm's sphere about the socket within the forearm's length of the target: the law of cosines gives the cap's half-angle about the socket→target direction, and its outboard edge in the lateral-down plane the dial swings in is the most abduction there is — less the margin; where no abduction in that plane reaches, the arm is left alone. The SHARE EASES IN TIME (`kHandLayElbowEaseStep` 0.05 a tick, decaying at the same pace when the lay lets go): a hand grabbed beside the hip at rest sits in the lay's band for a tick or two before it moves, and un-eased the reference nudged every such drag's shoulder at its start. TWO FIXES the first harness run showed: the share was set INSIDE the lay block and stayed at its last value once the lay dropped — the reference followed the hand through the rest of the drag (the all-fours route's hands pass the hips; the bench path, the arm across the chest and the hand on the chest moved by millimetres) — it is this tick's lay's now, reset before the gating; and the other ARM's volume is no hip (a hand laid on the other forearm hanging beside the hip read a lateral normal at the hips' height, and took its elbow out: `armSurface`, the nearest volume under an arm socket). Measured on all eight rigs (`--only hand-lay`, the hip phase): the upper arm 26-35° out (30-38 at the fixed 45, 8-15 before the akimbo), the palm 2-7° off the hip's normal, the head 2-13mm from where it was (17-25 at the fixed 45; 3-7 before the akimbo), the drag's worst tick 9-13mm; the gate reads `≥ 20°` and is INFORMATIONAL where the body's reach — the phase computes the same triangle off the end pose and reports it, `the most abduction the forearm's reach of the hand allows` — is under 30 (the heavy male 9°, the heavy female 13: their arms stand 8° out with the head 3-23mm, where the fixed ask leaned them 53-71); the head gate reads 40. All eight rigs 157/157, every phase but the hip lay's within a millimetre or two of the pre-akimbo baseline (the start transient of a hand grabbed beside the hip: knee tremble and worst steps by tenths of a millimetre, 3 at most); the 23-character sweep 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), the hip lay's head 2-26mm on every character (11-71 at the fixed 45), 22 of 23 with no real failure; in-app 512/512 with the hands gallery's three hip shots changed and looked at (the elbow out, the body upright, both hands on the hips symmetric) and the floor gallery bit-identical to the pre-akimbo run. SOFT SPOTS: a hand placed LOW on the hip (the harness's target, 6cm over the thigh socket: on a short heavy body the hip joint's level) gets little akimbo because the forearm cannot reach with the elbow out — a hand on the iliac crest, higher and further back, would (the gallery's hip shots place it there); a user who wants the arm straight drags the elbow back in (the reference lasts the drag); both hands laid on the hips get it on both sides.

**THE DANGLE — a figure lifted off the floor hangs from the hand** (2026-11-02; `m_jsDangle` eased in `dragIkTick` beside the lift's ease, the references in `ikPostureDofs` (armatureiksolveposture.cpp), the hang's plumb share in `findHangTurns`; `kDangle*` in armatureiksolvetuning.h; `IK_JS_NO_DANGLE` is the A/B; the user: "realistic, human-like, natural posing is the key"). Lifted by a hand past her reach the figure came off the floor and hung from it — and kept her STANDING pose in the air: the legs apart in the stance, the feet level, the free arm out at the A-pose's 46°, a mannequin on a hook (`28_side_lifted_by_hand` read as a hang from the side and gave it away from the front). Suspended (`IkRig::suspended`), the body HANGS: the legs come together and hang plumb from the hips, the thighs `kDangleThighForwardDeg` 5° ahead of plumb, the knees soften to `kDangleKneeDeg` 10°, the feet point `kDangleFootDeg` 25°, and the free arm hangs plumb (the hang's plumb share — the tilted trunk's — reads the dangle too). REFERENCES, at `kDangleStiffness` 20/rad² × a share that eases in at `kDangleStep` 0.03 a tick while the rig has her suspended (half a second) and out again at the same pace once she lands, so nothing pops either way (what a dangling body does is GIVEN, as the kneeling foot's lie and the akimbo's elbow are). The THIGH is referred through its two swing dials — the Side-Side and the Bend, found by structure like the akimbo's — to the channel values that stand it plumb and a little forward, read off its direction and each dial's sweep this tick (an iterated reference toward a fixed world direction: the error shrinks tick by tick); the KNEE's fold channel to 10° of flexion; the ANKLE's Bend dial to 25° pointed (the dial's positive side is "pointed" on every generation). The bones by structure: the rig's `legOf` names the bone BELOW the thigh socket (the socket is a girdle joint to the rig and has no leg of its own: the twist bone where a generation cuts the thigh's twist out, the shin where the twist helpers hang beside it), so the THIGH is that bone's parent unless the leg hangs straight off the root or the pelvis bone — found in a first pass over the bones (a single pass testing `legOf(b) == b` named the twist bone or the shin, whose dials are not the thigh's, and nothing moved; then `legOf(thigh)` is −1 and a test inside the loop never reached it); the KNEE is a leg joint above the foot with a fold channel; the ANKLE the foot-class joint whose parent is none. Measured on the reference rigs (`--only suspension`, gated: thighs ≤ 8° from plumb, knee ≥ 5°, the free arm ≤ 15°): the thighs 3.5-5° from plumb (the forward lean; 7-13 before, the stance's splay), the knees 10°, the feet 25° (the oldest generation's 31.6, its own), the free arm 6.8° from plumb (the hang's 0.12 of outward lean; 46 before); the lift's worst tick and the hand's resting miss unchanged; lowered back she lands and stands as before (`suspend-lower`: the hip 0.3-5mm off its start height, the hand on the cursor to 0.001mm). In the app (`gallery_limbs.txt`'s new `28b_front_lifted_by_hand`: `hang.lThigh/rThigh < 10`, `hang.rShldr < 25`, the knee's and the foot's channels): legs together and plumb, knees soft, feet pointed, the free arm at her side, the head level — looked at. All eight rigs 157/157 with only the suspension phases moved (the lowered-back landing calmer: knee tremble 41 → 13mm); the 23-character sweep 31 near-misses and ONE real (the known mirror asymmetry), the dangle's thighs 2.5-5.9° from plumb and the free arm 6.85° on every character; in-app 517/517 with the side lift shot changed and looked at. SOFT SPOTS: the trunk hangs rigid from the held arm (no sway, no sag of the shoulders); the dangle is one pose for every body (no gravity model: a heavier figure hangs the same); a figure lifted by the HIPS (Ctrl, the figure move) is carried as posed, by design, and never suspended.

The `Camera` (`scene/camera.{h,cpp}`) is a pure-GLM orbit camera; its projection negates `[1][1]` for Vulkan's flipped clip-space Y on top of the project-wide `GLM_FORCE_DEPTH_ZERO_TO_ONE`. **View hotkeys (Blender's numpad convention)** live on it: `setAxisView(AxisView)` snaps yaw/pitch (yaw 0 = the eye at +Z = Front, the side a default figure faces; +90° = Right, from +X; Top/Bottom sit AT the pitch clamp, which was raised from 89° to 89.9° — `kMaxPitch` — so the vertical views read as exactly vertical while lookAt stays numerically clean, and a following orbit drag continues smoothly from the clamp; Top leans a hair toward +Z and Bottom (yaw 180°) toward −Z, which is what puts the scene's front at the bottom of the screen in both), `flip()` adds 180° of yaw, and `frame(center, radius)` re-targets and sets the distance so the bounding sphere fills the narrower field (`VulkanRenderer::frameSelected` feeds it `Scene::framingBounds` — the selected model's world AABB, else the union of all). **The axis views are ORTHOGRAPHIC** (`Camera::setOrthographic`; `rebuildProjection` emits `glm::ortho` with half-height = distance · tan(fov/2), so the framing at the target plane survives the switch and dolly still zooms — dolly/frame rebuild the projection while orthographic); `orbit()` switches back to perspective (Blender's auto-perspective — the "lock" lasts until the user turns the view), `reset()` too, `flip()` keeps the mode. In an orthographic SIDE view the floor plane is edge-on and the grid shader's ray/plane intersection hits nothing, so `Scene::record` draws the floor's one visible line — the y = 0 trace of the view plane through the target along the camera's right vector — into the line overlay (the user's reference for these views was a DCC's fixed side camera: pure elevation, floor as a line). The keys are bound APP-WIDE as the View menu QActions' shortcuts (the user wanted them to work whichever panel has focus; text fields keep their digits/periods because a focused QLineEdit/spin box accepts the ShortcutOverride for text keys), each with two bindings: the number-row key — which the shortcut map also matches for the NumLock-ON numpad digit, since it retries a keypad key without its KeypadModifier — and the navigation key the numpad sends with NumLock OFF (End/PageDown/Home/PageUp for 1/3/7/9, Clear for 5, Delete or Comma for ".") bound WITH the KeypadModifier so the real End/Home/Delete keys stay untouched. **5 = the Home view** (`Camera::reset`, the same as the strip's Home button — the user's choice over Blender's ortho toggle). `VulkanWindow::keyPressEvent` keeps the same keys as the in-viewport fallback (like Ctrl+Z), folding the NumLock-off keypad codes onto the digits, skipped while a button drag owns the camera or pose. Natural next increments: more importers (glTF/USD) — each a new `MeshImporter` under `import/<fmt>/` plus one `ImporterRegistry` line — and richer materials (PBR map slots beyond the diffuse texture — normal/metal/rough — which need a richer source format than OBJ). Each slots into the existing `import/` registry, `Scene`/`Mesh`, and `VulkanTexture` modules without disturbing the others.

**Shared widgets** (`src/widgets/`): app-standard custom controls reusable across panels. `DragNumberBox` (`dragnumberbox.{h,cpp}`) is **the standard control for any bounded scalar parameter row** — use it instead of a `QSlider`/`QDoubleSpinBox` pair. It's a DCC-style scrub field: a left-to-right range fill (progress-bar style, `(value-min)/(max-min)`, in the app's muted accent `#314D7A`) behind a centered value label; press+horizontal-drag scrubs the value, and a plain click (no drag) opens inline type-in editing (Enter/focus-out commits, Esc cancels). The widget cursor is the left-right arrow (set once in the ctor) so scrubbability is visible on hover — the one deliberate exception to the app's arrow-cursor convention, since nothing else about a filled bar says "drag me"; the inline editor keeps its own I-beam. API mirrors the `QDoubleSpinBox` essentials (`setRange`/`setSingleStep`/`setDecimals`/`setValue`/`valueChanged`), so swapping one in is mechanical — see `EnvironmentPanel::addValueRow` for the row-builder pattern. It paints itself in C++ (no QSS rules needed); its colors are `Q_PROPERTY`s (the `AssetTreeView` pattern) with defaults matching the app palette (the inline editor's stylesheet is built from the same properties at every `beginEdit()`), so a container can re-theme it via `qproperty-fillColor:` etc. if ever needed; a lost mouse release (a popup grab mid-scrub, alt-tab) is treated as a release, so the Environment panel's depth-counted undo bracket can't be left open for the session. Two opt-ins came with the Transform tab (2026-09-30): **`setOrigin(value)`** — the value the fill starts FROM and the scrub's step grid is anchored AT (the minimum until set): a dial that runs both ways from a rest value (−7..100 about 0) is empty at rest and fills toward the value on either side, a tick at the top and bottom edge marking the origin, and a scrub lands on origin + n·step (anchored at a fractional minimum the grid stepped over 0) — and a DISABLED look (no fill, dimmed value, no scrub cursor; disabling mid-gesture ends the scrub or the type-in, so a listener's bracket closes); a value that rounds to zero reads "0", never "-0". `MenuPickerButton` (`menupickerbutton.{h,cpp}`) is **the standard dropdown**: a `QPushButton` opening an exclusive, checkable `QMenu` (`setItems`, silent `setCurrentIndex`, `setDetachedCaption` for a caption that is none of the items, `currentChanged(int)` on a user pick, `pickerMenu()` for per-menu styling, `setCaptionPrefix` for the strip's left inset). It exists because a styled `QComboBox` popup paints its item states inconsistently and repositions itself (the shader-picker note in the viewport section); the viewport's shading/view pickers and the Environment tab's backdrop Mode field are all instances — the LOOK stays with the caller (object name + QSS).

**Translatable strings** (2026-10-05; PR #33 by @DmitryAkDev, merged with two fixes): every user-visible string goes through `tr()` — `QObject::tr()` in a free function — so the interface CAN be translated. NO translator is installed and no catalog ships: the app is English-only. The PRs stacked on it (#34 a Language picker and the catalog build wiring, #35 an LLM catalog pipeline with a Russian catalog, #36 a Russian manual) were CLOSED as "not yet": a second language means a catalog refresh, a proofread in that language and a manual regeneration every release, while the interface text and the manual still change with nearly every one. The rules for new UI text: **ONE `tr()` PER MESSAGE** — a long sentence is adjacent string literals inside one call, never `tr("…") + tr("…")` (the PR had 25 messages cut at their line breaks; each fragment is then translated alone, and the catalog built on them read as broken grammar — rejoined at the merge); a string kept in a `QString` constant is invisible to the extractor and frozen before any translator exists (`Constants::TERM_COL_*` and the breadcrumb's idle-prompt constant are gone, written as `tr()` at their uses); the Qt-free picker tables (`kShadeModes`, `kViewEntries`) keep their `const char*` names and are looked up at display time with `QCoreApplication::translate("ViewportStrip", name)`, a no-op today — and since 2026-10-26 (PR #38) every one of the 25 names is listed once more in `viewportstrip.cpp`'s `kPickerNoops` as a `QT_TRANSLATE_NOOP("ViewportStrip", …)`, the source literal an extractor needs, with a `static_assert` on the count against `kShadeModeCount + kViewEntryCount` (an added mode or view fails to compile until it is listed; a RENAME is caught by nothing — change both); and nothing should be FOUND by a translated label where an id will do (`PreferencesDialog::selectTab` still matches the tab's label, which `MenuManager` passes as its own `tr("Assets")` — it holds only while both contexts translate alike). `tools/manual/check_manual.py` reads labels through the wrapper (the PR's wrapping had blinded it: 27 labels seen → 0 with the check still green; 42 now, the entries with an icon included — which surfaced the shading picker's tooltip, since added to the manual). WHEN LOCALIZATION COMES BACK, the review of #34-#36 found (each reproduced): a catalog needs `language="…"` on its `<TS>` element or Qt 6.11's `lupdate` refuses to update it; `lupdate` marked the picker names — no source literal backed them then — as vanished and `lrelease` dropped them (answered by #38's `kPickerNoops`, above); the CMake fallback for a bare `lrelease` handed file paths to `add_dependencies` and failed at generate time; `qt6_add_translations` failed in a checkout whose real path contains a space; Qt's own standard-button translations were not loaded; and nothing flagged a translated manual gone stale against the English pages.

**Constants** (`src/constants.h`): shared UI dimensions, colors, and timing values live here rather than being duplicated as magic numbers across widgets/delegates — add new shared layout/color/timing constants here instead of inlining them. (Viewport-internal tuning that no other subsystem needs — input sensitivity, frames-in-flight — currently lives next to its use in the viewport files; promote a value here only once something outside `src/viewport/` needs it.) `APP_VERSION` is stamped by CMake from `project(PoseStudio VERSION …)` (the `POSESTUDIO_VERSION` compile definition) — a release bumps ONE number plus the CHANGELOG. `COLOR_ACCENT` (`#5b87cc`) is THE accent (hover borders, check glyphs, the tooltip's extension text, the selection outline's linear twin in `postprocess.h`); `COLOR_ACCENT_BLUE` (`#497fd4`) is only the Asset Manager's drop line and drop highlight.
